## Chapter 1

### Notes

#### Work Notes

TW: Thoughts of infanticide, major character death, Ozai being an abusive asshole, and attempted underage non-con.

### Text

---

In the dead of night, during a winter storm, Princess Ursa gives birth to a baby girl. A girl without the spark. Ozai is tempted to throw the baby over the walls, but he catches the eye of his wife. He looks to the babe. Spark or no, she is a child of royal blood. She will be beautiful no doubt, especially if she takes after her mother. At the very least, she might be useful to secure a marriage. A vessel for a stronger seed. He lets his crying wife keep the child.

&#45;--

Iroh brings his son, Lu Ten to see their new family member. Ursa smiles tiredly at them and passes the sleeping baby to the eleven-year old. Lu Ten looks to his father and aunt with trepidation.

“I don’t want to hurt her.” He whispers.

Ursa smiles at the sweet boy, “You won’t just make sure to support her head. She’s too little to hold it up herself.”

With slow careful hands, Lu Ten takes the girl in his arms. He looks down, and he loses a piece of his heart to the sleeping babe. “She’s so tiny.” He whispers trying not to wake the baby.

Iroh grins at his boy proudly and places a hand on his shoulder. “She’ll grow bigger. Don’t worry. But you must protect her alright? You are her older cousin. She’ll look up to you.”

Lu Ten nods resolutely. “I promise, Father, Aunt Ursa. I’ll look after Zuko.”

Ursa and Iroh share a knowing look. “I know you will.” Ursa says. “Now why don’t you sit down? You can keep holding Zuko for as long as you want.”

“Well just as soon as I hold her.” Iroh teases.

Lu Ten brings his cousin close to his chest and sticks his tongue out at his father. “No. You’ll wait your turn, Father.”

“Ugh, the disrespect Ursa.”

“Please, Iroh. You love it.”

&#45;--

Princess Zuko is a sickly babe. There are doctors constantly prodding at her and giving her stinky bitter medicines. Lu Ten watches each of them carefully and holds onto his cousin’s little hand. After a particularly bad fever, the physician tries to prepare the family for the inevitable.

“Princess Ursa, I’m sorry, but…it doesn’t look good.” The doctor says sadly. “I fear she may not live to see her first birthday.”

Lu Ten’s fists shakes, and he pushes the doctor, much to the surprise of his family.

“No!” he shouts. “Zuko isn’t going to die. She’s strong. She’s got Agni’s flame burning in her. She won’t give up. And I won’t give up on her either!”

The physician stutters an apology, but she can’t just give them false hope.

“It’s not false hope!” Lu Ten yells. “It’s the *truth*.”

Iroh brings his son back. “Lu Ten, enough. The doctor is just trying to tell us the odds. Do not make this harder on your aunt.”

The boy swallows and turns to his aunt. She looks pale and sallow. Her famed beauty is overshadowed by worry and grief as she holds her child tight. Lu Ten goes to her and places a hand on her arm. “Auntie, don’t give up on Zuko. She’s a fighter.”

Ursa looks at Lu Ten with shining eyes. She puts a cold hand on his cheek. “Okay. I won’t. Our little princess won’t be giving up so I we won’t either.”

Lu Ten beams and kisses the baby’s hot forehead. “Fight, cousin. I know you’ll pull through.”

And Zuko does. She sees her first birthday. Then her second.

By that time, she hardly gets sick anymore, and Princess Ursa delivers another child.

This time, the child is born in the heat of summer, just as the sun is rising. Ozai holds her up to Agni’s rays and smiles. There’s a magnificent spark in the baby. One that’s only been seen since Sozin. He names her Azula.

Zuko doesn’t understand why her father looks at her with such giddy eyes. They’ve never been directed at her. But still, the young princess is happy to have a sibling.

“Are you excited, Zuko?” Lu Ten asks. Zuko nods hesitantly.

“I’m scawred doh.” She admits. “Wat if I’m bad?”

Lu Ten kneels down to her level, “You won’t be. You’ll be an amazing big sister.”

“How do you know?”

“Because you’re already an amazing little cousin. And I promise I’ll help you be a great big sister.” He says holding up his pinky.

Zuko stares at it quizzically but holds up her own pinky. Lu Ten laughs and gently hooks their pinkies together.

“This is a pinky promise.” He explains. “It’s something you only do if it’s a *serious* promise and you mustn’t break it.”

Zuko nods and looks intently at their hooked pinkies. “Okay, I pwomise.”

&#95;&#42;&#95;&#42;&#95;

Azula thankfully didn’t have the problems Zuko did in her early years. She’s a strong babe that only grows stronger as the years pass. She has her first flame at two, much to Ozai’s delight. Zuko is already five and still without a flame. Something her father never ceases to forget.

“Bring a flame, child.” He demands.

Zuko goes through the kata again and again. But nothing comes out. She’s crying inconsolably at this point, but Ozai only sneers at her.

“Pathetic.” He hisses gripping the crying girl’s hair. “Your sister can shoot a powerful flame, and she’s only three. You are five and not even smoke has come out. I should have thrown you off the palace walls. You are a disgrace to this family.”

“P-Please Father, I’ll do better. Please stop!” She cries. But it only earns her a slap.

“You can demand things, when you’ve shown me what a powerful bender you are.” Ozai says, and then he leaves the room in a swirl of dark robes leaving the girl crying on the floor. Two servants come in tentatively and help the poor girl up.

“Come princess.” One of the maids say quietly. “Let’s get you cleaned up. Your cousin is coming to see you.”

It’s enough to cheer her up slightly. She wipes the tears and snot with her sleeve and slowly follows the servants to the bathroom.

They do their best to comb the tangles out of her abused hair. They try not to wince as their princess whimpers at a particularly tough knot. They can’t do anything about the red mark on her cheek, but they secretly hope her cousin and Uncle find out.

&#95;&#42;&#95;&#42;&#95;

Lu Ten is bouncing eagerly in the room, waiting for his cousin to come. He’s on break from the Academy, and he can’t wait to play with his dear cousin. He needs to take as much time with her as he can. He’s sixteen, and he’ll be expected to join the military soon. When that happens, he’ll hardly have time to see his darling cousin.

The guards announce her arrival and Lu Ten shoots up ready to pick her up and twirl her around, but something catches his eye.

A mark on her cheek. Red and angry. Something in the normally sweet boy snaps, and his fire is roaring through him.

He goes over and carefully graces his fingers over it. Zuko winces at the touch and tries to shy away from his hand.

Anger.

*Fury.*

*Who touched Zuko?*

He breathes deep, like his father taught him, and releases his breath. Steam comes out.

“What. Happened.” He asks. Zuko bows her head and shakes it.

“N-nothing.”

“Zuko.”

The princess’s lips tremble. Her eyes start to water, and Lu Ten forget the anger for a moment and draws his little cousin close.

“Please.” He whispers. “Tell me. I’ll beat them up for you.”

“…it was Father.” She admits quietly.

Lu Ten feels the breath leave his chest. His uncle. Her *Father* hurt her. How could he have missed it? He always knew his Uncle Ozai is a distant asshole, but he never thought—he never *imagined*…

“Does he do it often?” he asks.

Zuko burrows herself deeper into Lu Ten’s robes. “Mama…he—he hits mama too.”

*Fuck*.

Lu Ten nods and holds her close. “Come. Let’s go to my room. I need to write a letter. Do you want some daifuku? I think the cooks just made a batch.”

“Yes please.” She answers with a bright smile.

&#95;&#42;&#95;&#42;&#95;

By the next week, Ursa is helping Zuko pack her bags. Azula is pouting next to her mother.

“Why's Zuzu leefing?” she demands.

Ursa smiles and strokes her youngest daughter’s hair. “She’s going to train with Lu Ten at Master Piandao’s place.”

Azula didn’t seem to like this answer and stamps her feet, making sparks rise up. “Not fair! I go too!”

Zuko smiles and hugs her sister, “You need to stay here and do more fire bending training. But don’t worry, I’ll come back for breaks and then we can play again.”

The youngest princess doesn’t seem appeased, but she shrugs. “Fine.”

Ursa brings her oldest child close. She buries her nose in the dark locks and breath in the scent of her child. She’ll miss her, but she’s glad her cousin is taking her away from Ozai even if it’s only for a little bit.

“I’ll miss you, my little turtleduck.” She whispers.

“I’ll miss you too, Mama.”

Azula flings herself between the two. “’Zula too!” she yells. Ursa laughs and brings them both close. She burns this perfect moment in her memory. Somewhere Ozai can’t touch.

&#95;&#42;&#95;&#42;&#95;

Master Piandao is everything her tutors and father are not. He’s kind and patient. He critiques her movements but is encouraging. She blossoms under his tutelage. Lu Ten grins every time he sees her swing those dao blades effortlessly.

“That was amazing, Zuko!” he exclaims holding her up after she executes a particularly difficult move.

Piandao sighs exasperatedly at the prince’s actions. “Prince Lu Ten. How many times do I have to tell you: stop disrupting Princess Zuko’s practice. You would be much better if you’d spend more time on your blade than on your cousin’s side.”

Lu Ten laughs sheepishly. “Sorry, Master. It’s just she’s so amazing. Did you see her!?” he says holding her up to the master.

Piandao’s lips twitch slightly, but he schools his features. “Yes, I did. Now put her down and go back to Fat.”

The prince pouts, making the little princess laugh. “Go, Lu Ten.” She urges. “I wanna beat you when you’re at your best.”

Lu Ten laughs. “Alright alright. For you, my sweet cousin.”

The swordsmaster sighs and shakes his head as he watches the boy leave. He turns to the young girl. “Let’s learn another set. I think you’re ready.”

“Will I be able to spar with Lu Ten soon?” she asks eagerly.

Piandao laughs, “You’ll be able to thoroughly *trounce* your cousin soon.”

There’s a distant, “Yes!” from the other side of the court, and Piandao stops himself from throwing a sword at the idiot.

&#45;--

At night when they lay on the soft grass and look up at the stars, Lu Ten turns to his cousin.

“Do you like it here?” he asks.

Zuko nods eagerly. “I love it here. Master Piandao is so amazing!”

Lu Ten smiles, “Good.” He takes his cousin’s small hand. “When I’m Fire Lord, I’ll make sure no one ever hurts you again.”

Zuko’s eyes are wide and full of fragile hope, “You mean it?”

He holds up his pinky. “I promise.”

&#95;&#42;&#95;&#42;&#95;

For a month during the summertime, Zuko and Lu Ten go to Ember Island and spend time with their family. They catch up on everything not said in their letters, swim in the beach, watch the Ember Island Players, and explore the island. Sometimes Azula is with them, but most days she’s with Father training or learning by his side. Father hasn’t even glanced at Zuko’s way during the time that she’s back.

On some level, she’s glad, but another part of her is sad that her father still won’t acknowledge her. Even her Uncle comes by and spends a few days with them before he leaves again.

“I hear from Master Piandao that you’re learning at a truly exceptional rate.” Iroh praises. Lu Ten immediately jumps in and brags about the wonderful progress his ‘amazingly talented cousin’ has done in year she’s been under Piandao.

Zuko blushes and smacks her cousin’s arm. “Lu Ten, stop. It's not like that.”

“You’re right.” Lu Ten nods seriously. “She’s a true sword prodigy, Auntie.”

Zuko bursts into a fire-lily red while the adults laugh indulgently. Ursa pats her daughter’s head. “I’m so glad you’re having fun, my love.”

The girl smiles and nods, “I am. I’m learning so much. I love it there.”

“Because I’m there right?” Lu Ten teases.

Zuko nods eagerly, “Yes! I always miss you when you leave, and I love being with you, Lu Ten.”

Said boy places a hand over his heart. Eyes shining at his dear cousin. “My heart.” he whispers before pulling the little princess in a bone-crushing hug.

Iroh and Ursa laugh at the display.

“When she starts seeing boys, he’ll be insufferable.” Iroh says to Ursa.

Lu Ten pops his head up and gasps, putting his hands over Zuko’s ears. “NO! Don’t even say that Father! She’ll never date anyone. *Ever*.”

“What’s dating?” Zuko asks.

Lu Ten nearly screeches, but Iroh puts a hand over his son’s mouth and smiles, “It’s when you take time to know a boy before you marry him.”

“Oh.” Zuko says. “So like…being friends with them?”

Iroh nods. “Yes, you must learn what traits you’d want in the person you marry. You should be good friends before you become married.”

The girl smiles, “That sounds nice. I think I’d want to marry someone who’s really nice, likes turtleducks, can spar with me, hmmm what else…? Oh he should be funny and…”

Lu Ten breaks free from his Uncle’s hold and interrupts, “And he needs to be a boomerang master! A master strategist! Be good at poetry! Have a space sword! Taller than you by a lot! And—and be friends with a flying lemur *and* a saber-tooth lion moose!”

Ursa and Iroh laugh, while Zuko frowns.

“Lu Ten, that’s impossible.” She pouts. “That’s an imaginary guy.”

“No no!” Lu Ten denies. “Don’t you see, he’s the perfect man for you! You’re the princess of the Fire Nation. You need deserve a man who’s just as amazing as you. Those are just the tests he needs to pass to show that he’s got the brains and mental capacity to stand by your side.”

“What does being a boomerang master have anything to do with that?” Zuko asks wrinkling her nose.

“Uh…he’s good at math. It’s very important to be good at numbers and calculations. Boomerang throwing needs to have a lot of mental and quick calculations.” Lu Ten bluffs.

But the princess seems to muse on this carefully. “Okay. That makes sense. I do have to do a lot of math so he should know it too.”

The prince sighs in relief that his sweet cousin bought the ridiculous bluff. The two adults try to stifle their laughter in their sleeves.

“So then I’ll marry someone who’s nice, funny, likes turtleducks, can spar with me, a boomerang master, a master strategist, a good poet, has a spacesword, is taller than me, and be friends with a flying lemur and a…” “—a saber-tooth lion moose.” “A saber-tooth lion moose.”

Zuko nods and holds her pinky up to her cousin. “Okay when I find him, you have to *promise* to accept my husband. Deal?”

Lu Ten smiles and hooks his pinky to her, “Deal. I’ll give that man my blessings.” After several shovel talk of course. “Father, you and Auntie have witnessed this binding promise. Zuko won’t marry a man unless he fits all the criteria mentioned above.”

The adults school their expressions into a serious one and nod.

“Yes, we bear witness to Lu Ten’s Ridiculously High Standards for Zuko’s husband.” Ursa replies with twitching lips.

“I’m excited to meet this young man. He seems like quite an interesting character.” Iroh says jovially.

Zuko nods, “Yes, Lu Ten will lead me to a good husband.” She says smiling up at her cousin who’s sweating bullets at this point.

“Yup, yes. I’ll make sure you have the *best* husband. You’ll love him so much.”

The princess hugs the teen and whispers, “But you’ll always be the *best boy* I’ll love.”

Lu Ten’s eyes grow hot and he looks away, trying to keep himself from bawling like a baby at his dear cousin’s declaration. To the side, his father and aunt wipe their own tears discretely.

&#95;&#42;&#95;&#42;&#95;

“I don’t like this.” Zuko says pouting over the many many scrolls spread on the table.

Lu Ten laughs. “I don’t either, but I need to learn them if I want to be a good Fire Lord.”

The princess is contemplative. “When you’re Fire Lord, will I be able to help you?”

“Of course! You’ll be my favorite advisor!” he proclaims. Zuko nods and then starts reaching for the scrolls. The lessons are hard, but she wants to be the best advisor for her cousin.

&#95;&#42;&#95;&#42;&#95;

Zuko turns seven, and she gets her first spark. But the occasion is overshadowed by the dread that her cousin is leaving for war. She hugs him tightly trying to memorize how her cousin feels. Lu Ten hugs her equally tight and buries his nose in her soft hair.

“I’ll be back. So make sure you take care of yourself, okay?” he says wiping away her tears.

Zuko nods and holds out her pinky, “I promise. But you still have to write me often okay?”

Lu Ten hooks his pinky in hers. “I promise.”

It’s lonely in the palace without her cousin and Uncle. She’s no longer allowed to go Master Piandao’s home for sword practice, but he’ll come to her once in a while to keep up on her training. It’s not as nice as the times she would spend in his home with Fat, but it’s better than nothing.

Azula is slowing becoming colder to her older sister. They fight more, and the little girl would often hurt her older sister. Small bursts of flames licking her skin. Pushing her off rooftops. Throwing sharp objects at her.

Fire bending practice is not easier. The masters are relentless, and her Father is adamant that she catches up with her younger sister. Sometimes during his private lessons, he’ll hit her. He hits her more often now that Uncle and Lu Ten are gone.

The only good things are the letters from Lu Ten. He’s always making her smile. Keeping her spirits up. But sometimes, it sounds like he’s straining over there too. Like he’s trying to be brave. So Zuko writes him long letters. Telling him everything that’s happened. The new turtleducks that hatched. The hot summer days at the palace. A funny crab she saw at the beach. She hopes that it’ll make her cousin happy. That it’ll remind him of home.

Her eighth birthday passes and Master Piandao sends her a new pair of dao blades. The ones forged by both her master and her cousin. She holds them up reverently and sees the gleam of the blades in the sunlight. They’re beautiful. She gives them a cursory swing and marvels at the balance. It’s *perfect*.

&#95;&#42;&#95;&#42;&#95;

She winces as the needle pricks her finger again. Her mother tuts gently and pats the well of blood away.

“Careful darling.” She says. “And be patient. Embroidery must be done carefully and slowly. With patience.”

Zuko huffs and goes again.

She doesn’t particularly like embroidery, but she wants to make something for her cousin for his th

20 birthday. He made her the blades, it’s only fitting that she makes him something as well. She thought about giving him calligraphy or a painting, but she also wants to give him something a little useful. So she asks her mother and tutors to help her with embroidery.

In the end her dragon is a little lopsided and the stitches aren’t perfect, but her mother says that it’s a beautiful work on the robe. Zuko smiles, the ache in her fingers go away at the praise. Her cousin apparently thinks so as well because he writes her a letter with tear stains on them.

*My Dearest Cousin,*

*I can’t believe you made this for me!!! I’m never taking it off. It’s beautiful. I’ve never seen* *anything so amazing in my life. I never knew you could embroider so well! I’m so so proud of my* *beautifully talented cousin. I’ve showed it off to everyone who sees it and they all say it’s amazing* *work!*

*I’m sorry I can’t be there with you. I miss you so much. But don’t worry, I’m taking care of myself* *and so is Father. We’re going to be doing a big campaign soon, and I hope that as soon we’re done* *we’ll be able to go home. War is…hard. I hope you never have to go through it. I’m going to help* *end this so you won’t ever have to. We’re so close to conquering everything. Then I’ll be able to* *stay with you forever.*

*But enough about that! How is your sword training going? How is your mother and sister? Are you* *eating well? Did the newest turtleduck brood hatch? Ugh I miss the cook’s sesame balls and* *daifuku. All we have is preserved fruit for dessert. I don’t think I can take another dried* *persimmon.*

*Take care of yourself.*

*Your favorite cousin,*

*Lu Ten*

Zuko starts embroidering more things to send to her cousin and Uncle. Her stitches get better, neater. She even starts stitching small prayers of protection and blessing in the fabric. In each long thread, she puts in her prayers to Agni. Her hopes that her cousin and Uncle will stay safe. With each letter from Lu Ten and Uncle Iroh, there’s a breath of relief that they’re both safe.

For her Father’s birthday, she gives him a scarf with an embroidered phoenix at the end and bright flames coming out. He just sneers at her gift and tells her to do something useful with her time.

It was the last time she offered him a gift.

But she doesn’t stop embroidering. It’s become a way to de-stress and to express her thoughts with thread. She’s gifted many handkerchiefs and scarves to her mother and Master Piandao. Once, she even gives a robe to her grandfather. He looks at it approvingly and nods. “It’s lovely, Zuko. You have real talent.”

During the next feast, he wears the robes she embroidered for him, and it makes her heart flutter with pride and happiness.

She tries to give Azula a robe with a mighty dragon spitting great plumes of flame, but the girl scoffs and burns it.

“There.” She says. “Those are real flames.”

Since Azula is out, she starts giving the to the guards and servants. As thanks and a token for their hard work. They all smile gratefully at the wonderful princess and keep it safe on them.

&#95;&#42;&#95;&#42;&#95;

Iroh succeeds in destroying the outer walls. He sends them a letter and gifts. The girls both get a dagger and a doll. Zuko smiles at the blade and caresses the beautiful doll. Azula seems to take more interest in the knife and throws the doll away. She smiles to herself and asks, “If Uncle dies, won’t Father be next on the throne?”

Zuko is horrified by the thought and clutches her doll close. Ursa stares at Azula wide-eyed.

“Azula that’s a terrible thing to say!” she scolds. “You should never wish that on anyone.”

“But am I right?”

Ursa doesn’t give a response, she only scolds her again and tells her to go to her room.

Azula pouts and stomps off. On the way, she’s burning the doll’s head. Zuko is careful to keep her own doll away from Azula’s hands.

&#95;&#42;&#95;&#42;&#95;

Lu Ten dies.

It’s a devastating blow for her. She cries for days. Screaming at Agni. Screaming at the servants. Screaming at the world for taking the closest thing to a big brother she had.

In the hallway, Azula says how Uncle is weak, mocking the man’s loss and their cousin’s death.

It’s the first time Zuko attacks Azula. She throws the biggest flames she’s ever produced at her sister. Azula is caught off guard, but she still manages to swipe them away.

“Oh? Did I hit a soft spot sister?” she asks. “What are you mad about? Lu Ten? How he’s such a good big brother?”

“Shut up, Azula.”

“Well he’s not such a good big brother now is he?” she continues. “He’s dead. Dead from some lowly Earth Kingdom soldiers. They probably smashed his brains and—!”

“SHUT UP!!” Zuko roars and takes out the blades from a nearby guard. Said guard shouts for the girls to stop, but Zuko isn’t listening. Fire is burning through her, shooting through the blade and swinging wildly at Azula. But her little sister keeps laughing mockingly. They burn most of the hallway before Ozai, Ursa and Azulon come and stop their fight.

“What is the meaning of this?” Azulon asks imperiously. The girls freeze in their tracks. The both turn to their grandfather and Zuko puts her blades down to bow low.

“G-grandfather.” She whispers, but Azula sits up first.

“Zuko attacked me first!” she accuses. “She went mad and took the guard’s swords to attack me.”

Ozai growls and moves to punish his eldest, but Azulon stops him. He looks to his trembling granddaughter. From what he knows and sees of her, Zuko is quiet and much softer than her sister. It would take a lot for the normally placid girl to attack so ferociously, and he has a feeling he knows what it is.

“What happened Zuko?”

Zuko flinches and stutters, “I-I did attack her first, but only because Azula was…she was making fun of Uncle Iroh’s suffering. She was speaking ill of Lu Ten and I couldn’t—I couldn’t stand it.”

Azulon nods and turns to Azula with hard eyes. “Azula, be *ashamed* of yourself. Your uncle has suffered a great loss. One that I hope you never have to feel. Do not speak ill of those who have died for our Nation. You are not yet a blooded warrior. Prodigy or not you have not experienced war.”

Azula is gritting her teeth and shaking at his point. She’s never been scolded by her grandfather before. He’s usually praising her progress or just not there. There’s a bubbling feeling of molten lava in her stomach. She hates it.

Azulon then turns to Zuko. “And you should not be attacking so recklessly. You could have damaged more than just you or your sister. If you must restore someone’s honor, you do it in a proper duel. One under Agni’s eye.”

Zuko nods, “Yes Grandfather.”

“Good. Now go to your rooms. I don’t want to see anymore fighting from the two of you. Ozai, you said you wanted to speak to me? Then let’s go to the throne room.”

The adults leave and the guards are left to escort the princesses to their rooms.

&#45;--

Later that night, Azula comes in smug and vicious.

“Dad’s going to kill you.” She says in a sing-song voice.

Zuko’s heart leaps to her throat. She’d always known her father wanted to kill her in the past, but she thought he was over that by now.

“You’re lying.” She hisses, “And shouldn’t you be in your room?”

Azula scoffs. “Why? Because *Grandfather* said so? He’s an old man. He’s powerless before me.”

“He’s still our grandfather. We owe him our respect.”

Azula laughs. “Oh sister. Would you still say that when it’s grandfather who ordered Father to kill you?”

Zuko pales. No that can’t be. Azula is lying. “You’re lying! Now go away.” she shouts.

The other girl laughs meanly and flounces off. “Whatever you say.” She says. As she leaves the room, she sings, *“Dad’s going to kill you. Dad’s going to kill you.”*

Zuko pulls the sheets over her head and breathes deeply. *Azula always lies*.

But in the very early hours of the dawn, her mother is at her side. She’s hugging her close and saying how much she loves her. And then she’s gone. Like a dream.

That morning grandfather is found dead, and Father is now Fire Lord. Zuko stands by her father’s side and feels stones drop in her stomach as she watches the Fire Sages crown him. There’s a mad glint in her father’s eyes as he looks over the cheering crowd. It makes her hair stand on end, but she pastes on a smile for the audience.

&#95;&#42;&#95;&#42;&#95;

Uncle comes back a half a year later. He’s a broken shell of a man; he’s gained weight, there’s more grey in his hair, but he’s her uncle. The last link to Lu Ten. So, she goes to his room, serves him tea and tells him stories. She sings songs, reads to him. Takes him to the turtleduck pond. Shows him the letters Lu Ten sends her.

Slowly the man gets better. He’s perking up, smiling more easily and hugging her fondly. He’s starting to look like her old uncle again.

Through it all, she holds her tears.

On Lu Ten’s first death anniversary, she gives him an embroidered cloth with Lu Ten’s portrait etched in thread. He cries and hugs her close.

“Thank you, my dear niece.” He whispers. “I just miss him so much.”

“I miss him too, Uncle.” She admits.

Iroh takes puts his weathered hands on her cheek, “Even in the worst of it, you always brought a smile to his face. Every time he pulls out one of your letters, one of your embroidered work, he smiled so brightly. He wanted to end it for you. So that you could live in peace.”

Zuko nods and buries her face in her Uncle’s robes. The smell of tea engulf her senses and she cries for the first time in front of her Uncle.

Iroh starts going to the war room again. He comes out grim every time. So Zuko is always ready with tea and a game of pai sho. He always looks at her gratefully and strokes her head. “Thank you, my dearest niece.” He always says.

She’s nearly thirteen when her father shows her off to the first of many potential suitors.

He’s sixteen, son of a general. He’s nice, but it’s awkward. She’s long known that her father probably won’t let her marry for love, but she had always hoped…

But this is part of her duty. She’ll grin and bear it like everything else.

Azula seems to relish in her discomfort. She bounces around every time she sees her sister with a suitor. Saying things like:

*“Did you know my sister is a late-bloomer? You might have to wait a long time before she can* *bear children for you.”*

*“Oh Zuzu is quite good at womanly pursuits like embroidery or sewing. Born and raised to be a* *bare-foot trophy wife.”*

*“I guess the only good thing is that she’ll give you pretty children. It’s not like she has any good* *fire bending traits to pass down.”*

Zuko would yell and shout at her sister every time, making the younger cackle.

“Why are you doing this?” Zuko asks on the brink of tears.

Azula checks her nails and shrugs, “Because this is all you’re good for Zuzu. Getting a husband, continuing the family line.”

“But you’re ruining it for me!” Zuko shouts. “I liked Khan. He was *nice,* and you chased him off.” Her younger sister rolls her eyes and glares. “Well maybe don’t be so obvious about it then. You don’t get to choose your husband Zuzu. You lost that right when you showed how weak you are. Father would never allow it. You’ll be lucky if it’s not some toothless old general.”

Zuko cries at that point and runs to her Uncle who soothes her and pets her hair. Some part of her is disgusted with such a display because Azula is right. She is weak.

&#95;&#42;&#95;&#42;&#95;

The suitors come in all ages and shapes. Some are her age, but some are nearly twenty. She doesn’t like those ones. The worst one is a man named Zhao. He’s a Lieutenant, but he’s rising above the ranks quickly. Worst of all he’s over twelve years her senior.

He gazes at her like a piece of hippo-cow meat, and there’s a dangerous glint in his eyes. When he’s in the palace, she does her best to hide from him. Unbeknownst to her, the servants and guards also try to keep the lecherous man away from their princess.

It comes to a head when the lieutenant has her pressed against the wall. His eyes on her and arms caging her in. She’s never been more afraid. All the self-defense and katas fly out her mind as the man leans into her space. She closes her eyes and prays to Agni that her voice will come out.

But then there’s a powerful roar and the Lieutenant is thrown away from her. She opens her eyes and sees her Uncle wreathed in powerful flames. It’s the first time since Lu Ten’s death that she’s seen him fire bending, and it’s a beautiful sight.

Even with his short stature, Uncle towers over the quivering man. “Do you have a *death wish* Lieutenant Zhao?” he hisses, steam escaping from his lips.

“I-I was j-just getting to know the princess. I *am* a potential suitor after all.” he answers.

“Is that so?” Iroh glowers. “Then consider this a dismissal of your candidacy. I will not let my daughter marry scum like you.”

Zhao glares back, “But she’s not *your daughter* is she?”

The words stall Iroh only for a moment, but the flames spring forth again. “She is my daughter in all but blood, Zhao. And you will not go near her *again*. Or I will remind you why I am called the Dragon of the West.”

The lieutenant swallows and nods. He scurries up and walks briskly away. Iroh tells the guard to make sure the man doesn’t come near the princess’s wing of the palace, and they nod.

Iroh kneels next to Zuko and carefully places a hand on her shoulder.

“Niece?” he asks quietly. It breaks her out of her stupor, and she throws her arms around her uncle and cries. The man holds her close and rubs her back.

“Hush my fire-lily. I’m so sorry, I wasn’t here sooner. I promise I won’t let that vile man come near you again.” Iroh apologizes. He picks her up with ease and walks to her room. The servants flutter by and try to offer the princess anything to comfort the traumatized girl. But they’re all declined and Iroh just asks for tea to be brought up.

He puts her on the bed and pulls the covers over her. He tries to go to pour the tea, but Zuko grasps his hand tightly.

“I—I was really scared.” She whispers.

Iroh nods. “I know. I should never have let my brother push those suitors on you. I should have stopped him before this.”

Zuko bites her lips. “I didn’t even try to stop him. I just…froze. I couldn’t even—” her breath stutters and tears fall in rivulets down her cheeks. She’s crying hard. Chest heaving as if her body is trying to push out all the fear, all the anxiety, all the anger through her tear ducts. Iroh gives her a handkerchief and wipes her eyes. He waits for her to calm down.

“It’s not your fault, Zuko.” He says. “Your body froze and it’s a completely normal reaction to events like these.”

“But—but I *trained so hard*. I trained to be strong. So why—why didn’t I?”

Iroh puts his other hand over hers. “You were scared. You didn’t know what to do. You weren’t mentally prepared. No amount of physical training will prepare you for the mental hurdle of *knowing* that you aren’t helpless. Of purposefully hurting someone to defend yourself.”

“Then how can I be any good?” she asks. “How can I prevent this from happening again?”

Iroh looks at her sadly. “The hardest part is moving past the fear and anxiety and keeping calm. You are a strong woman my niece. I will help you so that if it happens again, you’ll be prepared.”

Zuko nods and tears spring to her eyes again. “Will you hold me Uncle?”

The old man smiles and nods. He lies next to her and holds her close. She clings to his robes and soaks them in her tears.

“Did you mean it?” she asks. “When you called me your…your daughter?”

Iroh nods, “Every word.”

Zuko clings tighter. *I wish you were my real father.*

&#95;&#42;&#95;&#42;&#95;

The suitors stop coming. Whatever, Iroh said to her father is enough to put a sour look on his face, but he doesn’t *dare* go against Iroh.

Zuko is thirteen when she goes into the war room with Iroh. She kept quiet when they talk about putting a factory near a fishing village, she keeps quiet when they talk about making the draft younger, and she keeps quiet when they talk about raising the taxes. But she can’t keep quiet when st

General Fugi suggests to sacrifice the 41 division for a battle tactic that had other options.

She stands and yells at the general. “You can’t do that general! Those are *our* people your sending to die. I cannot standby and let you *betray* our people like this. There are better options, we don’t st

have to use the 41 division as a diversion, if you just get your head out of your ass—!”

“Zuko!” Iroh hisses pulling her down, but it was too late. The damage was done. Ozai looks down at his eldest in disgust.

“Zuko, you have brought dishonor here.” He says.

Zuko flinches, but stands firm, “I stand by my words Father. You can’t let them do this.”

Ozai nods. “Very well then. You will face the one you have dishonored in an Agni Kai tomorrow at sundown.”

Her heart palpitates in her chest, but she nods. She’s in the right. Agni will see that and judge her as such.

But it wasn’t what she thought it would be. Instead of General Fugi, it’s her Father. He walks to her, but she’s already laying prostrate against him. She’s apologizing for speaking out of turn. Please please Father.

But when she looks up, she only sees the terrible hatred and anger in his eyes.

He sneers. “You will learn your lesson, Zuko and suffering will be your teacher.” His hand covers the left side of her face, and it *BURNS*.

She’s screaming. Screaming until her voice gives. There’s someone running to her side. She hears her uncle shouting. The last thing she sees through her watery eyes is her Uncle’s face, streaked with tears as he comforts her.

&#95;&#42;&#95;&#42;&#95;

The next time she wakes, her face is aching. She feels both too warm and too cold, and she’s sweating buckets. There’s a soothing hand over her forehead.

***“Hush fire-lily. It’ll be alright.”*** Someone whispers. ***“Sleep. You need to save your strength.”***

“Lu Ten?” she croaks. Her cousin’s face looks strangely translucent and so sad.

***“Yes. Now sleep. You’ll be okay.”***

“I’m so tired.” She cries softly. “It hurts so much.”

Lu Ten strokes her hair, but it feels like there’s only air combing through it. ***“I know, but you’re so*** ***strong my darling sister.”***

“I wanna go with you.” She pleads. “I don’t wanna stay here anymore. Everything just hurts.” Lu Ten takes her hand and looks at her fiercely, ***“Zuko, if you really want to leave I’ll take you.*** ***But please think about what you’re leaving behind.”***

Zuko feels her heart seize. If she leaves Uncle will be alone. He barely survived Lu Ten’s death…

She swallows and nods as best she can. “Okay. Okay.”

Lu Ten smiles and kisses her forehead. The pain dissipates and she feels her body start to cool. He takes her hand and hooks his pinky in hers. ***“I promise, Zuko, I’ll always be here for you.”***

She nods blearily and slurs, “O’kay. Jusss stay here? Til I sleep.”

***“Always.”***

The next time she wakes, her Uncle is there cradling her hands in his. He looks like he’s aged ten years, but as soon as he sees her awake, he smiles. Tears streaming down his face.

“Oh Zuko, oh my precious fire-lily.” He blubbers as he strokes her hand and places his head against her shoulder.

“Un’le?” she croaks. Iroh immediately hands her some water and she drinks it carefully. “Wh-what h-happened?”

Iroh’s face is thunderous. “Your…*father*. Burned you. During the Agni Kai. He wants to speak with you when you wake.”

Zuko feels her heart drop to her stomach. Her breath catches with each lungful, but her uncle is by her side. He holds her hand tightly and rests it against his steady beat.

“Breathe with me.” he says. “Count to twenty. Slowly.”

She does and goes her breathing under control. Her heart is still beating rapidly, but it’s better now. Her Uncle helps her sit up and slowly feeds her plain broth.

“Take your time, niece.” He says. “You’ve been asleep for four days. We almost thought…the infection nearly killed you.”

Zuko nods. “I think I remember feeling hot with fever.”

Iroh nods seriously and continues to feed her until Zuko can’t take anymore. He’s about the lay her back down when a guard, knocks on the door. Iroh growls irately and goes to the door. He whispers in hush tones and comes back with a resigned look on his face.

“Zuko, your father knows you’re up. He’s coming.”

Zuko holds back the tears, but she can’t keep the vomit down. Iroh barely brings the trashcan to her mouth when she vomits out the liquid broth and bile.

She winces, “I-I’m sorry.”

Iroh waves it away, “Don’t worry. We’ll get you something else later.”

Then Ozai barges into the room. Sliding in like a snake coiling against the chair.

“Princess Zuko.” He says with narrowed eyes. Iroh fists are shaking and there’s an unimaginable hate in his golden eyes.

“Make it quick *little brother*.” Iroh spits. “Zuko needs her rest.”

Ozai glares at Iroh, “You need to leave, *brother*. This is between me and Zuko.”

“If you think I’ll let you alone with my niece you’re—”

“Uncle.” Zuko whispers. Iroh stops and looks at her. She takes his hand and gives him a shaky smile, “I’ll be okay. Just…stay by the door?”

Her uncle looks torn, but he nods and turns to Ozai. “Tread carefully, little brother.” He threatens.

Ozai shivers a bit at the fire pulsing through his brother’s eyes, but he keeps his head high. “Just get out.”

Iroh closes the door and Ozai looks down at his trembling *weak* daughter. If only this child had fought him. Then he would have an excuse to execute this pathetic welp. Even with the bandage, she still looks just like Ursa. It maddens him.

“Now you’re not even useful as a bride.” He spits disdainfully. “You’ve nothing to give to the royal line.”

Zuko flinches and tries to make herself smaller.

“The only way to regain your honor and prove your usefulness is to find the Avatar.” Ozai declares. “You have until sunset to pack your things and go. From today, you are banished from the Fire Nation and her colonies. Your banishment will be rescinded once you’ve captured the Avatar and bring him to me in chains.”

With that he leaves, and Iroh is immediately at her side. He holds her hand. “I’ve packed most of your things, niece. You just need to pack whatever personal things you want to bring.”

Zuko looks at him with wide watery eyes. “How—how did…did you know?”

Iroh nods. “Yes. The Fire Lord gave me the banishment papers a day after the Agni Kai. I’ve managed to add and amend some stipulations, but there was little else I could do.”

The princess nods and feels a bubbling anger course through her. Everything’s she’s done. Everything she’s been through. It means nothing now. She wants to scream and yell, but now is not the time. She’ll cry later. Now, she looks to her uncle and says, “Please help me to my room.”

She needs to pack for her new life now.

## Chapter 2

### Notes

#### Chapter Notes

Warning: Mentions of suicide, Period talk, mentions of human trafficking and sex slavery.

### Text

---

Jee doesn’t know what to make of the princess. When General Iroh first contacted him for the job, he expected someone more…

Well less angry to be sure and not looking like a boy.

To be fair the princess has a fairly androgynous face, but with the bald head and phoenix tail and armor she looks hardly like a princess or noble lady. More like a boy playing solider.

Teruko and Kyoko punch him when he tells them this.

“Hey if the girl wants to look like a boy then let her.” Teruko scolds. “You of all people know how dangerous it is for women traveling in the Earth Kingdom.”

Jee nods, “Yes. I guess it’ll be better for her to move through the shadier ports as a boy.”

Kyoko nods and turns to Teruko, “When we have time let’s make sure we teach her a few tricks to use against some…*insistent* men.”

Jee shivers at the look the two women give. Agni have mercy on whatever man thinks they could take advantage of the princess. Between these two mother hens and General Iroh, there won’t even be any ash left.

&#95;&#42;&#95;&#42;&#95;

Zuko is getting used to living on the ship. The crew are fine. Teruko and Kyoko are especially kind to her. They teach her things about how to defend against men who want to…abuse her, and she’s grateful to them. When she yells at the crew, she at least tries to keep it civil with those two. Uncle keeps trying to get her to make friends with the crew, but she doesn’t have the heart. She just wants to focus on capturing the Avatar and getting back home. She knows many think she’s a spoiled princess, and she’s fine with that. It’s the truth after all. She’s been spoiled by her time in the palace. She has to make her own bed, the food is not as luxurious as before. She doesn’t get anything she desires with a snap of her fingers.

At the same time, it’s…nice. There’s a strange independence on the ship. No one to tell her to stand straight, cross her legs or put on make-up. No one to look at her judgmentally when she decides to climb the mast of the ship…well not exactly. Her Uncle and Jee definitely try to keep on feet planted on the ground, but being up high, feet bare and dangling over the edge, is nice. It’s so quiet. Peaceful. She could just stay up there all day. But then her Uncle’s worried cries bring her back down.

He grips her shoulders tight and look at her worriedly. Zuko sighs and carefully takes his hands off, but she doesn’t let go of his hands.

“I’m not going to jump.” She assures.

Iroh’s face twists. “I know—I know, but…I just worry.”

Zuko swallows, stomach turning at the thought of making her Uncle worry. So, she hooks her pinky in his and looks him in the eye.

“I *promise* I won’t take my own life.” She says seriously.

A tear leaks from her uncle’s eye. “You’re only thirteen. You shouldn’t have to make such a promise.”

Zuko just shrugs. “If it’ll make you feel better, I will.”

“You indulge this old man too much.”

Zuko quirks a rare smile, “Only because I love him very much.”

The crewmen turn away when the general hugs the princess and starts bawling *loudly*.

&#95;&#42;&#95;&#42;&#95;

There’s terrible scream from the princess’s room. Jee and Kyoko rush over, but General Iroh already has the girl bundled in blood-soaked sheets and running to the infirmary. He uses his stout body to bowl over the two crewman without a second glance, and Jee takes a minute to regains his wits after such a tumbling. Kyoko groans and mutter something about clueless overprotective fathers.

“What do you mean?” Jee asks. “There was a *lot* of blood soaking those sheets.”

Kyoko rolls her eyes at him. “The princess most likely started her bleeding.”

Jee blinks. “…her bleeding?”

Kyoko throws her arms up in exasperation. “Her menstruation cycle.”

Jee goes red. “Wha—bu…th-that’s!”

The woman shakes her head, “Go get Teruko, I think we need to give the girl a little talk.”

Jee is still sitting on the ground, numb. Kyoko smacks his head, “Go! Unless you want to be the one to explain a woman’s menstruation cycle to the general.”

That got him up and surging towards Teruko’s room. He bangs on the door like the spirits are after him. The engineer glares at him for disturbing her rest, but as soon as he stutters something about blood and cycles, she’s rolling her eyes at him and pushing him out the way.

Jee heaves a sigh of relief. At least he won’t have to be the one there.

Inside the infirmary, Iroh is pacing madly as he waits outside. He almost demanded to be in the room while the physician looked over his dear niece, but one glare from his niece keeps him outside. Reluctantly.

There was just so much blood, and he knew his dear girl was in a lot pain last night. She kept complaining about the pain in her abdomen. It’s why Iroh went to her room early, to check on her and give her some tea. But when he found her curled up and blood soaking her blanket, he immediately carried her out and ran to the infirmary.

Wild ideas flew through his mind. What if she got hurt? What if someone poisoned her? What if an assassin came in? Is it an illness? Will she make it? Iroh honestly doesn’t know if he’ll survive her death, and it *scares him*. More than facing an army of Earth Benders, more than looking into the eyes of the dragons.

Suddenly, Teruko and Kyoko are beside him. Kyoko puts a calming hand on his shoulder.

“General, it’ll be okay. This is a natural process for Zuko to go through.”

Iroh feels his flame flare, “A natural thing?! How can you say that! She’s been complaining about abdominal pains and then she wakes up this morning with blood on her sheets near her…”

Oh.

*Oh.*

Iroh wants to slap himself, and he does. Kyoko and Teruko smile at him with bemusement.

“Yeah, why don’t you make some ginger or peppermint tea? Those usually help with the cramps.” Teruko suggests.

“Dr. Kusuri will probably give her some Eucalyptus oil too, but we should add more on our shopping list. Cramps and bloating are pretty bad during the early years.” Kyoko adds.

“Oh and ask Toshi for a heat pack. Those are magic when the cramps get really bad.”

Iroh nods and rushes off.

The physician opens the door and looks around. “Is the general gone?”

“Yup.” Teruko replies. “He’ll be a while. Is now a good time?”

“Yes, I told the princess you’ll be here to answer any questions about it. I’ve already briefed her on the physical changes to her body and reproductive diseases she may have to watch out for.”

“Thanks Kusuri.” Teruko says. The women enter the medical room and sit down near the princess. She’s a little pale, but nothing worrying. The sheets have been thrown with the dirty laundry and she has a new pair of fresh pants.

“How are you holding up?” Kyoko asks kindly.

The princess shrugs. “Okay. Just…shocked and a little embarrassed.”

“To be fair your uncle was worried you were dying.” Teruko replies.

“Yeah…it was…sweet of him. I guess.” She says hesitantly.

“Oh yeah.” Kyoko scoffs. “He bowled me and Jee over trying to get you to infirmary.”

“Oh…sorry about that.”

Kyoko waves her off. “Eh don’t even worry about it. My dad was the one who found me when I first bled too. He ended up running around the village like a headless chicken before the medicine woman knocked him out with a rolling pin.”

Zuko snorts at this, “Really?”

“Yeah, he had that bump for *days*. Don’t worry though, it’s honestly pretty normal for them the go bonkers when they see that much blood.”

“It was a lot of blood.” Zuko replies. “Is there always that much blood?”

“Sometimes a lot more.” Teruko replies.

Zuko winces. “So how do I know to…change?”

“You’ll get used to the feeling. Rule of thumb, on your heavy days check every two hours or so. As you go through more of these, you’ll know instinctually when it’s about to overflow.”

“…and the cramping?”

Kyoko smiles sympathetically, “An unfortunate side-effect. Ginger tea, eucalyptus oil, and a heat pack help the most. Sometimes they’ll be bad, sometimes you won’t even get them. They should get better as you get older though.”

“So does this mean I’ll be able to…get pregnant?” Zuko asks with a red face.

Both women nod. “Yes, so you’ll have to be careful. Kusuri, will you be giving her the Silphium herb?”

“Yes, I have a good supply now, but we’ll need to go to port to get more. Ideally, we’ll get a small greenhouse to cultivate it in the ship.”

“If we stop near Yu Dao, I have a cousin who can help us out with that.” Teruko informs.

“How often will I have to take it?” Zuko asks.

“Everyday if you want to be safe.” Kyoko says. “You just need a few florets. Kusuri will make sure you get the right dose.”

Zuko nods. “Okay.”

“But it only protects you against pregnancy, not sexual diseases.” Teruko warns.

Zuko blushes. “I-I don’t think I’ll be doing any of…*that*.”

The two women smile at her. “We understand, but well…you never know what happens. It’s better you know now than after you’ve had sex.”

The princess fights down the blush. “Okay…so what do I need to know?”

Kyoko turns to Kusuri and says, “Mind getting us a pot of tea and some snacks? This is going to take a while.”

Kusuri nods. “I’ll tell General Iroh that you’ll be talking to the princess for most of the day.”

The physician finds the general muttering over which ginger blends would be best for his niece. Kusuri shakes his head fondly. At least he’ll be a doting father about this. Even if he’s a little clueless.

“General Iroh.” Kusuri greets.

Iroh turns to the physician and beams. “Dr. Kusuri, could you help me? What’s the best ratio of peppermint to ginger to add for this blend to help with the cramps?”

“It really depends on the person, General. Tea might not even help at all.”

The general’s smile falls. “I see.”

Kusuri sighs, “How about you come by tomorrow and I’ll talk to you about what to expect with the princess’s bodily changes.”

“That would be most helpful, doctor. Thank you.”

“Good. Now why don’t you help me make some tea for the women? They’re going to be there a while giving the princess the sex talk.”

Iroh turns green and swallows. “Yes…yes she’ll need to know about…about *that*.”

Kusuri pats the man on the shoulder, “If it makes you feel better, the princess seems reluctant to partake.”

Iroh sighs, “Oh that’s a relief. Not that I would dissuade her from pursuing a safe and healthy…*relationship* but I just—it’s just…”

Kusuri laughs, “I understand, General. I’ve seen a lot of your type in my line of work.”

“Is it always so terrifying?”

“For clueless fathers? Yes. Generally.” Kusuri confirms. “But I know you’ll support her through this.”

“I’m just glad there are two wonderful women helping her through this.”

“Yes. Imagine if we were a crew like one of the Water Tribes or Earth Kingdom.”

Iroh shudders. “I don’t even want to think about it.”

&#95;&#42;&#95;&#42;&#95;

They are on land for two days to re-supply the ship. Jee takes Shindo, Yama, Toshi, and Chang out to a bar to drink. The princess runs a dry ship so the crew usually drink their weight in alcohol when they go to port. Her attitude’s been getting better. She gets along well with Kyoko and Teruko as well as Kusuri. Toshi has also been warming up to her since the princess comes to him for heat packs and food cravings.

She’s still a bit of a slave-driver though.

He leans back in the chair and lets the burn of alcohol wash through him.

*Ah that hits the spot*.

The others swig their drinks as well and they start gossiping. Talking about their families, the scandalous times they’ve had at sea. Jee is just finishing up a story about his time with contortionist twins when the princess sneaks up to them.

“Why would you be telling everyone about your exploits?”

Jee barely holds back his scream when he turns to see the Princess. She’s got a hood up and looks imperiously at the gossiping group of men.

“P-prince—”

“Shhh. I’m technically not allowed to be here.” She says putting a finger to her lips.

“Then *how* did you get in?”

“I came in through the window upstairs. Everyone was either too drunk or sleeping to notice me.”

Agni, Jee is going to die of a heart attack. He takes a deep breath and asks, “Why are you here?”

“I need your help to take out a human trafficking ring.” She answers.

*Uuuugggghhh. When did this become his life?*

Jee pinches the bridge of his nose. “Okay, where did you get this information?”

“I was sneaking on the rooftops when I overheard some people talking about a new shipment. I got curious and got closer to them. They used the code for human slaves when they were talking about the shipments. It sounds like they’ve got ten or so people locked up. I followed the men to the hideout but there were too many for me to take them all out. That’s why I need you to help me bring them down and save the people.”

Jee respects the girl for her determination he really does, but…

“Why don’t you just tell the local authorities? They’ll be able to handle it.”

Zuko rolls her eyes. “Have you seen that fat man who heads the local authorities? He’s a disgrace to fire benders.”

The men snicker. The local constable had given them a hard time when they were docking, and it’s nice to hear the princess mocking that rounded beach ball of a man.

Zuko continues, “Besides, it’ll take too long. By the time they get their asses off, the traffickers will be gone. They won’t be able to pull it off.”

“And we will?” Jee asks incredulously.

The princess smiles sharply. “You better. You’re my crew, and I expect nothing less.”

Spirits, damn if that doesn’t get his blood running. He looks to the men, and he sees they’ve fallen for it. Hook line and sinker.

“We’ve only got the five of us. Six including you. We’ll most likely be outnumbered.”

“Teruko and Kyoko are waiting for us outside. Besides, I heard Yama say it’s not the size that matters, it’s how you use it.”

The men all turn to glare at Yama who sinks in his seat and buries his head in his hands.

Jee sighs, “First of all, don’t *ever* say that in front of General Iroh.”

“Why?”

“Because he’ll *murder* us.”

The princess doesn’t look convinced, but Jee just continues, “And second, alright. We’re in. What’s your plan?”

Zuko smirks and Jee feels dread pool in his stomach.

Turns out, it was quite a simple plan. Jee and the three other men distract the guards by attacking from the front. The princess will take the nimblest three to sneak into the hide-out and release the people. Simple.

And absolutely *insane*.

He thought he’d be done dodging rocks from earth benders but he’s very *very* wrong.

Agni save him from the insanity of royalty.

They somehow do it though. Yama leads them towards an abandoned corner of the port while Jee and the others corner them from behind and blast them. Knocking them out and burning a few. Ah well, they were human traffickers, he won’t cry for them.

Jee leaves the three to tie them up and take them to the authorities while he goes back to check on the princess. He doesn’t need to worry though because he’s gets there just as he watches the princess spin her dual blades at two larger men and knocking them on their ass. She smacks them with the blunt end of her blade, knocking them out.

“Jee, get around back, Teruko and the others are getting the people out.”

Jee looks around at the total carnage and unconscious men scattered around. The he looks back at the princess and nods. She could take care of herself.

They end up saving twelve people ranging from sixteen to twenty-five. They were all fairly good looking which makes Jee’s stomach turn. He has an idea of what they would most likely become. The head of the port’s protection force sputter as the princess drags the unconscious men to him along with the victims.

“Y-you can’t—”

“I am Princess Zuko of the Fire Nation. I have the authority and duty to protect my people and land.” She says. “You better make sure these men don’t get out. Three of them are benders so they require your most secure metal prisons. They’ll need to be held there until a ship from the Boiling Rock comes to pick them up.”

“Bu—when did you?”

“Last night I sent out a hawk. They’ll be sending another hawk here soon, with instruction.”

“And the…victims?”

The princess glares, “I’ll be personally escorting them back to their homes. And another thing, if I find out you’ve been slacking on your job, I’ll be coming back for your head.”

The constable nods mutely and the princess leans back before motioning for her crew to follow. Jee schools an impassive look on his face and bites down the manic smile threatening to split from his lips.

“Our princess sure is something.” Chang says in amazement.

“Yeah. You don’t get one like that every dynasty.”

&#95;&#42;&#95;&#42;&#95;

Two of the people they rescue bow low before the princess.

“Princess Zuko, I am Ying from the Yu Dao colony. I just finished my apprenticeship as a metalworker, and I request that I serve on your ship.”

“And I am Hansung from the Teiko colony. I’ve finished my engineering apprenticeship.”

Zuko raises her brow. “I promised to take you all back to your families.”

“Yes, but we are both orphans, princess.” Hansung says. “We don’t have families to go back to, and you’ve saved us. We owe you our lives.”

“So if you would allow, we would like to join your ship and work for you.” Ying finishes.

Zuko sighs, “We don’t belong to the Fire Nation Navy. I’m a banished princess, I don’t wield any power. There’s nothing here for you if you join.”

“On the contrary, princess, we’re not looking to advance in the military. We just want to serve under you. You are an honorable and powerful woman. It would be our honor to serve under your ship.” Ying replies.

Zuko purses her lips and turns to her Uncle who just gives her a serene smile and continues preening like a proud mother turtleduck.

Well he’s not helpful.

Fortunately, Teruko comes in. “Princess, I am in need of another engineer, and we are missing someone to help with the metalwork around the ship.”

Zuko nods and turns to the two new recruits. “Alright, both of you will report to Chief Engineer Teruko. We’ll give you a three-month trial period. If Teruko feels that you are up to the task, we’ll take you in. If not, we’ll return you to your respective colony.

They both smile at her. “We won’t let you down princess.” They say and bow once more.

Zuko just waves them away and orders Chang to get them a pair of uniforms.

Iroh walks up to her and smiles, “That was very well done, niece.”

Zuko blushes and turns away, “It was nothing, Uncle.”

&#95;&#42;&#95;&#42;&#95;

Mornings are spent training, and Jee watches Kyoko and Chang struggle against the girl who’s only practicing her basics, and still getting used to her new vision.

In a year or two she’ll be able to completely wipe the floor with them, and he shivers at the thought. But perhaps the most terrifying thing is that she is easily a master swordsman. Jee watches her defeat Kyoko and Chang with ease, her dual blades spinning effortlessly as she parries their attacks.

“I didn’t know the princess was a master swordsman.” Jee comments to Iroh, who is watching her with eyes full of pride. The old general smiles back at Jee.

“Oh she’s one of Piandao’s most impressive student.” Iroh replies. “By her eleventh birthday, Piandao gave her the title of master.”

The sailors gap at him, eyes wide.

“M-master Piandao?” Hansung says. “*The* Master Piandao? I’ve only heard the legendary stories of his feats.”

“Oh yes, she’s been training with him since she was five.” Iroh says sipping on his tea to try and mask the aura of pure pride.

It doesn’t work. Jee can see straight through that old geezer. He’s loving this.

“She moves differently when she’s sword fighting.” Teruko notices. “If she moves like that during her bending, she’ll be quite the formidable opponent.”

Iroh nods, “I agree. But she lacks the confidence in her bending.”

Jee narrows his eyes. “Lacks confidence? That girl nearly beats us every time, and we’re grown men and women.”

The general shrugs helplessly. “It’s the lack for mental fortitude that’s stopping my niece from achieving true mastery of her bending. Once she gets over that hurdle, she’ll grow beyond any level she thought possible.”

Jee watches as the princess bends backwards so effortlessly and then springs back to attack Kyoko. He’s excited to see the growth in her fighting.

&#95;&#42;&#95;&#42;&#95;

Yama looks appraisingly at the beautiful white lotus embroidered on the general’s clothes.

“General that’s some good needlework. The stitching is amazing.” Yama comments.

Iroh smiles proudly, “Thank you, you have a good eye Yama.”

The sailor laughs, “Yeah my mom was an embroiderer. She made rugs and robes for nobility.”

Iroh nods, “A noble art. This was made by my talented niece.” He boasts.

Yama raises his brow and discretely looks to the princess who’s sparing with Shindo. Shooting fierce flames at the man.

“Really?” he says skeptically.

The general chuckles. “Don’t judge the scent of a flower by it’s looks.” He answers.

Yama doesn’t understand until the princess offers him a beautiful cloth with a scene of the green sand beach of his home island.

“…um…”

“I heard you’ve been homesick.” The princess bites out. There’s a fierce scowl on her lips, but her cheeks are tinted red. “So I did a small needlework of your home. It’s not that accurate though. I had to do it from memory.”

Yama swallows the lump in his throat. He takes the cloth reverently and smiles. “Thank you, princess. This is amazing work. You’re very talented.”

The princess only turns away and grumbles, “Just make sure you work better from now.”

&#95;&#42;&#95;&#42;&#95;

It’s a far cry from the extravagant alter at the palace, but it’ll do. Iroh places three peaches in front of the incense and photo and prays to his son. Zuko lights her own incense and says her prayers.

*It’s strange here Lu Ten, but I think you would have liked it. These people seem like the type you’d* *get along well with. Please watch over Master Piandao; I’m not allowed to send letters to him, but* *Uncle does on my behalf. Uncle misses you. I do too. Wish you were here. You’d like the dirty jokes* *the sailors make, oh but don’t tell Uncle I’ve been listening in on them.*

*I miss you so much.*

They sit in silence, watching the incense burn out. Remembering the bright short life of Lu Ten.

&#95;&#42;&#95;&#42;&#95;

For the princess’s fourteenth birthday, the women take her on her first spa trip.

“I don’t see why I have to do this.” she grumbles. “I’ve had massages before.”

Teruko smiles and slings her arms over the pouting girl, “That’s just a massage, this is a *spa* experience. With mud baths and fancy indoor waterfalls.”

“I had a fancy indoor waterfall.” Zuko replies.

The three women look at her and sigh.

“Rich people.” Kyoko says.

“If I could live in their shoes for just one day.” Ying laments.

“Indoor waterfalls aside, we just wanted to treat you to this. A girl should always spare some time for a day to pamper herself.” Teruko explains.

Zuko wrinkles her nose, “Why?”

“Because it makes you feel good.” Kyoko answers. “And when you feel good, you’re happier.”

“I’m never happy.” The princess mutters.

“And that’s why you need a spa day.” Ying says, urging the girl along.

The spa is well maintained and nicely decorated. The workers greet them with smiles and usher them along. Zuko lays herself on the massage bed and sighs. She doesn’t want to be here, but Uncle *insisted*. And he knows she can’t say no to him.

Whatever. She’ll just do this once and then never do it again. The massage was nice, the woman seems to know what she’s doing. But then she goes to the milk bath and as the steam and scent of milk and honey waft through her she sighs and sinks down. A woman behind her starts to massage and brush her hair and Zuko just melts.

By the end of the spa day, she feels clearer than ever before, and her skin feels baby smooth.

Teruko smiles at her knowingly. “How did you like it?”

Zuko blushes. “I suppose we could do it again.”

The three women share smiles and give secret high-fives.

&#95;&#42;&#95;&#42;&#95;

Jee thought he’d get used to the princess’s vigilante tendencies, but even after nearly two years he’s still surprised. This time it’s poachers who are shooting down saber-tooth lion moose and taking their cubs to become pets for nobility or wealthy merchants. The rich are a strange bunch.

He’s trying to hold back his screams as he clings onto his Komodo-rhino for dear life as a bunch of angry saber-tooth lion moose follow him. The babies coo in the pouch attached to the Komodo-rhino, and he dearly wishes he had better luck. Yama, Chang and him had drawn straw to see who would be bait and of course he drew the short one. He turns the corner and actually screams when he sees a massive saber-tooth lion moose waiting for him at the end. But the creature doesn’t eat him.

Instead it bellows at the other three who stop in their tracks. Jee wonders if this is some strange ritual the beasts do before eating their prey. Well jokes on them, Jee not particularly plump and tasty. He’s got raw muscles underneath the armor damnit. He’ll be the toughest thing they chew.

Fortunately, the predators don’t want to eat him because the *princess* pops up from the top of the saber-tooth lion moose.

Jee’s jaw drops, and his life flashes before his eyes. General Iroh is going to *murder him* and piss on his ashes.

“Princess what are you doing up there?!” he screeches.

Zuko frowns and rolls her eyes.

“Young lady don’t you roll your eyes at me!” Jee scolds. “You are on a *saber-tooth lion moose*. Get down from there! Before your Uncle murders me.”

“Relax Jee, he’s on our side. I think he’s the alpha around here. He’s just want to get the cubs back to their families.”

“Did you tell him that we’re trying to save their babies?” Jee asks sarcastically.

“Yep, he seems to understand.”

Ugh why does he even bother. So Jee just takes out the cubs (*carefully*) and backs away from the group. The animals seem to be calm and nuzzle against their children.

He watches the princess climb down the saber-tooth lion moose and pat his nose. “Thank you for your help.” She says.

The beast returns it with a fond lick and nuzzle before bellowing and leading the others away.

Jee sighs and turns to the princess. “What about the poachers?”

“Kyoko and Yama are taking them to the Earth Kingdom post. They’ll collect the bounties from them.”

“Okay good.” Jee looks down to see the princess look forlornly at the baby saber-tooth lion moose toddling after their mothers.

“No, you’re not allowed to take one as a pet.” Jee says.

Zuko pouts. “I wasn’t thinking about that.”

Jee raises his brow unimpressed.

“…okay maybe just a little.”

The lieutenant sighs and pats the princess’s shoulder, “You did a good job, princess.”

“You were good bait lieutenant.”

&#95;&#42;&#95;&#42;&#95;

It’s actually a wonder that it takes this long. Well, maybe not. The princess is usually in armor and the bald head usually make people think she’s a man rather than a woman. But now…

Teruko and Kyoko somehow got their hands on a dark-haired wig and place it on the princess’s head. She scowls as the women artfully makes the bangs cover the left side of her face. Ying puts on some rouge on her lips and khol on her eyes. The end result is…quite pretty. Jee understands why Princess Ursa’s beauty became legendary.

General Iroh claps happily as they show off his niece (daughter) adorned in a new bright red robe. “Princess Zuko you look stunning.”

“Uncle I really shouldn’t be out…” she fidgets with the belt.

“Nonsense my dear.” Iroh says. “You’re turning fifteen today. You deserve a nice night for your birthday. Yu Dao hosts some of the most fantastic festivals in the colonies.”

Zuko sighs and nods. “Okay let’s go then.”

“Excellent.”

Jee smiles to himself as he thinks about the many *many* drinks he’ll get during the festival. They’ll have some good stuff for sure.

It’s not even two hours in when the crew spot the princess with…a *boy*.

*Just ignore it Jee. You’re here to drink. The princess can take care of herself. You’re not her* *babysitter.*

But then the boy takes her hand and *kisses it*, and all the warning bells ring in Jee’s head. He knows that move. He’s *used* that move and not always with the best intentions. Well scratch that, *never* with the best intentions. He shares a glance with the other two men he’s with, Chang and Yama, who both nod to him. Jee sighs. So much for drinking himself to a stupor.

“Oh hey, you stalking the princess too?” Teruko asks.

Jee glares at her, “Shut up. I’m just trying to prevent the boy from dying at the hands of the general.”

“Riiight because you’ll be the one burning his body to ash?”

“No.” Jee denies.

Teruko chuckles and shrugs. “Okay well *I’ll* gladly burn his body to ash if he tries anything.”

“Oh good, I’m next to a homicidal maniac.”

“Shut up you old turd. You’ll gladly walk through lava for her. I know I would”

Jee definitely would, but he won’t give Teruko the satisfaction of knowing that. “Whatever. She should be fine right? She’s a smart girl, good head on her shoulders most of the time. And she’s never shown any interest in…*boys*.”

“Ah but she’s never had a chance to express herself before now, and no one’s ever really… expressed their interest in her.”

Jee winces. “Okay well then this is *your fault* for making her look...” He trails off when he sees Teruko’s unimpressed glare. “Well…I’m just saying maybe we should have kept her away from that for a little while. You know boys aren’t…” he gives vague wave motions.

“Thinking with their brains when they see a pretty girl?”

“Exactly.” Jee nods.

“You would know wouldn’t you? You probably knew the move he was pulling. Your whiskers sensed it.”

Jee rubs his beard self-consciously. “Leave my beard out of this. And you probably knew because your old emotional scars were throbbing.”

The woman sighs, “Yes, they were. I fell for a lot of those types of boys in my day.”

Jee looks discreetly at his companion. “And what’s your type nowadays?”

“Oh I don’t know. Silver raccoon-fox type. With a stupid beard and a soft spot of broken teenage girls.”

Jee looks disturbed. “*General Iroh*?”

Teruko rolls her eyes. “No. Agni you’re really dull aren’t you?”

“Hey!” he protests, but Teruko shoots him another unimpressed glare. Jee just let’s her have that.

Instead, he thinks hard, “Hmm do I know them?”

“Yup.”

A pause.

“…no I can’t think of anyone.”

“Oh you’ll figure it out, Jee. I have faith in you.” Teruko teases.

Suddenly the princess and the boy start to move away from their spot and go towards the woods. Jee feels his stomach turn. Oh no. *Oh no*. He probably pulling the old, let’s got to a secluded spot to see the fireworks move.

He will *not* let the princess have her first kiss with a stupid peasant boy who’s only looking for a conquest. Teruko laughs beside him.

“You look so serious.”

“This *is* serious.”

“This is what happens when you’re a normal fifteen-year old teenager.” Teruko assures.

Jee doesn’t look convinced. “Are you sure about that?”

“Yes.”

They hide behind some bushes. Crouching close enough to have a good view of the couple, but not close enough to hear what they are saying. The princess is blushing and fidgeting, while the boy smiles and takes her hand.

Jee is tempted to jump out and slap the hand away, but one look from Teruko stops him.

“Calm down mama platypus-bear. Nothing bad is going to happen.”

“Ugh I know. It’s just so…hard.”

“Our princess has to grow up and will kiss lots of boys and/or girls.”

“How about our princess just stays focused on capturing the Avatar and sparring with us?” Jee suggests.

“You and General Iroh are way too similar.” She replies fondly. “Like I told the general, she’s fifteen. She’s bound to get more interested in other people as she gets older.”

“I just don’t want her to heart to get hurt.” Jee says nervously.

“That’s just a part of life. Besides she’s fifteen, their hearts are pretty scrappy.”

Jee sighs. She’s right. He really should stop worrying so much.

But then the fireworks start, and the boy leans closer to his princess. He whispers something in her ear which makes her blush and then they’re leaning close. The fireworks explode above them just as their lips touch.

Jee looks away to try and give her some privacy. Teruko leans against him, her weight a warm comfort in the colder night. The fireworks illuminate the engineer’s face. Making her eyes glow beautifully. Jee clears his throat.

“Well, I guess there really wasn’t anything to be worried about.” He says. The pair of teenagers are still kissing, but at least the princess looks like she’s enjoying it.

Teruko laughs softly, “What did I tell you? But I’m glad you’re looking out for her. She needs more male figures in her life that care for her.”

“Daddy issues?”

Teruko gives him a *look*. “Her father’s the Fire Lord who banished her. That’s got to leave some mental scarring.”

Jee nods. “Right, makes sense.”

Come to think of it, he doesn’t actually know why the princess was banished. None of them do. Maybe he should ask the general? The princess wouldn’t tell them he’s sure of that.

The display ends and the boy takes the princess’s hand, leading her out of the woods.

They watch covertly as Princess Zuko says something and gives him a sweet peck on his cheeks before she goes off towards her Uncle. Jee looks back at the boy and grins.

Huh. Maybe he really didn’t have anything to worry about. The boy seems absolutely enamored by her.

*As he should*. Jee thinks indignantly.

Teruko elbows him playfully. “Looks like our princess is a natural heartbreaker.”

“Let’s try and keep it that way.”

The princess greets them as they head back to the ship. The rouge on her lips is completely off, and her wig is mused. Jee pretends that he didn’t just see her make-out with another boy and smiles. “Did you enjoy your birthday, Princess?”

“Yes.” She answers with a blush. “It was nice.”

The general stops him before he goes to bed.

“Thank you for watching over my niece. I’m glad you and Teruko were there to make sure nothing untoward happened.”

Jee raises his brow. “You knew?”

“Oh yes, but I asked Toshi and Kusuri to keep me at the pai sho table. I wouldn’t have the self-control you and Teruko have.”

Jee grimaces. “Ah well to be fair, Teruko was the level-headed one between the two of us.”

Iroh laughs, “Haha I should have known. But I’m glad you still had a good night watching my dear niece.”

“I did.” Jee answers with a small smile. “Have a good night, General.”

“Good night, Jee.”

&#95;&#42;&#95;&#42;&#95;

They’re at port again when the princess comes up to him with that *look* in her eyes. He turns to Teruko, who gives him a ‘what can you do’ shrug and sighs.

“What is it this time?”

“Pirates.”

Well, it’s been a while since he had a good pirate showdown.

&#95;&#42;&#95;&#42;&#95;

They’re sparring on deck when it happens. The princess flung a powerful fireball at Chang who doesn’t block it in time. His forearm gets burned, and he cries in pain. Kusuri is up and already going to Chang with cold water and salve. He hums and tuts, but only pours the ice-cold water on the burn. Chang hisses and Yama teases him for being a baby. Chang denies it vehemently. They argue until Kusuri smacks both of them and tells them to shut up.

With the commotion, Jee doesn’t even notice that the princess has gone deathly still. He only turns once he sees the General rush to the princess’s side. She’s hyperventilating, on the way to a full-blown panic attack. Iroh makes her sit and places her hand on his chest. He murmurs instructions to her, and Jee can see her starting to breathe normally. Chang goes to her hesitantly.

“Princess? It’s okay. I’m okay.” he assures her with a smile. “It’s just a small burn. I do worse on myself when I cook.”

The joke falls flat though because the princess shies away from him eyes wide. “C-Chang, I shouldn’t have…I didn’t mean to…” She gulps in air and then stands on wobbly feet. Iroh immediately goes to her side and helps her to her room.

Later that night, Iroh goes to the mess hall where the sailors are eagerly waiting of news. He sits down seriously and sighs. “I think it’s time for you to know what happened to the princess.”

By the end of the horrifying tale, Jee’s fists are shaking. He’s never felt this much *anger* before. It makes him want to storm up to the palace and bash the Fire Lord’s head to a banister, repeatedly. By the looks on the other sailor’s face, they think the same.

st Toshi clenches his fists and slams them on the table. “My-my cousin was in the 41 division. It broke my aunt’s heart when she found out he died. She ended up killing herself just recently. The princess was banished for speaking out against that tragedy…” Toshi is too angry to continue to speak.

Hansung bursts into tears and Ying goes to his side. “My friend was killed there too. I was coming back from her funeral when I got kidnaped. I can’t believe the princess was banished and burned for speaking out against that.”

Kyoko stands up and marches to the general. “When we bring the princess back, I will help her take down the Fire Lord.”

There’s a pause as the treasonous words sink in. But then Chang stands next. “Count me in. I want a piece of that asshole.”

“Me too.”

“And me.”

The rest stand, and they turn to Jee expectantly. The lieutenant just shrugs. “The hell are you idiots looking at me for? You *know* I’m ready to tear the Fire Lord to pieces.” The crew laugh and settle down when the General stands and bows to them.

“Thank you for looking after my dear niece.” He says. “But please keep this to yourself. She’s still young and searching for herself. She needs time to understand just how *awful* Ozai’s actions were to her.”

The crew nod and mutter in agreement. Many understand how hard it is to come to terms with abuse and the abuser. Especially someone as sensitive and loyal as the princess.

But it won’t stop them from spreading the word to their friends and families.

&#95;&#42;&#95;&#42;&#95;

The princess is quiet for a few days, but she soon goes back to her old grumpy self. The crew sigh in relief when she starts yelling order at them again.
