They’re just finishing a village evacuate from a fire, and the princess is yelling at the local lieutenant for not keeping up with the controlled burning of the forest.

He’s prostrating himself before her as sparks fly out of her mouth. When she’s done destroying the man’s ego, she turns to the village leader.

“If they don’t help you out with the fire safety send me a hawk. I’ll be sure to set him straight.” She says.

The elder nods gratefully and asks, “Is there anything we can do for you?”

“If you hear any rumors about the Avatar, let me know.”

Then an old woman walks up to them. “The Avatar?” she says. “My great-grandmother always told us she saw a sky bison cross here during a great storm. She said it was probably going towards the South Pole.”

Zuko’s eyes widen and she immediately orders the men to start charting a course to the South Pole.

It’s a miserable month searching through the South Pole. Everything is cold, and the only thing around are sea prunes or blubbery fatty animals. Jee spends weeks shivering in the cold icy air, but just when they’re about to give up, a great beam of light shoots up to the sky.

The princess’s eyes gleam. “It’s the Avatar.” She whispers.

---

### End Notes

#### Chapter End Notes

Iroh grew up with a brother and then had a son. I can almost guarantee that he doesn't know anything about caring and helping a young girl going through her first period.

I know Zuko didn't have the best relationship with his crew in the original canon, but having the women bond and help her through her body changes will help make her a little less crabby with them and then it'll give her an opening to start spending time with the other crew members. The vigilante-thing is just my own head-canon that I couldn't resist putting it here

Silphium is an ancient extinct plant used by the Greeks and Romans for birth control. Based on ancient drawing, it looks kinda like fennel. It was only found on along the coast of modern-Libiya, and couldn't be cultivated elsewhere. However, for the sake of this story, let's just say the growing conditions are not as stringent.

Teruko's name is from MuffinLance's many AU's about Zuko's crew.

Finally,  
Would you guys prefer shorter chapter and a shorter time between updates? Or these long chapters an a longer time between chapters?

Thank you for reading! I hope you enjoyed the art and the fic. You can probably see a better image on My Tumblr and I put some outtakes there.

## Chapter 3

### Notes

#### Chapter Notes

**Warnings:** References to rape/non-con and Explicit sexual content

### Text

---

“Unbelievable.” Chang sighs sinking to the ground. “How did we lose to a twelve-year old child?”

“I don’t know about you, but I’m ready for a strong drink.” Yama groans. “Just to drown in.”

“Stop bitching and help me fix this damage! I want her ready to go by the morning!” Teruko yells.

“I was turned into a popsicle!” Chang retorts. “I thought I’d lose my balls.”

“You don’t have any balls, Chang.” Shindo teases.

Chang retaliates by throwing a screwdriver at him, which Teruko yells at him for.

Jee rubs his temple and prays for patience. “You’re all immature idiots. That’s why you were beaten by a twelve-year old child.”

Ying laughs as she helps Hansung fix a part of the damaged engines. “Lieutenant you sound like you need a drink.”

“I’ll drink when this is done.” Jee replies.

Suddenly, there are unknown men surrounding them. The lead man comes up to Jee, shoving a paper in his face, “Commander Zhao needs to have all of you questioned.”

Jee feels his eye twitch. “Oh really? Under what grounds?”

“Suspicious activity.”

Jee barks out a laugh, “Riiight.” He drawls. “You’re going to need to be more specific solider. Under the military code 56.8a no one below the rank of a Fleet Admiral may interrogate the crew without proof of illicit activity.”

“You came in with a damaged ship.” The solider tries to say.

“Uh…we’re at war here? In case you didn’t know. I know you guys are pretty cushy here near the colonies, but out there is a lot different than what you greenhorns are used to. I can’t tell you how many pirates, poachers, and human slavers we’ve come across.”

“Yeah so unless you got some proof get lost.” Teruko shouts. “Unlike you guys, we’ve got real work to do.”

The soldiers look ready to fight, but the crew stand behind Jee. They stand tall before the other navymen. Muscles built and bulked up by countless hours training with the princess and going with her impossible schemes. Heck they even faced the *Avatar*. It’ll take a hell of a lot to make the crew of the *Dragon Claw* to back down.

The soldiers seem to understand this and scurry out of the ship. Once they’re away, Jee turns to the others. “Best to keep the little…problem under wraps. If anyone asks, we got hit by an Earth Kingdom ship.”

They nod and get back to work, with renewed vigor.

Once again, Jee wants to drown himself in alcohol. “I’m sorry, *what’s* going on?”

“The princess is going to fight Commander Zhao in an Agni Kai!” Yama says.

Zuko returns the boy and desperately tries to come up with a fake name on the spot. “I’m…Rina.” She says.

“Rina.” Tobio tests. “It’s a pretty name.”

The princess swallows and ducks her head. “Thanks. It’s my grandmother’s.” She says.

The teens shuffle awkwardly before Tobio finally speaks, “Hey do you maybe…wanna go do stuff?”

Zuko’s eyes widen. “Um…yeah sure. I’d like to…do stuff.”

Tobio’s smile is blinding. “Cool! Cool. How about we go over there? They have some cool games, and then we could get some fire flakes?”

Zuko nods eagerly. “Yeah, I’d—I’d like that.”

The teen offers his hand and she takes it. The fingers tighten around hers and she lets herself follow the cute eager boy around the festival.

Unbeknownst to her, the three women watch eagerly and pump their fists when they see her go with the boy.

She soundly beats Tobio at the darts game and archery game. But instead of being jealous, he laughs and says, “Wow you’re amazing!”

Zuko blushes under the praise and hides her head under the plush turtleduck she won. “…thanks. I was taught since I was little so…”

“Woah, military family?”

“Yeah. Kinda.” She admits.

Tobio nods. “My dad was drafted for the war, and I’ll probably be going soon too.”

The princess’s eyes widen. “But-but you’re only sixteen.” She stutters.

Tobio looks at her sadly. “You’re probably from the mainland huh?”

She nods, feeling a stone drop in her stomach.

“In the colonies, if you’re not already an apprentice you’re drafted at sixteen. I’m actually going to basic training in two days. All the boys in the village are. That’s why we’re having this festival, as a sort of send-off.”

Zuko grabs his hand and holds it tightly. “I’m sorry.” she says. She should have stopped them in the war room. She should have stood up for them when she was at the palace. Now she’s powerless. Only able to protect a few villages from bandits or human traffickers. She should have done more as a princess.

Tobio chuckles and places his other hand over hers. “Don’t be. It’s just the way it is. Hopefully, we’ll be able to win the war soon. Then all of this could end.”

That’s what Lu Ten said three years earlier. And they’re not even close. The Avatar’s appearance just increased the war efforts as the Fire Nation rush to get the Earth Kingdom under control. She grits her teeth. She needs to capture him soon. So Tobio won’t have to face war as a sixteen-year old. So the people fighting can go back home. So *she* can go back home to her throne. To her people.

Suddenly, there’s gentle lips pressing against her hand. Tobio smiles and says, “C’mon wanna help me make some good memories before I leave?”

Zuko nods, “Lets.” She says.

It’s funny how she always ends up in the woods during these festivals. But this time, at least gets to lay down on a futon. They’re in a small hideout that the village kids use sometimes when they want to get away from the adults’ prying eyes.

He kisses her gently, licking and nibbling at her lips. He’s as inexperienced as Zuko, but he’s eager and it makes a pleasant heat coil in her gut. He pulls back and strokes her hair.

“You’re so beautiful.” He whispers.

Zuko blushes and turns her head. “Stop lying.” She answers. He’s seen the scar. It’s a hideous disfigurement.

But Tobio just leans down for another kiss. “No, it’s beautiful. It shows that you’re strong enough to live through it. I’ve seen the burns on the discharged soldiers in the village. They’re a lot like you.”

“Surly and angry?”

Tobio laughs, “Yeah, but also strong. There’s nothing that gets them down. They aren’t afraid of anything. You’re just like that.”

Heat creeps up her face, “I’m not like them.” she denies.

Tobio just shrugs and caresses her face, “We’ll agree to disagree.” And he goes back to kissing her.

It’s wonderfully maddening how her body is reacts so strongly. It never did this with the other one, but then again, the other boy was a little dull. He wasn’t as sturdy as Tobio. He didn’t have as many muscles underneath his robes from hard farm work. There were no rough calluses on his hands. Tobio smiles and jokes. He’s not afraid to tease her, and there’s just something roguishly charming about it.

She feels her face heat. Maybe she does have a thing for rough and tumble peasants. June would never let her live it down.

But thoughts are soon flying out of her head as Tobio’s hands hesitantly inch closer to her breasts. They stop just before, but she takes his hand in hers and places them on her hardening nipples.

“You can touch.” She whispers.

Tobio beams and eagerly gropes her chest. It’s clumsy, but still feels nice as he touches her nipples through her robes. She’s arching her chest towards him, and letting her own hands roam over the hard planes of his pecs and abs.

She feels his harness press against her throbbing cunt and she gasps, lifting her hips up to try and get more friction. Tobio hisses above her and leans his head against her shoulder.

Zuko swallows. “Do you…do you want to…?”

Tobio’s eyes are bright against hers. “I do…but only if you want to, and—and we don’t have to… to go all the way. I’d be more than okay with just this.”

Zuko nods feeling more confident and secure in that knowledge. She dives back into the kiss and just let’s their passions lead them.

(She and Tobio end up naked. Connecting their bodies and writhing in the heat and ecstasy of the night.)

Zuko sighs contently as Tobio finishes on her abdomen. By some miracle, the wig remained on her head. Thankfully she resisted the urge to climb on top of the teen and ride him like a Komodo-rhino. She flushes at the thought.

*Maybe the next one.*

They both breathe heavily in the hot air of the tent. There’s something comforting in the way, Tobio holds her and helps her clean up afterwards. He ties the sash on her waist and kisses her cheek.

“Thank you for tonight.” He says.

Zuko nods and gives a small smile back. She knew this wouldn’t lead to anything. They were just two teenagers trying to find comfort before they’re shipped off to war once more. But she’s happy that she got to share her experience with him.

With careful care, she cups his cheeks and presses a soft chaste kiss on his lips. She murmurs the ancient prayers drilled into her by the sages.

*Agni, Lord of the Sun, Father of Dragons, and Bringer of Life, please watch over your child as they* *charge into the darkness of the world. Be their Light and guide them to victory. If they should fall,* *please lead them back into your fiery embrace. And let them be reborn into a new life.*

She pulls back and sees fresh tears in Tobio’s eyes. Panic floods her chest as she tries to apologize and wipe them away. But he stops her and hugs her tight.

“Thank you, Rina.” He whispers.

Zuko doesn’t say anything and hesitantly puts her arms around him.

When he leads her out of the woods, the trio of women are waiting of her. They eye Tobio suspiciously, but Zuko shakes her head. They relax and turn away, letting the teens say their last goodbyes.

It’s sweet. Tobio shyly gives her one last kiss and another thank you before he’s going down the road, back to the village square. Zuko turns to the women and says, “Let’s go back to the ship. Uncle might get worried.”

They nod and walk silently back to the ship.

&#95;&#42;&#95;&#42;&#95;

“Did you have a nice night, niece?” Iroh asks.

Zuko blushes and nods. “Yes.” She answers quickly. Her uncle nods and sips on the hot tea.

“…when did they change the draft age?” she asks.

Iroh looks pensive. “Hmm a year ago I believe, but only for those in the colonies.”

“Sixteen is too young.” She says.

“It is, but the Fire Nation needs more bodies to continue the war.”

“…I want to end this.” She admits softly.

Iroh doesn’t say anything, but he studies his niece carefully. This is the first time she’s said anything remotely against her father. It’s quite an improvement.

“I believe you can.” Iroh says. “You have a good heart if a bit reckless and prone to anger.”

Zuko frowns. “A good heart doesn’t stop wars.”

“It certainly can’t be the only thing.” Iroh agrees. “But it’s the catalyst for change.”

Zuko mules on the words, and Iroh lets her think on it in peace.

## Chapter 4

---

The crew of the *Dragon Claw* start falling into a routine as they continue sailing after the Avatar. It’s obvious that he’s heading to the North Pole, the only thing they need is a plan to extract the Avatar from the Northern Water Tribe and get out without them noticing. The princess has been in conference with her Uncle and the Lieutenant for days as they plan for the extraction.

Jee rubs his brow, “That’s not possible, Princess. You could get caught in a blizzard and *die*.”

“I have the breath of fire.” She insists.

“But it won’t last you that long in a Northern blizzard. Trust me. I’ve seen men come back from the poles missing fingers and noses after being caught in one of those storms.”

“Listen to the Lieutenant, Princess Zuko. He’s been on missions to the poles.”

“…fine. Then what about this…”

Unfortunately, the relative peace and consistency aren’t to last. They’ve heard rumblings and rumors in the port towns they stop in. Admiral Zhao’s been planning a big invasion to the North. They say it’ll be larger than the force that first attacked them 87 years ago. Zuko just scoffs and shakes her head at the idiotic man.

It’s overkill to send the entire Western Fire Nation fleet North. The Northerners rely too much on their seclusion and ice wall for protection. A few ships for distraction and an infiltration unit would be sufficient to take down the Tribe without a significant loss of life. A full siege of the North is just wasteful.

Then again, it’s not surprising Zhao would plan something on this scale. He’s always trying to prove how big his dick is.

But she didn’t expect the man to undermine her like *this.*

“You can’t do this Zhao!” she shouts, but the admiral merely smirks.

“I have the full authority of the Fire Lord.” Zhao says. “Taking down the Northern Water Tribe is of the highest priority.”

“That’s ridiculous.” Zukos hisses. “There’s no strategic advantage to take out the Northerners now, they don’t have any significant land or resources of value to us. Father should be focused on taking down Ba Sing Se and taking over Omashu.”

“What do you know of war and strategies?” Zhao sneers.

The princess’s fists shake. “I have been *born and raised* to serve my nation, Zhao. I’ve been studying politics, geography, tactics, and land resources since I was at my mother’s teet! I know better than anyone what the costs of war are and how to sustain a campaign. You are just a little narcissistic man who wants to prove how big his dick is!”

The incensed admiral lunges at her, but Zuko easily dodges his mad swipe. Iroh steps in and puts a firm hand on his chest. Jee and Yama stepping behind the general in a show of force.

“Do not lay your hands on her, Zhao.” Iroh warns.

The admiral leans back and combs his fingers through his hair. “It doesn’t matter. Orders are orders, and even the princess can’t defy the Fire Lord.”

Zuko growls, “You have the entire Western fleet at your back. Why do you need more men?”

Zhao smiles smugly and puts his hands out in a show of innocence, “Why Princess, this is to be the greatest invasion force in the history of our Nation. I need all the men to back it up if we are to succeed.”

“You can succeed with a fourth of the force and you know that.” she accuses.

“Hmm perhaps, but then we wouldn’t *crush* the life out of the savage Northerners.” Zhao answers casually.

Fear and hatred bubble in her stomach. She understands the price of war, but she would never purposefully go and demolish an entire Nation. For one, it goes against all codes of honor and two, it just fans the flames of rebellion. The Fire Nation does not hold onto her colonies through force. They use sweet words and gold coin to keep the citizens content and happy. A bloodless coup.

“I stand my ground, Zhao. I won’t let you take my crew.” She says. “Besides, Ying and Hansung are not part of the navy.”

“Those colonists are serving on your ship and are above the age of the draft. They should have been serving in the military already. And like I said princess, you don’t have a choice.” He claps his hands and men are swarming her ship. They forcibly take her crew, dragging them off her ship kicking and screaming.

Teruko is shouting some particularly nasty slurs against the men.

“Get your hands off me you pieces of Komodo-rhino shit! I’m not serving some fucking maniac with a small pee-pee complex. I’ll sabotage engines! I’ll make sure your ships don’t make it the North! I’ll bash all your tiny skulls with my wrench.”

Zhao sighs and pinches the bridge of his nose. “Gag that woman.” He orders.

Jee is furiously struggling against the soldiers as they try to gag Teruko. “Don’t you fucking dare! You’re all fucking idiots if you’re following the admiral.”

Zhao glares at the shouting men and turns to the princess. “Get them under control.” He orders.

Zuko raises and unimpressed brow. “What? You can’t get a few sailors in line? What kind of Admiral are you?”

The admiral leans in and hisses, “If you don’t get them in line here, I’ll brand them for *treason*.”

The princess glares at him but complies.

“Crewmen!” she yells. They stop and look at their princess. “Stop your struggling. By the order of the Fire Lord, you must work with Admiral Zhao.” She bows deeply to them. “I’m sorry I can’t stop this. You all deserve better. Thank you for serving under my command.”

Much to the invading soldiers’ surprise, they stop. Jee wretches his arms out of the soldiers’ grip. “You heard the princess.” He says. “We have to play nice with the Admiral.”

They grumble and groan, but they all still. The men slowly letting go of the tamed crew. Then the Lieutenant bows on his knees before the princess. The others follow after.

“Princess, we’ll be back. I swear to you.” Jee says with serious eyes. “We won’t ever abandon you.”

She purses her lips, “Just stay alive. That’s an order.”

They grin as they rise, waving goodbye as the soldiers escort them off the *Dragon Claw*. Zuko turns to Zhao, eyes burning fierce. “They’re only on loan. I’ll be taking them back as soon as your silly vanity project is over. I’ll take them by force if need be.”

The admiral scowls. “They’re only lowly disgraced soldiers. Why are you so invested in them?”

“Because I protect what’s mine.” She answers.

Zhao scoffs and rolls his eyes. “Very well. I’ll give you your little toys back, but I’ll be sure to tell the Fire Lord how…*invested* you are with them.”

“Go ahead. Father already knows I’m soft. It’ll be nothing new to him.” Zuko replies airily. “You’ll just be wasting his time.”

The admiral glares, “I’d watch yourself, Princess Zuko. You’re all alone now. *Accidents* can happen.”

“Is that a threat?” She growls.

“No.” Zhao grins. “Just a friendly piece of advice.” With that he leaves her ship, leaving only the princess and her Uncle.

She watches as he disappears into the crowd and stays rooted at the bow for several minutes after. Iroh puts a hand on her shoulder.

“Why don’t we go for a walk?” Iroh asks. “The night air will help clear your mind.

She only glares at him in response. Iroh puts his hands up, “Or just stand here, in the dark brooding. That’s fine too.”

He leaves her to it, and heads off the ship.

Zuko sighs and heads to her room. As soon as she closes the door, she slides down and buries her face in her knees.

*What am I going to do?*

She doesn’t know how long she stays like that, moping in the dark, but then she hears footsteps and metal clanking. Her head shoots up and heads to the door.

“Uncle?” she calls out cautiously. But there is no answer. She puts her hands up, ready to fight.

She heads to the brig, hoping to spot the person snooping on deck. Then she hears a squack beside her.

A green reptile bird.

*Pirates.*

She barely has time to create a flame shield before the explosion burst through and flings her out of the tower.

Her body hits the water like a stone, as brilliant red plumes paint the black sky.

*Pain.*

*Cold.*

*Fear.*

*ANGER*.

*Resignation.*

For a second, she thinks to just let herself drown. To stop thinking of the pain. To stop having to claw and fight for her birthright. For her home. But then she sees her Uncle. Crying and broken before her cousin’s grave.

*NOT TODAY.*

She won’t let this measly explosion be the end of her. She’s fought the Avatar. She broke through Pohuai Stronghold. She’s fought bandits, poachers, slavers. She’s weathered storms and everything the Spirits have thrown at her.

She won’t die by fire. They already tried that *once*.

So, she kicks, and struggles. She ignores the pain shooting through her body, and surfaces.

There’s a shout near her and a dagger just misses her. She turns and sees the pirates who stole her riverboat.

*Of course.*

She turns ready to fight, when a bright hot flame rushes past her and engulfs the pirate’s dinghy. Their screams muted by the roar of the fire in the port. The princess turns and sees her Uncle standing on the shore. Eyes like molten gold, fierce as a Dragon. He roars once more. Beautiful fire spitting from his mouth. Standing there on deck, she remembers why they fear him. The Dragon of the West.

She slowly paddles to shore and lets her Uncle lift her up. He carefully bundles her up and takes her away from the wreckage of her ship.

&#95;&#42;&#95;&#42;&#95;

Princess Yue is *nothing* like Princess Zuko. One could say the complete opposite in every way. The Water Tribe princess is the kind the Spirit tales talk about. Sweet, elegant, with a laugh like bells, and the scent of sweet flowers. She stole his breath away the moment they met eyes. Now he’s walking excitedly to the bridge where she asked to meet.

She’s beautiful illuminated in the moonlight. Instead of harsh lines and scowling lips, she’s soft, gazing at the water with a pensive look in her blue eyes.

He greets her genially, but something is off.

She turns away, closed off, unlike the previous time he talked to her.

“I’m—I’m sorry Sokka. This was a mistake.” She says and runs away.

His heart drops to his stomach. Shame, sadness, and anger course through him.

Of course, she wouldn’t want him. He’s just a lowly southern peasant. He’s nothing special, just the token non-bender in Team Avatar. He glares at the carving and chucks it angrily in the water.

Figures, he wouldn’t have much luck with this princess either.

&#95;&#42;&#95;&#42;&#95;

Jee stares mutinously at the stain in the clothes he’s washing. From a lieutenant under the Princess’s command to a lowly laundry man. How the tables have turned. Chang and Yama are cursing as they try to get…whatever off the floors. They’ve been delegated to *cleaning crew*. Shindo, Kyoko and Toshi are tending to the Komodo-rhinos. Teruko, Hansung, and Ying are polishing armor. Polishing. Armor.

At least Dr. Kusuri is helping out in the medical bay. If Zhao had put the surly doctor on cleaning duty, Kursuri would have mutinied on the spot.

Zhao is really enjoying this. He’s actually been throwing them suspiciously smug looks, and it grates the man. Something’s happened. He’s sure of it.

But Jee can’t do anything right now. He needs to bide his time.

&#95;&#42;&#95;&#42;&#95;

Iroh stifles the flames that are threatening to erupt from his chest. He takes a deep whiff of the tea and sips slowly. The motion helping him reign in the terrible anger as he sits across from the Admiral.

“I’m so glad you could join me in my invasion, General Iroh. Your expertise will be invaluable.” Zhao says.

“I will do my best to serve the Fire Nation and her royal family.” He answers with a tight smile.

“Yes…” Zhao drawls. “I heard about the explosion you niece perished in. A true shame. Have you caught the men who did it?”

“I burned them on sight.” Iroh replies. “I wouldn’t waste my words on the scum who killed my niece.”

Zhao shivers. “Right. Of course. Well, I hope you enjoy your stay on my ship. We have a quite a journey ahead.”

Iroh bows and stands to leave. He and a guard walk down the hallway.

“Zhao does not suspect.” Iroh says quietly. “I will do my part to make sure you stay hidden.”

“Thank you, Uncle.” Zuko replies.

“We’ll be meet with the rest of the fleet by tomorrow. You should rest and save your strength.” Iroh looks at her worriedly. “You’re not even recovered from the explosion.”

“I’ll be fine.” Zuko retorts. “I need to find out what Zhao is planning and get the Avatar before him. Can you distract him for a bit while I snoop in his quarters?”

Iroh sighs. “Yes. But please, be careful.”

She slides the faceplate back on her face, “I will.”

While Uncle distracts Zhao with dinner and a game of pai sho, Zuko sneaks past the guard and rifles through Zhao’s expansive room. She rolls her eyes in disgust at the opulence that decorate it. Gold shone in every surface and tacky gem encrusted brushes and paraphernalia glittered around the room. This place looked more like a magpie’s nest than a room. She picks the lock on the drawers and rifles through the various papers.

Nothing of interest in the drawers, so she moves to the chest in the corner of his room. It’s the only plain and undecorated item here, but there’s an impressive padlock on it. She takes a few minutes to pick it and opens the chest. Underneath the disgusting amount of pornographic material, she finds some old yellowed scrolls.

On a cursory read, a lot of them talk about the spirits of the moon and ocean. The source of water bending. One mentions a specific legend where the moon and ocean spirits took physical form to protect their people. It tells of a place in the North where there is warm air and green grass.

Something grows cold in Zuko.

Is this why Zhao is so adamant on taking down the North Pole? Is he seriously thinking of taking the spirits hostage?

*Is this man insane?!* She thinks.

The Fire Nation were a series of volcanic islands. Doesn’t he understand they need the moon as much as anyone? Without the moon controlling the tides, it would wipe out the local ecosystem and plantations that rely on the tides.

There’s no way her father would condone this…right?

She takes a deep breath and stashes the scrolls away. Looking carefully down the hallways, she doesn’t see any guards. She makes her way down, trying to look as nonchalant as possible, but then someone bumps into her.

She’s about to shout at them when she stops. It’s Chang.

Relief floods through her.

“Chang?” she says quietly. The sailor blinks for a moment, then almost whoops in delight. Fortunately, Zuko is quick enough to slap her hand against his mouth.

“Shut up!” She hisses. “I’m technically supposed to be dead.”

“Dead?! We gone for a day and someone tries to kill you?”

“Well, Zhao doesn’t like waiting.” Zuko replies dryly. Chang’s eyes widen and his lips turn into a snarl, but Zuko forcibly snaps him out of it.

“Now’s not the time, Chang.” She says. “Are the others here?”

“Yes. Zhao’s making us do newbie work. Probably hoping to embarrass us.”

“Good. Listen carefully, I think Zhao wants to do something to the Ocean and Moon Spirits—no don’t interrupt me—If anything *anything* out of the ordinary happens. *Run.* Get the ship away as far as possible. Uncle will forge some evacuation papers, use them, get control of the ship and get away.”

“What about you?”

“I won’t die so easily.” Is the answer.

Chang sighs. Typical.

“Understood, Princess.” He says bowing. “Be careful.”

“You too. Send my regards to the others.”

&#95;&#42;&#95;&#42;&#95;

Against all odds, the power of love changes Master Pakku’s ideals and agrees to train Katara. To add to that, Sokka manages to comfort the princess and now they’re in this strange, dating-but-not-dating limbo. Maybe he’s a fool for hoping, but maybe Chief Arnook could change his mind. He seems to like Sokka enough. He takes his plans and information seriously and treats him like a fellow warrior.

It’s nice. It’s everything Sokka ever wanted, and he can prove to himself that’s he’s not just the Avatar’s token non-bending friend. That he has earned his place beside Aang and Katara.

Everything is going so well, but as always it all changed when the Fire Nation attacked.

Sokka was taking Yue on a romantic ride on Appa when the black snow began to fall. His guts churn when he sees the black ash stain the pristine white snow.

“We need to go.” Sokka says. “We have to warn your Father. They’re probably not more than a day away. Maybe even less.”

Yue nods shakily and helps him get to her Father.

Meanwhile, Zuko changes into a white uniform. Uncle is fussing over her, reminding her to keep warm and double-checking her clothes. He leads her to the boat and hugs her tightly.

“I’m sorry, niece. I just worry. Since Lu Ten died I’ve seen you as my own child.” He says clutching the princess tightly.

Zuko sighs and hesitantly puts her hands on her uncle. “I know.”

They share a quick moment before Iroh releases her and helps her onto the boat.

“Remember your breath of fire!” he says.

“I will.”

“And keep your hood up it’ll protect you from the cold!”

“Okay Uncle!”

Strangely, she feels a little better after Uncle’s mothering. Not that she’ll ever tell him that.

Getting past the fortress took a little more work. She’s about to just melt through the walls when she sees the turtle-seals diving into a water hole. A crazy idea forms in her head.

She’s gasping for breath when she melts through the ice. Her whole body convulsing painfully in the cold. She struggles to get a hold of her inner fire and releases a few puffs of flame. Her heart palpitates as she sees Water Tribe warriors rush past her. Zhao will probably attack at daybreak. She has a night to get to the Avatar and…ask for a truce.

Her entire being is revolting against the idea, but it’s the only way to stop Zhao from destroying her country. She struggled with the idea for a long while, going through potential plans that would let her have the Avatar *and* stop Zhao. But none were feasible. As much as she loathed to admit it, she would have to work with Avatar for the good of her people.

The things she does for duty.

It’s easy to make her way through the city. Everyone is either in shelters or on the front lines waiting for the fight to begin. She makes her way to the oasis that Zhao’s maps point to, and to her surprise it’s as warm as the legends suggest. There’s green grass at her feet and from the distance she can see the pool in the middle.

She sees the water tribe girl talking to another one. There was no sign of the boy, much to her relief. But the Avatar is glowing strangely, and his body is still.

*Great. He’s not even awake* *to negotiate.* She thinks bitterly. The water tribe girl will have to do then. What was her name again…?

“Kana!” she calls out.

The girl swings her head to her, eyes wide with surprise and anger.

“You.” She hisses and water rises from the canal. The control is better, there is finesse in her bending now.

“Looks like you found yourself a master.” Zuko comments.

“I did, and I’ll gladly show you how much I’ve learned.” Katara growls shooting a wave of water at him.

Zuko dodges what she can and uses her fire to evaporate the rest. “I’m not here to fight!” she shouts.

“Then why are you throwing your fire around?” Katara retorts, flinging icicles at her.

“Because you’re fighting me you stupid peasant!” Zuko snarls. “And I don’t feel like dying today.”

Note to self, calling someone a stupid peasant probably isn’t the right way to negotiate a truce. It seems to incense the girl further, and she throws more ice at her. She calls back to the other water tribe girl to get help.

Zuko wants to scream. Why is she so bad at being good? She dodges another wave of water and sets a huge wall of flames between her and the water bender. She backs away and holds her hands up.

“Stop!” she commands. “I’m trying to warn you about Zhao’s plan.”

The other girl glares, but she doesn’t attack. “Why would you do that?”

“Because he wants to do something with the moon spirit.” Zuko replies. “I’m willing to have a…a *truce*. With the Avatar. Just to stop Zhao.”

‘Kana’ lowers the water just slightly, eyes narrowed. “How can I trust you?”

“You can’t.” Is the blunt reply. “If it weren’t for Zhao, I’d gladly take you down and capture the Avatar—”

“You’re not helping your cause.”

“—*But* Zhao seems to have conveniently forgotten that the Fire Nation is a series of *islands*. We are a seafaring people, we rely on the moon just as much as the Water Tribe.”

“Why is he doing this then?”

Zuko rolls her eyes. “We don’t have much in common, but I’m going to say you’ve probably seen your fair share of dick-measuring contests.”

“Yeah tell me about it.” The water bender scoffs. “They didn’t let me learn how to fight in my tribe, and these people wouldn’t teach me any water bending, only healing.”

Zuko makes a disgusted face, “That’s so backwards. And you wonder why we call you savages.”

“Hey! We might be sexists, but at least we don’t go conquering lands, raiding villages, and threatening the spirits.”

“That’s just one man!” Zuko shouts. “And we’re just as tired of the war as you are!”

“Oh really?”

“Yes! My people want this to end just as much as you do.”

“Then call it off. Stop the fighting.” She reasons.

Zuko scowls, “We can’t just call off the war. We need to subjugate the Earth Kingdom first.”

“Why?” the other girl asks incredulously.

“Because we’re not weak! We would be quitting just before we achieve our goals. Our pride as a Nation is on the line.”

‘Kana’s’ mouth opens and closes. Then she shakes her head and throws her arms up. “Are you listening to yourself?! That’s just the craziest thing I’ve ever heard.”

“Don’t mock me—”

“No, I will mock you, because you’ll let this war drag on for your stupid pride!” she shouts. “You’d rather lose more of your people for the sake of *pride*. Just—just—aagghh! It makes me so mad.”

Zuko glares and clenches her teeth. “Whatever, I’m not here to discuss my country’s values with you. Are you going to help me stop Zhao or not?”

The girl purses her lips and nods. “I hate it but…fine. *Fine*. We’ll work with you just once.”

“Oh I’m not liking this either, Kana.”

“It’s Katara, you moron. You’ve been chasing us for a month now and you don’t even know our names?”

Zuko bristles at the insult. “I don’t pay attention to your names. You’re just the Avatar’s lackeys and absolutely useless to me.”

“Well you sure know how to make a girl feel good.” Katara grumbles.

“What is the Avatar doing anyway?” Zuko asks pointing to the glowing boy.

“*Aang*’s in the spirit world. Trying to find the moon and ocean spirits.”

Zuko blinks. “Is he an *idiot*?”

“Hey! He fooled you more times than I can count.”

“Because he caught me off guard!” Zuko denies. “But that doesn’t matter now. *Why* doesn’t he know that the fucking fish are the moon and ocean spirits in physical form?”

By the look of shock on Katara’s face, she also didn’t know this information.

“You didn’t know either.”

“No…so he’s gone to the Spirit world for nothing?”

“Yes. Didn’t you ask a spiritual leader before you went jaunting off to the Oasis?”

The girl at least had the presence of mind to look embarrassed. “No, but Princess Yue showed us this place, and she never said anything about the spirits. She just said this was a spiritual place.”

Zuko nearly slaps her forehead. “How does the fucking princess of a nation *not* know where the fuck her deity spirits are?”

“Geez you curse like a sailor.” Katara mumbles.

“I *am* a sailor! Now answer the question!”

Katara just shrugs, “I don’t know, but well…it’s not like women play a big role here. It’s terrible. Princess Yue’s just being married off to someone she doesn’t love.”

“Oh please, I’ve been shown off to suitors since I was *twelve*. But that doesn’t excuse the fact that she doesn’t know what the fuck is happening in her own country.”

“Don’t insult her like that!” Katara defends. “At least she’s kind and sweet and generous. Unlike *you.* You’re just a surly, angry teenager who thinks she’s better than everyone.”

Zuko rolls her eyes and crosses her arms. “Oh I’m sorry I didn’t realize Princess Yue was *nice*. That changes *everything*.” She says sarcastically. “Yes, I’m sure niceness is a far more useful than learning how to manage and calculate crop production. And you’re right it’s a lot better to be *sweet* than know how to negotiate trade and understand government policies and law.”

Katara’s face goes red, “That’s not what I meant.”

“Really? Because it sounds like you di—NRRGH!” A sharp pain courses through her skull. She already had her brain rattled by the explosion so can people just *stop* hitting her in the head?

“Katara!” a voice calls out.

*Great*, *the other one’s here*. She opens her eyes to glare at the water tribe teen standing between her and Katara.

“What’s she doing here?” he asks.

“Sokka, she’s here to help us stop Zhao from doing something to the moon and ocean spirit.” Katara says.

The boy looks incredulously at her, “What? Are you serious? Why were you yelling then?”

“Because it doesn’t change the fact that Zuko’s a *bitch*.” Katara replies shooting the fire bender a nasty glare. Zuko returns it with an equally nasty hand gesture. Sokka stares at the two for a moment and the shrugs.

“Alright, well just don’t kill each other before Zhao gets here.” He says.

Zuko turns to him with narrowed eyes. “You’re accepting this too easily.”

Sokka grins and waves his hand, “Hey there’s just one of you, a really beat up version of you— Spirits Katara did you mess her up that badly?”

“No, she came like that.”

“Huh.” Sokka says. “Well anyway, there’s one of you and four of us. And we got a ten ton flying bison. I think we’ll be fine if you decide to double cross us.”

It grates her ego how easily this peasant dismisses her. She wants to shout and scream that she’s more than a threat to their little band of rebels. Instead she *breathes* and lets out a plume of fire from her mouth, trying to release the anger boiling up inside her.

The water tribesmen look warily at her, the white-haired turns a little pale.

“Yeah okay that’s not menacing at all.” the boy says sarcastically.

“Would you rather I breathe it in your face?” she growls.

“No no. I’m good right here.” He replies. “That’s the same move you used on Zhao right?”

“…yes?”

He nods. “I didn’t get to say this earlier since you…y’know chained us up, but those were some sick comebacks you dished out to mutton-chops.”

“Sokka!” Katara chides.

“What? They were. And since we both hate the guy, I think it’s okay that I tell her that.” Sokka sits down and leans back. “So, when exactly is Zhao coming?”

“I reckon we have until nightfall tomorrow.” She answers. “Zhao will attack at daybreak. It won’t take long to break through those walls, and by nightfall he’ll bring a squad of men here to attack the moon and ocean spirit.”

“Wait you know where the moon and ocean spirits are?” the white-haired girl says.

Zuko rolls her eyes. This must be the clueless princess. “Yes, they’re the two fish swimming in the pool. Wasn’t that obvious when you came here?”

“We always thought they were just representations of the moon and ocean spirits.”

“Well they’re the real ones. How could you not know this? You’re a fucking princess of your nation.”

Yue looks down in embarrassment. “We don’t—I don’t really take part in the discussions…”

Zuko wrinkles her nose. “The hell do you do then?”

“Sit around and look pretty.” Yue replies dryly.

The fire bender frowns and stands beside Yue. She turns her head to the side and awkwardly consoles her, “That’s rough, buddy.”

Yue laughs softly, “Yeah, it is.” She says quietly. “I wish I could have stood up more. I thought maybe I could just go to my father. Make him listen…but I was always too scared to talk to him about it.”

“Talking to your father isn’t as great as it’s cracked up to be.” Zuko comments.

“How do you do it?” Yue asks. “How do you get people to take you seriously?”

Zuko grimaces. “I’m not really the person you should be asking. Most of the time they don’t, or I just beat them up enough to get them to listen.”

Katara scoffs, “And you’re always shouting at people. That’s usually a big turn off.”

Zuko shoots her a nasty glare, “Like I said…I’m a bad example. My younger sister’s a lot better at it than I am.”

Sokka holds his hands up. “Wait, there *another* one of you?”

“We’re at war with the *entire* *world*, and the Fire Lord is a title passed down through the Royal Line. Two children are the bare *minimum* you should be having.”

Yue shifts uncomfortably. “I’m the only daughter.”

“That’s just poor family planning. Is that why you’re getting betrothed so soon?”

“Yeah.”

“Hm. Makes sense.” Zuko says. “I would have probably been betrothed at eighteen.”

“I don’t know if I should feel sorry for your future husband or for you.” Sokka quips. Zuko snarls and grabs his collar.

“Say that again?” she growls.

“The husband! I’d feel bad for the husband!” Sokka shouts. Katara rolls her eyes and splashes them both with a water whip.

“What was that for?!” They yelled.

“You two were being idiots.” Katara replies. “Zuko stop being so sensitive, Sokka stop trying to be funny.”

Zuko reluctantly lets go of the boy’s tunic and sits down. She lets fire close to her skin so the water would steam off her.

“Ugh no fair!” Sokka whines, “Why do I have to stay damp? Katara do your water-thingy on me.”

“Just for that I’ll let you stay wet.”

Sokka frowns and then turns to Zuko, “How ‘bout it, wanna do your fire-thingy on me?”

The princess is about to say no, but an evil grin spreads on her lips. Sokka’s about to take it back, but it’s too late. Fire comes shooting from her mouth. Sokka screams, ducks down, and rolls on the grass.

“Katara did you see that!?” he yells, “She almost turned me into water tribe flambe!”

Instead of retaliating, Katara and Yue are trying to hide their laughter behind their hands.

“Betrayal!” Sokka shouts. “I can’t believe you two.”

“Then stop calling our bending the water-thingy or fire-thingy.” Katara retorts. “Besides, you’re dry now.”

Sokka looks down and pouts. She’s right. He’s dry, and his clothes are a nice toasty temperature. It’s not fair.

Zuko’s just sitting there, smiling smugly at him. “Don’t worry, you’ll know if I want to burn you alive.”

“That’s reassuring.” Sokka retorts sarcastically. “So…while we’re waiting for Aang to come back and for this Zhao guy to get here, think you can spill some Fire Nation secrets?”

“Oh yeah sure. What would you like to know? The layout of the palace? The Fire Lord’s schedule? Our new top-secret weapons we’re building?”

Sokka’s eyes light up. “Everything! C’mon spill.”

“Okay, the palace is *biiiigg* building in Caldera City with golden pillars, fifty bedrooms and ten ballrooms. We’ve got a turtleduck pond and roof is made out of spun sugar—”

“Alright there’s no need to be sarcastic.”

“Wait wait, I haven’t told you everything yet.” Zuko says with a faux smile. “The Fire Lord goes out every day passing candy to little orphans and war vets and then goes to sleep on a bed made from Koala-sheep fur—”

“That’s so disturbing.”

“And we’re building a top-secret weapon that will make everyone submit to the fire nation through the power of love!” she finishes.

Sokka does a slow clap and asks, “Are you done?”

“Are you going to stop asking stupid questions?”

“Probably not.”

“Then I have the right to answer your stupid questions with *maximum* disdain and sarcasm.”

“Hey, I’m the sarcasm guy!”

Zuko blinks innocently at him, “Oh Agni really? I didn’t know there was a specific person who does sarcasm for *everyone* here.”

Sokka’s not amused. “Katara’s right, you are a bitch.”

“Gee, you didn’t know that after I thew fireballs at you? You’re a little slow aren’t you?”

Sokka puffs up and they squabble back and forth. Yue leans towards Katara and whispers, “Should we stop them?”

“Not unless Zuko tries to seriously maim him.” Katara replies. “Besides, this is kinda funny.”

Yue smiles to herself. “It is. Honestly, I thought the Fire Nation princess would be a lot… different.”

Katara nods and gazes at the bickering duo. In this moment, Zuko’s face looks a little more relaxed. With no taunt angry lines marring her face, she looks…normal. Like an angsty angry teenager than a weird maniacal girl trying to take them down.

“You know when she first came into our village, she always seemed like this massive immovable villain. The face of evil and the Fire Nation.”

“And now?”

“…I don’t know.” Katara replies. “Some part of me still sees her as this evil villain, but after seeing all the destruction from people like Zhao, she just doesn’t look as bad.”

Yue nods. “You know…it might be a bad thing to say but…I kind of *admire* her.”

Katara shoots her an incredulous look, but Yue continues.

“She refuses to be more than just someone who’ll sit back and let other dictate what she does. She’s going toe to toe with the *Avatar*, how many people are crazy enough to do that?”

“Not many.” Katara admits.

“And she’s protecting her people. No matter the cost. Asking for a truce was probably the hardest thing she’s ever had to do. It takes courage to go to your enemies and ask for help.” Yue looks down at her fiddling hands. “Meanwhile the most I can do is get married.”

Katara puts a hand on her shoulder.

“…Princess Zuko is crass, angry, and a little mean. But she fights for what she believes in. She doesn’t wait for people to hand her things. She doesn’t wait for someone else to save her.” Yue says. “I admire her for that.

Katara nods. “Yeah. I never really told anyone this, but when I found out she was a girl I was… happy. I’d never seen a strong female bender before. I mean I knew women were just as good as fighting as men. The Kiyoshi warriors proved that to my stupid brother, but I hadn’t seen a female bender fight…”

The water bender looks at Zuko, eyes ablaze as she gesticulates wildly at her brother. The Kiyoshi warriors were elegant in their fighting. Zuko is anything but elegant. She’s chaos and raw power, and there’s something strangely empowering about that.

“When I was fighting Master Pakku, I did it for me and to prove to him that women could be powerful benders. But…I also had this image of Zuko. Of her fighting us at every turn. She was my proof. I guess she was my image of victory.”

Yue nods, “Seeing her now, I’m not surprised.”

&#45;--

“Wait wait wait.” Sokka says. “You *followed* turtle-seals down their ice-hole to get *inside*?”

“Yes.”

Sokka doesn’t know if he should laugh or cry. “They can hold their breathes for *thirty* minutes.”

“So?”

“So, you could have *died?*”

“I’m not seeing what the problem is here.”

Sokka slaps his forehead. “You.” He points to her. “Dead.” He emphasizes this by miming a slash to the throat. “Am I getting somewhere here?”

“…no.”

The boy groans and rests his head on his hands. “You’re fucking insane, you know that?”

“I was ready to fight a 100-plus year-old man who mastered all four elements with only fire bending basics.”

Sokka looks up, “Wait…you’re only using basics? You been going head to head with Aang, a master air bender with *basics?*”

Zuko shrugs and looks away embarrassed. “Yeah, so? Took you guys down.”

“I’m trying to compliment you?! And tell you what a crazy person you are.”

A fierce flush overtakes her face. “Don’t patronize me, and I know I’m crazy. My lieutenant tells me that almost every day.”

Sokka shakes his head and sighs. “So…what do you do when you’re not chasing the Avatar?”

Zuko shrugs. “Dispatching bandits. Taking down poachers. Beating up human traffickers. Oh and the girls sometimes take me out on a spa day.”

“…you’re *unbelievable*.”

&#45;--

“You…found bones in the Southern Air Temple?” Zuko asks quietly.

Katara nods and glares, “Yeah since your nation decided to kill them and leave their bones there.”

Zuko looks down uncomfortably. “I’m…sorry. I thought I got all the bones.”

Yue looks at her with wide eyes. “…you thought you got all the bones?”

Zuko nods. “The first thing I did when I went to the air temples was burn the bones and give them funeral rites. There was just…a lot of dead bodies. Fire and Air alike. I guess my great-grandfather didn’t care about our deceased.”

Sokka puts a hand on her shoulder, “That was…really good of you.”

She shrugs, “Sorry I didn’t find the other ones. No one should have to see the remains of their parental-figure.”

Katara sighs and slumps her shoulders. “It’s okay. It wasn’t your fault. They were hidden pretty well.”

&#45;--

“I can’t believe your father almost married you off to Zhao.” Yue says horrified.

Zuko shrugs. “It’s fine. It probably wouldn’t have happened anyway. He wasn’t from a prominent family and is bending is only so-so.”

Yue gets a glint in her eye, “You know what I don’t understand? Why do the suitors always try to give you animal rugs? Or worse, mounted heads.”

The other princess rolls her eyes and throws her hands up. “I *know* right? What about me screams ‘I’d like a platypus bear head decorating my wall’? First of all, it would just clash with the décor.”

“They never think about the décor.”

“Never.” Zuko agrees exasperatedly. “And second, they’re just ugly. I always end up using them for target practice.”

“I *wish* I could have used them for target practice.” Yue says. “I ended up making a storage closet to toss them in.”

“That’s terrible. I actually prefer flowers or food over anything else. The other ladies say those are pretty drab gifts, but what am I going to do with a five-pound bracelet or bejeweled sword? They don’t even have the decency to give me a well-balanced sword.”

“I agree. Do you know how many stupid bracelets I have in one of my chests? Stop giving me jewelry! I don’t want to walk around jangling and blinding people on the streets. At least with food and flowers you can either eat them or they’ll die.”

Zuko sighs, “I missed those days where suitors would just hunt down a tiger-buffalo or fight to the death. I’d have less junk that way.”

“I know.” Yue commiserates.

Sokka frowns. “You *just* said you didn’t like dead animals!”

“No, we said we didn’t like *mounted* dead animals or useless pelt rugs. If only they had the decency to give us the meat with it. I’d at least be able to have a nice roast for all the trouble.” Zuko retorts.

Sokka shakes his head and turns to Katara, “I don’t understand princesses.”

She pats his head, “And you never will.”

&#45;--

“If you could only choose one meal what would you pick: five flavor soup or sea prune stew?” Sokka asks.

“I’d rather die. They’re disgusting.” Zuko answers.

“You don’t count. You’re not a woman of culture.” Sokka retorts.

“I’m a *princess!*”

“I stand by my claim.”

&#95;&#42;&#95;&#42;&#95;

It’s strange talking to the three water tribesmen. They’re not the uncultured troglodytes she’d been led to believe, and even the boy is…alright. He’s at least more intelligent than she gave him credit for. The Northern Princess is actually fun to talk to. She seems to understand the things about palace life she misses, and she has excellent taste in classic literature. Too bad the Northerners don’t really do many plays. Zuko’s sure she’d love some of the theatre productions the Fire Nation has.

The thought sours in her stomach. She’s not supposed to be gallivanting with the Water Tribe. This was supposed to be a cold alliance forged by convenience. But she can’t help herself from gravitating towards the conversations and light laughter. It’s been…well never since she’s been a part of a group of teenagers who are just…relaxing. It’s nice.

Then the sounds of the catapults jolt them from their conversation. Zuko can see the smoke rising and feel the fires starting to light around her.

She sighs and says, “It’s begun.”

### End Notes

#### Chapter End Notes

**Lu Ten:** Stahp! No! Bad Water tribe boy! Stay away from my sister damnit! Agni is this your punishment?!

The siege will begin next chapter!

Thank you for reading!

## Chapter 5

### Notes

#### Chapter Notes

**Warning:** Mentions of abortion

### Text

---

Aang is taking his sweet time to wake up. He didn’t know what was taking so long in the Spirit World. Momo screeches ahead, and the boy curses. Zhao is on his way here, and Aang is still not back. To make matters worse, he’s vulnerable in this state. He looks to Katara who looks to Aang worriedly. They didn’t have a choice.

He turns to Yue and says, “You need to get Aang out of here.” He says. “Take Appa and get somewhere safe.”

Yue looks defiantly at him. “No, I won’t leave you Sokka!” But Sokka takes her hand.

“You have to Princess. Aang’s vulnerable in this state, and we can’t protect him *and* the Moon Spirit while Zhao’s men are here. You have to protect him.”

Yue bites her lip and nods. “Okay.” Sokka nods and loads the young boy onto Appa. Just before Yue climbs up, she kisses him on the cheek. “Be safe.” She whispers.

Sokka gives her a small smile. “I will.”

He watches her leave on Appa and prays to the Spirits to keep them safe. Katara puts a hand on his shoulder, “They’ll be fine.”

“I know. We have bigger problems now.”

Zuko stands near the entrance, coiled and ready to fight. “He’s close.” She says.

“How can you tell?”

“I can sense their fire.” She answers.

True to her word, Zhao appears with several soldiers at his side. He narrows his eyes at her.

“Princess Zuko. It seems rumors of your death were greatly exaggerated.”

“It takes more than an explosion to kill me.” she retorts.

“And now you’re galivanting with the water savages.” Zhao continues. “You’re a *traitor* to your country.”

“You’re the traitor Zhao! You’ll doom us all if you harm the moon spirit.”

“I will save us all!” Zhao denies. “They’ll hail me as the greatest Admiral in Fire Nation History. And you’ll be nothing more than a traitor princess.” He sweeps his arms and unleashes a hot wild mass of flames. Sokka curses as the flames burn through most of this trip wire. Not even a few minutes in and things are already going bad.

Zuko sweeps the flames away from the water tribe siblings and unleashes her own flames at him. While the two battle, Katara and Sokka take the soldiers Zhao brings. Sokka uses the forms Suki taught him to fight the fire benders while Katara freezes them with the water.

Things seems to be going well, but then the soldiers wise up. They gang up on Sokka, and he finds himself on the end of a very hot burning fist. It’s enough to cause Katara to panic and freeze, and that’s enough of a moment for one of the fire benders to hit her with a blast of fire from behind, knocking her out.

“Katara!” Sokka yells, but the men wave their fists threateningly in his face. Two grab him and make him stand. The soldiers call to Zuko and both Zhao and her freeze. The admiral grins and looks to the princess.

“It seems I have the upper hand, Princess.” Zhao says smugly. Zuko glances to the siblings then to the admiral. She could keep fighting, Sokka might be able to go free. Anyway what’s one burn on a water tribesman when it comes to the *moon*? But…

She looks back at Sokka. His eyes shining and lips taunt. For a brief second his eyes flash to his sister, and Zuko understands.

He doesn’t care what happened to him, but his sister…

Zuko clenches her teeth and lowers her arms. She couldn’t do it. She remembers the pain from her Father’s hand, she wouldn’t wish it on anyone.

Zhao laughs, “Like I said before, Princess. You’re a coward and a failure.” He strides to the pool and grabs a bag. He scoops the fish and holds it up. Suddenly, the moon turns a blood red and Zuko feels her blood curdle.

“Zhao don’t do this.” she pleads. “You’re bringing disaster upon everyone.”

“No, I’m bringing victory on the nation.” He says turning towards her. He holds the bag up in one hand, and in the other his fists are burning brightly.

Then a powerful blast of fire shoots towards Zhao. The Admiral barely dodges, and he glares.

“General Iroh. Are you a traitor as well?” he snarls.

Iroh glares, “The only traitor I see is you, Zhao. Put down the spirit. You cannot comprehend the calamity that will befall you and our people.”

“I am trying to bring a great victory to the nation!”

“No, you are trying to seek glory for yourself!” Iroh retorts. “Please! Think about your actions. It’s not too late to stop.”

The Admiral pauses, and then slowly puts the fish back in the pool. For a moment Zuko feels relief.

Then Zhao does the unthinkable.

He shoots a blast of slam to the pool, and the moon goes dark.

Unbridled fury rushes through her. “ZHAO!!” she screams and both her and Iroh shoot a powerful blast towards him, but the Admiral gets away. Zuko doesn’t hesitate and follows after him.

*He’s doomed her people.*

&#95;&#42;&#95;&#42;&#95;

Aang wakes up with a gasp. Something is terribly wrong.

The sky is completely dark, and it feels like a vice is gripping his heart. He looks around and sees that he’s on Appa. Princess Yue is on top of the Bison, clutching her chest.

“Yue!” Aang exclaims. “What happened?!”

“I’m—I’m not sure but…I think—I think the Moon Spirit is dead.” She whispers.

Aang’s head reels.

No. No that can’t be. How could he let this happen? He’s the Avatar, he should have protected the Spirit. He should have prevented this.

*I failed again.*

“We need to go back.” Aang says. “There’s got to be a way to fix this.” he says desperately.

Yue nods, “I think…I think I know how.” She says.

Aang looks at her quizzically, and she tells the story of what happened to her when she was born.

“The Moon Spirit gave her life to me. I think—I think it’s my destiny…my duty to give it back to her.”

“Yue…” Aang starts to say. “No, no there’s got to be another way.” He says fiercely and urges Appa forward.

They land in the Spirit Oasis, and Aang feels his heart drop when he sees Katara unconscious and Sokka kneeling at the edge of the pool with the familiar Fire Bender that’s always with Zuko. In his hands is the dead body of the Spirit.

Aang doesn’t wait for Appa to land. He rushes over and looks despondently at the dead moon.

Sokka looks at him sadly. “Aang…I’m sorry we couldn’t—we couldn’t save her. Zhao…Zhao killed it.”

An indescribable fury enveloped Aang. He doesn’t hear Sokka and the old man shouting at him as he enters the pool with the Ocean Spirit. Suddenly he’s himself, but not really. Everything feels… muddled. And there’s so much anger—fury—sadness—despair—*loneliness*.

*The Fire Nation.* ***Agni’s children did this****. They murdered* ***my wife****—*the moon—***We****(*I*) need to get* ***revenge****(justice).* ***They need to pay****.*

&#95;&#42;&#95;&#42;&#95;

Sokka’s eyes grow wide as Aang’s body is enveloped in a blue light and the Ocean Spirit drags him down, moving through the canals and into the Tribe. Sokka goes to follow him, but Yue’s arm stops him.

“Don’t.” she says pleadingly. “He’s not himself, he’s consumed by the Ocean Spirit’s anger. The only way to stop it now is to restore the moon.”

“How?” he whispers.

Yue smiles sadly. “I need to give the Moon her life back.”

Sokka’s eyes widen, and he shakes his head. “No, no. What—what are you talking about?”

“When I was a baby, I wasn’t breathing. My parents came here and asked the Moon Spirit to gift me life…And she did.”

The teen feels his heart drop and his chest is restricting. He grips her hand tightly. “No, no you can’t, Yue.” He begs.

“I have to Sokka, for my people.” She kisses him and slips out of his hands. She walks to Iroh and nods. The old fire bender bows in reverence.

“You are a brave young woman.” He says.

Yue smiles, “Princess Zuko would have done the same.”

Iroh’s eyes widen, but Yue is only focused on the fish. It’s instinctual. Perhaps from when the moon touched her the first time. She places her hands on the fish and light envelopes her. All at once, the images of her life flash before her.

*Father, I’m sorry I couldn’t say goodbye.*

*Sokka, thank you for letting me feel love and happiness with you.*

*Katara, thank you for being a friend.*

*Aang, please don’t blame yourself, this was always going to be my fate.*

*Princess Zuko, thank you for inspiring me to be brave.*

She feels a warm presence fill her being. Suddenly, she’s Yue and yet…not. Something more. She opens her eyes and sees Sokka starting at her with awe and sadness. Katara is still out cold near the pool, so Yue bends down and touches the water bender’s forehead. Light travels through her and heals her wounds.

Then she goes to Sokka and places her hands on his cheeks. She leans forward and kisses him sweetly. The mortal part of her despairing for the time she could have had with him and rejoicing for the time she had with him. But there’s an incessant pull, and she has to let go.

With one final smile, she says, “***Thank you for everything, Sokka. I’ll always be watching over*** ***you***.” And she leaves the mortal plane behind, ascending into the sky and becoming the moon.

The world suddenly lights back up again, and the Ocean Spirit looks up from its rampage. The soothing rays of its love, dampening the anger. It turns away and heads back towards the Oasis, but before it goes back to its wife there’s just one more thing it needs to do.

Zuko is running through the road, throwing fireball after fireball at Zhao. The man gives as good as he takes and retaliates. They’re at a bridge when Zhao turns around and goes into a fighting stance.

She mirrors it and is ready to start firing at him again, when a giant blue hand sweeps over their heads. The princess manages to duck, but it grabs Zhao. She doesn’t even think; she runs to the bridge and yell, “Take my hand!”

For a second, the Admiral starts to reach his hand out, but at the last second, his face morphs to disgust and he takes it back. Letting the Ocean take him.

Zuko grimaces as she watches the Admiral disappear below the water. She wouldn’t wish his fate on anyone. Without the adrenaline coursing through her, she slides down the railing and shuts her eyes for a moment. It was over.

Somehow the moon was restored, but she wonders if it came on time.

Was the fleet okay?

Did her crew get out in time?

How many survived?

She didn’t know. She just wants to get out of this cold place. There’s a warm hand on her shoulder and she sees her Uncle there, helping her up.

“We must go. The Northerners are rounding up any Fire Nation soldiers that are left.” He says urgently. Zuko numbly follows him and they get on a raft. Iroh pushes them off and starts paddling away. She sighs and turns on her side. Her head feels heavy. She just wants to sleep. Her eyes close, and she blissfully falls into oblivion.

&#95;&#42;&#95;&#42;&#95;

Sokka grimaces as he looks at the wanton destruction from above. They’ve been combing the area with the remains of the Northern fleet to salvage supplies and find survivors. So far, he can only see the bloated bodies of Fire Nation soldiers and the tattered remains of the fleet. Aang tried to go the first day, but after seeing the death he indirectly caused, the poor boy couldn’t stomach it, literally. He’s staying at the tribe with Katara, helping with the rebuilding efforts.

The boy sighs as he sees another downed ship in the water. It’s probably safe to say that all of the Fire Nation’s Western Fleet is gone. On some level, it makes Sokka glad. It’s just one more thing they don’t have to worry about, but on the other hand…

He remembers the desperate look on Zuko’s face when she talks about her people. The sheer anger at Zhao’s careless actions. He thinks about his small village waiting for their husbands, fathers, brother, children. He wonders how many widows and orphans they made last night.

He wonders if it could have all been avoided if he was stronger.

Sokka clenches his fists. It was his fault. If he hadn’t been taken down so easily, Katara would have been fine, Zuko wouldn’t have yielded to Zhao…

Yue would still be here.

The pain is still fresh in his heart. He couldn’t look Chief Arnook in the eye when he told him of his daughter’s death. The man had cried bitterly when he heard the news. He’s only now just managing to pull himself back up to run the Tribe.

In his musings he almost misses it, but then Appa grumbles below him. He looks up and sees a smoke on the horizon. He urges the bison forward and he sees a few ships. Damaged but otherwise alright enough to sail slowly along. Sokka sees the crew shout and point to him. He swallows, feeling torn.

On one hand these are the Fire Nation ships, they invaded the North. They should be captured and interrogated. But on the other hand, he looks back towards the sunken vessels and the floating bodies. He closes his eyes and tells Appa to turn back around.

There’s been enough death.

(He’s secretly happy that not all of Zuko’s people perished here.)

The Northern tribe slowly recovers, and they stay a week to help out before heading out again. They held a beautiful funeral for the late Princess Yue, and Sokka sheds many tears that day. Aang slowly regains his cheery temperament, and soon they start packing for the next leg of their journey. Master Pakku and some other Northerners are going to head south to help their sister tribe.

It’s a relief for Sokka. He knew that Gran Grand and the others would be fine, but it’s reassuring to know a Master water bender and various warriors are going to help look after them.

He packs the last of the supplies and hops onto Appa. He looks out to the Northern city. Pristine snow covers the length of the land. If he wasn’t there for it, he would never have thought the Fire Nation attacked. He could pretend Princess Yue is down there, waving good-bye. Maybe he could have even persuaded her to join them.

But there’s no sense in dreaming now. The other two join him shortly, and they say their last good-byes to the Northern Water Tribe.

He sighs and leans back. It was their first major battle against the Fire Nation, and it ended in a pyrrhic victory. Their first taste of true war, and it leaves a bitter feeling in his mouth.

*It won’t happen again.*

&#95;&#42;&#95;&#42;&#95;

It took nearly a week for them to get to land. After she slept for nearly two days, she couldn’t rest any longer and stayed awake throughout the rest of the way. Sometimes at night, she thinks she can hear the moaning and cries of the dead soldiers. Once, when she stared too deeply into the ocean, she saw hundreds of faces staring back at her. Begging her to lead them home. Their swollen faces crying out against the darkness.

After that, she started keeping a memorial flame lit. No more faces haunted her since.

(The only comfort out of that ordeal was that none of the faces belonged to her crewmen.)

The spa they landed in knew her Uncle and let them stay to recuperate. She won’t admit it, but it was nice to have people pamper her again after the rough few weeks she had.

The fingers dug deeply into her muscles, and she sighs in contentment. They work out the knots and stresses that are plaguing her, the scent of lavender and citrus easing her into a light doze. When she wakes, the masseuse is asking her to turn over. Strong fingers rub her shoulders and work their way up her neck to her head. Zuko sighs as those long fingers massage her scalp and lightly ghost over the recovering bruises and cuts on her face.

When she finishes, Iroh is waiting for her in their room.

“So, I’ve called up some contacts, and they say that they’ll have a ship ready for us in a week or so.” Iroh says.

“What about a crew?”

“Ah well…that’s a little trickier.” Iroh says. “With sailors being…rarer commodities now, the navy’s keeping a tight grip on whoever’s left.”

There’s a painful ache in her chest as she thinks about the loss of their Western fleet. She hopes that Jee and the rest of the crew were able to get to safety.

“That would make sense.” She says. “…I guess it’s fitting that I need to find a new ship and crew on the third anniversary of my banishment.”

Iroh sighs sadly. “Perhaps we can stay here a little longer, niece. Relaxation is good for your spirit. You’ve been through a lot these past few weeks. You should take time to rest and recuperate in a safe place.”

Zuko rolls her eyes. “There’s no safe place for me, Uncle.”

“I think you just need to take the time to find that place for you, Princess Zuko. And if it happens to be on a nice beach somewhere, all the better!” he jokes.

The princess crosses her arms, “I don’t want to stay here any longer than we have to. We’ll have another night to gather supplies and pack our things. Then I want to go.” She doesn’t wait for Iroh to answer and heads to bed.

Outside Iroh sighs and looks up. “Ah what am I going to do, Lu Ten?”

After much cajoling, Iroh manages to get Zuko to collect shells with him. The princess grumbles and kicks the sand throughout the endeavor, but she does pick a few shells for her Uncle. It reminds her of the adventures she and Lu Ten would have when they went to Ember Island.

Iroh holds them up and grins, “Ah how beautiful.” He says. “I’ll enjoy these for years to come.”

Zuko huffs in irritation. “Uncle, we don’t need to be hampered by more junk. We’re carrying all our stuff now.”

Uncle’s about to reply, but an old familiar voice calls out from the darkened corner of the room. “Hello, Sister, Uncle.”

*Azula.*

Her heart drops, and her blood goes cold. Her younger sister has hardly changed in the three years she’s been gone. A little taller, face a little sharper, but there’s still the tell-tale baby fat in her cheeks. The dark kohl on her eyelid and lashes make her eyes look narrower, maturing her face, and the salmon pink lipstick plumps her normally thin lips.

But despite her appearance, she’s still the same Azula. The young teen still sits like she owns every bit of land she touches, and her eyes are still a cold golden hue.

“What do you want Azula?” Zuko asks.

The question seems to irate the younger girl. She destroys one of Uncle’s shells and insults them for dithering around. But then…

Then she brings news that makes Zuko’s heart soar.

Father wants her back. He *cares.*

*But Azula always lies*.

No, no she wouldn’t lie over something like this.

*Are you sure?*

Zuko growls at the voice in her head and shakes it out as she packs. No. Azula wouldn’t lie over something like this. It’s too big, if Azula brought her back to the Fire Nation under false pretenses then it would spell trouble for the younger girl. Azula would never jeopardize her position for Zuko.

*Isn’t this too convenient?*

Of course not. Didn’t Azula say that there’s been rumors of a coup? Father probably wants to bolster his defense and claim to the throne. Besides, Father probably heard about the fiasco in the North and realized that Zuko protected her people. He’s probably going to reward her service by bringing her back. Zuko smiles to herself. She’s done it. She’s proven her worth to her Father.

Now if only Uncle would go with her.

The lines are set deep in his face as he watches his niece smile and pack excitedly.

“Zuko…I’ve never known my brother to change his mind. I don’t trust this.”

Zuko throws some clothes on the ground and glares at her Uncle. “Why?! Because you think I don’t deserve to be recognized for my services?! I’m not a lazy old man like you! I’ve given my blood, sweat and tears for my nation. Father has finally seen my worth and he’s bringing me home!”

Then she deflates and grips her Uncle’s shoulders. “Please Uncle, I don’t want to go back without you. We could go home again. We could see Lu Ten’s grave; we could have tea under the wisteria-willows again. We could…we could go back to *normal* again.”

Iroh sighs and takes her hand in his, “Zuko, listen to me. *Please*. Ozai does not hold your honor or worth. *You do*. You’ve done more for our people than anyone. You don’t need Ozai.”

“I *need* my family!”

“*I* *am your family!* I’ve been your family since I held you in my arms when you were a baby. I’ve watched you grow. Laugh, cry, and *suffer*. Please, think about this carefully.” Iroh pleads. “When has your father ever changed his mind? When has he ever cared about *family*?”

Anger bubbles under the surface of her skin. She tears herself away from her Uncle. “Fine! I don’t need you anyway.” She shouts. “Stay in this dirty stingy town. I’ll regain my rightful place by my Father’s side, and I won’t need you anymore!”

She slams the door to her room and roars as she lets out a strong burst of flame. It chars the walls, but she can’t bring herself to care. She slides into the bed and closes her eyes.

Iroh wasn’t around that morning, much to her disappointment. She slowly gathers her things and starts walking down the path to the ship. Her stomach is in knots with each step she takes. What will Father say? How will everyone take her return? Will she have to start seeing suitors again?

The thought makes her blanche. She hopes her Father will wait before starting it again.

Suddenly she hears her Uncle calling. He’s in his old armor and running down the steps with his pack. “Wait for me, Zuko!” he yells.

Zuko feels a weight lift off her and she smiles brilliantly. “Uncle, you came.”

Iroh returns the smile and pats her shoulder. “Family sticks together.” He says.

The princess can’t stop the grin from overtaking her face. It’ll be alright now. Uncle will be with her every step of the way. The thought comforts her and she takes his hand as they go down towards the ship.

A line of soldiers awaits them, and they part as Zuko walks through. Azula is standing at the top of the gangplank. She welcomes them with open arms.

“Brother, Uncle. I’m so pleased to have you on my ship. You’ve been away from home long enough. Prepare to go!”

The captain nods, “Yes Princess, bring the prisoners up!”

&#46;

&#46;

&#46;

It’s only a second, but it feels like the moment stretches for hours. Icy rage fills her veins, and she lunges for Azula. Uncle tries to call out to her, but Zuko isn’t listening. All she can see is her sister, smiling smugly at her as she toys with Zuko.

“How could you?!” Zuko roars. “Why would Father do this?!”

“Because you and Uncle are solely responsible for the utter defeat in the North.” Azula explains. “You’ve done nothing but bring shame on our family. So, Father’s going to lock you up deep inside the palace where you won’t shame him anymore!”

She smiles as she starts the kata for bringing forth lightening. Zuko can only stand in shock as she watches her baby sister gather the cold fire in her fingertips. Azula thrusts her hand forward, but suddenly, Iroh is there, grabbing her hand. He flings his free hand out and lightening shoots into the mountain face. Then he throws Azula overboard and grabs Zuko’s arm.

They run.

Zuko doesn’t know how long they go, but then Uncle stops and looks back. “I think this is far enough.” He says.

The princess kneels at the riverbank. Her head is numb as she takes out the dagger. It’s only by instinct that she holds the knife to her phoenix tail and slices it off. She hands it wordlessly to her Uncle who cuts his topknot off.

That settles it then.

She’s a traitor of her nation.

She can never go back.

Uncle leads her to the woods and lays her catatonic body against the tree. He’s gone for a long while, but when he comes back, his armor is gone, and he’s wearing plain dark grey robes. He hands her a pair of clothes with a similar shade. She takes it and heads to the bushes to change.

It’s only when Uncle, sits and starts a fire with spark rocks that she finally breaks down and cries. Iroh gathers her up gently and holds her through her convulsing cries.

&#95;&#42;&#95;&#42;&#95;

Zuko growls as another rabbit-gopher eludes her. She picks herself from the ground and stomps through the bushes, scaring away any small creatures she might have been able to catch.

“I can’t live like this Uncle!” she shouts. “I’m not meant to be a fugitive!”

Maybe she sounds like a spoiled princess when she says this, but in her defense, she is a spoiled princess. Even on the *Dragon’s Claw*, she still always had food and supplies that were beyond the means of commoners, even those living comfortably in the middle-class. She’s always been afforded the luxuries of her status. Banished or not.

Now she has to wear these itchy rags and *forage* for food. She grits her teeth as her Uncle tries to explain the strange plant he’s enamored with.

She slaps his hand away from the flower. “Don’t touch random plants you don’t know!” she scolds. Honestly, he’s the adult here. He’s the one who went to war here. Shouldn’t he know dangers plants and how to hunt Earth Kingdom creatures? Then she remembers that he was a royal general in the army. He didn’t do the foraging or hunting, his subordinates did.

Great. Two royals stuck in the wilderness. She’d give them three days before one of them croaks.

Zuko shakes her head and proclaims, “I’m going fishing.” Maybe those were easier to catch. The water tribe peasant seemed to be proficient at it based on the stories he told. If stupid Sokka can do it, how hard could it be?

It takes her maybe an hour before she catches the smallest sorriest excuse of a fish on her disproportionately enormous stick. She trudges back to the clearing and finds her Uncle’s become a swollen hideous mass.

She drags her hands over face, “Uncle…you *didn’t.*”

“I did. And it *wasn’t*.”

She takes back what she said earlier. They’ll be dead by morning.

&#45;--

They manage to find a clinic that’s kind enough to help them out for only a few coins. The healer, Song is putting on a green paste over Uncle’s body and scolding him for ingesting the poisonous plants. “You must not be from around here.” Song says. “What are your names?”

Zuko’s brain falters for a moment. “Uuhh, ah my name. Yeess of course you would want to know our very *real* names.”

Uncle is snickering behind Song and Zuko throws a nasty glare at him. “I’m…Lin and that’s my uncle—erm—Mushi.”

‘Mushi’ is not amused by the name. Not that Zuko cares.

Song smiles, “Lin and Mushi. It’s nice to meet you.”

Uncle spout off some nonsense about her name coming from her mother, but it seems to endear them to the girl. She invites them to dinner and while the offer is tempting, Zuko doesn’t want to spend too much time with them. Uncle doesn’t seem to have any qualms and accepts the invitation for both of them.

Before they leave the clinic, Song turns to Zuko and asks, “Is there anything else I can get you?” she asks with a significant look.
