“…Sanitary pads and silphium if you have it.” Zuko says taking out their meager coin. Song refuses the money and gives her a thick pack.

“There’s enough silphium for a month.” She says. “I’m sorry I can’t give you more.”

“…it’s more than enough.” Zuko mumbles unused to this charity. The healer only smiles and leads them towards her home. It’s spacious and comfortable. Nothing like Zuko’s home. She eats methodically, not really tasting the roast duck her Uncle is singing praises for. When she’s done, Song takes her plate and insists that she sits down. She awkwardly sits while Uncle and Song’s mother converse.

Finally, Song’s mother rises and says, “Lin, if it’s alright, could I borrow you for a bit?”

Zuko eyes her suspiciously but nods and stands. If she tries anything, she could probably fend her off. The woman leads her to a room stocked full of herbs. She takes out a packet filled with dried powder and hands it to Zuko.

“It’s a mixture of Moonshade and Profinis roots.” She explains. “It’s to abort unwanted pregnancies.”

Zuko grows cold. She’s heard of these herbs vaguely. Most women in court use manual means to abort the baby, few have turned to herbal remedies.

Song’s mother grips her hand tightly. “It’s dangerous out on the road for young girls like you. If the unthinkable should happen…at least you’ll have a choice.”

The teen swallows and nods. “Thank you.” She says hoarsely. The thought hadn’t even occurred to her, but thinking about it now…

She looks down at her raggedy clothes. They’re boyish yes, but they don’t hide her figure like her armor did. There’s the definitive swell of her breasts, her curves and wide hips. Even when she was on her ship, she never once thought about hiding her sex. Why would she? It was just another part of her.

But now she’s no longer surrounded by loyal soldiers. She’s alone with her Uncle on the roads. She’s saved enough victims to know what bandits or robbers do to unprotected young women. It makes her sick to think of it.

Song’s mother puts a hand on her shoulder, “I don’t mean to scare you dear, but it doesn’t hurt to be prepared.”

“I appreciate it.”

The matronly woman smiles and ushers her back to the dining room. “Come, let’s have dessert.”

As Iroh and Song’s mother play a game of pai sho, Zuko sits outside pensively looking into the night sky. For a moment, she thinks of the water tribe siblings. Are they here now? The Avatar should be looking for an Earth teacher next. What would she do? A part of her screams to chase after to catch him. Maybe her Father would rescind his order.

*Or maybe he’ll just throw you in the cell next to the Avatar*.

She growls and chucks a rock at the pond. It gives a dissatisfying splash and she huffs, leaning back against her elbows.

“Mind if I join?” Song asks.

Zuko doesn’t answer, but Song seems to take it as an invitation. She sits beside the other teen quietly. “You’ve been hurt badly, haven’t you?” Song asks.

Zuko is tempted to point at her scar, but Song continues. “I don’t mean just physically. I mean…in your spirit.”

The princess tenses, but Song ignores it. She brings her leg up and lifts the hem of the hanbok. Zuko winces when she sees the scarred flesh. Her people did that, to a young girl. Why? She isn’t a soldier, she’s just a civilian. But Zuko doesn’t ask.

“I was hurt too. It took me a long time to come to terms with it.” Song admits quietly. “But I think you’re strong enough to do it too.”

Zuko wants to deny that she’s broken. She wants to rail out against the world and just…scream. She doesn’t though. She bites her tongue and says, “I don’t need you to tell me that.”

Song chuckles and nods. “You’re right, but I think little encouragement never hurts.”

They go to leave in the dead of night, but Song and her mother meet them at the front. They’re holding the reigns of an ostrich-horse and smiling at the two. Song hands Zuko the reigns.

“You need her more than we do.” She says.

Zuko purses her lips and tries to push the hand away. “You’ve given us enough.”

Song only pushes back, “It’s our gift to you. From one refugee to another.”

Iroh nudges her side and nods. Zuko sighs and takes the reigns. She helps Iroh up and turns to Song.

“I’ll pay you back.” She says.

The healer only laughs. “Okay, I’ll look forward to seeing you again, Lin.”

As she rides down the road with her uncle, she can’t stop thinking about the incomprehensible actions of the two women.

“Why did they help us?” She asks. “We’re strangers to them. Just another pair of travelers.”

Iroh hums and muses. “Perhaps because they’ve seen true horror and destruction, and they saw it reflected in you.”

Zuko scowls. “Why do people keep insisting that I’m broken?”

“On the contrary, I think they know you’re not broken. Just hurt and lost, and they want to offer a bit of kindness to someone who hasn’t seem much of it in her life.” Iroh explains.

It still made no sense to her, but she bites back her questions and continue riding up until dawn.

&#95;&#42;&#95;&#42;&#95;

They camp in the forest and beg on the streets for coin. It’s debasing and humiliating as she watches her uncle ‘dance’ for the man. He shouldn’t have to do this. He should be enjoying tea in the comforts of the palace. She’s told him more times than she could count how humiliating this was for them, but her uncle continues to smile and insist that there is honor in humility and living in poverty. How insane.

She watches him dance and sing on the streets. How could anyone call this *honor*? Even with the coin the scrounge up during the day, it isn’t enough. She sees her uncle slowly lose weight. His face growing sallow and paunch slowly receding. When she tries to offer him more, he just smiles and pushes it back to her.

“You’re a growing girl, Zuko. You should eat more. I’m an old man who can afford to lose a few pounds.”

She can’t watch this anymore. Not when there’s something she can do about it. At first, she’s tempted to rob people. Well…she did rob one man, the pompous one with the dao swords who slashed at her uncle’s feet. It was child’s play to sneak up on him and take the swords, and she relished his screams when she came down from the rooftops.

It would be easy to rob people. Most don’t know any self-defense, but…

She remembers Song and her mother.

It would be wrong to rob from the locals. They were also just trying to get by. She’s sighs as she walks through the streets trying to find cheap fruits or vegetables for her Uncle to eat. When suddenly, an older woman tosses out a young man.

“You call this EMBROIDERY?! My grandmother has better stitching, and she’s blind as a bat! Get out! I don’t want to see you again, you sorry lazy excuse of an apprentice!” and she slams the door. Zuko’s interest is piqued. When the sobbing man leaves, she makes her way to the door and goes inside.

The cranky woman looks up from her sewing and says, “You won’t find any coin here, beggar.”

Zuko glares and nearly storms out. But she reigns in her temper and says, “I’m here to offer my… services.” She grits out.

The old woman scoffs. “Doesn’t sound like you want to offer any services child. Now get out.”

“I can embroider!” she yells. “And it’s good enough for royalty.”

She doesn’t mention that most of her gift recipients were a doting cousin and uncle and a somewhat kind grandfather who just happened to be part of the royal family line.

The woman raises her brow and tosses her a clean blank white cloth.

“Prove it.” She says pointing to the corner with hundreds of threads and needles. Zuko immediately sits down and gets to work. Her fingers moving swiftly across the white cloth and weaving a picture. She finishes about an hour later, on the cloth is a beautiful image of a badgermole training Oma and Shu. The woman’s eyes widen and looks to Zuko.

“Not bad.” She says. “Who did you apprentice under?”

“My mother.”

“Did she have a name? Any notable works or figures who adorns her art?”

Zuko shakes her head. “She was an…actress. She only knew enough for costumes, and she taught me.”

The old woman nods slowly. “Very well. Come in tomorrow, and I’ll give you some work. Depending on how much you’ll finish I’ll pay you accordingly.”

“Okay.” Zuko answer, but the woman grabs her arm.

“And before you go, grab something out of the clothing rack. I won’t be having a dirty beggar working in my store.”

“I don’t have any coin to pay for it.”

The woman holds up her work. “This piece is worth at least three outfits.”

Zuko looks incredulously at her. “That’s not possible, it’s just a silly piece. I wasn’t even that careful with the stitching around the edges.”

This seems to catch the older woman off guard. She peers carefully at the work and notices the miniscule imperfections in the boarders stitching. Still, if the girl thinks this is considered a lowly piece…

“You heard me girl!” she yells. “Grab three outfits, and I’ll even throw in this sleeping robe.” She tosses a soft pale green robe at the dirty girl who grunts and sputters as she grabs the offending article.

“You crazy old hag! You can’t just throw clothing at people!”

“I’m a 75-year old woman! I’m entitled to do whatever I want to young stupid children who talk back to their elders.” She retorts. “Now get to it, before I start throwing shoes at you.”

Zuko glares and stomps to the back where there are clothes already on the shelf. She picks out three simple outfits. All a muted brown or dark earthy green. The old woman nods in approval, but notices one is much too big for her.

“That one’s not your size child.” She says.

Zuko turns her head away. “It’s for my Uncle.” She responds. The old woman scoffs.

“Alright then. What’s your name girl?”

“Lin.”

“Hmph common.” She mutters. “I’m Shandi, you’ll come here just after sunrise and I’ll put you to work. And I don’t want to see you in your dirty beggar’s clothes. We get some important rich merchants here.”

“Fine.” Zuko says. “But I can only stay here for a week or so.”

“Then you better churn out as many of those embroideries as you can.” Shandi responds.

Iroh looks at the clothes Zuko present to him. “These are quite nice, niece. Where did you get them?” he asks suspiciously.

“…I’m going to work for the local seamstress this week.” She says. “I’m hoping, I’ll earn enough money for us once we head back on the road.”

Her uncle smiles, “Oh Zuko, you didn’t have to do that.”

The girl only scoffs in response, “I’m not going to sit back and watch you starve. Not if I have something that can help earn some coin.”

Iroh laughs and pulls her close to him. “I’m so lucky to have such a wonderful niece.”

Zuko huffs but let’s herself fall into Uncle’s warm embrace.

Shandi is a grueling task master. She hands her a list of items she wants done, gives her some rough sketches and lets her go work on her own. Zuko is fine with that; she doesn’t want a crazy old lady hovering over her anyway. She hands the finished pieces to the woman who inspects them and nods. Zuko finishes the pile early and Shandi brings her over to help with the sewing. Zuko’s never sewed clothes before, but the stitching is familiar enough, and she’s quick to pick it up.

Sometimes Shandi would scold her and tear the stitching apart. Making her re-do it. Zuko tries very hard not to smack the old lady. But most times she looks over her work and grunts something like, “You’re not half-bad for a beggar’s child.”

At the end of the day, Zuko comes out with enough gold coin to buy warm meals for a week.

It goes on like this, Zuko leaving early after running through cold katas and heading to the shop. Shandi hurls more work for her, and she finishes them. Then she gets paid and goes to buy food. After finishing a particularly complex commission, Zuko goes to the market and indulges on a small tea set for her Uncle.

He happily accepts it and brews tea that night.

At the end of the week, Shandi comes up to Zuko and tosses her a set of beautiful formal robes and a box of threads and needles. She looks quizzically at them. “What is this?” she asks.

Shandi rolls her eyes. “Consider it your bonus for doing such a good job. I’m very impressed with your work, girl.”

“When am I even going to use this?” she says pointing to the formal robes. Shandi shrugs.

“I don’t know. Whenever. Sell them if you wish. But every woman should have a nice set of formal clothes. You never know when you might want to impress someone.” Then she takes out a seal and hands it to her. “This is my seal. If you ever need to find work in a seamstress shop, just show them this.”

The seal is simple, a white lotus flower with Shandi’s name on the inside. Zuko takes it skeptically. Why would any shop hire her on the word of some backwater seamstress? Well, she doesn’t want to be rude. The princess takes the items and bows.

“Thank you, Shandi.” She says.

The woman waves her away, “Yes yes. Now get out. Don’t you have places to be?

Zuko rolls her eyes and heads out. She glances back one last time and waves good-bye to the old seamstress.

### End Notes

#### Chapter End Notes

**Lu Ten:** Seriously, Dad? You did this last time too.

Sorry I killed Yue. I had to think about it for a while, but in the end it fit the narrative better to have Yue die.

Thank you for reading! I hope you enjoyed it.

## Chapter 6

---

Iroh and Zuko end up having to stop at a small village to buy more feed for their ostrich-horse (that Zuko secretly named Lulu). Iroh goes inside to browse for whatever junk catches his eye while Zuko stays outside. She pays for two bags of feed and two hot meals when she spots some children chuckling behind a corner. They throw an egg and it lands on the Earth soldiers head with a solid plop. The angered soldier glares and comes up to them. He leers down at Zuko.

“If you just wanted my attention sweetheart, you could have just asked.” He says.

Zuko glares at the offending soldier and turns away. “It wasn’t me, and I’m not interested.”

The soldier glares and grabs her arm, pulling her towards him. “I’d watch my mouth, girl. You’re looking at a group of *fine* Earth Kingdom soldiers.”

“Oh really?” Zuko drawls. “I don’t see any. Just a bunch of insecure men trying to compensate for their small dicks.”

The main soldier growls and leans close enough that she can smell his rancid breath.

“Listen here you *bitch—*”

Zuko’s lost patience for this man and lands a solid kick to his knee. He immediately howls in pain and lets her go, clutching his leg. She draws her daos and presses them close to his neck. The other soldiers gather their wits and point their spears at her.

She growls menacingly, making a few men tremble. She’s running through the scenario in her mind, she could take them all. Maybe even without her bending. She can smell their fear and see the slight tremble in their hands.

These men haven’t seen battle or blood. How pathetic.

“Lin, what are you doing?” Iroh calls. Zuko looks up and sees her uncle rushing towards her with the bags of feed. There’s a look of deep exasperation in his eyes.

“Let the man go, Lin.” He says.

“He dishonored me.” Zuko replies.

“And you’ve retaliated it seems.” Iroh says. “Now, let go. It’s not worth it.”

Zuko looks hatefully at the trembling man, and scoffs. She draws her sword away. The other soldiers slowly lift their spears and back away as she walks to Iroh. The leader stands, rubbing his neck and glaring at her. He rips the bags of feed from Iroh’s hand.

“Consider this compensation for you daughter’s behavior.” He growls. Then he turns to Zuko. “I wouldn’t want to lay with a *disfigured* *bitch* anyway.”

Zuko would have gladly severed his head off, if Iroh didn’t come in-between her and the man.

“I would watch what you say.” Iroh says seriously. “My daughter is much too good for someone like you.” Then he puts his arm around Zuko’s shoulder and leads her back to Lulu. They’re about to pay for another bag of feed when the same boy who chucked the egg came up to them.

“Hey miss! Why don’t you come with me? My parent’s will feed your ostrich-horse.” He says taking the reins from Zuko.

The princess is about to retort that it’s the boy’s fault for getting them in this mess, but Iroh interrupts.

“Why thank you, young man. It would be a great help to us.” He turns to Zuko and smiles encouragingly. Zuko groans. Why does she even bother?

The boy, Lee, lives on a farm not too far away from the village. They mostly raise pig-cows and pickens, but there’s a generous field behind their house, filled with golden wheat. Lee’s father, Kai, goes up to them and introduces himself, apologizing for his son’s trouble.

“They’re just bullies, extorting us while the real men are off serving our nation.” He says. “But that doesn’t mean you should be antagonizing them.” He gives his son a long look. Lee has the decency to look sheepish and apologizes.

“Ah young children are always acting so rashly.” Iroh agrees glancing at his niece. Zuko turns away, feeling a little embarrassed.

“Oh but she was so cool Dad!” Lee says. “She beat up Gao like he was nothing and just stared down at the soldiers holding her at spearpoint!”

Zuko grimaces as she watches his parent’s eyes widen. From what she’s seen of the Earth Kingdom, women take a domestic role while men fight. It’s almost unheard of for a woman to be taking up arms. Lee’s father looks at her and stares at the blades at her hip. He clears his throat.

“Well, why don’t you stay the night? Have dinner. I owe you for helping my son out.” he offers.

Iroh smiles, “We’d be honored, and we will gladly help with any chores around the farm in return for your hospitality.”

Zuko nearly balks. Why did her uncle have to rope her into these ridiculous things?

Thankfully, Iroh’s helping Kai with the barn, but that leaves Zuko in the kitchen with Nami. She stares at the badly mangled potato she was supposed to peel. Nami takes pity on the girl and takes the potato away. “Why don’t you just chop the vegetables?” she says kindly.

Zuko takes the out and starts chopping the vegetables, while glaring mutinously at the potatoes.

Lee comes in soon after chattering away and asking invasive questions. She tries to ignore the little gnat buzzing around her.

“So how’d you get that scar?” he asks.

Zuko nearly slices her finger and curses when the knife nicks her skin. Nami turns to her son and scolds him.

“Lee stop bothering her, and don’t ask invasive questions. Go outside and feed the pig-cows.”

Lee grumbles but goes on out.

Nami turns to Zuko apologetically, “I’m sorry, we don’t get many visitors here, and he doesn’t know when he’s being too invasive.”

“It’s fine.” Zuko mutters. It’s not but she can at least pretend it’s okay for the kid’s mom.

At dinner, Iroh spends the nights telling stories of their journey, and entertaining the young boy with spirit tales. His parents smile indulgently and keep pushing food towards Zuko, who keeps having to awkwardly turn them down.

“Don’t be shy, dear. Take some more.” Nami says, pushing some vegetables at her. “You need to keep your strength.”

Zuko finally relents and picks at the food she hands him.

“So where are you going?” Lee asks.

“Lee, these travelers don’t have to tell you their life’s story.” Kai scolds.

Iroh waves him off. “Children are curious. We’re not sure where we’ll be going.” He lies.

“Well, I hope you stay safe on these roads.” Nami tuts. “It’s becoming more and more dangerous now.”

“We’ll travel carefully.” Iroh confirms. After dinner, they lead the pair to the barn and hand them some blankets. Zuko lies down on the hay and looks up at the dark ceiling.

“What are we doing, Uncle?” she asks.

Iroh turns to her. “Well, seems to me we should be getting ready to rest. We have a long journey ahead.”

“Why are we even bothering to go find refuge? Father will conquer this place soon, and then there’ll be no where else for us to go. We’ll be executed.” She shuts her eyes tightly. “What’s the point of trying anymore? It’s just…hopeless”

Suddenly, Iroh’s beside her gripping her hand. “Zuko, do not give in to despair. In our darkest moments, we must find hope within ourselves. That is the true meaning of inner strength.”

Zuko turns to see her Uncle looking at her with sad golden eyes. “Is that what you did when Lu Ten died?”

“Yes. That and I had a wonderful loving niece who helped me through it.” He affirms.

A small smiles forms on her lips. “I didn’t do much. I just served you tea. I had no idea what I was doing.”

“Sometimes the smallest gestures are enough. Now please try to get some rest.”

Iroh is soon snoring beside her, and Zuko closes her eyes hoping to get a few hours of rest. But not too long into her light doze, she hears the creak of the barn door open. She discretely peeks one eye open, and watches Lee take her swords.

She waits for a moment and then sighs, getting up and brushing the hay off her robes. There’s small child with sharp pointy blades somewhere. She better get to him before he pokes his eye out.

Lee is easy to find. She just has to follow the sound of inexperienced hacking. It’s almost unbearable for her to watch him stab her precious swords into a stump. Master Piandao would have a heart attack, and then smack the child over the head before putting them on sharpening duty.

“You’re holding it wrong.” She says dryly. The boy jumps and swings towards her. The blades cutting effortlessly into the sunflowers. It’s truly a miracle this dumb kid hasn’t accidently hacked off his limbs yet. She carefully walks up to him and holds her hands out. At least he has the decency to look apologetic, and enough sense to hand her the blades hilt first.

She grips the blades firmly and stands back. She lets it dance across her, slicing the air with swift motions. “Don’t think of these as two separate blades. They are one in the same, just split apart. You need to move like they are connected.”

She demonstrates a few katas before handing the blades back to the boy. Lee takes it carefully and copies her motions. He smiles when he cleanly cuts the sunflower and turns to her, waiting expectantly.

Zuko nods. “Better.” She says.

They continue for another hour before Zuko calls for a stop. “I’m tired. I need to sleep.” She lies.

Lee is disappointed but understanding. He hands her back the blades and they walk back to the farm.

“You’re really amazing.” He says. “I didn’t even know girls *could* fight.”

Zuko rolls her eyes. “Anyone can fight as long as they are taught and trained. It’s not segregated to a particular gender. If you have the will, you can fight.”

The boy nods, “I guess so, but everyone always says girls are supposed to like house-stuff like sewing or cooking and boys like swords and fighting and fixing things.”

This is the kind of thinking that pisses Zuko off to no end. She saw it happen to the water tribe siblings, and she sees it with him.

“I like embroidery.” Zuko says. “Does that make me less of a warrior?”

Lee’s eyes widen, but he shakes his head. “No, I just didn’t expect you to like girly things.” Zuko shrugs. “I like embroidery. I like fine clothes and nice shoes. I enjoy taking care of my hair and skin, and love going to the spa. But I also like swords, climbing rooftops, and sparing with tough opponents. None of these things makes me any less of a woman or any more of a man. It’s just…me. I’m a woman and I’m a warrior. But I won’t let the misconstructions of gender prevent me from doing the things I like.”

She turns to Lee and puts a hand on his shoulder. “And you shouldn’t judge people on what they like either.”

He nods slowly and grins at her. “You’re pretty awesome you know?”

Zuko blushes and turns away. “Whatever.” She fingers the dagger in her pocket, and after a moments deliberation she takes it out and hands it to him.

“Here. This has been with me since I was young. You should have it.” She offers.

Lee looks at her with bright eyes and unsheathes the dagger. “Never give up without a fight.” he reads in the moonlight. He looks up at Zuko with shining eyes. “Thank you. I’ll take good care of it.”

Zuko huffs and turns away, “Sure. Let’s get you back home before your parents worry.”

When she gets back to the barn, she lays back onto the hay and sighs contently. In her sleep, she dreams of her mother.

The next morning, Nami has packed them lunches for the road, and Iroh is thanking the family for their hospitality and generosity. Zuko does the same, but quieter. Just when they’re about to leave, the pesky Earth Soldiers from the other day ride up to him. Gao taunts the family when he tells them their elder son’s battalion has been captured. They’re about to move onto the family when Zuko and Iroh get in-between them.

She glares fiercely, “Not another step.” She warns. The men hesitate and then leave with a parting barb at the family.

Iroh turns to Kai and says, “I’m so sorry for your family’s misfortune.”

Kai sighs sadly, “It’s the price of war.” He says. “I’ll be going to see the local general and seeing what they are going to do for my son.”

“Good luck to you.” Iroh says.

They leave with a final farewell, heading back down the road. It’s not even noon when they end up stopping near a wide grassy field at Uncle’s insistence.

“Ah look at this Zuko.” He sighs contently. “What a beautiful view.”

Zuko just grunts and lays down on the plush grass. Her arms pillowing her head as she closes her eyes. It’s not even an hour later when she hears the clacking of wheels and stomp of hooves. She sits up and sees Nami riding towards them with a frantic look in her eye.

“Please you have to help us!” she cries. “Lee’s been taken by Gao and his men.”

Iroh goes to her and asks how it happened.

“They were talking badly about his brother and father, and Lee—Lee pulled a knife. I don’t even know where he got it from! Now they have him tied up in the town square. They say if he’s old enough to hold a blade, he’s old enough to fight” she stops and cries into her hands.

Guilt churns in Zuko’s gut. When she gave him the knife, she never thought something like this would happen. With determination, she walks up to the crying mother and says, “We’ll help. Lead the way.”

Iroh looks at her proudly from Nami’s side.

They arrive at the town at sunset. Lee is tied to the town’s water tower and looking listless. The men pause and look up to Zuko who’s striding towards them.

“Let him go.” She orders.

Gao laughs, “We don’t take orders from you, wench.”

“Then maybe you’ll listen once I knock some sense into your heads.” She says putting her hands on her blade. Gao glares and orders his men to attack her as he sits back at watches.

It’s almost laughably easy how she dispatches the men. She hardly has to unsheathe her sword, preferring to break the feeble spears with her legs or arms. The men look at her with shock and fear. They run away at the sheer display of strength and skill. One by one they leave until only Gao is left.

He snarls and pulls out his hammers. The hammers dig into the earth, sending a block of it flying towards Zuko. She slices with her swords and keeps destroying the rubble. But even with her skillset, she can’t dodge everyone. A stray boulder hits her stomach and she’s sent flying. She barely regains her stance when the earth bender sends a wall of earth towards her. Zuko braces herself, but is still sent flying. She hits her back painfully and growls. She feels her fire start to boil through, but she tamps it down.

Her uncle told her not to fire bend, and she’s not going to lose to a third-rate bully who can only send pitiful pebbles at her. The Earth Kingdom soldiers who kidnapped her uncle had more power in their pinky than this guy with his stupid dual hammers.

She bounces up and *charges*. The man panics and sends a barrage of rocks at her, but there’s no finesse, they’re easy to read and she slices through them all. With a final push, she jumps and twists her body, sending a powerful round-house kick to the man’s face and sending him flying to a building. He crashes through the wall and goes limp.

Zuko walks up to the body and takes out the dagger he stole from Lee. Uncle and Nami are already there, untying him when Zuko gets to him. She hands him the dagger.

“Here. Don’t lose it again.” She says.

Lee takes it gratefully and then flings himself against her. “Thank you.” He says. “You’re my hero.”

Zuko feels heat climb up her neck and face. “’M’not a hero.” She grumbles. “I just did it because it’s kinda my fault you got in that situation.”

Nami puts a hand on her shoulder. “Well, I still think you’re our hero.” She says kindly. “We’ll never forget this.”

The princess scrubs her hand over her short hair. “Sure. Do whatever you want.” She mutters.

Iroh laughs and pats her shoulder. “You’ll have to excuse her. She’s not used to taking a compliment or praise.”

Zuko glares at her uncle and hisses at him to be quiet, but Iroh just shrugs and says, “It’s true.”

After the townspeople gather up the unconscious men and tie them up, Zuko slaps Gao’s face to wake him. He comes to and reels back when he sees her glaring at him.

“We’ve informed the local army commander about your corruption. He’ll be here to arrest you and your men.” She says. “Be grateful I didn’t sever your pathetic heads off when I had the chance.” With those parting words she leaves them and goes to Nami and Lee.

“Take care.” She says.

The mother and son smile and bring her in for another hug. Zuko stiffens and stands awkwardly, unsure of what her hands are supposed to do. Fortunately, they release her and she goes to subtly hid behind her uncle.

This time, all the villagers are waving good-bye to them as they leave. Once the village is out of sight, Zuko turns to Iroh and ask, “If I fire bended, do you think they would have the same reactions?”

Iroh winces and sighs, “I would like to hope so, but most likely not.”

“…I see.”

&#95;&#42;&#95;&#42;&#95;

After weeks of not seeing a hide or hair of the Avatar, Zuko finds…a hair of the Avatar. More specifically, the Avatar’s bison. She grips the white fur tightly. Mixed emotions rushing through her.

*What are you waiting for? Chase him!*

What is the point though?

*For your father! For your destiny.*

What does that even mean anymore?

*When did it matter so little to you?*

Why did it matter to me in the first place? I was to be married off to the highest bidder. I would have been nothing more than a puppet Fire Lord.

*Why are you hesitating now?*

She growls and looks to the sky. She doesn’t know. She just doesn’t know why she’s hesitating. All her life, she just wanted to serve her people and make her father proud. Whether it’s on the throne or just as an advisor. When she was younger, she’d promise to always have Lu Ten’s back when he became Fire Lord. Now, even after everything she’s done, she has nothing. Her father sees her as a traitor and sent her sister to arrest her. Why is she even trying anymore?

She looks back down to the fur.

But maybe this is her chance. Maybe if she captures the Avatar, he’ll let her return and welcome her with open arms. The Spirits lead the Avatar to her for a reason, they must be giving her a path back home. She starts packing the camp and loading up Lulu. Iroh looks quizzically at her.

“Why are we packing, niece?” he asks.

Zuko shows him the piece of bison fur. “The Avatar is near. I can capture him and then take him to Father. Then he’ll have to take us back home.”

Iroh looks pained. “No Zuko, I don’t think capturing the Avatar will solve our problems.”

“Well what do you want me to do?” she yells. “I can’t just leave him and let him slip past us! He’s the one chance I have of returning home, of being happy. Of fulfilling my destiny!”

Her uncle shakes his head fiercely, “No, Zuko you *think* you need to return home to be happy, but when have you ever been truly happy in that place?”

Zuko clenches her fists but doesn’t answer.

“Zuko, you’re chasing ghosts and phantoms of memories that are no longer there. You need to stop, and start looking inward. You must decide for yourself what you want with your life and see what kind of person you want to become.” Iroh says strongly.

“I want my destiny!”

“Your destiny? Or a destiny someone forced onto you?” Iroh asks seriously. “How are you even going to deliver him to Ozai? We are weeks away from the nearest Fire Nation port. How do you expect to hold the Avatar at by while we travel?”

Zuko turns away, “Does it matter?”

Iroh throws his arms up, “*Yes!* This is your problem Zuko. You never think of what happens after! If Jee hadn’t discouraged you, you would have taken the Avatar and frozen to death in the North!”

She growls and pushes her uncle away. She doesn’t need to hear this. She doesn’t need to hear her uncle throw all her failures and insecurities back in her face.

“Please, stop Zuko.” Iroh begs, but Zuko’s already getting onto Lulu.

“I’m going to get the Avatar.” She says. “I’ll be back to get you once I’m done.”

With that she kicks the ostrich-horse and is galloping off, following the trail of white fur.

Azula is there. Of course, she’s there. She would be an idiot to not be able to follow such a simple trail. Her younger sister sneers at her.

“Aw Zuzu. How nice to see you again.” Azula taunts. “Sorry to say, but I’ll be the one capturing the Avatar this time. You’ve never had much luck capturing men anyway.”

“Low blow, Azula.” Zuko grits out. “Not like you’re a queen bee either. How’s Ty Lee? Still stealing all the attention from you?”

For once, Azula’s the one scowling and Zuko’s the one smiling smugly. It doesn’t last long because Azula shoots blue flames towards her. Soon the three are caught in a battle royal. Zuko makes a misstep and ends up falling off the second story into the hard ground. She grumbles and pulls herself up slowly. Outside, she can hear the water tribe siblings and another voice join. She runs out of the building and is shocked to see her uncle also joining the Avatar.

“Uncle.” Azula says dryly backing up as the circle tightens around her. “I should have known you would join the Avatar. It’s only logical after you betrayed the Fire Nation in the North.”

“I have betrayed no one, Azula.” Iroh replies. “I have always had the Fire Nation’s best interest at heart.”

Zuko joins at her uncle’s side and glares at her sister. “Give it up Azula, you’re surrounded.” She says.

Azula’s eyes narrow and they stand tense in this stand-off. Her eyes flicker wildly between them and then they zero in on Uncle. Zuko’s not fast enough to react. None of them are.

Azula sends a blast into Iroh, sending him flying to the floor.

Horror floods Zuko as she screams and goes to her Uncle’s side. Somewhere in the chaos Azula escapes, but it doesn’t matter in the face of her Uncle laying pale on the ground. Zuko falls to the ground beside him and hovers her hands over his prone body.

“Zuko!” Katara shouts. “I can help.” She says.

Zuko wants to shout at them. She’s tempted to send out a burst of fire to them, but…

She looks pleadingly at Katara and Sokka. She knows them, mostly as adversaries, but she also once knew them as allies. She knows Sokka and Katara are honorable warriors. And Uncle looks like he’s in so much pain.

With great reluctance, she stands aside. “Please.” She whispers.

Katara nods and Sokka puts a hand on her shoulder. “He’ll be okay.” he says. “Katara’s really good at healing.”

Zuko nods numbly and watches the water in Katara’s hand glow blue. She watches the water bender heal her Uncle and stays by his side the entire time. Soon, the angry burnt flesh goes pink and only a small burn remains.

Katara leans back, breathing heavily. “That’s all I can do for now.”

Zuko nods. “Thank you.”

“You’re welcome.” Katara hesitates and looks to her brother.

Sokka just shrugs and asks, “Hey wanna come with us? We can take you and your Uncle to a safe place.”

Zuko scowls and glares at Sokka. “I accepted Katara’s help because I know her. We were allies, but I draw riding your bison. I’m not planning on traveling with you. Ever.”

Sokka sighs, “Alright alright. No need to chomp my head off. But look it’s getting late, you should take shelter soon.”

“I’ll move my Uncle when he wakes up.” she says stubbornly. The water tribe boy sighs again and goes to the group. They whisper furtively amongst each other before Sokka finally nods and goes back to Zuko.

“Okay, I’ll stay with you until your Uncle’s awake and ready to move.” He says.

The princess looks at him incredulously. “What are you talking about? Go with the Avatar! Aren’t you his ‘plan guy’?” she quotes.

Sokka preens, “Why yes I am, among many *many* other titles, but you can’t stay here alone with your uncle like that. It’s not in the warriors code to leave an ally in a vulnerable position like this.”

Zuko glares. “I can take care of him, myself.”

Sokka puts his hands up. “And I have no doubt you’d most likely be able to take down anyone dumb enough to attack you, but on the off-chance that someone does attack you, you’ll have me to get your Uncle to safety while you fight off the baddies.”

The princess purses her lips. That…made a lot of sense. Reluctantly, she agrees and lets the boy stay.

“Excellent.” He says. Then he turns to the others, “I’ll blow the whistle at the rendezvous point when I’m ready.” The rest of the gang nod and hop onto Appa. Katara hands him a sleeping bag and gives one last hug before they fly off, disappearing into the distance.

Sokka turns back to Zuko. “So…not mad that you couldn’t capture Aang?”

“…Uncle’s more important.”

Sokka nods, “Yeah I figured. C’mon let’s get him inside one of the houses. He should stay sheltered, and I need sleep.”

“Yeah, your bags are bigger than a racoon-dog’s.” Zuko mocks.

“Hey, I was chased by your crazy sister for a day straight! Cut me some slack.”

“Hm sounds like her.”

They make their way into one of the nearby homes, and Zuko lays down some blankets for her Uncle to lay on. They move him towards the back, and Zuko checks his breathing again before making a fire and settling near it. Sokka is yawning widely and lying in his sleeping bag.

“I’ll keep watch.” Zuko says.

“Hmm you gunna wake me when it’s my turn?” he mumbles.

“No.”

“Figures. That’s fine I’ll wake myself up and make you sleep.”

Zuko scoffs. “Do your best then.”

Sokka’ soon snoring loudly, and she has a chance to look closer at the water tribe boy.

He hasn’t changed a bit since the last time she saw him. Maybe his muscles were a little more developed now, but he seems exactly as he was in the North.

She wonders if he thinks she’s changed a lot. Some days when she looks at herself in passing mirrors or streams, it feels like she doesn’t recognize the person staring back at her. Those days where all she worried about was capturing the Avatar seem so far away now. Zuko pulls up her legs, knees to chest and hugs them tightly against her. She rests her chin on her knees and watches the door, eyes gleaming in the firelight.

Sokka wakes to groaning and Zuko’s hushed gentle voice. He turns discretely and watches her help the man up and passing a waterskin to him. In the soft morning light, she looks…calmer. Less angry. Without the bald head and ponytail, she looks more approachable. The rays seems to bounce off her smooth ivory skin, giving her an ethereal glow. He wonders if all fire benders glow with the sun like her, but probably not. Azula certainly didn’t, even her Uncle doesn’t.

After running from her for so long, it’s strange to be here with her. Watching her care for her uncle like a normal person. She always loomed larger than life back in the good old days, but now…

“I see you’ve had company, Princess Zuko.” The old man says, turning to Sokka. The water tribe warrior smiles sheepishly after getting caught staring. He goes over to Iroh and gives him his hand.

“Sokka of the Southern Water Tribe. Son of Chief Hakoda.” He introduces.

Iroh’s eyes gleam with amusement and he grasps Sokka’s forearm in a strong warrior’s hold. “Iroh, Uncle of Princess Zuko and tea connoisseur.”

Sokka grins. “I always knew you were a chill dude.”

The princess glares at him and smacks his arm. “He’s the Dragon of the West not some ‘chill dude’.” She says scandalously. However, Iroh laughs and placates his niece.

“Ah those days are far behind me, niece. I like being known as a ‘chill dude’, much better title I’d say.” He replies. Zuko just scrunches her nose and huffs.

It’s actually quite cute, in Sokka’s opinion. She’s like a surly cat-owl.

“I’d like to thank you though, young Sokka for keeping my niece company.” Iroh says bowing slightly.

Sokka waves him off, while Zuko just shouts, “He was asleep the *whole time.*”

“Hey, I helped you carry him here.” Sokka cries.

“Well I kept watch the whole night.” Zuko grumbles.

“Only because you’re too stubborn to wake me up and ask for help.”

“Not like I’d need help from a *peasant*.”

Sokka throws his arms up. “There you go again, insulting peasants. What did they ever do to you? You know peasants make up like 90 percent of the population right?”

Zuko blushes and turns away, “Nothing. You’re just not as good as me.” she mutters.

“Oh yeah? Wanna bet? I think you’ll be surprised.” Sokka challenges.

The princess can’t help herself. She knows she’ll regret it later, but right now the water boy threw a challenge at her. “Yeah. Let’s, and don’t come crying when I beat you.” she says.

Sokka smirks, “Okay *princess*.” He drawls in a way that makes shivers go down her spine. She’s instantly reminded of the wet dreams she had of him and pushes them violently away to a tiny box in her mind that’s then chained and throw into the deep pits of her brain.

“We’ll both have an hour to scrounge up some breakfast. Best one wins.” He says. “Iroh can be the judge.”

“You’re not scared Uncle’ll be biased towards me?” she says lifting her brow pompously. Sokka only grins widely.

“Nah, he seems like the guy who’ll keep your ego in check.”

He’s not wrong, but damn does that make her want to wring his neck. Iroh chuckles in the background and claps. “I think this is a wonderful idea.”

*Of course he does*. Uncle thinks anything that’ll teach Zuko a lesson or help her ‘make friends’ is a good lark. It’s too late to back down now. Not unless she wants the stupid boy to hold this over her head.

She tilts her head high and says, “Bring it on then.”

Forty-five minutes into it, and she’s only gotten a few nuts and berries. She hasn’t even caught any meat or found any kind of proteins.

Ugh this is a disaster! What kind of person would like lychee nuts and hazelberries for breakfast? She’s also pretty sure a few pebbles made it’s way into her bag while she frantically picked the nuts and berries. She tried to get some eggs, but when she came across the bird nest with those pale blue eggs winking back at her…

She couldn’t do it.

She’s going to lose because she’s such a soft weakling. Well, what else was new? Father’s probably right to lock her up for the countless embarrassment she gave him. The errant princess sighs and leans against a tree, slowly hitting her head against the bark. Suddenly, a squeak catches her attention and she looks up to see a sugar-glider staring at her with it’s big round eyes.

*Ugh why are you so cute?* She thinks despondently. Is it wrong that she wants to pet it? She never had pets at the palace. Mostly because Azula would immolate them, and Zuko would rather be petless than have a poor creature be the target of her sister’s sinister intentions.

But Azula isn’t here now…

Impulsively, she reaches into the bag of nuts and berries and holds some out. The creature is hesitant at first, but it slowly inches closer. The first time, it reaches into her hand and quickly snatches a nut away. It nibbles on it with its tiny paws and the princess can’t help but coo at the adorable sight. Zuko reaches into her bag again and holds out a berry. This time, the sugar-glider is less hesitant, it slowly makes its way to her hand and peers into her with its big soulful eyes.

Whatever it sees seems to assure it, and the small creature twines it’s tail on her wrist, motioning for her to put her hand down. She cups her hand and the sugar-glider hops on. Zuko marvels at the soft fur and she pets it gently. Honestly, even her impending loss can’t make her smile any less because now she has this impossibly tiny and trusting creature curled in the palm of her hand. “I’ve only just met you, but I will literally die for you.” She says seriously. The sugar-glider merely blinks at her and continues to eat.

“I’ll name you Fluffy.” She declares. ‘Fluffy’ could really care less as long as it gets more nuts and gets to stay in this strange mammal’s warm hands.

Zuko is absentmindedly petting the creature’s head when she starts speaking lowly to it. “You know, I didn’t really want to do this stupid challenge.” She admits. “I was just too prideful to turn him down. I don’t really care that he’s a peasant. I never would have slept with Tobio if peasants actually bothered me.” she blushes slightly at the memory. “…Sometimes I wonder if my life would have been easier if I was a peasant. I wouldn’t have all these…obligations and expectations on me.”

The creature squeaks, and Zuko pretends that it’s agreeing with her.

“But…mother always said the Spirits put you in your path for a reason. When I was younger, I used to believe it was because the Spirits wanted me to help my cousin lead our people…now though…” she blinks her tears away. “Now…I don’t know why the Spirits put me here. I’m just so…*angry*. I think I chased after the Avatar because—because it was just something I always did. Fat good that did me though. I got Uncle injured.”

She sighs and bows her head. “And now I’m talking to an animal in the middle of the forest trying to win a *stupid* challenge.”

Suddenly she feels a tiny nose rub against her cheek. She looks up and sees he sugar-glider peering into her and putting its paws on her cheek. Zuko smiles and rubs its little head with her finger. Content and at peace.

Not that it lasted long, because *Sokka* comes barreling through the underbrush.

“Hey, that’s not really going to make more than a mouthful.” He teases.

Zuko looks scandalized and hisses fire at him, while pulling the sugar-glider close to her breast. Sokka looks strangely tense as he watches the motion.

“Fluffy is not food!” she says.

Sokka snorts, “Fluffy? That’s cute.”

Zuko blushes at the slip-up and growls, “Whatever. It looks like you’ve won. There’s no point in making Uncle judge.” She says eyeing the rabbits he caught.

The water tribe boy shrugs and says, “We can tell him we worked together.”

“Are you *pitying* me?”

Sokka rolls his eyes, “No, I’m letting you bow out gracefully.” He replies. “Besides, I kinda figured you weren’t really into it as much as you insisted you were.”

Zuko turns away, pressing the sugar-glider closer to her, as if to comfort herself. It squeaks nuzzles into her.

“At least you got a friend out of it.” Sokka laughs.

Her lips twitch slightly, and she looks down at the little creature. “I guess…but it should go back home.”

She stands and carefully brings her hand up to a branch. It clambers on to the branch and wiggles its nose at her. Then it flicks its tail, almost as if asking her to follow it. Curious, Zuko and Sokka follow the creature as it glides from tree to tree. It finally stops in a small clearing and sits next to a familiar flower.

Zuko’s eyes widen and crouches down to inspect it. It’s…almost exactly like the White Jade Bush that gave Uncle rashes, the only difference is the flower has a pink center and the leaves were pointed. Could it actually be…?

Zuko carefully plucks a leaf and sniffs it. A grin overtakes her face. She gathers a few leaves and turns to Sokka smirking.

“I think I might just win this.” she declares.

After a delightful breakfast of nuts, roast rabbit and the White Dragon tea, Iroh sighs contently and pats his belly. “Ah that was exquisite you two. A job well done.”

The two teens preen and then look at him expectantly.

Iroh strokes his beard and nods. “Yes, I can say without a doubt that this is clearly…a tie.”

“*What*?!” Zuko shouts.

“Aw c’mon, Uncle!”

“He’s not your uncle water boy!”

“Psh, he’s everyone’s uncle. Learn to share fire princess.”

“No! He’s mine. Get your own.”

“You only barely got a tie because your freaky princess animal powers got you the tea leaves! If that thing hadn’t led you to it, you would be the obvious loser.”

Zuko glares, “What are you *talking* about? I don’t have ‘princess animal powers’ and even if I did they wouldn’t be *freaky.* You’re just jealous.”

Iroh just sits and sips his tea contently as he watches the two bicker. Ah youth.

Sokka finally slumps and crosses his arms. “Fine whatever. I still proved that peasants are just as good as you snobby nobles.”

Zuko purses her lips. “Yeah okay.” she mumbles.

“I’m sorry what was that?” Sokka crows, leaning in and cupping his ear.

The princess seemed to have enough of the boy’s teasing and grabs his ears and *twists*. Sokka’s shrieking bloody murder and rubs his ears petulantly. On the other hand, Zuko is smirking evilly.

“Did that fix your hearing for you?” she asks.

Sokka glares at her. “You are unnecessarily violent you know that?”

“It’s the only way to get through your thick skull.”

“Lies. You just don’t know how to act like a normal person.”

“I’ll have you know, I’ve been trained and drilled in all manners of etiquette and manners!”

“Riiiight. Let me guess, you have to know which spoons to use? Or how to passive-aggressively tell someone they’re being annoying with a fan gesture?”

Zuko’s red flush and Iroh’s guffaw are telling.

“…so what if I did?!”

“You *know* that’s not what normal people do right?” Sokka asks.

“…”

“…”

“…yes.”

The water tribe boy laughs. “You’re *lying*.”

Zuko turns a bright red and seems to puff up in embarrassment. “I’VE INTERACTED WITH COMMONERS BEFORE!”

This just makes Sokka laugh more. “Okay, first of all: saying commoners just cements the fact that you were a cloistered princess. Second: what exactly did those interactions entail?”

“…well I saved them from bad people. And I…kissed a few boys.” She doesn’t mention that a ‘few’ meant *two*. But she figures since she went all the way with Tobio, he should count at least double.

Sokka shakes his head fondly. “Yeah those don’t really count.”

“Why not?!”

“Uh because you were rescuing people, which is pretty awesome by the way, but they’re not going to act like a normal person. As for the kissing part, guys are definitely not going to act normal when they’re looking at a beautiful girl they want to flex for. *Trust* *me*.”

&#46;

&#46;

&#46;

…*Sokka thinks I’m a beautiful girl?*

Zuko stares at him with wide eyes, unable to form a coherent sentence. Fortunately, Iroh (the amazing uncle) notices and cuts in.

“Oh yes. It’s very true. I remember when I was trying to catch the eye of my dear late wife.” He reminisces. “I dove in the deepest, shark infested waters just to get her the rare black pearls to adorn her hair. Once I even went to ride on the fiercest Komodo-rhino at the stables.”

Sokka laughs and turns to her uncle. “You live the life of a party, Uncle.”

Zuko’s still too red and embarrassed to correct him. But at least now, Sokka and Uncle are getting into a conversation about pai sho strategies. It’s nice. Domestic even. She lets the voices wash over her as she leans against the wall, tea warming her hands and anchoring herself to something solid. The deep slow cadence of her uncle’s voice combined with Sokka’s slightly higher and quicker tone end up lolling her to a comfortable sleep.

Iroh looks fondly at his niece and carefully takes the tipping cup from her hands, setting it aside.

“I’m glad she feels comfortable enough to let herself rest.” He says looking gratefully at Sokka. The water tribesman shrugs.

“She looked like she needed it.” He says. “I was hoping all the strenuous hunting would knock her out sooner. Not that she really did any hunting. All the little animals have to do is give her big googly eyes and she’ll let them go.”

Iroh laughs, “My dear niece has a soft spot for little creatures. Back in the palace she would take care of the turtleducks that lived in the garden pond. Once, she tried to smuggle a hoard of duckling into her room. They tracked mud all over the imported rugs.”

Sokka grins widely. Somehow the thought of a tiny Zuko caring for the little turtleducks seemed just right. “She was probably a troublemaker when she was young huh?”

“Oh yes, her and my son, Lu Ten, would get in trouble whenever they got together.” Iroh answers fondly. “My Lu Ten would always take her on ‘adventures’ and they’d come back with muddy torn clothes without fail.”

“I never knew you had a son.” Sokka says.

Iroh sighs, “Hmm. He died tragically young. It’s one of my greatest failures.”

Sokka swallows tightly and puts a hand on the man’s shoulder, “I’m sorry.” he says.

The old general smiles back and pats the comforting hand. “You’re a good man, Sokka. I’m glad my niece befriended someone like you.”

The teen laughs, “I don’t know about befriend, but…” he looks to her. “She’s…a lot different than before.”

Iroh nods. “She has been through much in her young life, and she’s at a point in her life where she is starting to question everything.”

“…Zhao said she was banished. I always thought she must have done something *terrible* to get her banished, but…seeing her now. I don’t think she’s even capable of doing anything that awful.”

At this, Iroh sighs and closes his eyes. “Her banishment is my greatest failure and shame.”

Sokka pauses and looks at Iroh closely. The man seems to have aged ten years. His shoulders slump and he looks…defeated and so so sad.

Iroh takes a moment to sip his tea and look seriously into Sokka’s eyes. “I will tell you the story if you are interested, but I will only tell you of the events. I can’t say what my niece thought or what she believes in.”

Sokka nods eagerly. It’s always been a question that swirls in his mind. At this, Iroh takes a breath and starts with the fateful day when she was thirteen and asked to join the war room …

“…And so for her evident weakness and uselessness, the Fire Lord banished her. She would not be allowed home until she captured the Avatar.” Iroh finishes.

The fiery rage in Sokka’s gut has iced over into a cold fury by this point. He turns to her, eyes tracing over the scar on her face and he *sees* it. The palm, the slight indentation of his fingers that curve all the way to her ear.

*How did I not see it before?*

He wants to hold her close. He wants to tell her what a strong amazing woman she is. He wants to. Tear. Ozai. Apart.

His hands shake at the thought of her father pressing a flaming hand on her young face.

*She was thirteen. Not much older than Aang. She was sent out into the world at thirteen and* *banished under threat of death.*

He looks at Iroh, “When I meet Ozai, I’m going to smash his face in.” he says.

For his part, Iroh seems a little happy at the declaration. “I would expect nothing else. You’re a protector at heart.”

“You never wanted her to capture Aang, right?”

Iroh smiles, “What makes you say that?”

Sokka scoffs, “Because if you *really* wanted Zuko to capture Aang, we’d never have gotten past you at the South Pole, and Aang would be in chains in Ozai’s dungeon.”

“Haha you give me too much credit.”

“*Please*, you would have melted our village, knocked out the Avatar and put him in chains before he could even wake up. You’re not soft like Zuko; you would have been merciless.”

Iroh hums and nods. “I suppose you’re right.” He answers. “You’re quite perceptive.”

Sokka shrugs, “I’m the Avatar’s plan guy. I’m supposed to be perceptive. Where will you be going after this?”

“Ah well…I’m actually thinking of brining her to Ba Sing Se. It’s a well protected city, and she can recuperate and heal there in relative safety.” He answers. “I’m sure she’ll be happy to see you and your sister if you ever go there for a visit.”

Sokka smiles, “I’ll be sure to look you up if we ever head over there.”

The two pass the morning talking about strategy, history, tea and everything in-between. Sokka finds the man a true treasure trove of wisdom and experience. It’s no wonder people still revere him as the Dragon of the West. Even his enemies know what a powerful force he is.

Zuko stirs sometime in the afternoon. Sokka and Iroh are just about to grill the fish when they hear her snuffle and yawn cutely. She blinks at them, bleary golden eyes slowly acclimating to the light.

“U’cle wuzzat?” she mumbles rubbing her eyes. “Whyz da light weird?”

Sokka stores the image of Zuko, eyes half-lidded and still filmed with sleep, looking so soft and vulnerable. He feels a little bad that he’s getting a little peak at her without her usual guardedness, but she just looks too cute for him to turn away.

Zuko finally seems to remember that he’s here and suddenly she tightens her emotional reins and glares at him.

“Sokka what are you still doing here? How long was I asleep?”

“Uhh helping you out remember?” he quips. “And you were pretty much dead for most of the day.

She groans and scrubs her hand over her short hair. “Alright well…I guess it might be too late for us to get going to the next campsite.”

“I’m always happy to have Sokka here for another night. He’s a delightful young man.” Iroh adds.

“Aw Uncle, you’re the best too.” Sokka returns.

At this point Zuko’s convinced her uncle’s just adopted another stray. Whatever, it could be worse. She sighs and goes to sit with them as they dine under the stars.

The next morning, Zuko offers Sokka a ride to the rendezvous point.

“I won’t try to capture the Avatar or anything.” She says. “I owe you one anyway.”

Sokka just laughs, “Ah don’t worry, I wasn’t even worried about *that*.”

Somehow, it’s less insulting and more…flattering. She tries not to think too hard on that. As soon as they reach the clearing, Sokka blows on a bison-shaped whistle that seemingly makes not noise. Zuko shuffles awkwardly until Iroh nudges her encouragingly.

“Um…” she starts. “Thanks. For…staying with us.”

Sokka grins, “Anytime.”

“You’re…pretty good. For a water peasant.”

“You’re not bad either for a fire princess.” Sokka returns. They both smile at each other, eyes locking as they slowly gravitate closer to the other—

“Sokka!” a voice from the sky calls out.

They both jump away and look up, seeing the bison and the Avatar waving at the two. Zuko clears her throat and says, “Well, I—uh better go…there. Um. Good luck.” She holds out a hand, but when Sokka grabs hold, he *pulls* her into him and gives her a tight hug.

She’s completely red by the time he pulls away.

“You too.” Sokka grins. “And listen to your uncle more. He’s a pretty smart guy.”

Zuko scoffs. “I’ll try.”

With that Zuko, climbs onto the ostrich-horse and trots away with Iroh waving enthusiastically from behind.

### End Notes

#### Chapter End Notes

**Lu Ten:** This can't be happening. Dad, how could you betray me?

These two kids are slowly getting along ^^

Thank you for reading!

## Chapter 7

### Notes

#### Chapter Notes

**Warning:** Explicit Sexual Content.

### Text

---

Iroh and Zuko end up stopping in an abandoned cabin on the mountainside. There, Iroh teaches her about lightening, cold fire. Unfortunately, like everything in her life, it blows up in her face. Literally.

Every time she tries to separate the energies they somehow…mix again, and the results are *explosive.* Iroh tries to distract her by teaching her a new technique: lightening redirection.

It feels better. Like it was something natural for her.

Zuko could feel her fire and energies flow though her like a stream of water. When she feels like she's gotten a handle of the technique, she asks her Uncle to help her test it out. By shooting lightening at her. For his part, Iroh looks equal parts horrified and deeply exasperated.

“No.” he says plainly. “I’m not shooting *lightening* at you.”

“Why not?” Zuko asks. “It’s the only way to see if I got it.”

“No! It’s too dangerous, and I wouldn’t be able to create lightening if I knew it was going towards you.”

Of all the times Uncle has to be *soft*. Zuko huffs and yells, “Fine if you won’t do it. I’ll find another way.”

By the will of the spirits, there's a storm front coming towards them. So she takes Lulu and leaves to meet the storm, ignoring the sad look from her Uncle.

When the storm finally reaches her, it feels like the heavens themselves have split and pour itself onto the earth. Zuko shouts and cries until she’s hoarse, begging for them to strike her, just so she could prove to the world that she could fight back. The rain and wind batter her face as she screams at the sky.

*“You’ve never held back before!”*

But nothing reaches her. Not a single bolt.

*“Strike me! I can take it! And now I can give it back!!”*

Even when the world is *not* screwing her over, it’s still screwing her over.

After the storm passes, she ends up trudging back to the cabin. Soaked. Eyes red and voice trembling. Iroh doesn’t say a word, and only puts on a pot of tea.

&#95;&#42;&#95;&#42;&#95;

The Misty Palms Oasis is less of an oasis and more of a glorified cesspool of outcast and wanderers. She glares at the various patrons glancing at them. At the bar one has the gall to sidle up to her and smirk.

“Hey pretty girl. What’s a sweet thing like you doing in a place like this?” he asks in an oily slick voice.

“None of your business.” She bites back and shoulders her way around him. The man still whistles wolfishly as he leers at her backside. It takes Uncle’s hard hold on her wrist and shake of his head to keep her from gouging the man’s eyes out.

“Come niece, he’s not worth your anger.” Iroh says.

“They’re insulting me! Looking at me like a prized hippo-cow.” She hisses. But Iroh stands firm.

“Zuko, there are times when you fight, and times when you let it go. These sad people are not worth your words or your anger.”

But Zuko feels herself shaking. How could her Uncle say that? Didn’t he understand that if she doesn’t *beat it out of them* they’ll do it again? They’ll see her as lesser? How is her pride, her honor, *not* worth fighting for? She can’t stand the thought of these ugly disgusting men leering at her, cat-calling her. Didn’t they have anything better to do? Why do these idiots feel the need to do it?

But her Uncle keeps a tight hold on her and leads her to a corner of the room, where a man sat next to pai sho table. She wants to scream so badly.

“This is no time for pai sho.” She hisses.

Her uncle just sighs and shakes his head, smiling at the man across from him. “Forgive my niece, she’s not the most patient or understanding of these…hidden arts.”

That was true, but really uncalled for. So, she pulls up a chair and sits down aggressively. She crosses her arms and glares at the floor, listening to the old men banter and the tiles clacking.

Joltingly, her uncle shakes her shoulders and smiles, “Come niece this man is going to help us cross the dessert.”

Zuko looks skeptically at her uncle and stares at the seemingly frail old man. With a nod, the pai sho player suddenly shouts, “I know you! You’re both fugitives of the Fire Nation, you’ve got a generous bounty on your heads.”

In an instant all eyes were on them. Zuko curses loudly and is about to unleash all her fury and frustration at these people, when her uncle grips her arm.

“No niece, he’s helping us.”

“How? He just outed us!” she growls. This is the last time she lets her uncle play pai sho with weirdos in bars.

But without warning, the people start fighting amongst each other. Glass and tables shattering all around them as the earth rumbles.

“This way.” Unhelpful pai sho player says.

She and Uncle quietly sneak out through a backdoor amidst the chaos. They end up in a flower shop of all places, and Zuko is rudely turned away at the door. She snarls, hissing flame from her mouth, but Iroh puts a placating hand on her shoulder. “I’m sorry Zuko but ah…it’s members only.” He says apologetically. “I’ll be back soon.”

She doesn’t understand any of this and it just pisses her off. Why in Agni’s name is her uncle being so…secretive. He’s never kept secrets with her…right? With a huff, she crosses her arms and sits down next to a particularly nice orchid. At least the view wasn’t terrible.

They had to leave Lulu with the people there. It’s a sad good-bye and Zuko caresses the animal forlornly.

“Thank you for everything.” She says resting her cheek against Lulu’s beak. The ostrich-horse returns the sentiment and coos.

They end up being smuggled in pots, and then hitching a ride on a caravan through the Si Wong dessert. The sand is aggravating.

“I hate sand.” She grumbles. “It’s course and irritating and it gets everywhere.”

Iroh laughs, “Don’t be such an angsty edge-lord.” He teases.

Zuko swivels her head quickly, “Did Sokka teach you that?” she accuses.

“What? You think I didn’t come up with that myself?”

“No. You’ve never said edge-lord in your *life*.”

“I could learn.” He insists.

“You’re not cool enough to.” She quips back. Iroh draws away putting a hand over his heart.

“How could my own niece be so cruel?” He says feigning hurt.

Zuko rolls her eyes, “You already have one niece that’s trying to kill you.”

“Very well. How can my own *daughter* say something so cruel?” he corrects with a smile.

The princess feels her face heat up and blush furiously. “Shut up.” she mutters but doesn’t try to correct him.

The strange pai sho people were able to create false passports for them to get ferry tickets. Uncle flirted uncomfortably with the ticket-lady and Zuko tries to keep the blatant disgust off her face. At dinner, the passengers are given a meager helping of rotted stew. She sniffs disdainfully at it while her uncle shows off the gaudy hat he bought.

Every day seems like a new struggle. She’s lost a significant amount of weight since becoming a refuge, and her hair and skin badly ache for moisturizer and shampoo. She misses having a roof under her head. She misses having servants bringing her fresh fruits and cakes. She misses the soft cotton and silk clothes that don’t itch against her skin. Agni, she even misses those horrible music nights on the ship.

“I sick and tired of living like this.” she says hatefully.

“Aren’t we all?” a voice from behind says.

She turns around to glare at the eavesdropper and ends up close to a tan Earth Nation boy with wild hair and a stupid stalk of wheat in his mouth.

*Ugh gross*. She thinks.

“You look like you could fight.” Wheat boy says. “How about you help me liberate some food from the main cabin? I heard the captain eats like a king and gives us his scraps.”

Iroh swallows thickly and asks, “What kind of king?”

“The fat happy kind.”

Her uncle drools at the thought, and Zuko sighs. The things she does for family. To be fair, she also wouldn’t mind something that hasn’t obviously been in a trash pile. So, she chucks the bowl off the ferry and says, “I’m in.”

Apparently, wheat boy’s name is Jet, and he’s with Smellerbee and Longshot. Strange names, but whatever. All three seem to be pretty adept at sneaking around, and Zuko wonders if it’s because they were touched by the war. They have that hard look in their eyes that you only get after seeing war. It makes her stomach twist painfully at the thought. How many more child soldiers did the Fire Nation create in their conquest for domination?

She pushes it to the back of her mind. Now’s not the time to be thinking of it.

True to Jet’s word, the captain’s cabin is filled to the brim with food. She swings her daos gracefully and packs the food tightly before putting it in the bag. When she turns, Jet is looking at her appraisingly, and her stomach flutters unintentionally.

*Not now.* She thinks to herself.

It doesn’t help that Jet looks damn good with his hook swords.

They pass the food to the passengers, and Uncle happily eats the roast turtleduck she specifically packed for him. He looks to Zuko gratefully and smiles.

“Thank you, niece.” He says. “You did a good thing.”

Zuko blushes and looks away, “Your stomach would have kept me up at night if I didn’t.”

“Haha that’s true.”

While Iroh makes conversation with Smellerbee and Longshot, Zuko takes this opportunity to slip away towards the secluded corner of the ferry. She leans against the rails and looks up at the moon. She wonders if Yue is happy up there. She grimaces at the thought.

She never told Uncle, but…she regrets leaving Yue so abruptly. It’s just that Zhao was right there, and he just doomed her people…

Still, she wishes she was there for Yue’s last moments. The princess was kind. Too kind for her. But she understood duty to her people and country.

Perhaps in another life, they could have been good friends.

Zuko’s golden eyes look somberly up at the silvery moon. “Did you ever regret it?” she asks. “Would you have been happier being…normal?”

There’s no answer of course, and she just sighs and rests her chin against the railing.

“You were pretty good there.” Jet interrupts, sliding next to her.

Zuko gives him a half-hearted glare. “I guess.” She mutters. “It wasn’t a hard job.”

“Hmm true, but I liked the way you handled your swords.” He says. “Very elegant, but wild. Kinda like you. It’s…*hot*.”

“If this is your version of flirting, you better try something better.” Zuko gripes.

Jet just laughs, “You’re right. Normally I’d sweep a pretty girl like you off her feet and lift her up to the treetops.”

“Romantic.” Zuko replies drolly. “Taking a girl up to your glorified treehouse.”

Jet shrugs, “Hey, if it works, it ain’t broke.” He’s smiling so roguishly and leans closer to her. Zuko feels her heart start to race. He’s an asshole, but that stupid part of her brain kinda likes the spirits-may-care attitude and the slight edge of danger in his every line.

She swallows thickly. “You’re going to have to think of a new routine now that you’re painfully out of trees.”

He chuckles and rests his arms on either side of her, caging her in and pressing his body close to her. “How about seducing a mysterious pretty girl under the moonlight in a secluded corner of a ferry?”

*Fuck. Why do I think this is hot? This is literally the stupidest thing. How is it working on me*?
