“What do you think?” she growls. “We’re refugees.”

“You don’t look like it.” He says motioning to her dress. It’s a fine dress and it looks even better on her. The fabric hugs and shows off the curves of her body so well. Those hips, the teasing lines of her thick thighs, the swell of her ample breasts. He has to drag his eyes away from them, but that means having to look at her pretty face.

Spirits she really is a beautiful girl. Even when she was (is?) the enemy, he can’t help but admire the pale skin, golden eyes, and delicate high cheekbones. Now that she’s hacked off that stupid ponytail, she looks softer than before. Her short, cropped hair falling around her forehead, giving her a sweet look.

“This is for work.” She says. “I can’t exactly go around in my rags. Especially since I work in the upper ring.”

Sokka’s brows shoot up, “You’re working in the upper ring? As what? Where? Why haven’t I seen you there before?”

Zuko rolls her eyes, “I work as a seamstress, but I’ll be going to the palace more often—”

Sokka immediately grabs her arms. “You’re going to the palace!?” he exclaims shaking her.

In retaliation, Zuko growls and kicks his shin, making Sokka yelp in pain and let go. She dusts off her sleeves, “As I was *saying…*” she says pointedly. “I’ll be going to the palace to put the finishing touches on King Kuei’s new robes.”

The water tribesman looks at her with shining eyes. “You have access to the Earth King?” he says breathily. Zuko leans back, unsure of what the tribesman might ask and/or do.

“Yes? And no I’m not going to go along with whatever your planning.”

“Aw c’mon!” Sokka pleads, “I need him to help with an invasion of the Fire Nation!”

She nearly smacks his head. “That makes it even worse!” she cries. “You think I’m going to help you invade my home?!”

“Uhh well…” Sokka says sheepishly. “What if I said it was to take out your shitty dad?”

Zuko is even less amused by that, and this time she does smack him in the head. “No. That doesn’t make it any better! And he’s not a shitty Father!”

“Suuurree.”

Zuko ignores that and snarls, “And another thing, don’t just give out your plans to someone! That’s the stupidest thing I’ve ever seen you done! I thought you were smart!”

“You think I’m smart?” Sokka beams.

“Not. The. Point.” She enunciates with a finger jab to his chest.

But Sokka just laughs, “Alright alright. No need to have a conniption, princess.”

“Don’t call me that!” she hisses.

He shrugs, “Fine, but it’s not like I told a total stranger. I told you.”

“…I’m not following.”

“I trust you.” Sokka says slowly. “You’re a good person. Or well trying to be. I think you know deep down that this war isn’t right.”

Zuko swallows hard. She’s reminded of Song, of Tobio, of the hundreds of refugees that she passes by every day. Hell, she even spares a thought to that asshole, Jet. But what about the honor of her nation?

“It would be cowardly of us to just…stop and pull away.” Zuko whispers. “It would be… dishonorable.”

The warrior looks at her contemplatively. “Why do you think that?” he asks.

She subconsciously touches her scar, and his stormy blue eyes follow the motion. “…that was the lesson he taught me.”

Sokka’s hand goes up to reach out and *touch*, but he stops. Letting it hang in the air just close enough to Zuko.

Maybe it was the long day of sewing. Maybe she just misses having a friendly touch. Maybe she looks into his blue eyes and only sees compassion and warmth.

Slowly, so slowly. She leans towards the hand and lets him touch her scar. Sokka looks startled at first, but he’s reverent and so so careful. Watching her face with intense observation. His thumb caresses the wrinkled bumpy skin, and from the little feeling she has left, it feels…warm and surprisingly safe.

She leans further in the hand and closes her eyes. Sighing contently. They stay that way for a while, Sokka rubbing his hand on her face and Zuko quietly basking in his comfort.

“You know that wasn’t a lesson right? That was *abuse*.” He says stonily.

“How would you know?” Zuko rasps. “I deserved it. I…I wasn’t a good daughter.”

“I call bullshit.” Sokka says plainly. “And I know because I was a real troublemaker when I was a kid. I almost got Dad and Bato killed because I was carelessly playing on the ice.”

“What did they do?”

Sokka smiles, “Dad gave me a stern talking to and grounded me for three weeks. He never laid a hand on us. If he did, the whole village would have rioted.”

Zuko is silent as she takes it in. Then she pulls away turning her head so he can’t see her scar. “I don’t want to talk about this anymore.”

“Okay.” Sokka agrees. He looks up to the red and pink sky. “It’s getting late. Can I walk you home?”

The princess hesitates, but nods. “Yes. Uncle probably wants to see you again.”

He follows her with a silly grin on his face. It looks like the day wasn’t a total bust.

### End Notes

#### Chapter End Notes

**Lu Ten:** Nice one Yue!

**Yue:** Now will you consider Sokka?

**Lu Ten:**...I'll think about it. I guess he's not so bad.

**Yue:** Told you. ^^

Thank you for reading!

## Chapter 8

---

When Sokka offered to take her home, she didn’t think about how crowded the trains would be. She’s pressed tightly against Sokka’s front. The boy insisted on standing behind her to keep most people away from her. She tried to explain that she took this train *every day*, but he merely smiled and said it was the ‘honorable warrior’ thing to do. Ugh he knew she had a weakness for honor.

It’s unbearably nice how his front feels against her. The hard chest providing a warm and solid cover for her vulnerable back. She pushes down the giddy bubbling heat that’s surfacing in her gut and tries to steer her mind away from his musky scent of metal and the sea.

“So…are you going to tell you *friends* that I’m here.” She asks quietly.

“Hmm no. Not unless you’re going to cause trouble.” He replies. His breath hot against her ears. Zuko tries to ignore it.

“I won’t.” she says. “Uncle likes it here. He likes making tea for people.”

“Why doesn’t that surprise me?” Sokka chuckles. “Think I can score some free drinks while I’m there?”

“Not as long as I’m around.” Zuko grouses.

“Stingy&#126;”

“Damn straight.”

At this, Sokka laughs making her feel the rumbling vibrations in his chest. A slight bubble of happiness bursts in her. She made him laugh.

They continue talking quietly throughout the ride, Sokka is mostly telling some of the stories of his time with the Avatar, mixed with smatterings of his childhood in the South Pole. It’s strangely soothing, to just listen to him. Commenting here and there. She always thought she’d hate someone who talked a lot, but Sokka isn’t bad.

They get off on the lower ring, and she leads him to the shop. Uncle’s most likely going to be closing soon, and she wants to walk home with him.

Sokka’s looking at her with a strange (she would almost say fond) look in his eye.

“What?” she asks.

“Nothing, just…you really love your Uncle.”

Zuko blushes, “Of course I do. He practically raised me after Mother…left.” Even now the thought still stings. She never knew what happened to her dear mother. Wherever she is, Zuko hopes she’s happy and safe.

Suddenly a warm hand twines in hers. She looks down and then up at Sokka’s face.

“I’m sorry.” he says. “I lost my mom when I was young too.”

Zuko doesn’t want to ask, but she has to know. “Was it the—was it my people?”

There’s an odd look in his eyes when she says that, “Yeah. During one of the raids. She saved Katara’s life.”

“…I see.” She knows about them from her tutors. How her people raided the South Pole for *years*. Taking away the water benders and destroying the tribes because it was to protect the Fire Nation and her interests. She never really understood it when she was young and naïve. Lu Ten always got a strange pinched look in his brow whenever the raids were mentioned.

Now that she’s seen the world. Seen the destruction her people caused. She understands why Lu Ten always looked so angry when the tutors went over certain subjects. There were just some things, that were so *terrible* nothing could justify it, and her people have done them without a second thought.

She squeezes Sokka’s hand as a sign of comfort and apology. “I’m sorry, that never should have happened. My people shouldn’t have destroyed your nation in the name of security and glory.”

Sokka stop and he’s looking at her with the strangest expression on his face. Almost as if he’s seeing her for the first time.

“You’ve really changed haven’t you?” he says softly.

Zuko frowns. “I’ve been living on the run for the past few months, of course I’d *change*.”

“Hmm truuuee, but…it’s different somehow.” Sokka contemplates, “A few months ago you wouldn’t say a bad thing against your nation, and now…well now you’re apologizing for what they did.”

She purses her lips and turns away. “Like I said, it’s been a rough time.” She tugs on his hand and leads him to the tea shop. For whatever reason, she doesn’t let go of his hand, and to Sokka doesn’t seem to want to let go either.

Iroh is positively beaming when he sees Sokka and Zuko come up to the shop. “Lin, you brought Sokka! What a wonderful surprise.” He says coming over to hug his niece and then the water tribesman. Sokka eagerly hugs Uncle Iroh back. He’s so warm and sturdy. It lessens the ache of missing his father and tribe.

“What brings you here?” Iroh asks.

“Oh I was just walking Zu—erm Lin home.” he answers motioning to the girl quietly watching their interaction with a small smile. It’s gone as soon as both eyes land on her, and she pastes on her usual frown.

“He was very insistent.” Zuko lies. Sokka doesn’t correct her and only laughs loudly.

From the annoying twinkle in her Uncle’s eye, he knows she’s lying through her teeth, but doesn’t comment on it.

“Well then you must come join us for dinner.” Iroh invites. “We’re just having some leftover curry, and I’ll put on some tea while you tell us how you’ve been doing.”

Sokka glances quickly at Zuko who just shurgs. “I’d love to, Uncle!” he answers.

“He’s not your uncle.” Zuko mutters without any heat.

“He’s everyone’s uncle. Learn to share.” Sokka quips.

*He’s not my uncle, he’s my father.* She wants to say, but she just huffs in response.

Sokka’s not surprised to see such a stark contrast in wealth between the upper rings and lower rings, but it’s still jarring to see the dilapidated apartment Zuko and Iroh live in. The inside is only slightly better. There’s a single small room, that no doubt belongs to Zuko, a small rickety table on the verge of collapsing. A rusted stove area, peeling walls, and worn tatami floors.

He knows that Zuko would not appreciate his sympathies and would probably burn him if he tried to help them out, but he wishes there was something he could do for them. The princess takes one look at Sokka’s earnest face and scowls.

“Don’t you fucking dare.” She says.

“What?”

“I know that stupid look on your face.” She answers. “I don’t want your charity.”

Sokka brings his hands up, “I know you don’t, but it doesn’t make me want to help you any less.”

“Well you can shove that help up your—”

“Now now niece.” Iroh interrupts by putting the steaming cups in front of them. “There is no shame in accepting help from those who offer it. And like I’ve said before, there is an honor and humility in living simply.”

Zuko seems to have heard the words before, and surprisingly doesn’t talk back to her uncle. She only sips on her tea, hands delicately wrapped around the cup.

“So how’d you end up working in the tea shop?” Sokka asks.

Iroh laughs and starts on his story. Zuko interrupting when Iroh makes a teasing jab at her.

“I’d say it’s been a peaceful few weeks.” Iroh replies serenely.

Zuko retorts, “We were nearly outed as fire benders by a jerk a week ago. Which was your fault by the way.”

Iroh nods. “I’ll admit, my need for hot tea threw all rational thought out of my head. But they didn’t believe that young man anyway.”

“Who was it?” Sokka asks, eyes narrowed.

Zuko suspiciously blushes and looks down. “Just a stupid teen named Jet.”

Sokka nearly spit takes, “*Jet?!* Like wheat in his mouth Jet? Stupid hook swords Jet?”

“Hook swords aren’t stupid.” Zuko retorts indignantly.

“They’re stupid when *Jet’s* using them.”

“…point*.*” She acquiesces. “Wait…how do you know Jet?”

Sokka goes to tell his story about the crazy Freedom Fighter. How he hated the Fire Nation. About the group of children and teens living with him in the trees near the dam. How he flooded a town and nearly killed everyone just because they were Fire Nation.

Zuko’s shaking with rage by the end of it. “I can’t believe that—that fucking piece of Komodo-rhino *shit*.”

“Oh yeah, he’s a real piece of work.” Sokka agrees. “I’m just glad he didn’t seriously hurt you guys.”

“He tried.” Zuko mutters heatedly. “He came into the shop, swords out and threatening to hurt Uncle and trying to force him to fire bend.”

“What?!”

“I took that asshole down of course.” Zuko continues. “Some secret police force took him away.”

Sokka looks serious. “Dudes in black clothes and wide hats?”

“Yeah.”

He nods. “That’s the Dai Li. Bad news, you better stay away from them. They’re the ones that are keeping people from saying things about the war.”

Zuko and Iroh’s eyes widen.

“That…actually explains a lot.” Zuko replies. “I’d never heard anyone mentioning around us; I always thought people just didn’t want to talk about it since it feels…safe here.”

“Nope, secret police force.” Sokka says. “But I’m glad you two are okay.” And he means it. In the short time he’s known them, he’s really come to like the princess and her uncle. They’re good people, and after all the things Sokka’s seen, he knows that Zuko could have seriously hurt or killed him in their first encounter, but she didn’t.

It doesn’t excuse it, but it puts perspective on it.

“We’re happy to see you safe as well, Sokka.” Iroh replies with a smile. “Now I think the curry should be just about heated and the rice ready.”

Zuko goes to stand, but Iroh tells her to sit. “Let me, niece. You’ve been working long hours these past few days.”

“Not that long.”

“You’re gone before Agni’s even risen from the sky. Now sit.”

Zuko reluctantly does as she’s told, and now that Sokka’s looking, he can see the light shadows under her eyes, the weary and tired slope of her shoulder now that she’s home and comfortable.

“Is seamstress work demanding?” Sokka asks.

“Not really, but I go in the shop to do extra work. Sewing stock patterns and basic embroidery for Master Hina. It brings some more coin for us.” She answers with a heavy sigh. “I just…” she trails off, looking to her Uncle’s back.

Sokka understands, but he doesn’t go to mention it. “Sure you can’t do a little investigating on the Earth King for me?” he asks to change the subject.

It seems to perk her out of the melancholy, and she glares at him, “For the last time: no. I’m not *spying* on the Earth King for you. I could go to *prison*.”

“I would break you out!” He insists.

Zuko puts a hand over her heart mockingly, “Wow, how sweet.” She coos saccharinely. “A big strong man will break me out of prison.”

“I’m going to ignore that sarcasm.” Sokka replies primly and then flexes his biceps. “Became I *am* a big strong man.”

Zuko squints and then reaches out to pinch the slightly swelled bicep, making Sokka yelp and scramble away from her. “No, that doesn’t feel like a big strong anything.” She teases.

“I’m still *growing*.” He retorts petulantly. “Just watch, I’ll tower over you once I’m done, and I’ll have biceps as big as your thigh! You just wait princess, we water tribesmen go through a massive spurt a little later than you Fire Nation people.”

Zuko laughs, “Yeah well, we’re not exactly the paradigm of tall people.” She answers glancing at Iroh and then motioning to herself. “This is probably as tall as I’ll get.”

Sokka coos, “Aw you’ll be my wittle fun-sized princess.”

Zuko retaliates by tossing her empty cup at his head.

“Ow, Uncle she hit me!”

“You *bitch* I didn’t even throw it that hard!”

Iroh merely laughs and puts down the rice and curry. “Ah to be young again.” He reminisces. “Now let’s eat up!”

After the (intensely spicy) curry, Iroh brews another pot of tea for them. Sokka is leaning back and fanning his tongue. “Whew that’s spicy.” He complains.

“I wish it was spicier.” Zuko sighs.

Sokka looks incredulously at her, “See this is why you people are so grumpy all the time. You eat really spicy things and then have spicy poops. Now your assholes are burning and next thing you know you’re trying to dominate the world.”

Zuko pushes him down, “You’re *disgusting*.”

“Hey pooping is a natural process and you shouldn’t be ashamed to talk about it.” Sokka says sagely. “But seriously how can your asshole *not* be burned off after eating so much spicy shit?”

“You get used to it.” Zuko mumbles, “And stop talking about our assholes.”

“I’m thinking of it as reconnaissance.” Sokka says.

“What does our assholes and poop have *anything* to do with reconnaissance?”

“Well maybe I’ll target the fire nation plumbing and force Ozai to stop the war unless he wants his palace to drown in sewage.”

“Oh Agni, you’re so stupid.” She says, but there’s a smile growing on her lips.

“No no *think* about it! It’ll be a bloodless battle. I use the food supplies to distribute a new extra extra spicy chili pepper to all of the capital, then once everyone’s gotten a taste of it and it’s permeated the cuisine, I’ll sabotage the sewage pipes, and *BAM!* The whole capital’s full of people drowning in spicy shit! Ozai will *have* to broker peace.” He lays out proudly.

Zuko can’t stop herself, she laughs loudly. It’s the first time Sokka’s ever heard something like it coming from her, and by the awed look in Iroh’s eyes, it’s probably been some time since he’s heard it too. There’s a suspiciously wet look in his eyes as he watches his niece fold herself over, clutching her stomach and laughing.

Sokka grins so wide his cheeks hurt, and as he watches her sit back up, catching her breath and wiping the stray tear from her eye, he can’t help but think how beautiful she looks. So free and happy. Her eyes shining at him and a smile on her lips.

“That—that is *such* a terrible idea. I thought you were the plan guy?” she says chuckling.

The water tribesman can take one for the team, so he just shrugs. “Hey not everything that comes out of this marvelous brain can be gold. That just wouldn’t be fair for people like you.”

“Riiiigghht. You keep telling yourself that.”

They keep bantering back and forth, teasing and joking with each other until the moon rises high.

Sokka looks up and winces, “Ah I think I might have missed the last train.” He says.

“Why don’t you stay here for the night.” Iroh offers. “It’s our fault we kept you here so long.”

“I wouldn’t want to intrude.” Sokka says.

“Nonsense! You’re a friend here.” Iroh says, “We still have the sleeping bags from the road. You’ll just have to sleep out here with this old man.”

“He snores. Loudly.” Zuko quips.

“Haha can’t be worse than Toph’s.” Sokka says. “I’ll take the sleeping bag then. Thanks.”

Zuko nods and takes out the sleeping bag from their pack. She also takes out a small bucket filled with soap and shampoo. “I can also show you where the communal bathrooms are.” she says. “I want to bathe before going to bed anyway.”

“You can take my bucket.” Iroh says with a smile. “I can go after you.”

“Thanks, Uncle. I’ll probably rinse off.” Sokka says looking at dust on his arms. He takes Iroh’s bucket and goes up to follow Zuko to the bathes.

At least the area is fairly clean. He doesn’t know if he wants to go barefoot here though, the floors look like they’ve seen better times. He heads off to a stall and fills a bucket with tepid water. It’s a little cold on his skin, but he’s used to it after growing up in the South Pole.

An older man comes in, and squints. “I haven’t seen you around.”

Sokka shrugs. “Oh yeah I’m just visiting Lin and Mushi. They offered to let me stay for the night.”

“Aaahhh so *you’re* the reason why some of the neighborhood boys are looking a little antsy.” He says with a grin.

“…What?”

He chuckles, “Well Lin’s never brought anyone near here, and then you show up making her smile like that. A lot of them might start upping their game.”

Sokka feels a clawing jealousy in the pit of his stomach added to a fluttering pride and happiness. He made Zuko laugh and smile. Something no other guy here had done.

“Well, good luck to them. She’s…not easily impressed.” He says.

The older man laughs, “Oh I know. Best of luck to you, young man. Lin’s a real keeper.”

Sokka doesn’t tell him that he’s just a…friend? Enemy? Frenemy? He’s not going to court Lin…

He shakes his head. No of course he won’t. They’re technically at war with her people and shitty father. Ugh just thinking about Ozai makes his blood boil. When he gets his hand’s on him, he’ll clock him so hard with his boomerang.

He’s still in a sour mood as he exits the bath, but then he’s hit with the scent of fire-lilies and citrus. He turns and sees Zuko, coming out of the bath, hair damp and skin still a little wet. She’s in a light pale green sleeping robe that falls gracefully on her frame. The neckline showing off her pale delicate neck and collarbones. Sokka swallows thickly as he watches a bead of water drop down that alabaster column and disappear underneath the robe into the valley of her breasts.

“Sokka?”

“Wh-what?” he says eyes snapping up.

She’s frowning at him and inches closer. “Are you okay? I’ve been calling your name. Did you hit your head in there? You know you can’t afford to lose anymore braincells.” She jokes.

It’s true he can’t afford to lose anymore braincells, but all the blood and thoughts are just going straight down into his dick. He clears his throat.

“Uh ah well haha yeahh&#126;”

There’s a pause.

Finally, Zuko rolls her eyes. “Okay weirdo. Let’s go before you get even stupider.” She goes ahead and Sokka is hypnotized by the way her hips move. The way her ass teases him from under the soft robe.

*Tui and La, I need to get a grip—no bad wording—get a hold of my—no still bad—need to dunk my* *head in an ice bucket.*

At this point he doesn’t know which head should be dunked first.

Then Zuko sees someone drop something in the hallway, and she *bends down*.

His lower head. His lower head for sure. It’s just a *mass of sin*.

He somehow manages to get into the apartment and hands Iroh the bucket with a strained smile. When Iroh leaves, Sokka’s painfully aware of how alone they are. They are sitting close together, knees almost touching. Both are quiet, and Sokka is fine just sitting there with her, just basking in the atmosphere.

Suddenly she turns to him with wide golden eyes, “Hey wanna see something cool?”

“Uh, *always*.”

“How’s your climbing?”

“…pretty good.” He lies.

Zuko seems to see right through it, but she just smirks and goes over to the window. she opens it and *climbs out*. Sokka nearly has a conniption, but he sees her legs disappearing *up* and goes to investigate. He peeks his head out and looks up, Zuko’s sitting on the roof and she’s holding her hand out to him.

Sokka carefully climbs out and pulls himself up, using the windowsill to balance himself as he grips onto Zuko’s hand and the roof’s ledge. He’s sitting next to her, legs dangling over the edge and she has an eager look in her eyes. She points up and says, “Look.”

Sokka looks. And he’s stunned.

The night sky is lit with the pale twinkling lights of the stars. A belt of light blue and silvery light cuts through the dark background. He can clearly see the Warrior pointing towards the True Star. The constellations of the polar-bear dog draping above the warrior. And on the side hanging above them is the big bright round moon. Glowing so beautifully up among the heavens.

“I thought you’d might want to see Yue.” Zuko whispers. “I like to sit up here and watch her.”

Tears gather in his eyes. “I like doing that too. I still talk to her sometimes.”

“Me too.”

Sokka lets a tear fall, “I miss her.”

Zuko nods beside him. “I didn’t know her well, but…she was kind and nice. I wish…I wish I could have had a friend like her growing up.”

“Yeah.”

They sit in silence, both looking up at the moon. At some point they end up sitting flush against one another. Zuko’s warmth leaking into Sokka.

“You know…after I *fooled around* with Jet—”

The atmosphere broke. “You did *what* with Jet?!” Sokka screeches.

Zuko blushes heavily, “Just…just *things*. Whatever. That isn’t important.”

“Ummm no—no that’s *very* important, are you saying you—you *slept with Jet*?”

Zuko punches his arm. “Yes I did.” She hisses. “And…I really regretted it.”

Sokka deflates and looks at her. Zuko looks…ashamed and angry. Oh shit he didn’t.

“Oh shit…did he—did he—”

“No, no.” she says. “No I—we consented to everything. It’s just…he wasn’t really that good.”

Sokka snorts and puts his hand over his mouth, trying to keep in his laughter. Oh just knowing that Jet’s bad at sex is enough to make this conversation worth it.

Zuko catches his eye and she also chuckles too. “Yeah, he was *so* bad. Like just not even trying to make me feel good.”

“What a *dick*.”

“Oh, his dick wasn’t anything to write home about.”

Sokka nearly falls off the roof laughing. He holds his hands up, “Oh Tui, high-five that’s amazing.”

Zuko hesitantly high-fives him, but she’s smiling. “Yeah and he said the stupidest shit during it.”

“Oh oh let me guess.” Sokka clears his throat and makes it purposefully raspy in a mocking mimic of Jet, “*Oh baby does my big dick make you feel good? Are you going to come?*”

Zuko is rolling on the roof with laughter. “Agni *yes.* I ended up faking it just so it would be over.”

The water tribesman covers his hand over his face, “Oh Tui, that’s terrible. *Please* tell me the universe took revenge on that jerk.”

Zuko nods, “Yeah, Yue sent a nice wave that sent him tumbling on his ass.”

Sokka laughs uproariously and sends a thumbs up at the moon. “Nice job, Yue!”

The moon merely glows, but Zuko swears she hears the tinkling laugh and feel a soft brush of fingers through her hair. Sokka’s blue eyes are positively glowing in the moonlight, and the silvery light, seems to highlight the strong lines underneath the babyfat. It’s different from when she looked at Jet, from Tobio or the other Fire Nation boy.

It feels, comfortable. Like she doesn’t have to worry or hide any part of her. Maybe it’s because he’s already seen her at her lowest, at her angriest. Maybe it’s because he seems to understand what it means to work and strive for his father’s love and approval. Maybe it’s because he understands the pain of wanting to go home.

Either way, she lets herself close her eyes and bask in this moment. Soft wind caressing her face. She hears Sokka’s breath hitch and she turns to him quizzically.

“Something wrong?”

“N-no.” he stutters in a choked voice. “Nothing at all. You’re just so—em I’ve never seen you relaxed.”

Zuko turns away, there’s an inkling of shame bubbling in her. The voice of her father and Azula putting her down. But then Sokka’s hand is holding hers, and she shyly peers at him through her lashes.

“I like it.” He says. “It’s a good look on you.”

“…thanks.”

They stay out like that for a few moments longer. Before Zuko says they should be getting back inside. She has an early day tomorrow.

She goes to bed more relaxed and happier than she’s been in a long time.

Sokka trudges back into their home in the wee hours of the morning. He tries to keep quiet as he stumbles in, but he’s so *tired.* Iroh was not joking when he said Zuko got up *waaayy* early for work. She really enjoyed watching him grumble and sleepily glare at her for making him wake up so early. The only good things about going early, is that the trains are pretty empty. They got to sit down and he got to lean against her and take a short nap. Zuko was at least kind enough to not bother him until they got to the checkpoint of the Upper Ring.

Sokka vaguely recalls Zuko apologizing to the guards for his fumbling, and she ended up having to give the guards his passport. She turned red for some reason and muttered something to the guard, who just laughs. Then she walked him to the front of his home, and she said good-bye with an amused smile on her lips. Sokka isn’t sure if he said bye back. The next thing he knows, he’s falling onto the bed next to Aang and falling straight back to sleep.

After dropping Sokka off at his place, Zuko heads over to Master Hina’s store. The seamstress gave her a spare key since she knew the girl would be there early every day. Looking at the time she had a few hours before a carriage came to take her to the palace. She’s working on an outer robe and just finishing up the last stitch before she adds some embroidery on it. It’s a turquoise green, unusual for Earth Kingdom garb, but blended colors seem to be coming into fashion in the courts.

Suddenly, she’s struck with a sudden inspiration. She let’s her fingers and thread weave the images in her head. Master Hina comes in finding her still working on the robe. She peers over her shoulders and hums appreciatively at the work.

“A boomerang.” She says. “That’s an unusual design, but…it seems to work well with this. A bit water tribe-esque. Especially with these colors.”

Zuko blushes. “Yes I guess I was just…inspired.”

“Hmm not bad. Perhaps we should start incorporating other nation’s images and blended coloring. It could start a new fashion trend.”

Zuko just nods and continues working.

By the time the carriage comes, the robe is practically finished. An intricate boomerang gracing the back of the robe. Master Hina wants to put it up front as soon as possible. She says it might attract new business.

While the carriage brings her to the palace, she thinks about what Sokka might be doing. Probably still sleeping. They stayed up late on the roof. She smiles at the memory. She never had someone her age that she considered a…friend. Yue perhaps for that brief moment of time but spending time with Sokka is nice. Easy.

Azula always had Ty Lee and Mai, and Zuko was always jealous of the trio’s friendship. Her father never let her go to the Academy, due to her being the Crown Princess and because he didn’t want her to embarrass him. So, after Lu Ten died, her closest friends were the turtleducks in the pond or the wolfdogs that guarded the palace. Mai tried, but it was hard with Azula hovering around them all the time. Even then, it never fully staved off the *loneliness*.

She wonders idly if the palace would be less lonely if she ever came home. Would she be able to make friends? Would anyone have even missed her while she was gone? Would this longing for home finally be filled?

Uncle’s words echo in her head,

*“You’re chasing ghosts and phantoms of memories that are long gone…”*

She winces. She doesn’t want to think about that now.

The guards escort her to the king’s chambers. Kuei is waiting for her at the seat with Bosco by his side.

“Lin, it’s good to see you.” Kuei greets. “I can’t wait to try on the new robes.”

Zuko bows. “I hope they are to your satisfaction.” She says, and she takes out the robes from the case the guards brought up.

Kuei’s eyes light up with delight. He quickly walks to her and hesitantly puts his hands up. “May I?”

“Of course.” And Zuko hands him the robes. He runs his hands along the soft cloth and then over the beautiful embroidery of Bosco.

“This is amazing work.” He praises. “This is one of the best pieces I’ve ever seen since Master Shandi.”

Zuko bows again. “Thank you. I’m glad it’s to your satisfaction, but we still need to do a final fitting before we can give it to you. And…we’ll need your…bear to try on the tunic.”

“Oh yes of course of course.” Kuei says. He claps and the valets come, one takes the robes and the other walks with the king to the large partition. They help the king change and Kuei is out in minutes in the grand robes Zuko and Master Hina slaved over. He runs his hands on the fine fabric and marvels at the neat stitching.

“Wonderful. Beautiful. It feels perfect.” he declares. “I don’t see how we need to do anymore alterations.”

Zuko nods. “That’s good, but I would like to check just the same.”

She motions for Kuei to stand on the podium while she inspects the stitching and the way the fabric moves. She makes him do various motions just to see how much movement the heavy robes impede. That is the one thing she doesn’t miss. The heavy formal robes that make her feel like she’s carrying fifty pounds on her.

Kuei is quiet and when she peeks up, he sees her staring at her scar. She sighs, and the king seems to notice how much he’s staring.

“Ah forgive me. I just…I’ve never really seen a terrible scar like that on someone before. Not even on my soldiers.” He says sheepishly.

Zuko nods in understanding. “It’s fine, your majesty. I’m used to it.”

“…May I ask what happened?”

Zuko bites her lip. She really doesn’t want to answer but, he is the king. She doesn’t know how well he’ll take rejection.

“An…incident with my father.” She explains. “He burned me.”

Kuei sucks in his breath and he looks pale. His hands are shaking.

“Who is he?” he asks. “Give me his name.”

Zuko’s mouth is agape. “W-what? Why?”

“W-why? Because your *father burned you*!” Kuei exclaims. “I can’t allow something like that to happen to my citizens and let it go unpunished!”

“No, no you misunderstand, your majesty.” Zuko tries to explain. “I deserved it, I spoke out of turn and I dishonored him. That’s why he burned me.”

Instead of placating the king, Kuei looks even more horrified and angry. Even some of the guards are shuffling with indignation.

“Excuse me?!” Kuei shouts. “Di-Did you just say you *deserved* it? Wh-what that’s just—this is—! I need to sit down.”

One the servants rush with a seat for the king and another for Zuko, who hesitantly takes a seat after a matronly looking woman gives her a stern look. Kuei’s head is in his hands, and he’s breathing deeply. Another servant comes in with a large fan and starts fanning the king. Bosco seems to have sensed his master’s distress and waddles over, nuzzling his nose against Kuei.

“Um…if it’s any better, it was three years ago.” Zuko says quietly.

Kuei looks up in despair. “How old are you now?”

“…sixteen.”

She never expected the weak looking king to curse so well. He could put some of the sailors to shame with the verbose strong of curses that leave his lips.

He finally takes a breath and looks at her seriously, “Lin, I *promise* that you’ll be safe. I’ll bring you into my staff, you’ll be protected in the palace. Just tell me where your father is. I’ll bring him to justice.”

What is with people trying to bring her father to justice for her? For their nation and to stop the war she gets, but not for *her*. She’s just…insignificant.

“Your Majesty, it’s alright—”

“No it’s not! A father should not be *burning* their child!” he exclaims.

“…he’s an important official. He needed to make an example of me.”

“That—that doesn’t make things any better. You realize that right? I may be king, but if I ever burned a thirteen-year-old child, my people would *riot* and ask for my head. Especially if it was my own child.”

Zuko winces. “It’s in the past now.”

“That doesn’t mean it shouldn’t go unpunished. He needs to be brought to justice. Please Lin.” He pleads.

Zuko’s hands are trembling. This is wrong, why would the king of her enemy nation want to help her? Why is he so insistent that her father needs to be punished on her behalf? She can’t. She just can’t.

She falls to the floor and prostrates herself.

“P-please King Kuei.” She begs. “Please, please don’t ask me anymore. I just—I just want to forget about it.”

King Kuei is silent. The entire room is silent and heavy. Finally, she feels the king looming above her. She doesn’t dare look up, but he’s kneeling now. His hands are gently wrapped around her arms and helping her up. She keeps her head down. She doesn’t want this man to see the tears swimming in her eyes.

“…Lin, I’m sorry.” he says. “I didn’t mean to push you. I will respect your wishes. I just…I’m just so *angry*. I can’t believe there’s someone in my kingdom who’s capable of doing something like that.”

Well he’s not actually from Kuei’s kingdom, but she doesn’t say this.

“It’s alright.” She whispers. “You’re…king. You should look out for your people.”

Kuei stands there silently. He wishes he could do something more for her, but she’s made it clear that he’s not to ask her again. But…

He catches one of the guard’s eyes and they nod. They’ll get the guards on it.

Bosco seems to feel the anxiety and sadness radiating from Zuko and shuffles over. He butts his head against her hip and warbles. Zuko smiles and pats his head.

“Well, Your Majesty I think now is a good time to see if the tunic fits Bosco.” She says.

Kuei pastes on a smile, “Yes that would be lovely. I can’t wait to see how Bosco looks in it.”

For a bear, he’s fairly well-mannered and easily lets Zuko slip the tunic on him. Kuei nods appraisingly.

“He looks amazing, Lin. You and Master Hina do great work.” He compliments. “And Bosco seems to really like you.”

At this Bosco rumbles and nuzzles against Zuko’s stomach. The princess strokes the bristly fur and murmurs sweet compliments to the creature.

“I’m glad you both like it. I hope that my first work meets your expectations.”

“And beyond!” Kuei exclaims. “I’ll be proud to wear your work Lin. Master Shandi was the one to clothed all of the Earth Kingdom royalty when she still lived here, and Master Hina now takes her place. I’m glad you are now working with her.”

Zuko blushes. “You honor me.”

Kuei smiles and then motions for one of the servants to come over. He brings over a bag and Kuei hands it to her.

Zuko is dumbfounded by the gold inside. “Y-your majesty, you’ve already paid us.”

“It’s your tip.” Kuei says. “You’ve done an amazing job on this robe and you’ve finished it so quickly. It’s only fair that I compensate you well.”

Well she won’t complain about getting the extra gold. Once she saves enough, she could get a nice home in the middle ring for her uncle.

She thanks him again and packs up her things. She bows once more to King Kuei before she leaves with the escorts. They take her back to Master Hina’s shop, and she continues to work until the afternoon.

She shouldn’t be surprised when she finds Sokka waiting for her outside the shop. He’s grinning and holding up a sweetbread as an offering.

“Hey!” he greets.

Zuko sighs, “I should have known you would have found me.” she says taking the bread. She takes a bite and the sweet custard fills her mouth. Zuko moans in delight savors the taste. When she goes to thank Sokka, he’s still as a statue, and his normally tanned face seems to be burning red.

“Hngm—there’s er…that’s a—” Sokka makes abortive motions to the corner of his lips, but Zuko isn’t really getting it. Is this a commoner thing?

“Cream!” he shouts. “Th-there’s cream here.” He says gesturing to the corner of his lips.

Zuko swipes a thumb over the area and licks the custard off her thumb. “Did I get it all?”

Sokka shakes his head. “N-no you still got some.”

She does two more swipe throughs, but apparently there’s still some left. Finally, Sokka reaches out and wipes the area just under his bottom lip. Her breath hitches when the pressure of his thumb graces over her lips. Electricity seems to shoot through her and her toes are curling in her boots.

“T-there.” Sokka says.

Zuko nods, probably looking flushed. “Thanks.” She says.

They stand there awkwardly, both trying to get their hearts to settle down.

“Um you—you wanna get dinner…with me?” Sokka asks.

Zuko bites her lip. Her heart starts to palpitate unreasonably. Why is she getting so excited? He probably just wants to ask how her meeting with the king went. She should say no, but tonight is Uncle’s Pai Sho night with the other people in their building, so he won’t be waiting for her.

“Sure…dinner sounds good.”

Sokka grins, “Great, I found an amazing place that has the *best* dumplings and they even have one that’s as big as your face!”

Zuko snorts, “You’re such a pig-cow.”

“I’m a growing boy and I need my meat.” He proclaims.

The restaurant is in the middle ring, but Zuko can see a lot of upper ring nobles sitting in the nicer tables. The waiter takes one look at Sokka and is falling over himself, gesturing to a private booth that is probably only used for the highest nobility.

“Lord Sokka! It’s an honor to be serving you and your girlfriend, tonight.” The waiter remarks with a wide sunny smile.

Both teens turn red and stutter.

“Sh-she’s not—”

“We’re not—er—he’s not like—”

The waiter nods in comprehension, “Ah of course you’d want to keep your involvement quiet. I stand corrected, it’s an honor to have Lord Sokka and his *lovely friend* for dinner this evening.” He winks.

The correction is not any better, but Sokka and Zuko just lets it slide. There’s no point in correcting the man.

Sokka snickers to himself. “So…*lovely* *friend*…”

Zuko tosses a peanut at him. “Shut up, *Lord Sokka*.”

He shrugs, “Being friends with the Avatar has it perks, but I could do without the Lord Sokka part.”

“Isn’t your father a Chief? You’re technically a lord.”

Sokka shakes his head, “No we don’t really do the whole ‘bloodline rules’. We vote for a new chief when the old one retires.”

“…so you just let *people* choose your next leader?”

“Yup.”

Zuko shakes her head, “Isn’t that dangerous? What if your next leader is a complete imbecile? They weren’t born into the role and were never trained from birth. You could end up with a rampaging psychopath on the throne!”

Sokka looks pointedly at her.

…why is he looking at her like that?

“What?”

Sokka sighs, “Don’t worry about it. You’ll figure it out.”

Zuko purses her lips. She doesn’t like knowing that she missed something. What was it? He got that look when she said someone could end up with a rampaging psychopath…

…

Oh.

She sighs. “My father is not a psychopath.”

“I was referring to your sister, but your dad counts too.” Sokka replies mildly. “If it were up to me, I’d cut off his hands and dick, chain him up and leave him on an iceburg in the middle of the ocean and let him die of frostbites.”

Zuko wrinkles her nose, “That’s my father you’re talking about murdering. Gruesomely, I might add.”

“Please, we both know you think Iroh’s your real dad.”

Zuko turns red. “H-how did you—no I don’t!”

Sokka gives her a *look*.

“…I just…don’t correct people’s assumptions.” She says quietly. “I already have a father, and he’s…”

“A fucking dick?”

“Hard to impress.”

Sokka humphs, but then looks back at her. She’s hunched her shoulders in, eyes downcast and trying to look smaller. Ah he hates making her feel like this.

“Look, I’m sorry for harping on it. I just…I just don’t like that someone hurt you badly.”

“It was a long time ago.”

“Three years isn’t really that long, and just because it did doesn’t mean he should go unpunished.”

Zuko chuckles mirthlessly. “You and King Kuei said the same thing.”

Sokka’s eyes go up. “I completely forgot you were meeting the king today!” he says. “How did it go? Did he like your stuff?”

Zuko blinks. “Wait, you didn’t ask me to dinner to interrogate me about the king?”

Sokka looks like he’s about to smack her. “No, I just wanted to get dinner with…a friend. A *lovely* friend.” He adds jokingly.

The princess blushes and turns away. Her traitorous heart is pounding in her chest. The thought that Sokka considers her a friend is…good. Nice. But…

“And if I decide to go after the Avatar?”

Sokka scoffs. “Well I probably wouldn’t be too surprised or happy, but…I’d still think of you as a semi- friend. One that’s trying to capture the hope of the whole world for her shitty dad, but we can’t be perfect. And I think I can take you with my trusty boomerang.”

She should be so mad. She should be blazing and shouting that he shouldn’t take her so lightly, but…

But his declaration just made her so *happy*.

“I could deflect your stupid boomerang with my blades.” She retorts.

“…what? You have blades? That’s not *fair*. You’re already a fi—er bender!” Sokka exclaims. “You can only choose one!”

“Pft, who says *that*?” Zuko chuckles. “Besides if I didn’t know how to use my swords, Jet would have seriously hurt someone.”

“You beat Jet with *swords*?”

“What did you think I used? My *bending*?”

Sokka opens his mouth and then shuts it. Oh he hadn’t actually thought about that. “That’s super cool. You should teach me.”

“What?”

“Teach me swordbending!”

Zuko feels her brow tick , “Okay first: don’t call it swordbending. Second: Why?”

Sokka shrugs, “I’m just…I know I’m important to the group. I’m the *plan guy*, but well…it’d be nice to not be the weakest link when we’re fighting. I mean I’d still probably be the weakest link but I shouldn’t be like *weak weak*. It’s just hard to keep up with people like the Avatar, my sister, and a genius 12-year-old Earth bender.”

Oh.

*Oh.*

She gets it. She understands the feeling of being pitted against pure talent and genius. It was hard enough being compared to Azula, but he’s being compared to powerhouses like the Avatar and genius benders like Katara and the Earth girl.

She actually never thought about the other teen’s position. Sokka always seemed so…confident and self-assured. She’s never seen him so downcast.

Zuko clears her throat. “Um…I have three days off out of the week. We can train then. Just come by my place.”

The result is immediate, he perks up and beams at her. “Seriously? Thanks! I can’t wait!”

They’re food comes shortly after, and Sokka’s too busy stuffing his face to continue talking.

Zuko nibbles on her food a bit more delicately, but it’s good. The dumpling skins are soft and chewy and the filling is well seasoned. The other dishes are quite good as well. The hand-pulled noodles are delicious and paired well with the broth. Sokka is nearly bouncing out of his seat when the giant dumpling comes. It’s truly massive and actually as big as Sokka’s face.

“Zuko, I think I’m in love.” He sighs drooling over the dumpling.

The princess shakes her head exasperatedly. “You better eat your ‘love’ soon or I’ll be taking it.”

“What jealous?” Sokka asks wiggling his brow. “Don’t worry there’s enough of Sokka to go around.”

Zuko smacks his arm for that, but either her hits are getting softer or he’s getting a higher pain tolerance, because he merely laughs as he splits the dumpling apart for the both of them.

He’s probably just getting a higher pain tolerance.

“Uuggh I don’t think I can move.” Sokka says leaning back against the seat.

“I told you not to order dessert.”

“But the chocolate mochi’s are *amazing*!”

They were amazing. Zuko ate two of them and they were so rich and decadent. Sokka ate the other four. The waiter comes by with the bill and Zuko moves to grab her purse, but Sokka’s already handed the waiter the coins. Sokka then leads her out before she can protest in the restaurant.

“Sokka.” She hisses.

“What? You made dinner yesterday. It’s my treat today.” He says.

“That was just curry.”

“The best curry I had.” Sokka retorts. “So I took you to the best dumpling place I know. Because I can’t cook for shit. Anyway, consider this a congratulations dinner.”

“For what?”

“For getting hired by the Earth King!”

“Oh, that’s not a big deal.”

Sokka looks at her with a deadpan expression. “It’s a big deal. Trust me. You should have heard the way the people were talking about you.”

“What people?”

Sokka laughs and herds her to the train, “How do you think I found you? I asked around the local artisan shops. Mostly the textile places. They all told me the best seamstress in the city is Master Hina, and she just hired a girl from the lower ring.”

“Oh…I didn’t know that people talked about me.”

“Apparently you came in with a seal of approval from a big-shot seamstress, and Master Hina hired you on the spot. Everyone’s really impressed. They said the seal isn’t given to just *anyone*. Of all the apprentices that old lady’s had she’s only given it to four people including you.”

Zuko blushes. “Ah well…I always thought Master Shandi was a crazy old lady, who just happened to be good at sewing.”

“Haha happened to you too huh? We went through the same thing in Omashu, I’m telling you that king is a few cabbage heads short of a cart…”

By the time, Zuko’s realized it, Sokka had already walked her back to her place, again. Iroh smiles when he sees Sokka and offers him tea, but the teen declines, saying that he shouldn’t stay too late if he wants to catch the last train. Zuko catches his sleeve before he goes.

“Um…tomorrow at sunrise. I can help teach you some sword katas.”

Sokka beams, “Awesome. I’ll be here. Bright and early.”

“Better be.” She grins back.

He leaves with another good-bye and Zuko shuts the door. She goes out her window and waits by there until he sees him coming out of the complex. He looks up and waves enthusiastically at her. She returns it with a smaller wave and watches him leave down the road. When she turns around, Uncle has the biggest shit-eating grin on his face.

“What?” she snipes.

He shrugs, “Oh nothing. I’m glad you and Sokka are getting along.” He says. “What were you two up to?”

“…just dinner.” She replies. “He took me to a dumpling place in the middle ring. I think you would like it uncle…” she goes on to regal Iroh with the details of their time together. Telling him the funny things he said, the stupidly happy look he gets when food comes. Iroh listens to it with a content smile on his face.

It’s been so long since he’s seen his darling niece so lively. He smiles secretly to himself, Sokka is funny, kind, a brilliant strategist, it sounds like they’re about to spar together, and he’s even friends with the Avatar’s flying lemur. Plus, going by what Iroh recalls of the Southerners he’ll grow to be quite tall.

Strange how the spirits work sometimes. He wonders if Lu Ten is watching this now.

Iroh chuckles to himself.

Poor boy might just be tearing his hair out.

Toph is up early, which is weird and *very suspicious*.

“Toph.” Sokka greets.

“By all means.” Sokka invites. “It’s really not that interesting.”

She leads them to a secluded area, near what was probably once stables. She takes out her blades and wraps them in a leather cloth.

“Just a few more seconds Sokka. If you don’t stretch you might tear your muscles during or after training. It’s something even benders should learn to do. It keeps your body spry.”

She easily goes down into a split and Sokka is sure that if he tries that he’ll bust his nuts. That perverted part of his brain wants to know just how flexible she is, and Sokka has to punt it into the deep dark recesses of his mind.

Ten minutes of stretching and warm ups later, and he does actually feel a little looser. Damn that woman. Why did she have to be right?

She tosses him her sword and says, “Show me how you grip it.”

He does and she looks it over.

“Not bad.” She says, “You’re probably used to carrying a top-heavy weapon right?”

“Yeah my old club.” He says, impressed that she could tell.

She nods. “That would explain it.” She fixes his grip slightly and asks, “How does this feel?”

“Weird, but like it’s right.”

“Good, now I’ll show you how to hold a proper stance.”

They’re at it for hours, and Sokka is starting to feel good about his form. They even do a small mock spar, with Zuko mirroring the basic strokes and stances. They’re sweating heavily by the time they’re doing their cool down stretches. Sokka groans as she pushes against his back and he tries to reach further into the V.

“I don’t think my back can bend anymore.” He groans.

“You’ll need to start doing more stretches, you’re pathetically stiff.” She says.

“Hey not all bending and fighting is filled with crazy acrobatics like you or Aang’s.” he grouses. “We can’t all do a jump kick and split in mid-air.”

“Aang does more acrobatics than me.”

“Yeah but not by much, and at least he has the excuse of being able to fly. You just fling yourself out there and hope you don’t fall and die.”

“…okay but you should still be more limber. It’ll help keep you loose during battle and reduce any muscle injuries.”

“Good to know.” he sighs as she finally lifts herself off him. “Wanna get something to eat after?”

She lifts the collar of her damp shirt and gives him a look.

“After we shower of course.” He amends.

“…okay. I know a nice restaurant with good noodles nearby.”

“Alright!”

They head back to the apartment to shower, but while Zuko has some spare clothes, Sokka did not bring any. He’s strutting around the apartment in a towel as he cautiously sniffs his sweaty clothes.

*Ugh.*

He really doesn’t want to put these back on.

Zuko comes out of her room and nearly has a heart attack.

“Sokka!” she cries.

“What?”

“W-what—what why aren’t you dressed?!”

He holds up his dirty sweaty clothes. “Eeehh I kinda forgot I just had this one pair.”

Zuko groans. “Okay s-stay there.” She heads back into her room and comes out again throwing a tunic and pants at him.

“What are these?” he asks.

“They’re mine.” She answers. “We’re about the same height and size. You can borrow those. They’re my spare training clothes.”

He looks down at the clothes and then back to her. Zuko’s averting her eyes and there’s a vibrant blush on her cheeks.

Well, better than wearing his sweaty clothes.

It is at the same time better than wearing his sweaty clothes and worse than wearing his sweaty clothes.

The tunic and pants fit him well enough, it’s tight around the shoulders and a little loose around his hips, but it’s doable. They’re soft and clean and smell like her. Every time he breathes, he gets a waft of fire-lilies and citrus. It’s intoxicating and so very very distracting.

Zuko is not doing any better on her part. She likes the way Sokka fills out her clothes. The cut of the tunic and pants were originally meant for a man, but she altered it to fit her figure better. She can see how his broader shoulders strain at the sleeves, and he cinches the pants tight along the hips which exaggerate his trim waist.

Agni, she needs to get a hold of herself. Just because a handsome boy is wearing your clothes doesn’t mean that you should start panting after him like an animal.

She waves to some of the familiar neighborhood people, who all smile and give her discrete thumbs up. She doesn’t want to know what they’re thinking. They eventually get to the restaurant and sit down. The shy awkwardness slowly lifts and conversations flow easily from there.

“So does everyone in your family know how to sword fight? Oh Tui and La if your maniac sister starts swinging a sword at me I’d just run.”

“Ha no, only me and my cousin did. We studied under Piandao. I used to live at his estate for two years before I had to move back, but he still came by the palace to train me.” she says, sighing wistfully at the memories.

“Well I’m glad then that I don’t have to worry about your sister or father surprising us by going staby-stab.”

Zuko chuckles wryly, “You won’t have to. It’s considered demeaning for a bender to learn weapons.”

Sokka’s mouth gaps, “What? Why?”

She shrugs, “Because it means you’re not a good enough bender. So you have to rely on weapons to protect you.”

“…um I’m sorry, but you’re not a bad bender? You’re actually really good?”

“Don’t patronize me Sokka.”

“I’m not! Trust the guy who’s used to having benders do their magic bending things at them—” “It’s not magic bending things.” “—You’re one of the worst people to fight against. Other than Azula, you’ve really been the only one to have Aang on the ropes.”

“…oh well. Thanks and…sorry?”

Sokka laughs, “Well, it kept things interesting at least. I don’t think Aang would have gotten to the North Pole as fast as he did without you hounding his tail.”

She nods, “I guess.” Then she suddenly turns quiet.

“What’s wrong?”

“…I’m wondering if my crew got out okay. I’ve been so busy on the run, I haven’t even spared them a thought.”

“Oh…” Sokka rubs his hand over the rough stubble of his hair. “Well…the Ocean Spirit definitely sunk a lot of the ships, but…I saw some retreating. Not that many, but a few. They were probably on them. I think I recognized on of the guys there. Some dude with a weird whisker beard.”

Zuko’s eyes widen, “That might have been Jee.” She says with relief. “Maybe he was able to signal a retreat in time.”

“Hey if they managed to survive under your command, they can manage an Ocean Spirit.”

Zuko scoffs. “Yeah you’re probably right. I put them through hell and back. I’m surprised they stuck by me all this time.”

“It’s your charm.” Sokka teases. “You’re like an angry cat-owl. Temperamental but very cute. People just can’t resist.”

“Shut up!”

“See? Temperamental.”

Sokka doesn’t dodge the dumpling on time and it pings him straight between the eye.

They walk around the market afterwards, Zuko picks up a few things for her Uncle and making stilted small talk with the vendors who keep asking her about the ‘handsome young man’ she’s walking with. She tries to tell them that he’s just a friend (acquaintance? Former-enemy? Guy-who-frequently-stars-in-her-wet-dreams?)

They just give her a “okay dear” and send her on her way.

“You’re pretty popular huh?” Sokka asks, effortlessly taking the wares and putting it in the bag he’s carrying. After so many arguments, she doesn’t even protest.

“Uncle’s the popular one.” She says. “I just end up tagging along. His friendliness really ended up helping us a lot, now that I think about it.”

Sokka nods, “A good smile can make all the difference.” He says deeply, trying to mimic Iroh’s voice.

Zuko giggles and shoves his shoulders. “That was pretty good.”

“I’ve been working on my Iroh.” He says cheekily. “I’m hoping to use it against the others whenever he tries to do something stupid.”

“Hmm and how’s Katara?”

“Good. She really likes it here. Even did a spa day with Toph.” He answers. “…maybe you should —uh come see us? Er or well Katara at least.”

Zuko turns away. She doesn’t quite know what she would do if she ended up face-to-face with the Avatar. Some part of her might just leave it alone, too tired to keep chasing after him, but another part, the burning angry desperate part, might just try to capture him again.

And what would that bring her?

*Home*

*Honor*

*Love*

It’s so tempting, she can feel the poisonous tendrils grasping at her heart and soul. But she thinks about Uncle, happily serving tea to the refugees. She thinks about the small apartment that’s beginning to feel like a home, more than the palace ever did. She thinks about Sokka, who looks at her with such an earnest expression. The walks to her home, the meals, the sweet gestures.

She puts a hand over her heart. There’s two opposing sides inside her that’s desperately trying to tug at her in either direction. If she could just, scoop out all that anger and bitterness and leave everything else…would she be happier? She could be. She could be happy here, with Sokka, working with Master Hina. Living simply.

But…

She knows Sokka. He would never just settle down while there were things to be done. He’s an *important person*. He’s the Avatar’s right-hand man. He wants to end the war, bring peace, go on to do important amazing things.

He would never stay here for long. This is just…a temporary space. Another transit point He could leave her at any moment.

And it scares her that in just the few days she’s spent with him, he’s carved out a massive space in her heart just for him.

Suddenly there are warm hands on her shoulder, and she’s looking at his blue eyes.

“Hey, hey.” He says soothingly. “I’m sorry I didn’t mean to push.”

She shakes her head, “It’s fine. Maybe some other time?”

Sokka smiles, “Okay. We should get going. You wanted to show me how to make rice balls?”

Most of the rice balls turn out misshapen. The picken filling is not as sweet as it should be, but Sokka inhales it all like it’s his last meal.

“Ugh these are so good. I didn’t know you could cook.”

Zuko scoffs, “I can make maybe four dishes. And this is one of them. My mother and I would make these during Agni’s Day and pass them out to the orphans in the city.”

“…That’s so sweet. Your mom sounds amazing.”

Zuko smiles and focuses on rolling the rice into a nice oblong shape, “She was. I never met anyone kinder than her. She was always there for me, and…and she protected me.”

“…from your dad?”

Zuko nods mutely, and she can hear Sokka curse under his breath.

“You know every time I hear a little more about him, I hate him a lot more.” He says.

Zuko doesn’t answer, but the silence lays heavy on them.

“…sorry. I always feel like I’m always being a downer.”

Sokka shakes his head, “Nah, you’re also pretty funny.”

“Oh? Are you going to give your funny guy title to me?”

“Please, you’re a little funny, but *I’m* the master comedian here. One could say I’m a comedy bender.”

The princess rolls her eyes, “You know adding ‘bender’ to the end of everything doesn’t make you some magically amazing person at it, right?”

“Oh really? Because I just magically pull out sick jokes like you magically push out fire from your fingertips.”

“For the last time, that’s not magic.”

“Really? Explain it to me then.”

Zuko huffs, “Well…uncle said that fire bending is not from the muscles it’s from the breath. The breath feeds the energy in your stomach and that energy becomes fire.”

“…I’m sorry but that just sounded like a bunch of magic to me.”

She throws her arms up, “Ugh don’t ask me I’m not a chakra scholar. That’s just what I know from uncle.”

As if he was summoned, Iroh opens the door and sniffs the air.

“Ah are you making rice balls, niece?” he says, “I can’t wait.”

Zuko smiles and hands him a plate. “Sit down, Uncle. I’ve put on some tea and it should be ready soon.”

Iroh obliges and sinks into the cushion. “Aah it does this old man well to see his two favorite people here.”

“Zuko was trying to tell me that bending’s not magic, but she wasn’t doing a good job of it.” Sokka says.

“Ah well I guess it is a bit like magic.” Iroh says contemplatively.

“Uncle!” Zuko yells betrayed.

“Haha it’s true though niece, there’s some magical aspects of bending that scholars find difficult to explain.” He says. “But if I were to try and explain it…bending is the manifestation of your spiritual energy. That energy is determined and given to you by the parents you were born to, the natural energies of your environment, and the guardian spirits of that element. When benders move, we are moving the inner energies to align with the external energies and that creates a reaction. Fire benders are unique in that we carry our element internally. We take in oxygen to feed our chi which acts as the fuel, and the instant our energies align with the energy of the sun, that creates the heat that will spark the fire.”

“Huh…I guess when you put it that way, it sounds a lot less like magic.” Sokka admits. Zuko crosses her arms triumphantly.

“I told you.”

“You did not explain it half as well as he did. You said some weird thing about the stomach and the breath.”

“Well I *tried*. It’s been a while since Uncle gave me the breath spiel.”

The two end up bickering again as they continue molding the rice balls. Iroh just sighs in contentment and sips on his tea.

Hmm much better than his niece’s first brew.

&#95;&#42;&#95;&#42;&#95;

It goes like this for some time. During Zuko’s off days, Sokka trains with her. Most days after work she finds him waiting for her outside with a smile and an invitation to dinner or a request to walk her home. Once, he even comes in during her lunch break and asks Master Hina if she could be free for a moment. The seamstress smiles and eagerly shoos Zuko away, telling her to take the rest of the day off and to *enjoy herself*.

It was absolutely embarrassing when she came in the next day and the other workers were giving her salacious winks.

“Was *he* your inspiration.” Master Hina asks gesturing to the robe hanging on the mannequin near her workbench.

Zuko’s blush is telling enough.

Sometimes she goes to the palace, getting sketches and requests from the King Kuei. Somehow he always ends up inviting her for tea, and more often than not she finds herself conversing with the king. He’s astonished when he learns how well-versed she is in classic plays and literature as well as politics, agriculture, and cultural restoration.

“You’ve quite the broad education.” Kuei says amazed after their discussion on the thematic threads in Love Amongst the Dragons.

Zuko smiles, “My mother was an actress before she married my father. She loved to take us to plays and read to us when I was younger. I never really stopped after she…passed.”

He smiles sympathetically and then starts a new topic. This time about increasing agricultural yields.

&#95;&#42;&#95;&#42;&#95;

She notices that Sokka tends to pay for a lot of their food (most of their food), and she can’t just let him do that in good conscience. She wants to pay him back somehow, but she honestly doesn’t know where to start. After a day of wracking her brain she finally goes to her co-worker Mintra, who’s around her age (22 but close enough) and married.

“What is a good gift for…a friend who’s been very good to you?” Zuko asks haltingly.

Mintra grins, “Well&#126; my husband always says that the best gifts are practical and have a little touch of me put in it.”

Zuko squints. “What counts as that?”

“You do work in a seamstress shop.” Mintra says. “Master Hina wouldn’t mind you taking some yards of cloth and some thread to make something for your…*friend*.”

Turns out, Master Hina is more than okay with her borrowing a few supplies for a new tunic for Sokka.

She gets the local dyer to make it a deep blue, and fashions it in a Southern tribe style. She embroiders a special something on them and smiles at the work.

Mintra comes over and says, “Well…that’s definitely different.”

“It’s an important water tribe thing.” Zuko replies.

“Hmm strange people. I didn’t even know those could survive there.”

“Me either, but…I heard it from a reliable source so who am I to question it?”

“True.”

She gifts him the new tunic after swordbending training (and she’s only calling it swordbending because Sokka keeps *insisting* on it.)

He’s ecstatic as he runs his hands over the rich blue-dyed fabric. “This is amazing!” he exclaims. “You’re really good. No wonder the king keeps asking for you and—you even embroidered it!”

He runs his hands over the boomerang in the front. Then he turns it around and he pauses.

Zuko wrings her hands nervously as he squints at the animals. “Wow…frogs. I was not expecting that.”

*Oh shit.*

“S-Sokka, I can take it back. I can re-make it. I’m so sorry—”

“What no!” he says pulling the tunic close to his chest. “No way, no take-backsies. I’m just surprised you put frogs. It’s really amazing and so detailed. They look really familiar…”

“Wait…I thought frogs were super important for your people.” Zuko says slowly. Did she misheard the Avatar? To be fair she was trying to escape a high security stronghold. Oh Agni, she hopes they don’t mean something terrible. What a great time for a cultural misunderstanding. But Sokka only laughs, “Where did you hear that from?”

“Uh…the Avatar.”

“What? Aang? When did Aang tell you that? No offense but I don’t remember him telling you that, and I kinda doubt he’d be talking to you about water tribe culture.”

Zuko shifts, “It was when he was captured by Zhao.”

“…*What?!*” Sokka exclaims, jaw dropping.

“You…didn’t know?”

“NO! What the—when did *that* happen?!”

Zuko runs a hand through her hair, “Um…sometime after that big storm hit on the western front. He got caught catching frogs for you? He was really upset when they were escaping and kept saying that they were important to you guys. I always figured it was for some cultural ritual or special cuisine.”

“Oh Tui and La, I can’t believe he got caught catching those stupid frogs for us.” Sokka mutters putting head in his hands. “So wait…how did you know?”

Zuko shifts again. “I might have…rescued him. And helped him escape, by threatening to kill him. Not seriously of course, but it was the only way to get away from Zhao. And then I threw a fire ball at him after he rescued me.”

“Wait, I thought *you* were rescuing *him*?”

“I was. And then an arrow hit my head and then he rescued me.”

“An *arrow hit your head?!*”

“…yes? Doctor Kusuri checked it and nothing was wrong. Just a bump.”

Sokka groans and looks up at the sky. “I don’t even know what to be mad about first.”

“Um…sorry about the frogs?”

Sokka looks up at her, she’s looking at him with wide apologetic golden eyes, her hands clasped tightly together, and he honestly wants to kiss her. So so bad.

But he takes a deep breath instead and lays his hand on her shoulder. “It’s not about the frogs. Aang got those frogs to cure our sickness, so in a way they were pretty important. I’m glad you ended up helping him because I’m pretty sure Katara and I would have died without treatment.”

Zuko swallows hard. She can’t believe such a small stupid thing had such a huge impact. Like tiny embers lighting together to create a forest fire.

“Well, I’m glad they have a good meaning behind them.” she says.

Sokka smiles back, “Me too. Let’s go to the tea shop, I wanna show it off to your Uncle and his pai sho group.”

“Why?”

“Because the famous amazingly talented seamstress, Lin, gave me one of her masterpieces.” He says plainly.

Zuko blushes a bright red and tries to get him to stop saying such embarrassing things. It wasn’t a big deal.

He just brushes her off and tugs her out the door once he’s pulled the tunic over his head.

It looks good, the fit is perfect, and she’s happy that the rich blue compliments his dark skin nicely. There’s butterflies, fluttering in her stomach as she watches him strut around with his new clothes. Clothes that *she* made for him.

They’re just coming around to the tea shop when they both halt in confusion.

There’s a large crowd near the tea shop, and it’s not because of the tea. The people almost look like they’re rioting against the Earth Kingdom soldiers, who are trying to push them back and in the center of it is…*Uncle and King Kuei.*

Zuko feels her stomach drop. What happened? Did they find out his identity? Will he be okay?

She doesn’t think, she just runs to them, Sokka calling her name behind her.

“Uncle!” she shouts.

King Kuei looks up, “Uncle?” he says.

The guards part to let her through, and she throws herself between the king and her uncle. Every part of her body is trembling. This is the second time she’d be facing a monarch and defying his will.

“King Kuei, I won’t let you hurt him.” She says. The crowd roars behind her.

*“What did I tell you?!”*

*“Listen to Lin! She loves Mushi more than anything!”*

*“You have the wrong man, your majesty!”*

*“Release Mushi!”*

King Kuei looks to the crowds and then to Zuko who looks back at him defiantly.

“I think…there may have been a misunderstanding.” King Kuei says. He turns to Pao. “I will need your tea shop for a bit. There’s a private discussion I must have with these two.”

Pao nods quickly and the guards surround the tea shop, making sure no one comes close as the King goes in with Zuko and Iroh following after.

Zuko then remembers Sokka’s voice calling to her, and she turns back spotting him behind some guards who are holding a spear out towards him. She quickly walks over and asks that he be let in.

“Please, he’s a close friend.”

The guards look to each other and then let him pass. Sokka sighs in relief and grasps her shaking hands.

“Are you okay?”

“Y-yeah. I think…I think there’s just a misunderstanding.” She gulps. Sokka sees her trembling, and he pulls her close to his chest. Zuko is surprised at first, but she let’s herself sink into the comfort of Sokka’s embrace. The scent of metal and the ocean sooth her frayed nerves. She pulls away and nods to him gratefully before leading him to the tea shop.

Iroh and Kuei are already sitting across from each other at a table, two guards standing nearby.

Zuko sits close to her Uncle and Sokka takes the seat across from her. They four sit in silence before Kuei clears his throat.

“I think I may need to explain.” He says. “After you told me about your father, I had the guards track down some information about him. After listening to the people, they heard that your father, Mushi is actually living with you here in Ba Sing Se. So…I thought he might have been still abusing you. I came here personally to make sure he was arrested for his crimes.” He pauses and looks to Iroh. “Forgive me. It seems I must have been mistaken. You’re not the father who hurt her?”

“No, but I appreciate the effort you put in, King Kuei.” Iroh says serenely. “I am happy to say that my niece is far *far* away from the monster who hurt her.”

King Kuei nods, “I’m glad to hear that.” Then he turns to Zuko. “Please forgive my over-stepping. I just wanted to make sure you were living safely in my city. I did not mean to hurt your adoptive father.”

She nods, “It’s alright. I appreciate the effort. I’m glad the people here have a good king who wants to help them.”

King Kuei smiles wryly, “Well to be honest, seeing the lower ring and how the people are living, it seems I may have become too lax in my duties. I’m just glad you have people here for you.”

She bows, “Thank you, your majesty.”

He nods and then turns to Sokka, “I’m sorry the guards didn’t let you come in with your girlfriend. I didn’t know you were with Lin.”

Sokka turns a bright red but doesn’t correct him. “Um yeah no problem. Like you said. You didn’t know.”

Finally, he stands and bows to them. “I’ll make sure to send compensation for all the trouble I’ve caused you.” Kuei says.

“Oh you don’t need to.” Iroh tries to say, but the king is firm.

“No no, please. I should never have assumed so poorly of you, Mushi. And besides, your daughter is the finest seamstress I’ve ever seen. Her work never ceases to amaze me. I feel like I owe you a great favor for helping her here.” He turns to Sokka and grins, “You’re lucky to have a talented girlfriend who can make you such fine clothes.”

Sokka smiles sheepishly and turns to a red-faced Zuko. “Yeah she’s pretty amazing.” He says fondly.

Kuei nods and looks closely at the boy’s tunic, “Hmm frogs and boomerangs. What a wonderfully unique design.” He says to himself. He bows one more time to the family and then leaves with his entourage.

Once the guards go, the neighbors and loyal customers surround Iroh, asking him: ‘*Are you* *alright?’ ‘What was that all about?’ ‘Did Lin’s biological father actually scar her?’*

Iroh smiles at them placatingly and answers their questions while Sokka helps lead Zuko away from the crowd. They end up ducking in a nearby alley.

Zuko’s color seems to have returned and she’s breathing better now. They just stay there for a while, listening to the sounds of people moving about.

“Everyone’s pretty mad at my father huh?”

“That’s probably putting it lightly.” Sokka answers.

“…That was the first time I ever hear Uncle call him a *monster*.”

“It’s pretty accurate if you ask me.”

Zuko pauses, letting it sink in. “For the past three years, I always thought—I still sometimes think he did the right thing. That he’s an infallible judge and knows what’s best for our nation. That I…*deserve it* because I was so weak and a failure.” She says bitterly.

Sokka has to plant himself firmly on the wall to keep himself from just taking her in his arms and holding her close to him. But she needs to say this out loud first. Once she’s goes through this, he’ll scoop her up and make sure nothing ever hurts her again.

“Father always said, the best thing I could offer was my body, to carry the next powerful heir to the throne. I tried so hard to prove him wrong, that I could be *more* than just…a broodmare.” Tears pool in her eyes and she swipes them away. “After the Agni Kai, he said I wouldn’t even be good enough to marry off. I was too ugly and damaged. But…maybe he’s not as right or as infallible as I thought.”

There’s a pause.

“I still feel so torn and confused about all of this.” she admits. “I feel like I should hate him, but I also still want his approval and love. I just…don’t know anymore.” She slides down the wall and puts her head on her knees. Sokka goes down with her and carefully wraps his arms around her shoulders.

“…I can’t even imagine what you’re going through, but…I think it’s pretty normal to feel conflicted about these things. It’s hard to go against what we’ve been taught for so long. It takes a lot of effort to change and be someone better. But I’ll be here for you, no matter what.”

Zuko looks up, “You will?”

Sokka nods resolutely, “I promise. I’ll help you through this.”

Zuko nods and then looks seriously at him. She holds up her pinky.

“I promise I won’t try to capture the Avatar.” She says. Sokka is gaping at her, eyes wide.

“W-w-what? But isn’t he the only way you can get home? A-are you sure? I mean—I’m pretty happy about that but—”

“Just give me your pinky.” She interrupts. She needs to do this before she backs out. Sokka looks quizzically at her but he does reach his pinky out towards hers, and she hooks them together, just like Lu Ten and her used to do.

“I don’t know if it’s the right thing to do.” She admits. “I’m still so conflicted, but I think the best thing I can do for myself is…to slowly pull away from the idea of going home. If I take away the temptation of capturing the Avatar—of *Aang*, maybe…maybe I can find a better way for myself.”

Sokka’s looking at her in that weird way of his again. Like he’s seeing for the first time, and almost in awe.

“You’re amazing.” He says.

“I literally just told you that I’m a huge mess.”

“I know, but I think it’s amazing that you want to make a change, even if you’re a huge mess.”

Zuko scoffs, “Whatever.”

Sokka smiles in response and looks down at their twined pinkies. Her pale one contrasting sharply with his dark one. On an impulse, he brings them up and puts a gentle kiss over her pale digit. Zuko turns a bright red, and when he let’s them go, she holds the pinky close to her heart. It’s endearingly cute.

“W-what was that for?” she whispers.

Sokka shrugs, “You have your way of making a promise and I have mine.” Is the answer.

“You’re so weird.”

“Says the sixteen-year-old who still makes pinky promises.”

Zuko laughs softly, “It’s one of the first things, Lu Ten taught me.”

“He sounds like a fun guy.”

“He was.”

They stay in their little alley for some time before Sokka and Zuko head back to her apartment. Iroh is already there, ready with cups of tea and a bubbling pot of stew. Sokka ends up staying the night. When Iroh goes to bed, Zuko and Sokka are up on the roof, looking at the stars and a waning moon.

Just because Sokka wants to spend more time with Zuko doesn’t mean he always gets to. He still had a job to do, and Aang’s been getting more and more despondent as the days go by. He’s trying to make Appa portraits while Aang and Katara go off to get more paper flyers.

Toph is laying on her back relaxing beside him.

“So Snoozles, how’s your girlfriend?” she asks.

Sokka’s brush veers wildly, ruining Appa’s arrow. “Wh-what the?! I d-don’t she’s not my *girlfriend*!” he sputters.

“…huh you’re not lying.” Toph says.

“Of course not!”

“But you *wish* she was your girlfriend.” She concludes with a shit-eating grin.
