Sokka doesn’t even try to deny it. Toph would hear his pounding heart and know in an instant. Instead he focuses on the flyer.

“Is she that girl that was with Uncle?”

“Yeah, Zuko. But she’s going under Lin here.”

“Hmm is that why you haven’t told Sugarqueen and Twinkletoes?”

“…yeah. I promised her that I wouldn’t tell them.”

“Is she planning on capturing Aang?” Toph asks bluntly.

“No. No she’s just living her life with her Uncle. She’s actually a seamstress.”

“I know.” Toph says. “I followed your footsteps a couple times, and you always go to that shop.”

“Oh. Makes sense…are you going to tell the others?”

Toph shakes her head, “Nah, if you say she’s cool. She’s cool.”

Sokka smiles and ruffles her hair, which is slapped away almost instantly and then he gets sucker punched in the arm.

Why are all the women in his life so violent?

“I wanna meet her though.” Toph says. “She’s always making your heartbeat fast, and I just wanna size her up.”

“Pft okay. Maybe I’ll take you to her after the flyers are put up.”

Zuko working back at the tea house on her off day. Since Sokka’s busy they didn’t get to train that morning, but at least she gets to spend time with her Uncle working through the shift. Right now he’s talking to two officials from the Earth King, and by the grin on his face it’s probably good news.

She goes out to sweep the porch when a paper falls from the sky. She catches it almost instinctively, and her eyes widen.

*The bison is missing.*

That’s why Sokka’s here. She always thought it was to get an audience with the king, but there was another motive. Something churns in her gut, that cloying voice that tells her she could use this. But then she looks down at her pinky. The phantom kiss lingering on it.

She can’t use the bison to capture the Avatar—Aang.

But maybe she can still help him find it.

While Uncle gushes about the new tea house the king has deeded him, Zuko is plotting and planning.

“What do you think, Zuko?” Iroh asks.

“Hmm okay.” she says absentmindedly.

“…you really like the name ‘The Tea Weavil’?” he says skeptically.

That jolts her out of her thoughts. “What? That’s stupid.”

“You just said you liked it.”

“I didn’t hear it properly. I thought you said something else.” She lies.

Iroh narrows his eyes. “Niece, what is going on?”

“…nothing Uncle. I’m just happy for you.”

As she leaves, she knows he isn’t convinced.

&#95;&#42;&#95;&#42;&#95;

Of all the people he ran into. It had to be *him.*

Sokka feels rage bubbling up in his stomach. He surprises them by punching Jet in the face.

“The fuck?!” Jet shouts. “I said I was sorry to your sister!”

“That’s not for my sister asshole, that’s for trying to rat out Lin and her Uncle.” And for having terrible sex with Lin, but he keeps that to himself.

“Lin?” Jet says skeptically. “What are you talking about? I haven’t seen Lin since she and I parted at the ferry.”

“Do you think I’m stupid?” Sokka shouts. “You can’t lie to me! Lin told me you attacked her uncle, and she would never lie about that.”

Jet narrows his eyes, “Sokka, I don’t know what you’re talking about. Lin was the one who didn’t want to join the Freedom Fighters. I’m not going to fight someone over that. Even though she was a really good la—”

“Finish that sentence and I’ll smash your face in.” Sokka growls.

Jet smirks. “Oooh touch-y much? She your girlfriend now? Does she get on her knees for you too?”

*Oh that’s it.*

Sokka brings his fists back to punch him, but Katara and Aang hold him back. “Sokka calm down!” Katara shouts. “What has gotten into you?”

“It’s more like what *hasn’t* he gotten into.” Jet mocks.

“I’m going to *shove my boomerang up your fucking—!”*

“Sokka!”

Before he can complete his promise, Smellerbee and Longshot interrupt the fight and tell them that Jet’s been missing for weeks now.

They make an uneasy truce, and Sokka has to keep his fists by his side or else he might just hurt the teen.

&#95;&#42;&#95;&#42;&#95;

It’s surprisingly easy to get the drop on a Dai Li agent and get a map of their base. Zuko sneaks in under the cover of night into Lake Laogai. The hallways are a sickly green, and practically a maze. She would have gotten lost if it weren’t for the map. Distantly she thinks she can hear the sounds of fighting, but she ignores it, and continues moving forward.

She breaks down the door, and stares into the eyes of the chained bison. The creature roars and throws itself against the chains, trying to knock Zuko down. It gets especially agitated when she pulls out her dao. She carefully stalks forward, eyeing the chains and finding the right angle to cut them.

“Zuko what are you doing?!”

She turns and finds Iroh standing at the doorway. His normally kind eyes are filled with anger, and it sends a chill down her spine.

“I’m releasing the bison, Uncle.” She says.

“And then what will you do? You want to store him in our tea house? I don’t think we have enough room for him in our apartment!” Iroh says sternly.

*What?*

“You never think these things through, Zuko! Do you think you’ll be able to control the bison to capture the avatar?”

*…Oh. He thinks…*

She sighs, “Uncle.”

But Iroh’s off the handle now. “You need to stop chasing after a destiny someone has forced on you and think about what you want! It’s time you look inside and find out who *you are* and who you *want to be—*”

“UNCLE!” She yells. That stops Iroh in his tracks. She just huffs and goes over to the bison and slices the chains away. She shoos the bison away and tells her Uncle to stay back. Then she throws a powerful blast of flames up into the ceiling and blasts a hole through it. The bison looks at her and then up at the hole.

“Go.” she says. “Aang is looking for you.”

The bison’s eyes are wide and bore into her with a strangely deep comprehension. It licks her and coats her with its nasty saliva.

“Ugh!” she groans, but before she can do anything, the bison has taken off.

Iroh looks at her with misty eyes, and Zuko really can’t take another emotional breakdown right now. So she jerks her head towards the exit. “Let’s go before we’re caught.”

Once they’re out, Iroh smiles at her. “I’m proud of you, niece.”

Zuko shrugs and then looks at her mask. The white fangs gleaming in the sunlight. She always felt like the Blue Spirit was her way to secretly good things. To be more like her mother while still trying to prove herself to her father.

Maybe she doesn’t need that anymore. Maybe she can do good things like her mother always wanted her to, without having to hide herself.

She drops the masks and watches it float down into the dark lake.

As they walk home, she feels more and more dizzy. Her body feels like it’s *burning*. She leans against the doorway, while her uncle starts puttering in the kitchen. His voice is muffled and it feels like she’s underwater.

Iroh feels a giddy happiness fill him as he takes out his favorite tea pot. His dear niece had done such a good thing. She’s really starting to go on the path of good, and he couldn’t be prouder. He turns to ask her what tea she wanted, but his heart stops.

She’s pale and flushed. She mutters a “I don’t feel so good—” before her eyes roll, and she’s falling to the floor.

*Oh no.*

&#95;&#42;&#95;&#42;&#95;

Sokka tries to swallow the scream crawling out of his throat as Appa dodges the flying boulders coming straight at them. Thank the spirits that they’re traveling with an all-powerful bender because he would have died a hundred times over.

They end up storming the Earth King’s palace and Kuei looks shocked to see Sokka among them.

“You!” he says. “You’re Lin’s boyfriend. What in the world are you doing here?”

Katara and Aang are giving him strange looks, but Sokka uses this opening to tell the king before Long Feng can shut him down.

“Your Majesty, I’m Sokka and this is the Avatar.” He says gesturing to Aang. Then he points to Long Feng. “King Kuei, Long Feng has been lying to you all this time. He deliberately stops information about the war from reaching you so that he can have control of the city.”

The king looks shocked and turns to his advisor. Long Feng scoffs at the idea and tells him that he’s lying, but Kuei looks at Sokka appraisingly. He’ll take that.

“Your Majesty, Lin got her scar because of the war.” Sokka says. “Her father burned her for speaking out against an immoral battle strategy he wanted to use for the war. She didn’t tell you this because she would have been imprisoned by Long Feng and the Dai Li.”

Kuei sucks in a breath and stands. He turns to Long Feng, “Is this true? Have you really been lying to me all this time? Is this why you don’t want me to pass those restoration bills for the lower ring?”

Long Feng is sweating now, and it’s enough for Kuei to bring down the guards on him. Then he turns to Sokka. “I will need to see proof of this.”

Sokka smiles and leads him to the Dai Li hide out. It’s unfortunately gone, but Sokka knows of another place. He takes Kuei to the outer wall and shows him the drill. The earth king is understandably upset that his world has been pulled under him like rug. He stands there looking at the drill morosely.

“I wish this war really was a lie.” He says. “Then maybe I wouldn’t have to face the fact that I’ve been so ignorant for the past twenty-two years.

Sokka pats his shoulder sympathetically. “Well maybe you can make up for it by helping us with the invasion.”

Kuei smiles weakly. “Of course. I’ll gladly mobilize the Earth Kingdom forces and offer them to you for the invasion. I’ll call a meeting of the generals immediately.”

The water tribesman feels a sigh of relief. He can’t believe how lucky they got. He needs to thank Lin for giving him a rapport with the king; it probably wouldn’t have gone as smoothly if she hadn’t known him.

“So…Lin…” Kuei starts. “She’s Fire Nation right?”

Sokka winces. “Yeah, but she’s good. She wouldn’t do anything.”

Kuei smiles, “I know. She and I have tea sometimes. We talk about literature and plays.”

“Oh spirits, did she already give you that spiel on the misogynistic themes in *A Stranger in a* *Strange Land?*”

“No but that sounds like something she’d do.” Kuei chuckles.

Sokka smiles back, “I know you probably went along with us so easily because of Lin, but…thank you. For giving us a chance.”

The king nods. “Of course. But I also felt that Lin would never date a dishonorable man.”

Sokka turns away blushing. “Right.”

Kuei laughs and pats his shoulder. “I envy that love you two have.” He says before Aang calls him over.

His stomach flutters in that familiar way whenever someone refers to Lin as his girlfriend, but when Kuei referred it to love, it felt like his heart was exploding.

*I need to see her soon.*

### End Notes

#### Chapter End Notes

**Lu Ten:** I'm so proud of her.

**Yue:** Are you *crying*?

**Lu Ten:** Shut up! Let me have my tearbending moment!

**Yue:** All you Fire Royals are so dramatic.

&#45;-

I hope Zuko's development makes sense. I used the King Kuei moment as a catalyst for her to see how much people don't like her father, and I hope I captured the internal struggle of going against everything she believes in.

Thank you for reading!

## Chapter 10

### Notes

#### Chapter Notes

**Warning:** Violence

### Text

---

First, the Earth King agrees to join the invasion, then Toph’s parents want to reconcile, and now Dad’s waiting for them by Chameleon Bay! It seriously can’t get any better for Sokka.

Katara insists that Sokka should go see their father first, and Aang is happy to give him a ride there. He needs to stop by the Eastern Air temple anyway to train with this Guru Patik.

“Okay but before we go, I need to see someone first and tell them that I’m leaving for a bit.” Sokka says.

The younger kids all smile at each other and smirk. “Is it your mysterious girlfriend, Lin?” Katara teases.

Sokka blushes, “She’s not my girlfriend.” He mutters.

“But he *wishes* she was.” Toph pipes in.

“Awww Sokka that’s so sweet.” Aang coos. “I’m rooting for you buddy!”

“You guys are the worst.”

“Bring her over next time!” Aang suggests. “I really wanna meet her. Especially since the Earth King seems to like her.”

“Uh…I’ll ask. She’s a little reluctant to meet you. Because you’re the Avatar and all that.” Not technically a lie so Toph can’t seem to tell the difference.

Aang’s face falls. “Aw, well tell her not to be nervous. I’m just a chill normal-ish 12-year-old.”

“With the power of a thousand master benders behind you.”

“Po-*tay*-to, po-*ta*-to.”

&#95;&#42;&#95;&#42;&#95;

Sokka gets to the apartment in record time. He eagerly knocks on the door and is about to tell Iroh the good news, but when Iroh opens the door, he feels his heart drop.

Iroh looks ragged. His eyes have bags, and his skin looks thin.

“U-Uncle wh-what happened?”

Iroh sighs and lets him in. Sokka sees Zuko, flushed and panting under a blanket. Her inky hair matted to her forehead. Sokka immediately kneels down and carefully touches the heated skin. It’s so hot. Is it normally that hot? Even when some of the kids were plagued with winter fever, they never got that hot.

“Will she be okay?” he asks.

Iroh nods, “She will be fine. This is a sickness born of the war inside her. It’s been a long time coming.”

Sokka chalks it up to a bender thing, but he still can’t make the lump in his throat go down as he watches her mutter and moan underneath the sheets. He rewets the cloth with cool water and pats her forehead.

“You should get some rest, Uncle.” Sokka says. “I’ll stay here with her.”

“Sokka, you don’t have to—”

“I *want* to.” He insists. “I can’t just leave her like this.”

“…very well.” Iroh replies. “But let me know if anything changes.”

He doesn’t know how long he stays there, holding her hand and watching over her, but suddenly there are hard knocks at the door. Sokka jumps and goes over. Two soldiers from the king’s army greet him.

“The Avatar wants to leave soon.”

*Oh shit. He had completely forgotten.*

“Please tell the Avatar that I can’t go with him. He should bring Katara and I’ll stay here.” Sokka says. The guards nod and leave. Iroh comes out of Zuko’s room and looks at him.

“Sokka, if you must go, I’m sure Zuko would understand. She knows how important duty and your work with the Avatar.”

He shakes his head, “No, it’s just…my dad called us over to Chameleon Bay to meet up. Katara can tell him all the things I can.”

Iroh puts a hand on his shoulder, “Family is important too, Sokka.”

“I know. Trust me if she wasn’t sick in bed like this, I would be there in a heartbeat, but…” He looks to Zuko, weak and pale. Delirious with fever. He can’t just leave her like this. He’d never be able to sleep without knowing that she’s okay.

Iroh seems to understand and smiles, “I’m glad she has you watching over her.”

Sokka smiles wanly. “I’m glad she made a truce with us during the Northern Water Tribe Siege. I don’t think we would have gotten along as well if we never properly talked there.”

“The smallest of embers can light the largest flame.” Iroh replies sagely. “I’m going to the market to get something for a light broth for her. If something happens and you need to go, just leave a note.”

Sokka nods. “I will. But you don’t have to worry. I’ll stay right here.”

&#95;&#42;&#95;&#42;&#95;

It feels like she’s been dreaming forever. She mostly sees her mother and Lu Ten. Sometimes they’re in the garden. Sometimes they’re on Ember Island. Most are memories, but some feel like *wishes* and *desires*.

When she dreams of Lu Ten, she sees him blue and translucent. Following behind her every step. On the worst days he’d hold her close and sing to her. During the Siege, she sees him whisper to her, urging her to check Zhao’s cabin. She sees him smiling proudly as she asks for the Avatar’s assistance.

Sometimes the images move like a play, and other times they’re just like paintings. A moment captured in time. He’s looking surly as she talks to Jet. He’s standing beside Yue and laughing as they watch Jet fall. He’s looking sour when she and Sokka are having dinner. But the look turns less sour and more into disbelief before going into amused resignation.

When she’s not dreaming of Lu Ten or her mother, she dreams of her banishment and Agni Kai. The heat of the flames, the searing pain in her scar. Sometimes she thinks she cries out, but then there are warm comforting hands on her, cooling her down, holding her hand. She dreams of the blue sea and the chilly winds of the South Pole.

In her dreams she’s laughing and smiling. Face unblemished and hair braided and tumbling down her back. Sokka is there with her. Older more rugged and filled out. He’d smile as he came back home from hunting and sweep her into his arms. Spinning her around as he laughs.

The colors would morph. They’re in the palace. He’s decked out in red and smiling brightly at her. She says something, and he’s close to tears. He bends his head down and kisses nearly imperceptible swell in her belly.

The dream fades and she’s on the throne. A blue dragon coiling on her right and a red on her left. They fight and contradict the other. Zuko doesn’t know who to listen do.

*You want to be strong*

***Of*** ***course***

*You want to be kind and good*

***I want that too***

*Just give in. It’s so much easier to let yourself fall.*

*No! Zuko you must never give in! Remember the meaning of true inner strength!*

The blue dragon is laughing and she’s falling into the abyss.

She wakes up screaming, and there are hands on her, a soft voice calming her down.

“Shhh shhh it’s okay. It’s okay. You were just dreaming. Lay back down. You’re fever’s still really high.”

Zuko shakes her head. “No no don’t want to, it’s scary.” She mumbles.

“I know. I know, but I’m right here.” A strong hand is gripping hers tightly and another is combing through her hair. She tries to focus her eyes but only sees blue. She’s slipping again.

“Promise you’ll stay?” she pleads.

A pinky hooks into hers. “I promise.”

This time she only dreams of home, of childish laughter, and the soft petals of the wisteria-willow trees fluttering by as two small figures run around the gardens.

The next time she wakes, she feels better. Lighter than ever before. She groans as the sunlight hits her face, and she hears someone fumbling beside her.

“Zuko!” Sokka shouts. She winces at the volume and he backs away, contrite.

“Sorry.” he says quieter, and then hands her some clean water, “Here, you should drink.”

She takes the ladle and takes a deep drink, some of the liquid dribbling down her chin. “H-how long have I been asleep?” she rasps.

“It’s the third day now.” Sokka says. “You were really sick, I—I was so scared. I didn’t know what would happen.”

Zuko nods. “Well I’m okay now.” she says. “I feel…lighter.”

Sokka smiles, “I’m glad. You definitely look better and thank you.”

“…for what?”

“For saving Appa.”

Zuko flushes at that. “Oh. Yeah. That.”

Sokka is quiet for a moment. “Why didn’t you tell me?”

“…I—I didn’t want you to think that I was going to use the bison against the Ava—erm Aang. And…I guess I just needed to know that—that I could do something good for *me* and not because you were there beside me.”

A pause.

And then Sokka sighs, “Well I’m glad it turned out okay, but next time just…please let me know if you’re going out to do something dangerously stupid.”

“Okay, but I do that a lot.”

“I know.”

Her stomach gurgles, and Sokka laughs. “I think there’s still some leftover jook in the pot. I’ll go grab some.”

“Sokka you don’t have to…”

“Nope.” He says. “You literally just recovered from a spirit-y, bend-y sickness. You’re staying put.”

Zuko sighs and stays put; she pretends that her sickness made her too tired to argue.

Iroh is relieved when he finds her sitting up and eating little bites of jook. It’s the first time in two days since Sokka’s face has lit up, and he’s looking at her with a fond expression. Iroh declares that he’ll make his special curry, but unfortunately, Sokka can’t stay long. He’s been relying on the palace messengers to take his notes and maps of the invasion to the king and generals. He needs to get back soon so they can have a proper discussion and maybe he’ll have time to catch up with Suki and the other Kyoshi warriors.

He reluctantly waves goodbye and makes Zuko promise to take it easy, and then he’s off to the palace.

The invasion meeting goes a lot more smoothly than he anticipated. All the generals are on board and willing to move some of their battalions to Chameleon Bay where the Water Tribe forces will be waiting. Those forces towards the west will meet up with them at Full Moon Bay.

Sokka whistles to himself as he heads back to his apartment. He wishes he could go back to Zuko’s place, but the meeting runs late and none of the trains are running. Plus, he has another early meeting tomorrow. He walks down the familiar shops, and nearly misses it if it weren’t for the shine of the gem by the firelight. His eyes are immediately drawn to the stall.

It’s a hair clip, shaped like a fire-lily. The metal is painted a shiny red and topaz gems are set in the center. He immediately goes to the vendor and asks, “How much for the hairclip?”

The small package is resting safely in his pocket, and he’s happily making his way back home. He’ll give it to her before he leaves.

&#95;&#42;&#95;&#42;&#95;

“Zuko are you sure you’re alright to start working?” Iroh says helplessly. “I’d really prefer it if you stayed home to rest a bit more.”

The princess shakes her head, “No I’m okay Uncle. I promise to take it easy. Besides I don’t want to miss your big opening day.” She replies. “I’m so happy for you.”

Iroh beams. “Thank you, Zuko. It’s truly a dream come true.” Then he hugs her tightly and smiles. The first day goes off without a hitch. The customers are humming appreciatively as they taste the divine tea. They even get an invitation from the king to serve them tea.

“This is an amazing opportunity.” Iroh says. “I must choose the right blends to serve King Kuei. He’s the reason why I have the shop in the first place.”

Zuko smiles happily as she watches her uncle rifle through the various tea blends he’s made. She wants this peace to continue forever.

&#95;&#42;&#95;&#42;&#95;

Sokka is about to head out of the palace to Iroh’s new tea shop when he bumps into someone.

“Oh sorry there!” Sokka says, and then looks down to see who it was. “Hey, you’re one of the Kyoshi warriors!” he exclaims happily.

The warrior smiles. “Yes I am! Good to see you again.”

A shiver goes down his spine. He knows that voice. Something niggles in the back of his mind and he takes a closer look at her.

Grey eyes.

Light brown hair.

Round face.

She’s one of *Azula’s*.

Why is one of Azula’s—

*Oh shit.*

He plasters a smile, “Hey you wouldn’t happen to know where Suki is would you?”

“Suki?” she asks tilting her head. “Umm I’m not sure I know her. There’s a lot of us y’know.”

That’s definitely not a Kyoshi warrior.

He nods tightly, “Right right.” He needs to leave *now* and warn the Earth King.

He turns around and comes face-to-face with Azula.

*Fuck.*

“My my. Look what we have here.” Azula drawls. “A little mouse in an armadillo-lion’s den.” She snaps her fingers, and Dai Li descend from the ceiling. They bind him tightly with chains and push him down. Azula looms over him imperiously.

“Put him in the cavern, men.” She orders. “It’s time to set the trap for the Avatar.

*Double-fuck*.

&#95;&#42;&#95;&#42;&#95;

Zuko’s had a strange niggling feeling all morning. Like something bad is going to happen. She tires to ignore it, but the closer they get to the palace the more that feeling tickled her. She tries to look around and find the source, but nothing is there.

*I’m just being paranoid.* She thinks as she climbs up the steps with her Uncle. They’re taken to a room and sit there for a long time.

*Okay apprehensive feelings may have had a point*.

She stands and heads to the door. Locked.

Uncle is by her side and looks weary. “It seems we’ve been had.” He says sadly. “I can’t believe I wasted good tea leaves for this.”

“Really Uncle? *That’s* what you’re worried about? We’re probably about to die.”

“Bah, we’re always about to die.” He replies nonchalantly, “But nothing’s killed us yet.”

“That gives me *so* much confidence.” She grumbles before shooting a blast of fire and blasting the door away. They run, and they’re nearly at the end of the hall when Da Li fall from the ceiling, surrounding them.

“Leaving so soon, Zuzu?”

*Of fucking course.*

Zuko turns and faces her sister. “Azula. I should have known you’d show up again. I thought my life was missing that little touch of homicidal urges.”

“Haha, so funny dear Zuzu.” She retorts. “I see that living as a dirty peasant hasn’t dulled your tongue.”

“I see that being away from Father hasn’t made you any less of a bitch.”

Azula narrows her eyes, “Always so crude, sister dear. I guess sucking on peasant dick would do that to you.”

Zuko smirks. “Don’t discount peasants, Azula. They’re a lot more fun behind closed doors.” And she sends a powerful wave of fire at the Dai Li agents. Iroh shoots through the wall with lightening and they jump onto the topiaries. While Iroh’s about to head towards the exit, Zuko *turns back*.

“Zuko! We need to get out of here!” He yells.

“You go, I need to get to make sure Sokka and King Kuei are alright.” She shouts back, already sprinting through path and leaping steadily to the roof.

Iroh slaps his forehead. He loves his dear girl with all his heart, but she can be *so reckless.* There’s only one thing he can do for now. He needs to get some back-up.

Zuko sneaks around the guards and is heading to the Earth King’s chambers when daggers fly towards her. They miss her by a hairs-breath, and she turns.

Mai and Ty Lee.

“Oooh Zuko! It’s so good to see you!” Ty Lee claps her hands delightedly. “And you look good in those robes. Did you make them yourself?”

“Ty Lee. I thought you were at the circus?” She says staying completely still. Those daggers were only to get her attention. Mai can aim as well as a Yuyan archer, she would have Zuko pinned to the pillar like a butterfly in a case.

“Oh I was.” Ty Lee answers. “But well…Azula asked me to help.”

“Is ‘ask’ another way of saying she set something near you on fire?” Zuko quips.

Mai grins sharply at this while Ty Lee grimaces. “You know how your sister is.”

“Don’t I ever. How did she find me?”

This time Mai answers, “She saw the Earth King’s robes. The ones you made for him, and she recognized the work and the signature. I think you can guess the rest.”

“See this is why I hate being popular.” Zuko sighs. “So…are we going to duke it out like old times?”

Ty Lee smiles wistfully, “I do miss sparring with you, Zuko. You move like no one else, and I have to say, your aura is looking particularly nice nowadays.”

“Thank you, Ty Lee. Since Azula’s not here I’m assuming we’re doing two-on-one?”

“Yup.”

“Can I ask just one question before we go?” Zuko asks.

“Ask away.” Mai answers.

“Did you happen to capture a water tribe teen?”

“OOoh the cute one? Yes, we just did.” Ty Lee smirks slyly. “Ah your aura changed there. Do you *like* him? Is that why you came back? That’s so sweet. I’m sorry I flirted with him; I didn’t know you were interested. I’ll back off.”

“You always had the weirdest taste in men.” Mai adds. “He’s in the catacombs below the palace.”

“That’s all I needed to hear.” Zuko says and throws fire at the two girls.

Ty Lee and Mai avoid it easily and rush her. She’s dodging knives and keeping Ty Lee’s dangerous fists at bay while also trying to move far enough away from them. They end up in a familiar rhythm as the three girls fight.

*Dodge-dodge-punck-block*

*Punch-block-kick-kick*

“That was a nice flip-kick.” Ty Lee compliments as she bends backwards to avoid the fire blast.

Zuko side-steps a dagger and twists around Ty Lee to avoid her punch. “You did a cool wall run and summersault. Teach it to me sometime?”

“Only if you show me how you did that high-kick jump going into a spin.”

Mai sighs and throws her daggers at Zuko. “You two talk way too much during fights.”

“You love it Mai.” Ty Lee teases throwing a swift punch and then sweeping her feet to unbalance Zuko.

Zuko jumps away in time but can’t avoid the dagger that snags her sleeve and pins her to the column.

“Nice teamwork.” Zuko says, and then puts up a huge wall of flames between them. Ty Lee and Mai have to step back at the intensity of the heat, by the time the wall is gone, Zuko is already further down the hallway.

Mai groans, “Ugh more running. She should have just done that the first time.”

“I think she misses us.” Ty Lee remarks with a grin.

They go after her.

Zuko’s nearly to the chambers when Azula comes out of nowhere. Blue flames singing the tips of her hair. Typical, her sister always loved burning her hair.

“Zuzu, how nice to see you so soon.” Azula says smirking. “Why did you come back?”

Zuko goes into a fighting stance, “To challenge you to an Agni Kai.” She lies.

Azula rolls her eyes, “You’re *so* dramatic sister.”

“Runs in the family.” Zuko quips. “Just look at Father, that goatee is *so dramatic*.” And she sends a fireball at her younger sister. Azula vanishes it with a flick of her wrist, of course she does.

And the Dai Li send their hands at her, locking her to the floor. Azula looms over her with a triumphant smile.

“Using other people in an Agni Kai is cheating.” Zuko says.

Azula scoffs, “I never accepted your stupid Agni Kai. So I can use whoever I want to deal with you.”

Ty Lee and Mai finally catch up and glance at Zuko with worried looks.

Azula turns to the two girls, “I can’t believe you two couldn’t catch Zuzu. She’s more pathetic than normal today.” she says.

Ty Lee shrugs and smiles tightly. “We thought you might want to do the honors.”

“Hmm I guess you’re not wrong. I *do* enjoy beating my elder sister. It’s like kicking a rambunctious puppy.” Azula muses.

“You’re such a psycho.” Zuko mumbles.

“I prefer, ruthless and determined.” Azula replies checking her nails. “Take her to the prisoner cell. I have a coup arrange.”

The Dai Li grab her roughly, and she’s tossed in a tunnel going deep under the palace. She lands in a heap and groans. Someone familiar is immediately on her, helping her up and asking if she’s okay.

“Ugh Sokka?” she asks blinking her eyes.

“Yeah. What happened? Why are you down here?”

“My sister happened.” She replies. “I’m guessing same thing happened to you?”

“Yeah, I think she’s going to try to catch the Avatar.”

Zuko winces. “I think it’s less capturing the Avatar and more…killing or seriously maiming the Avatar.”

“She’s a fourteen-year-old girl.”

“She pushed me off a cliff at six.”

“…your family is *so* messed up.”

Zuko barks a harsh laugh, “Yeah tell me about it.”

“…So any plans to get out?” he asks looking at the walls.

The princess shakes her head, “Maybe if I could shoot lightening, but I’ve never been able to separate the energies well enough. Not like Azula.”

Sokka turns to her with wide eyes. “Excuse me. Your sister can shoot *lightening*?”

“Yeah I thought you knew?” she says. “I was sure she would have shot lightening at you before now. She’s really proud of it. The youngest member to ever master coldfire.”

“You sound suspiciously proud of her.” Sokka says with narrowed eyes.

Zuko shrugs. “She may have tried to seriously hurt and torture me, but she’s still my sister. I can feel a little proud of her accomplishments. At least, the non-maiming and killing kind.”

Sokka sighs and shakes his head. He scoots closer to the girl and leans his back against hers. “You’re seriously amazing you know that?”

“…you’ve already said that.”

“I’ll say it a million more times.”

Zuko lets herself smile at that one. “What about you? Any plans to get us out of here?”

He grimaces. “Nothing really comes to mind. The only plan I have requires a lot of explosions.”

“Well…maybe I can help you with that.” Zuko offers and stands. Sokka looks at her quizzically.

She takes a deep breath and focuses on the energies. Static tingles at her fingertips as she moves through the kata, the positive and negative separating, but her hold on them weaken, and then come crashing together in an explosive *BOOM!*

Sokka yelps and flings himself aside as Zuko is thrown back. She groans and then looks at him.

“Is that good?” she asks.

The boy is gaping at her, “No. No that was *very much* not good. Are you *okay*?”

Instead of answering she groans and lies back down. “I can’t even make a decent explosion. How pathetic.”

Sokka doesn’t know why he likes this girl so much. She’s so stupid sometimes. It’s probably all those hits to the head. They really shouldn’t have hit her so much. It’s addled the little sense she has in there.

He sits beside her and puts his hand over her eyes. “You’re not pathetic. I’m not going to use your explosions because you could *seriously* get hurt.”

“But you would escape.”

“But you would be *hurt*.” He tries to emphasis.

Zuko is suspiciously silent. “…I’m not getting your meaning here.”

Figures. He really *really* wants to throttle Ozai.

“Let’s just say that it’s not worth escaping if you’re going to get seriously hurt…or killed.”

Zuko pulls the hand away from her eyes and glares at him. “What are you talking about? Of course it is. You’re the Avatar’s right-hand man. It shouldn’t matter what happens to me, you’re more important.”

Is it possible to want to kiss someone *and* slap them with common sense? He’s starting to get used to this feeling, and it’s probably not a good thing.

He sighs and lays down across from her. “Okay, think about it this way: if we were two peasants in this mess and you had the chance to escape, but it would leave me really hurt. Would you still go through with it?”

Zuko looks horrified. “No! Of course not.”

“And why?”

She shifts. “Because you’d be hurt. I don’t want to you to get hurt if it means that I’ll escape.”

“That’s actually really sweet.” Sokka grins. “Now maybe flip that around and think about how I would feel?”

“You would feel…not good about it?”

“A little more than not good but yes.”

She huffs, “Okay but we’re not peasants! You’re an important person, and you *need* to get out of here.”

The water tribe teen just sighs and lays his head on the ground. Baby steps. Really *tiny* baby steps. He just changes the topic.

“So was that you trying to make lightening?” he asks.

Zuko nods. “Yeah but Uncle says that you need a peaceful mind to properly separate the energies. That’s why mine keep…exploding.”

Sokka taps his chin. “Interesting…” he muses. There’s an inkling of a thought coming to his mind. “What are you thinking about when you’re making lightening?”

Zuko shrugs. “Trying to calm my mind.”

“…alright I think I know your problem.” He says. He sits up, motioning for her to do the same. “What’s something that makes you feel calm?” he asks.

The princess thinks on this. Something that calms her…

*The rhythm of their swords when they spar.*

*The steady weave of the thread in the clothe.*

*The silence they share as they look into the stars.*

*Uncle and Sokka playing pai sho as she sews.*

*Quiet moments as they walk besides each other.*

Zuko blushes, “Um…I guess sewing. That makes me feel calm.”

“Should have expected that.” Sokka says. “So just…think about that while you’re doing the lightening thing.”

“That’s just a single moment though. How would that help?” She argues.

“You don’t need to be calm all the time.” Sokka says. “Just in that moment that you’re bending. Uncle said it was a state of mind, right? So just…ease yourself into that mindset with that memory helping you. When you’re hunting, you need something similar so you can tell when to strike. My dad taught me how to get into that calm mindset, and I think making lightening is the same.”

Zuko looks skeptical, but she nods. “Okay.” she says and takes a deep breath.

She lets herself think about the motion of the needle through the clothe. The rhythmic sounds of metal and feet as she and Sokka spar. Quiet cool air on her face. Twinkling starlight.

Her hands start the motion.

Static tingles at her fingertips.

Taste of ozone on her tongue.

The charges separating.

Connect them.

And—

*You’re a failure. A disgrace. Weak.*

The sparks connect together in a massive explosion and throws her back again. Sokka is yelling and running to her.

“Oh shit, Zuko are you okay?!*”* Sokka yells, helping her up. “What happened? You were doing so good. I saw the lightening, but then it just…exploded.”

Zuko squeezes her eyes and turns away, “I failed. Like always. Even when you’re helping me, I still can’t do it.”

The remaining static on Zuko’s body causes Sokka’s wolftail to stick up in all directions, but he still presses himself tightly against her. “Hey, hey. These things take time, you almost got it. You were so close.”

“And then I messed up. Father was right.” She mutters pressing herself against her knees. “Everything I ever do just blows up in my face.”

The boy grimaces and holds her comfortingly. “No, Zuko. Ozai’s an idiot. He doesn’t know what he’s talking about. You’re not a failure. Yeah sometimes you fail, but even when things are tough you keep moving forward. You don’t stop, and that’s one of the most amazing things about you.”

Zuko pulls back, eyes glimmering. “I keep tell you to stop saying that.”

“I’ll keep saying it until it sinks in your head and even after that.” Sokka retorts.

She can’t stop the slight wobble of her lips and the heat between her eyes, so she turns away, “Shut up and get to work on a plan.”

Sokka laughs, “Yes, Princess.” His eyes catch the scorch marks on the ground, and he kneels to inspect it.

“Hmm…exploding our way out of here may still be an option.” He muses.

Zuko narrows her eyes. “You just said you didn’t want me to make anymore explosions.”

“No of course I don’t, but maybe we can try to mimic the loud sounds.” Sokka explains. “You can try blasting some of the rocks in the right places and it’ll cause echoes and vibrations in the ground. I think we can use that to lure the Dai Li here, and then take them out.”

“We just got beat by the Dai Li.” She says.

“True, but now we have the element of surprise.” Sokka grins.

Zuko rolls her eyes. “You’re crazy.”

“Pot meet kettle.”

Well can’t deny that. She just huffs in resignation and goes to follow Sokka to find good blasting sites. But suddenly the earth explodes form another section.

That was fast.

Sokka and Zuko get ready to fight, but instead of Dai Li it’s Aang and Iroh. The tea maker rushes forward and brings his niece in a tight hug, and Aang goes to Sokka.

“Thank Agni you both are safe.” Iroh says.

Zuko lets herself sink into the hug before pulling back, her eyes narrowed. “Why are working with the Avata—em Aang?”

“We’re saving you!” Aang replies.

Zuko’s about to shout that she wasn’t asking him, but Iroh grips her tight and tells the other two to go ahead. He looks seriously at her.

“Zuko, this is a pivotal moment in your life. You have grown wiser and stronger and freer than you have ever been. It’s time to choose the path of good.” He puts his weathered hand on her cheek. “You are your mother’s daughter, and I *know* you want to do the right thing.”

“How touching.”

Zuko turns and watches, horrified, as crystals trap her uncle.

“Azula.” She growls.

Her sister smirks and motions for the Dai Li to leave.

“Zuzu. I expected traitorous thoughts from your Uncle, but *you*? I thought you knew better than to listen to a doddering old man.”

“Release him, Azula!” She yells. “He hasn’t done anything wrong. He just wants to live in peace.”

Her sister laughs. “Live in peace? Are you an idiot, Zuzu?” she mocks. “After everything Uncle’s done, he doesn’t *get* to live in peace. He either lives his life serving the Fire Lord or in a prison so deep not even Agni can see him.”

“You’re wrong! You can just leave him be. He’s given *enough* for our nation.”

Azula sneers. “You’ve always been so soft sister. But no matter, I’ll still give you this golden opportunity to go back home.”

It’s like thunder crashed in her ears.

“W-what?”

Azula smiles, “Help me capture the Avatar, and you’ll be welcomed with *honors* back home. You’ll get everything you ever wanted. Your home, your honor and Father’s love and acceptance. Just join me here. Don' let cousin's sacrifice be in vain.”

Uncle is shouting at her. Pleading for her to not listen, but she can’t hear him. Everything is muted, and all she can think about is *home*.

To the same gardens she and Lu Ten used to play in.

To the wisteria-willows that are probably in bloom.

And her father.

*Her father.*

Her heart palpitates. Her Father would give her his love, his acceptance. Maybe he would even see that she would be worth more than just a marriage prospect.

That’s all she ever wanted. She would be free to choose her destiny. To choose…

To choose...

*Blue eyes. Tanned skin. A wide brimming smile.*

Her pinky burns and she clutches it close to her chest. She made a *promise*.

*Lu Ten’s eyes burn like Agni’s light, “This is a pinky promise. It’s something you only do if it’s a* *serious promise and you mustn’t break it.”*

She can’t. She just *can’t*. But the sweet temptation of home, of love and acceptance, are a siren’s song that’s luring her towards the inky darkness of her soul.

She opens her eyes and turns back to Iroh. He looks desperately at her, and she can’t bear to look at him any longer. She turns away and flees to the exit.

When she gets out, she sees Azula facing against the Avatar. The Dai Li have Sokka surrounded and trapped. He’s desperately shouting at his friend. There’s a familiar look in the Avatar’s eyes. One of resignation and desperation. One that is burning with the need for power, and that he’s willing to do anything to get it.

Zuko feels her breath hitch. In that moment, he looked so *young*. Then she sees her sister, eyes blazing and grin at her mouth. She already knows the kata Azula’s preparing.

And she’s running.

Ozone fills her mouth.

Static clings to her body.

The bolt is released from Azula’s fingertips, and it connects to Aang.

But Zuko is there.

She siphons off the force of the lightening and redirects it near the group of Dai Li that have Sokka hostage. Aang still falls, and in that moment, the ceiling erupts. A pillar of water shoots down and Zuko sees Katara coming with the Earth bender. She washes the Dai Li away and takes Aang.

Azula is about to lunge at her, but a plume of fire stops her path. Iroh gets in-between the retreating kids and yells, “Go I’ll hold them off.”

Katara nods and hefts Aang up. The little earth bender saves Sokka and tries to take him, but Zuko sees him yelling and motioning to her. Blue eyes blazing desperately, but she gives him an imperceptible shake of her head and inches closer to her sister. Even if she could…they wouldn’t make it. Azula would hunt them down. And…and some desperate scared part of her just wants to see *home*.

Sokka is stunned, and the earth bender uses this drag him out with the others. The four are gone in a blink of an eye. Uncle continues to keep the Dai Li away, but then trap him tightly in crystal and rocks. He’s resigned and closes his eyes.

The cavern is quiet, but Azula is now turning her blazing eyes towards her.

“What have you done?!” she screeches.

Zuko holds her ground. “You shouldn’t have tried to kill him!” she says. “He is the bridge between our world and the spirit world. He is practically a spirit. Don’t you remember what happened in the North? We lost most of the western fleet in *seconds*. Once he’s gone, who will be the new Avatar?”

Azula pauses and comprehension seems to dawn in her eyes. “A water tribe.” She whispers.

Zuko swallows hard. “If we don’t want the ocean spirit to swallow our home, we need time to destroy the Water tribes.” She takes another breath and says. “You shot him in the spine. If he’s not already dead, he’s paralyzed. That’s as good as dead.”

“Father might not think so.” Azula hisses, a fear growing in her eyes. “He ordered me to put a stop to him.”

“And you *did*.” Zuko insists. “It’s going to take months…even years for him to recuperate. By that time…we’d have won the war.”

“And what if Father asks?” Azula demands. “I won’t be seen as a weakling just because of your softness.”

“…then tell him I killed the Avatar. If he ever comes back, it’ll be me who’ll take the fall.”

Azula is contemplative. “Fine. I’ll accept that. It is your fault anyway. But if you try to blackmail me with that information, I’ll burn you to the bone.”

“I wouldn’t expect any less from you.” Then Zuko glances at Iroh who looks at her with dawning comprehension. “Are you going to release Uncle?”

Azula laughs harshly. “No, consider him my *leverage*. Keep quiet and I’ll make sure he’s comfortable.”

“Azula—”

But here sister points her sparking finger to her face, “Don’t test me *sister*. You’ve pushed your luck to the brink today. Be grateful that you somehow managed to convince me not to bring you home in *chains*.” And with that she leaves. The Dai Li taking the imprisoned Iroh with them. Zuko wants to call out to her uncle, but the words stick in her throat.

He wanted her to go with the Avatar, but she couldn’t. She didn’t think she could face the utter disappointment in his face. She betrayed his trust by choosing her sister and Father.

What else is new?

&#95;&#42;&#95;&#42;&#95;

High above the skies, Katara places the spirit water over the terrible burn on his back. She prays to Tui and La that she’ll be able to heal him. The mess of knots in his chakras resist against the pull of the water, but she slowly eases the blockage.

His arrows glow and Aang *breathes*. Katara lets out a sob of relief and holds him close.

He’ll make it.

Once the initial panic subsides, a wave of rage crashes over her. Azula and Zuko. They were the ones who did this. She couldn’t believe Zuko would side with Azula. After everything Azula’s done, she even hurt Uncle Iroh. Zuko must not have cared for him as much as she thought. She grits her teeth. She’ll never forgive them.

Unbeknownst to her, Sokka is tearing himself apart for letting him leave her with Azula. Zuko helped Aang. She ran to stop her sister’s lightening and…re-directed some of the power away. Aang still got hit, but she had tried to save him. Now she and Iroh are there with Azula, traitors to the Fire Nation.

What’s going to happen to her?

Why didn’t she leave with him?

Did she think he wouldn’t be safe with him?

His hand goes to his pocket where the hair clip still laid. He grips it tightly and squeezes his eyes.

*I’ll get you back. I swear, so please stay safe.*

The eclipse looms ahead like a promise.

&#95;&#42;&#95;&#42;&#95;

Zuko watches despondently as the Fire Nation troops invade the city. The walls have fallen, and a sea of red swept into the city. The flags of the Fire Nation now adorn the palace walls, and the soldiers are given two options: to cooperate with the new regime and live in peace or to go in chains. Most choose to work with the Fire Nation.

She should be rejoicing. They took Ba Sing Se. They’ve won. Lu Ten’s sacrifice was not in vain. And soon the entire Earth Kingdom will be swept in red. There would be peace at last.

But…

She sees the look of fear and disbelief in their eyes when they parade through the city. The neighbors, the vendors, all the tea shop regulars. It makes her heart clench, and bile threatens to gather in her mouth.

Some of the ones that aren’t cooperating are being led away in iron cages are screaming obscenities at her.

They trusted her.

They pitied her.

She was just a filthy Fire Nation bitch, looking for weaknesses.

Well they weren’t wrong, but it still hurt. The soldiers and servants she had gotten along with are now looking at her with disgust. One even spits on her, accusing her of lying to the king and making up a fake sob story to gain his pity and trust. The Fire Nation soldier quickly grabs the mouthy man roughly and holds burning fist up threateningly.

Zuko steps in, “Wait stop.” She says.

“But Princess Zuko, this Earth Nation *filth* disrespected you.” The soldier says.

Zuko sighs, “I know, but it’s not worth wasting your time or energy on. He’s punished enough to have to work for his enemies.”

The soldiers look skeptical, but they bow to her request and let the servant go.

Azula watches from the sidelines with a disgusted look on her face. She walks over to her sister and says, “You’ve really gotten too soft, sister.” She says, and then a blue whip of fire forms in her hands. With a flick of her wrist, she hits the servant. Screams echo through the hallway and Zuko is paralyzed by the smell and sizzle of burning flesh. Azula hit the servant in the face, an angry terrible burn is left on the right side of his face, as if in a mocking reflection of her own face. The servant is screaming in pain as he clutches the wound.

“That’s how you punish rebellious peasants. Pain is the only lesson they understand.” Azula says. “Take him away to the dungeons.”

The soldiers bow and take the still screaming servant away, and Zuko is left standing there, cold with horror by what she just witnessed.

They stay in Ba Sing Se for a week to get the Fire Nation soldiers and officials settled and messenger hawk outposts built. Zuko stays inside the palace walls for most of that time. She doesn’t think she can bear walking through the streets while people look at her with sheer distrust and betrayal. But she does go out once, to her apartment to gather the few things she has. She stays for a moment and just sits on the cushion at the table.

For just a moment, she closes her eyes. The phantom sounds and smells of her home permeate the air. She can still hear Uncle puttering in the kitchen. Sokka telling stupid jokes by her side. In that moment she lets a single tear fall.

*This will be worth it. I’ll be regaining my honor and my home. I’ll take back my throne, and it will* *be all better.*

She’ll be happy at last. She can finally take her rightful place beside her father. He’ll finally love her. These memories that anchor her here should mean nothing to her in the face of her Father’s love and home. After taking a breath she stands, looks to the room one last time, and leaves.

### End Notes

#### Chapter End Notes

**Lu Ten:** I’m sad now.

**Yue:** Me too.

—

I’m so sorry guys.

But thank you for reading!

## Chapter 11

---

Zuko is standing in the crow’s nest high above the ground, letting the wind tousle her hair. The captain looks worriedly at her from below, while Azula just rolls her eyes and calls her dramatic. Whatever. Azula can call her what she likes, but that won’t stop her from watching for the first glimpse of her home.

Suddenly, the Gates of Azulon crest the horizon. The massive statue of her grandfather, looms in the distance, and Zuko feels her breath catch. She’s actually here. She’s finally home. The sea breeze carries the slight humidity and heat of her island home, and her heart palpitates.

“Zuko get down from there you drama queen.” Azula shouts from below.

Said girl glares down at her sister, but Azula ignores it. “You need to get changed before we disembark. I don’t want you looking like a pathetic beggar when I step off the ship. It would just *destroy* the image I’m going for.”

Zuko reluctantly slides down, much to the consternation of the crew who watch the daredevil princess with sweaty nervous hand.

“I don’t really have any clothes to change into.” Zuko replies. “If you hadn’t forgotten, *someone* branded me a traitor and I had to go on the run. Didn’t really have time to get my good armor.”

“Well lucky for you, *I’ve* come prepared.” Azula quips. “I had one of our servants tailor a new set of armor for you. It should work for now.”

“That was surprisingly thoughtful of you.”

Azula smiles sharply, “Don’t get used to it. If you came in your Earth Kingdom rags, I would be embarrassed to be seen next to you.”

Ah that made much more sense.

Ty Lee bounces up to the siblings with a smile, “I can’t wait to get back on land. Being on sea is *so* boring. We should go to a beach or something when we finally get home.”

“Ugh can’t we just stay at the palace where it’s a little cooler and a lot less sunny and *crowded*.” Mai grumbles as she sharpens her daggers.

“I think I want to see Lu Ten before I go anywhere else.” Zuko says.

Ty Lee’s smile droops and Mai stops sharpening her daggers. Azula is the only one who scoffs. “What is the point of seeing a dead person’s grave? They’re dead. There’s nothing they can do for you. You’re just talking to dirt and ashes.”

Zuko gives her a glare, “It’s the respectful thing to do.” She replies. “I haven’t seen him in three years, and I *know* you haven’t been keeping their grave cleaned or maintained.”

“That’s what the servants are for.” Azula answers airily. “Why does it matter if we’re not the ones doing it? We’re certainly the ones who are *paying* the servants to do it.”

“It’s the thought and effort you put in.” Zuko replies. “No one should have to be forgotten by their loved ones.”

“Hmph this is why Father always said you were *weak*.” Azula snorts before walking off towards her cabin. Ty Lee hesitantly walks up to her and gives her a smile.

“I think they’d like to see you too.” She says. “I’m sure they missed you.”

“…thanks Ty Lee.”

Mai adds, “I’d rather spend some time in a graveyard than in the palace anyway.”

Zuko laughs softly, “I’d expect nothing less from you, Mai.”

&#95;&#42;&#95;&#42;&#95;

The eldest princess fidgets as she holds her arms up for the servants to put on her armor. It’s been a long time since she’s had people dress her, and she’s forgotten just how *touchy* it can be. Of course, they were nothing but professional, but they had to stand close to her to fix the ties around her. Thankfully, they’re mercifully fast, and Zuko looks at herself in the mirror. Her hair is pinned up in a top knot for the first time in the years and her armor is gleaming in the firelight.

She’s the image of strength and power, but somehow she feels like she’s wearing a costume. The servants bow low to her.

“Is there anything else you require, Princess Zuko?” they ask.

Zuko shakes her head, “No that would be all, thank you both.” And the servants go, leaving Zuko alone in the room, staring at her reflection in the mirror.

It’s like looking at a stranger. The armor feels heavier than it’s ever felt, and the topknot feels like it’s holding a vice-like grip on her head. She takes a breath. It’s just nerves. She’ll be facing her Father for the first time in three years, of course she’s a little nervous.

Mai soon comes in to tell her they’re going to dock soon. She takes one look at Zuko’s face and sighs.

“Look you just need to stand there and wave. Your resting bitch face looks serious enough to appease those old farts and make the citizens think you’re Agni reincarnated or whatever bullshit the royal family is saying nowadays.” She says.

“Thanks that’s…comforting.” Zuko replies. “It’s been so long since I’ve been home, I’m just nervous that I’ll fuck it all up.”

“I mean I’m pretty sure you’ll fuck something up.”

“Gee thanks Mai you really know how to comfort people.”

Mai shrugs, “You want to be comforted go to Ty Lee. She’s the most well-adjusted one out of all of us.”

“She ran away to the circus.”

Mai raises a brow, “As opposed to searching and chasing an all-powerful bender for three years, occupying and burning cities, and sitting at home being bored to death because you’re too afraid to go against your parents?”

“…you have a point.” Zuko concedes.

“I always do. Now let’s go before Azula starts bitching at us.”

When they dock, Zuko stands besides Azula, staring into the gathered crowd of citizens and an entourage of soldiers and servants from the Home Guard. True to Mai’s words all she needs to do is wave, and people seem to be satisfied. As the carriage takes her up to the palace, she takes in the familiar paved roads of Caldera City, the hot humid air, the flowers blooming. It takes her mind off of the palace looming in the distance.

When they arrive, they’re immediately ushered to the throne room. Her heart feels like it’ll pop out of her chest. Mai and Ty Lee have to stay outside, while Azula and Zuko go to face their father.

The huge gilded doors swing open effortlessly, and she faces a sight she’s only seen in her dreams.

The flames dance around her father soaring high to the ceiling and obscuring his face, but then he stands and the fire parts.

Fire Lord Ozai looms over his daughters like a great shadow. He smiles and stretches his arms out.

“Azula and Zuko. My daughters.” He rumbles. “It seems you both accomplished the impossible. Taking over Ba Sing Se and killing the Avatar.” He steps down from the throne and walks towards them. He places his hands on their shoulders and smiles sharply.

“You’ve proven your worth, Princess Zuko. I’m finally proud call you my daughter. Welcome home.”

Zuko feels her breath catch. That was it. Those were the words she always wanted to hear.

But they just taste like ash against her tongue.

&#95;&#42;&#95;&#42;&#95;

Sokka breathes a sigh of relief when they see the blue and white ships. On the ground, Hakoda awaits them with open arms, and Sokka jumps down to greet him, rushing to his father.

“Dad!” he exclaims.

Hakoda gives a booming laugh as Sokka charges at him and envelopes the boy in a crushing hug.

“Sokka! You’ve grown so much!” he exclaims. “I’m so happy to see you well.”

“And I’m so glad you’re okay, Dad.” Sokka whispers. The comforting warmth and strength of his father is a balm against his battered weary heart. It feels like he has a pillar again, solid ground to stand on.

But they can’t stay long like that.

They need to get Katara a room to heal Aang, and they need to get out before the Fire Nation ships find them.

Sokka wastes no time and tells them that they need to hijack a Fire Nation ship. Fast. It’s the only way for them to go undetected and keep Aang safe.

Fortunately, with Toph’s new metal bending and Katara’s water bending, they easily secure a ship. As the water tribesman load onto the ship, Sokka says his goodbyes to the Earth king.

Sokka grips King Kuei’s hand tightly. The monarch wanted to head off on his own and find any remaining Earth Kingdom troops to fight with them during the invasion. It’s probably for the best; he wouldn’t be any safer traveling with them into Fire Nation territory. The king looks at Sokka sadly.

“I’m sorry. It’s my fault the city fell…I should have been more careful with those girls. And then…” he trails off. No doubt thinking about Zuko.

Sokka grimaces. “It wasn’t your fault. I told you to trust them, and Azula took advantage of that. As for Zuko…” he pauses. “She made her choices, but she didn’t end up in your palace to spy on you. Believe me.”

“…I know.” Kuei says. “I just never thought she’d be Ozai’s daughter. Princess of the Fire Nation. I always thought the royal family would be…well more like Azula.”

“Yeah. Tell me about it.” Sokka scoffs.

“But she still stood by as Azula took Ba Sing Se. I can’t forgive her for doing that.” Kuei replies. “Those people were kind to her and Mushi.”

“…I don’t expect you to.” Sokka answers. “Things are…complicated.”

“Ha you’re telling me.” Kuei retorts. “Sometimes I wish I was still ignorant of all this, but I’m glad that I got to meet you. With you in charge, I have no doubt we’ll be victorious. You’re a good man and a good leader Sokka. I wish you the best.”

The young warrior blushes and looks away. “You give me too much credit. I’m just the plan guy.”

“So you say.” Kuei says mildly.

They soon load him with supplies and send him on his way towards the nearest village. Sokka watches him until he disappears and hopes that the king will be alright. He’s…naïve and a little too trusting, but a good man. And Sokka wouldn’t want to wish any harm to befall him.

&#95;&#42;&#95;&#42;&#95;

The steel and taste of ash is so different than what Sokka’s used to. The water tribe boats rely on wind to move them through, but these huge ships move through fire power and steam. The boilers and pipes whine and hiss all around him as he tried to make sense of all the numbers the gauges are pointed to. They thankfully find a schematic plan in the engineer’s quarters, and Sokka spend days reading through it to understand how to properly run the steal beast.

Many of the water tribe warriors just think you need to put coal in the fire thing and let it go, and Sokka nearly bashes all their heads in. They don’t seem to understand the concept of overheating or pressure. To Sokka’s big surprise, once they bring Pipsqueak and the Duke on board, the huge Freedom Fighter manages to help Sokka maintain the ship’s engines.

“I had no idea you knew how these things operated.” Sokka says in awe.

Pipsqueak just laughs, “Haha well I used to be an apprentice for an engineer in a Fire Nation colony.”

“You were a *colonist*?” How did Jet not kill on sight is the question that hangs in the air.

“Hmm not exactly. I was an Earth Nation resident who was born in a fire nation colony. The engineer took pity on me since I was an orphan and taught me how their machines worked. Unfortunately, he died, and his other students didn’t really like the idea of a dirty Earth Nation orphan tainting his workshop. So, I had to go on the run until I eventually joined up with Jet.” Pipsqueak explains.

Sokka blinks. “Woah that…really sucks.”

Pipsqueak shrugs. “Well, nothing you can do about it. I was really angry for a while, it’s why I ended up with Jet, but after he tried to destroy that village…I guess I realized I was pretty much doing what those other students did to me. So, I left and took the Duke with me.”

Sokka puts a hand on his shoulder, “Well I’m glad you’re here now. I don’t think I’d be able to make as much progress without you helping me out.”

“Don’t sell yourself short Sokka. You’re getting concepts that took me months to understand properly and you’re so quick with your calculations. I’m just glad I can use my engineering knowledge again.”

Sokka brings up his fists, “Engineering buddies.”

“Engineering buddies.

&#95;&#42;&#95;&#42;&#95;

“Why exactly do we need to have someone down here at all times?” Hakoda asks. “Don’t these things just…go?”

Sokka sighs heavily, “No Dad, these pipes lead to the ship’s turbines which allow them to move forward without any wind. We can’t just stuff coal in the boiler and call it a day. If we don’t bring the steam to the right pressure, we won’t get any movement.”

“So just put a lot of coal in.” Hakoda suggests.

“No! If we do that the entire engine could explode!” Sokka exclaims. “Not to mention that we need to make sure the water levels are sufficient so we don’t heat the boiler dry, the oxygen levels are stable and the pressure is—”

“Okay okay. Can’t just add more fire to make ship move.” Hakoda says. “Honestly, sometimes I think the Fire Nation is using magic with all these things.”

“It’s not magic it’s *science*. Careful meticulous science that they’ve honed and studied for years while most of the world were still using stone-craft.” Sokka retorts. “They built a giant *drill* to take over Ba Sing Se, and they almost succeeded. If we weren’t fast enough to get there, Ba Sing Se would have fallen *waay* earlier.”

“Hm still magic to me.” Hakoda teases.

Sokka chuckles and lightly nudges his father. “I know you’re just saying that to make me mad.”

The older warrior tussles his hair in response. “I am, but I’m also very impressed and proud of you, Sokka. We old warriors wouldn’t be able to make heads or tails of all this. It’s good to see the new blood taking over so well.”

Sokka blushes, “Thanks Dad. I’m just glad I can do my part to help end this.”

For a moment, Hakoda looks so sad and tired. He puts a heavy hand on his son’s shoulder, “Oh Sokka, I never wanted you kids to finish what we should have stopped. You, Katara, Toph, the *Avatar*. You’re all so young. I’m tempted to just put you all in a nice igloo back in the South Pole.”

“Dad, no. You already tried that with me and Katara and look how well that turned out.” Sokka replies. “This war has touched everyone, and I can’t just stand by and watch people fight while I stay safe at home. I’m a warrior too.”

Hakoda looks proudly at his boy. He’s changed so much from the little one who used to tag along the other warriors, dragging a club and boomerang.

“I’m so proud of you, son.” Hakoda says.

Sokka beams. That’s all he ever wanted to hear from him, and it brings a lighthearted flutter to his chest.

&#95;&#42;&#95;&#42;&#95;

Now that the Earth Kingdom army is in shambles, Sokka needs to modify the original invasion plan to fit a smaller force. He spends most of the day conversing with his father and Bato, trying to find the best way to invade. Their table is covered with maps and drawings of the Fire Nation as well as intel on the capital.

“The hardest part will be getting through the Gates of Azulon.” Sokka explains. “They have fire netting to keep out any boats that try to go for a naval assault. So if we can’t go through from the surface, we’ll have to go through from the bottom.”

“How would that even be possible?” Bato asks. “It would be exhausting for the water benders to keep us hidden long enough to get to the shores.”

Sokka smirks, “We build a machine to go underwater and we power it with water bending.”

“…is that even feasible?” Bato asks incredulously.

Hakoda pats his friend’s shoulder. “With these kids, I wouldn’t be surprised if they made a flying ship.”

Sokka winces. He’ll wait to tell his dad that he already helped the Mechanist build a flying ship.

At night, he’ll go to Katara and sit by her side as she anxiously moves healing water on his burn. Most of the time he falls asleep within an hour, and by the time he wakes up, Katara is asleep on the cot next to Aang. He sighs and lays a blanket on his sister, stroking her hair softly.

Toph comes in to bring a tray of food to him. “How’s Twinkletoes and Sugarqueen?”

Sokka sighs, “Well Aang hasn’t woken up yet and Katara’s exhausted, so no change.”

The younger girl nods solemnly, and then punches his arm. “Don’t worry Snoozles, Aang’ll wake up soon.”

“I know. He’s a tough kid. A little lightening won’t stop him.” Sokka says.

“…And how are you doing?” Toph asks.

Sokka raises his brow, “What do you mean? I’m the last person you should be worried about.”

The blind girl just scoffs. “You had to leave your girlfriend behind in those caves. I nearly had to force you to go with me.”

He looks away, “She didn’t want to come.” He says. “…and I couldn’t convince her to go with us.”

It’s a bitter medicine he’s had to come to terms with. For whatever reason, Zuko wanted to stay with Azula and go home to Ozai. He wishes he could have just kidnapped her and gone, but there was a steely determined look in her eyes. One that he’s seen on her countless times before; the one that says that she’s going to do something and not even the spirits can move her.

“But she tried to save Aang.” Toph says.

“Yeah…but that doesn’t mean she was ready to go with us.” Sokka explains. “She promised me that she wouldn’t capture Aang again, but that doesn’t mean she’s given up on trying to go home.”

“Azula saw her save Aang though, right? Wouldn’t that make her a traitor to her nation? I don’t think the Fire Lord will like hearing that.”

“We just have to hope that she can spin it in a way that sounds good.” Sokka says. “Anyway, once we get to the Fire Nation, I’m going to get as much information as I can. No matter where she is, I’m going to find her, and I’m going to take her away from Ozai.”

Toph smirks and punches his other arm. “Okay, Snoozles you’ve convinced me. I’ll help rescue your princess.”

Sokka laughs, “Thanks Toph. I know I could count on you.”

&#95;&#42;&#95;&#42;&#95;

Lu Ten’s grave is spotless and neatly manicured, just as Azula predicted. Zuko still pours clean blessed water from Agni’s temple and lights a memorial flame over the grave. She throws flowers over the small mound and lights an incense for her prayers.

It’s blissfully quiet and she let’s herself fall into the memories of her cousin.

“It’s been so long since I’ve properly prayed here. I’m sorry no one’s visited you in since I’ve left, but I’m here now.” She pauses. “Uncle can’t be here, but I’m sure I can get Father to give an exception for one day. He’s…imprisoned right now, but don’t worry. I’ll get an audience with Father, and he’ll be lenient once I tell him…”

Once she tells him what? That Uncle always watched over her? That he only let the Avatar escape because he thought it would protect her?

There’s really no way for her to spin it in a good light. So she swallows hard.

“I’m not really sure what I’ll tell him. To be honest…I’m not sure why I feel so empty. For three years I always thought that if I could just go back home, everything would be okay. I’d be happier.” She pauses again. Wind tussling her hair. “I thought the only thing I needed was Father’s love and acceptance, but why doesn’t it satisfy me? Why am I still so angry and tired and just…*empty*?”

Silence answers her.

“I thought—I thought this yearning would go away once I’m here, but everything just feels numb and tastes like *ash*. I have everything I’ve ever wanted. So why am I not *happy*?”

The breeze just passes by, and she lifts her head towards the sun.

“Why can’t I just be *happy*?” she cries into the unending sky.

She heads back to the palace after visiting Lu Ten, the cold numbness in her heart hasn’t left her and she just topples onto her bed, feeling a bone-deep tiredness. It’s barely been two days since she’s returned, and already she feels like she’s drowning. Her hands clutch the soft sheets, she just needs to give it some time. She’s just adjusting back to palace life; this is only temporary.

“I can do this.” she whispers. “I can be happy here. Tomorrow will be better.”

Tomorrow is not better. Neither is the next day. Or the day after that. A week passes and it feels like she’s a ghost drifting through the halls. The servants all give her sympathetic smiles and try their best to make her comfortable. Making her favorite pastries, leaving bird feed for the turtle-ducks, offering her massages and lotions.

Nothing works.

Even Azula has started to notice her sister’s increasingly depressed aura, and it’s irritating her.

“What are you doing?” Azula asks as she watches her sister sit under the wisteria-willow tree.

“What do you mean?” Zuko asks fiddling with the fallen flowers and fashioning them in a crude flower crown.

“I mean why are you acting like I burned all the baby turtle-ducks? I haven’t done that in *years*.”

Zuko gives her a half-hearted glare. “I don’t know. I’m just…tired and sad.”

Azula rolls her eyes. “Oh Agni is this one of your pathetic angst-parties? You are a princess of the Fire Nation. You have wealth and power beyond anyone’s imagination, you should be ecstatic. Did you *want* to remain an unknown Earth Kingdom refugee?”

*Maybe*.

“…no. I just…I guess I pictured it differently.”

“What? Did you want a huge party thrown in your honor?”

“No, I’m not *you*.” She quips.

“Well you must be feeling somewhat better if you’re talking back to me.” Azula huffs. “Would visiting our Uncle make you feel better?”

Zuko winces. “No, I don’t think so.”

“Good because I don’t think Father would like you visiting after you asked him to pardon Uncle.”

Again, Zuko grimaces. That conversation did not go well.

*Father had looked at her with narrowed eyes.*

*“You want me to pardon that traitor?”*

*“Yes. Please Father, he just wanted to protect me.” Zuko pleads.*

*Ozai looks at her with burning eyes and rises. Zuko flinches and tries to keep herself from backing* *away as her father walks closer to her. He leans down and whispers in her burned ear, “Don’t ever* *come to me with such foolish requests again.” And he leans back, looking down on her with such* *baleful eyes. Zuko immediately bows low and runs.*

*She vomits everything when she gets back to her room.*

Zuko shakes away the memory and glares at her sister. “Why do you even care?” Zuko asks. “Wouldn’t you prefer me docile and sulking?”

Azula scoffs. “No where’s the fun in that?” she answers. “The whole fun of having a sibling is the thrill of destroying their spirit and subjugating your will on them. You acting so…*Mai-like* takes out all the fun.”

“I’m so glad you think that way.” Zuko replies sarcastically.

“Would sex help?” Azula asks curiously.

Zuko flushes a bright red, “What the *fuck,* Azula? You can’t just ask people that!”

She just shrugs, “Why not? It’s just *sex*. It’s not like anyone here cares if you’re a virgin or not. Don’t get me wrong they’ll judge you for laying with filthy Earth peasants, but not for having sex. We’re not prudes or savages here.”

“I don’t want sex.” Zuko replies heatedly. “I just want to be left alone so I can have some peace and quiet.”

“To do what? Finish making that strange monstrosity in your hand?”

“Yes. Now please *leave*.”

Azula glares and huffs. “Fine.” She says and turns away. But not before sending a spark of blue fire to burn the flower crown in Zuko’s hands. Zuko yelps and has to drop the burning crown.

*Ugh why is she such a bitch?*

&#95;&#42;&#95;&#42;&#95;

More time passes and Zuko isn’t feeling any better. Her days mostly consist of training, lessons, sitting by the pond, visiting Lu Ten, and maybe spending some time with Mai and Ty Lee. At least with the two girls, she feels a little more comfortable. But even they can tell something’s wrong.

Ty Lee is the first to broach the subject, “Hey…your aura isn’t looking so good.” She says.

Mai nods, “Yeah you’re making *me* look like a ray of sunshine in comparison.”

“I’m just…adjusting. I’ll be back to my normal levels of depression and angst soon.” Zuko answers.

The two girls look at one another meaningfully.

“Well then why don’t you come out with us tonight?” Ty Lee suggests. “There’s a festival in the city, and that might cheer you up! Maybe you’ll even find a cute boy to—”

Mai elbows the girl who realizes what she just said. Ty Lee backtracks quickly, “Or well I mean— er help *us* find a cute boy to kiss.”

Zuko doesn’t look convinced, but Mai finally sighs and says, “Look, come or don’t come, but I could use some company if I’m going to be stuck with aura-girl and your crazy sister all night.”

Said aura-girl beams in response.

“…okay.” Zuko agrees.

“Yes!” Ty Lee cheers. “We’ll need to go to your room.”

“For what?”

“So I can dress you up!”

“…I’m already starting to regret this.”

“No take-backs.” Mai deadpans.

The festival is nice. Azula gets *wwaaayy* into all the games and Zuko fears that she might burn down a couple stalls. She doesn’t, but only because the vendors let her take whatever prize she wanted. There’s a familiar feeling of comfort as she walks through the lit streets and watch the people mingle and smile. It’s jarring to see how differently the people of her nation live in comparison to the Earth Kingdom.

Her hood is raised so people don’t seem to recognize her, especially now that she’s away from Azula and her two friends. She looks through the stalls until she comes across one with beautiful embroidered rugs draping around the stalls. She can’t help herself; she has to go and see it.

The designs and lining are impeccably neat and imaginative. They shimmer in various shades of color, and Zuko is impressed by the craftsmanship.

“See anything you like dear?” the woman at the stall asks.

Zuko looks up and nearly stumbles. She’s only seen the woman’s face in portraits, but she recognizes her.

“Siqi?” Zuko says quietly.

The woman seems startled. “…why yes. Do I know you?”

Zuko swallows, “Um…I used to sail with your son, Yama.”

Siqi’s eyes light up, “You did? When? What ship? What’s your name dear? Yama writes all the time about his crewmates.”

“He—he does? He still writes you letters?”

Understanding fills her eyes. “Oooh you must have heard about the Western Fleet didn’t you?”

Zuko nods mutely.

“Well I’m glad to say that Agni spared my son’s life that day.” Siqi replies with a smile. “From what he’s written to me, it sounds like all the crew from his previous ship made it out. They were able to get a few ships away when the whole mess with the moon started.”

Zuko breathes a sigh of relief. “That’s—that’s so good to hear. I’m glad they’re all okay.”

Siqi smiles and then peers at the girl…there’s something—Oh. *Oh.*

“Oh, your highness.” She whispers.

Zuko’s eyes shoot up, and she huddles herself further into the hood. Siqi waves her hands placatingly. “My apologies. I won’t tell anyone.” Siqi assures.

“…thank you.”

“Of course, dear.” The woman replies. “I’m so glad to meet the brave young lady who took care of my son for three years.”

Zuko blushes. “Your son…the whole crew really were the ones who helped *me* out. I’m just—I’m so sorry that I failed him in the end.”

Siqi takes Zuko’s hand into hers. “No, my princess. You did your duty to the very end.” She assures. “I was so happy to hear that he was under the command of someone who truly *respected* their soldiers.”

The princess averts her eyes at this. “Not really. I used to yell at the crew a lot.”

The embroiderer laughs, “You seem to forget that you’re only sixteen. Thirteen when you first got command of a ship. I’ve known grown men who still throw tantrums when they don’t get what they want. I think you did just fine with what you had. Yama always spoke highly of you in his letters.”

A flutter of happiness floods her body. It’s the first time she’s felt this feeling here. Her eyes grow hot and she bows her head to prevent the woman from seeing.

“T-thank you. It was an honor to have your son serve in my ship.” She rasps in a wavering voice.

Siqi smiles and bows her head, “Thank *you* princess for always looking after our people. Even when you were banished, you held yourself with honor. Please don’t ever forget that we’ll support you. Always.”

Zuko’s lips tremble, but she manages to nod. “I won’t, and please give Yama my regards.” She discretely wipes her eyes and points to a rug with wisteria-willow flowers on them. “I’ll also take that rug, please have it sent to the palace.”

The princess gives her the impression of her royal seal as well as a bag of gold that’s worth much more than the rug. Siqi tries to give her back some of the money, but Zuko insists that she takes it. She leaves the stall feeling lighter and happier.

When she meets up with the other three girls, Ty Lee smiles broadly.

“Your aura looks much happier.” She claps delightedly. “I told you the festival would cheer you up.”

Zuko quirks a small smile, “It did. Thank you, Ty Lee.”

“If you two are done ‘bonding’ I have another prize to snag.” Azula says impatiently.

“Calm down, Azula. The prizes aren’t going anywhere.” Zuko retorts.

“I’m not letting a sniveling peasant win the grand prize before *me*.”

“What about a crown princess of the Fire Nation?”

“Hmph in your dreams Zuzu. And even in dreams, I’d still crush you like the insignificant gnat you are.”

Zuko rolls her eyes. “Agni, Azula you need to calm down before you have an aneurysm at fourteen.”

The younger sibling huffs and crosses her arms. “Well at least you finally seem to be back to your old self. It’ll make destroying you all the more satisfying.”
