“Sure sure. Whatever you say, *little sister*.” Zuko dodges the sharp nails in time, but Azula zaps her with a little spark of lightening. Well that was a milder reaction than Zuko predicted.

Behind the two princesses, Mai sighs. “What is wrong with those two?”

Ty Lee giggles. “That’s just their way of showing their love.”

&#95;&#42;&#95;&#42;&#95;

Katara sighs as she bends the broth into Aang’s body. It’s been nearly two weeks, and Aang’s still not up. It’s making her worry. Sokka keeps telling her that it’ll be alright, but she can’t feel the same confidence her brother feels. What’s worse is that he keeps insisting that Zuko *saved* Aang. It’s not possible. If Zuko did save Aang why didn’t she come with them? Why did she stay with Azula?

Sokka would stutter and try to explain, but Katara isn’t going to hear it. For all Sokka knew, Zuko may have made it worse. He said he saw her connect with the lightening and shoot some of it off, but what’s to say that she didn’t just add her own lightening? They’ve always known that she wanted to capture Aang, what makes this time any different?

Just because Zuko helped them out in the North doesn’t mean she’s on their side. She’s *Fire* *Nation*. The same people who killed their mother. Koh, she’s even worse because she’s from the royal line. Someone related to the monster who ordered the destruction of the Air Nomads and later the Southern Water Tribe. Zuko even told them that she would have gladly captured Aang if it suited her.

Sokka keeps insisting that she promised not the capture Aang anymore, but her brother must be thinking with his penis because there’s no way he would have naively believed the ashmaker would keep her promise. He was just too distracted by a pretty face. Not even a pretty face, a face with a *hideous* scar on it.

Their conversation degraded into a plain old screaming match after that. Hakoda and Bato had to separate them forcefully.

Hakoda sighs deeply, “I don’t know what’s gotten into you two, but we can’t afford to be fighting like this.”

“Blame it on Sokka. He’s the one *in lust* with a royal ashmaker.” Katara spits angrily. She frees herself from Bato’s grip and stomps off to her room. The men look at Sokka with strange looks, and Sokka feels himself shrink from the judging eyes. Hakoda is stony and he puts a hand on Sokka’s shoulder.

“Son, I think we need to talk.”

Sokka swallows. “Okay.”

He leads him to a private room and sits the boy down. Hakoda leans back and scrubs a tired hand over his face. “Alright. Please explain to me what your sister meant by that.”

And he does. He tells him about the princess. How she came into their village to hunt the Avatar, the times she relentlessly pursued them, their truce in the North, their encounter in the abandoned town, the time he spent with her in Ba Sing Se, and finally the encounter in the crystal catacombs.

At the end of it Hakoda looks…resigned and *very* tired. He understands that his son is a growing boy with a growing body, and sometimes there can be…lapses in judgement.

“Son, I can understand why you might have…some affection for the princess, especially after the time you two spent in Ba Sing Se, and I won’t judge you for it—”

Sokka gives him an unimpressed look.

“—alright I’ll judge you a *little bit*.” Hakoda amends. “But how can you be so sure she didn’t help her sister hurt Aang?”

“I just told you Dad, you can only bend lightening if you have a peaceful mind, and trust me, Zuko does not have a peaceful mind.” Sokka explains exasperatedly.

“You just told me that her father would revoke her banishment if she brought captured or brought down the Avatar. That might give her the push to achieve that peaceful mind.”

Sokka shakes his head, “Oh no, thinking about Ozai would definitely not give her a peaceful mind.”

“Why wouldn’t it? And why are you calling him Ozai?”

The boy groans, “Oh right I keep forgetting that this isn’t common knowledge. Strap yourself in, Dad.” and Sokka goes into another explanation.

By the end of it, Hakoda is also pale and shaking. He takes a deep breath and releases a huge puff of air. He leans forward and puts his head in his hands.

“Alright that explains a lot.” He says.

“Believe me now, Dad?”

“Yes. And I’m sorry I judged you.”

“I mean…you can judge me a little bit.” Sokka jokes. “I know I’m a sucker for strong independent women who can kick my ass.”

Hakoda chuckles. “Well you are my son. But you need to clear this up with Katara.”

Sokka frowns, “I know but she just—ugh she’s just so stubborn and she can hold a grudge like no other. Especially when she’s made up her mind about it.”

“Then you need to learn to calmly explain it to her.” Hakoda replies. “I know how stubborn your sister can be, but she’s a kind soul at heart. She’ll understand once she hears the full story, and not just the little bits and pieces you give her.”

“…okay Dad.”

Hakoda smiles and pats Sokka’s back, “I’ll talk to the men. They’ll understand why you fell so hard for her.”

“No I didn’t!” Sokka denies.

Hakoda raises his brow and Sokka blushes. “…I didn’t.” he mumbles.

His father just laughs, “Whatever you say son. But just one more question…”

“What is it?”

“Is this invasion also a rescue mission?”

Sokka shifts. “Yeah.”

“I see.” Hakoda muses. He puts a hand on Sokka’s shoulder, “I don’t want to be pessimistic, but you have to understand, son, victims in these abusive relationships…well it’s *hard* for them to leave. Especially when it’s the parents who are abusing the child.”

“Dad I can do it.”

Hakoda’s smile is strained. “I know, but…just be aware. You’re asking her to leave everything she’s ever known and ever wanted. It takes a lot of strength and fortitude to let go of something like that.” He pats Sokka’s back once more before he leaves, letting his son muse on those thoughts.

Ah why couldn’t his children ever choose the easier path? Well at least there’s never a dull moment. Hakoda smiles to himself, he’s looking forward to meeting this princess who caught his son’s eye.

Miraculously Aang wakes up soon after, and Katara is immediately by his side. She helps him sit up slowly and tries to keep him in bed.

“Aang you need to take it easy.” Katara urges. “You’re still healing and you need rest.”

“I feel like I’ve rested for a lifetime.” Aang replies. “What happened? The last thing I remember, Azula shot me with lightning in the caverns.”

Katara glares, “She did, and you nearly died. We got there just in time to save you and Sokka. I had to use all the water from the spirit oasis to heal you.”

Aang nods. “And what about Uncle Iroh and Zuko?”

Katara’s expression sours. “Zuko sided with her sister, and Uncle Iroh bought us time to get you out. I think he’s captured now.”

Aang’s face falls. “I can’t believe that happened. I was so sure Zuko would have gone with us.”

“Well you can take the princess out of the Fire Nation, but you can’t take the Fire Nation out of the princess. She betrayed your trust. All our trusts.” Katara says vehemently.

Aang seems to chew on this. “Sokka must be so sad.” He says.

Katara scoffs. “You’d be surprised. He’s insisting that she tried to *save* you. I think he’s just in denial. It’s not healthy.”

The young boy swivels his head to her. “Wait he said that? That’s great news! So maybe she’s not fully on Azula’s side.”

The water bender looks incredulously at him. “Aang didn’t you hear me? I just said she sided with Azula and as far as we know she could have also shot you with lightening. Sokka claims that she took out some of the power, but I don’t believe him. He’s not a fire bender, he doesn’t know what the movements look like.”

Aang rubs his head, full of hair which is a strange sensation. “Well, he might have. They did hang around a lot during Ba Sing Se.”

“…wait *what*?”

“Uh…Zuko is Lin. That mysterious girlfriend we were teasing him about? I mean I only found out because Iroh told me while we were going to rescue him and—whoa Katara where are you going?”

Katara isn’t listening anymore. She’s stomping out of his room towards the deck. Aang groans. He just woke up and he’s already causing the siblings to fight. *Great*.

“Uh-oh.” Toph mutters.

“What? Is something happening to the boiler?” Sokka asks.

“No but something’s about to explode.” Toph says and motions for Sokka to turn around. He sees the absolutely terrifying, murderous eyes of his sister.

“Oh shit.” He mumbles. The waves are rocking their ship, and the other warriors look worried. Sokka goes up to her and says, “Katara calm down, let’s just go down and talk this out—”

“No!” she yells. “We’re dealing with this here and now.” she demands.

Sokka winces. “Katara please, you need to calm down.”

“Oh no Sokka you don’t get to make anymore demands.” She growls. “I can’t believe you didn’t tell us that Zuko was in Ba Sing Se!”

Ugh he really should have cleared this up sooner.

“Katara, they were just living peacefully.”

“Were *they*? Zuko helped her sister take over Ba Sing Se. She was probably the reason why Azula was able to infiltrate the city and take over the Dai Li.”

“No Katara that’s not what—”

“Don’t interrupt me! I can’t believe you’re *defending* her. She’s Fire Nation! The same people who killed mom!”

“She helped us in the North!”

“Because it benefited her country! Not because she’s a good person! Only a complete psycho would want to bring destruction to their country!”

“Zhao did! She’s not as bad as Zhao or Azula.”

Katara rolls her eyes and waves her hands around, “Oh well thank Tui and La, Zuko’s not as bad as Zhao or Azula.” She says sarcastically. “That doesn’t change the fact that she hunted us down and made our lives miserable for a good part of this year.”

Sokka has to take a breath, “No it doesn’t, but she’s *changed*. She’s trying to do better.”

“No you just think she’s changed! She’s just manipulating you Sokka! What she just bats her eyelashes and you’re suddenly on team Zuko?”

“That’s not what happened.” Sokka says. “I saw her change, Katara. I watched her struggle through this, she didn’t help Azula get in, she didn’t even know her sister was there, and she didn’t help Azula take down Aang.”

“Katara he’s telling the truth.” Toph adds, but Katara just swings her glare at Toph.

“He just *thinks* he’s telling the truth, Toph.” Katara snarls, “And did you know about it?”

“…yeah. I followed Sokka’s footsteps to a seamstress shop. And I recognized the heartbeat and voice.”

Katara’s eyes blaze. “And you didn’t tell us?”

“I asked Toph not to tell you.” Sokka defends. “And I promised Zuko that I wouldn’t tell you guys about her.”

His sister looks speechless. She opens and closes her mouth a few times before finally shouting and a whip of water comes hurling towards Sokka, splashing him and making him fall his ass.

“Woah Katara!” Toph exclaims. “That was uncalled for!”

“Shut up Toph!”

“You don’t tell *me* to shut up!”

“I will when you hide important secrets from me!!” Katara screams. “You never cared about stopping the war! You were just using us to get away from your shitty parents!’

Toph clenches her fists, “Okay Sugarqueen, that’s it!” the earth bender slams her foot down and brings up chucks of metal plating. Katara is ready with a huge wave of water coming overhead and she—

“What is going on?!” Hakoda shouts.

Everyone freezes.

“Toph, Katara, put your hands *down*.” He says authoritatively.

The two girls slowly bring down their elements. Toph at least looks a little sorry, but Katara’s eyes are defiant.

“Did you know about this? Did you know Sokka was galivanting with the enemy?” She asks.

Hakoda sighs. “Yes he told me—”

“And you didn’t do anything about—”

“Katara.”

She clamps her mouth shut. Hakoda’s tone brokers no argument or interruptions. He looks hard on his daughter, and nearly sighs. Her eyes have deep bags, there’s tiredness in every line of her body, and her skin looks pale.

He puts a hand on her shoulder. “Katara, get some sleep. When you wake up, we’ll have a discussion.”

“But—”

“That is an order from your father and your Chief.”

She presses her lips together and she looks like she’s about to argue, but then Aang’s voice rings out.

“Katara, please sleep.” Aang says. They all turn to the Avatar who’s leaning against his staff to keep himself upright. “You’ve been healing me for nearly two weeks nonstop. You need rest. *Please.* We’ll talk when you’re rested. I don’t want to see you guys fighting.”

At this Katara finally nods stiffly. She brushes past her father and goes to Aang. She lets him lean against her and helps him to his room.

Hakoda releases a sigh and helps Sokka up. “Are you alright?”

Sokka nods, “Yeah that wasn’t the worst hit I’ve experienced.”

“Think she’ll actually listen after a good nap?” Toph asks.

“I think so. At the very least, it’ll help keep her calmer.” Sokka replies and he scrubs a hand over his face. “What a fucking mess.”

“Tell me about it.”

The next day, Katara looks a little better. The deep bags have slightly lessened and she looks like she got a decent day’s rest. They’re all sitting in Hakoda’s room, with the chief near the corner watching the kids with careful eyes. He’s only going to jump in if it looks like a brawl might start, but this problem is between the young teens.

Sokka is the first to start. He looks to Katara and says, “I’m sorry I never told you about Zuko. I didn’t mean to make you feel left out of the group.”

Katara presses her lips together but she nods and says, “I’m sorry I hit you with my bending yesterday. I should have stayed calmer.”

“…Will you listen to the whole story?” Sokka asks.

“Okay.” Katara acquiesces. “I don’t like the idea of you being so close to the Zuko, but I want to hear your reasoning. You’re not a complete moron after all.”

Sokka chuckles. “No I’m not, but you have to promise not to interrupt. Any of you.” He says looking to all three people sitting. Toph and Aang nod quickly, and Katara does after a few seconds of deliberation.

So Sokka starts from the time he stayed to look after Zuko and Iroh in the abandoned town.

By the end, all three have varying degrees of sympathy and understanding in their faces.

“I can’t believe her father did that.” Aang whispers.

“Yeah and I thought my parents were bad.” Toph says. “At least I always knew they loved me in their weird way.”

Katara is silent still, but she’s contemplative and a lot less angry.

Aang turns to Sokka, “You know I think she did try to save me.” he says. “When the lightening first connected it felt like an indescribably heat and pain, but a second later, it was…weaker. I thought maybe that might have been because Azula couldn’t make a prolonged blast, but maybe Zuko did take out a lot of the energy that would have hit me.”

Sokka grins. “Yeah and she blasted it towards the Dai Li that had me hostage.”

“That would explain the scorched Earth and unconscious agents.” Toph says. “I thought Azula had missed, but that never made sense in my head. She’s way too good to miss so badly.”

Aang nods and then pauses. “I never told you guys this, but…Zuko actually saved me before. When you guys were sick with fever, I was getting frogs and I got captured by Zhao. She rescued me and got hit. When she came to, I asked if we could have been friends, but she just…”

“Threw a fireball in your face?” Sokka ends.

“Yeah. Did she tell you?”

Sokka nods. “Remember that robe with the frogs and boomerang?”

“I mean I don’t, but I remember Twinkletoes and Sugarqueen commenting on it.” Toph replies.

“Well she made me that after you kept telling her that the frogs were important for us.”

Aang snickers, “Yeah I guess it wouldn’t make sense to her. She’s surprisingly good at sewing though.”

“Good enough for the king.” Sokka preens, but Toph smacks his arm.

“He wasn’t complimenting you, Snoozles.”

“I can feel proud on her behalf!”

The two younger kids laugh, but Katara remains stubbornly silent. They pause and turn to her, but Katara just stands.

“I—I need some time to think.” She says.

Sokka nods. “Okay.”

She leaves the room, and after a minute Aang asks. “So now that I’m back what’s the plan?”

Sokka and Hakoda winces.

Right that was another problem they needed to deal with.

### End Notes

#### Chapter End Notes

**Lu Ten:** I'm gunna pull a Hei Bai and fuck Ozai up.

**Yue:** I would help. I'll drown that ass.

Thank you for reading!

## Chapter 12

---

Aang is predictably angry when he hears what they’ve done. It especially gets bad when a Fire Nation ship found them, and he had to stay hidden, even when the ship started firing on them.

“I can’t believe you told everyone I *died*.” Aang shouts.

“Technically, Zuko and Azula told everyone you died. We just never corrected it.” Sokka corrects.

Aang is not amused by that technicality.

Sokka sighs and tries to comfort the boy, “Look I know this isn’t easy. I know you think you’re abandoning the world again, but you’re not. This is just tactics and strategy. Our invasion force was significantly cut when Ba Sing Se fell, and right now our secrecy and stealth are the only things that can make the invasion possible.”

The younger boy has a stubborn look on his face, but he finally sighs, “Okay fine.”

Sokka beams, “Great! Thanks for being so understanding.”

Aang just grumbles and trudges to his room. Toph snorts besides Sokka, “Well that went well.”

He glares down at the girl, “Not helping Toph.”

“Not trying to Snoozles.”

What is with all the sass in his life?

Katara comes to his room later that night. Sokka is still up, looking through plans and schematics when she comes in. He watches her sit down on the edge of his bed and fiddles her hands.

“…I’m sorry I called you naïve and stupid.” Katara says. “I understand why you trusted her and kept her secret in Ba Sing Se.”

Sokka nods, “Okay. That’s honestly all I can ask for.”

“I don’t know…I don’t know if I’m really ready to trust her, but I’m willing to work with her if she does come aboard.”

The older sibling smiles and puts a hand on her shoulder. “I don’t expect you to trust her immediately. Things like this take a lot of time, but I’m happy that you’re willing to give it a chance.”

Katara nods and flings her arms around Sokka’s neck in a tight hug. “Thanks.” She whispers. When she pulls back there’s a grin on her face.

“I really should have known. You have a thing for women who can kick your ass.”

“Whatever Katara, I can’t help it I like a woman who can put me down.” He says with a salacious wiggle of his brow.

Katara immediately lets go of her brother and smacks him. “You’re disgusting! Eww ewww ewww! I don’t ever want to hear anything like that ever again!”

Sokka snickers. “Then don’t tease me sister.”

She rolls her eyes. “Ugh *boys*.”

&#95;&#42;&#95;&#42;&#95;

With his sibling relationship on the mend, and after spending some time at sea, Sokka brings the gang together to get some dinner on the mainland.

“Okay that sounds good!” Aang replies happily.

“Oh but you should cover your arrows.” Sokka says.

That did not go well.

Sokka is looking at the vast expansive night sky and feels like bashing his skull against the railing. But instead, he turns to the other two and asks, “Everyone got their stuff?”

“Yup.”

“Okay let’s go get our idiot friend.”

As Sokka flies overhead, he notices that the waves are acting strangely. They all seem to be heading towards Crescent Island. On a whim Sokka looks up at the bright round moon. He swears he sees Yue there pointing the way to Aang, and he urges Appa on.

*Thank you, Yue.*

They do find Aang on the remains of the island. He's staring at the beaten remains of his glider, but looking calmer than he has since he woke up.

“Aang, buddy?” Sokka prods. “You okay?”

The younger boy answers with a familiar smile, “Yeah I think I will be. Thanks for coming to get me guys.”

“No sweat Twinkletoes. We couldn’t let you just walk around the Fire Nation all by yourself.”

“It actually works out.” Sokka says. “We were going to head here anyway. We can get some intel and lay low in the last place the Fire Lord would be looking for you. Maybe you’ll even be able to absorb some Fire Bending while we’re here through some weird spirit-y thing.” He emphasizes the weirdness by wiggling his fingers.

Katara just sighs and slaps his hands away, “Sokka, I don’t think that’s how it works.”

“Whatever Katara just let me have this.”

All of them laugh lightly for the first time in weeks, and a peaceful atmosphere seems to fall onto the group.

Still, as they set up camp for the night, he feels an anxiousness inside his chest. He’s in the Fire Nation, and Zuko’s just a few islands away. He slips his hands in his pocket and holds the hair clip tightly.

&#95;&#42;&#95;&#42;&#95;

Meanwhile in Caldera City, Zuko is choosing which clothes to pack for her weekend trip to Ember Island. Apparently, Father is having a big meeting, and he wants them out of the palace for that time. It’s a little demeaning to be sent away like an errant child, but she’s been sent away once (banished) before. Besides a trip to Ember Island sounds…nostalgic. She wonders if she can find all the secret treasure spots Lu Ten and her used to have. Those days filled with innocent adventure and laughter seem like a lifetime ago.

The sea is smooth and pristine and the hippo-whale boat cuts quickly through the water. Ty Lee stretches her arms out to the sky and smiles, “I can’t wait to get to the beach to do nothing!” she exclaims, and then she turns to the rest of them. “What do you guys wanna do?”

“Try not to get burned.” Mai states dryly.

“I suppose I could chat with some of the noble’s and general’s children.” Azula answers. “I don’t really see the point of just sitting around and doing nothing.”

Ty Lee turns to Zuko, “What about you?”

“Hmm maybe go into the forest and try to find all those hiding spots Lu Ten and I found.” She says. “We buried some ‘treasure’ there and I’m wondering if it’s still there.”

“Aww that sounds so sweet! I’d help you on your treasure hunt!” Ty Lee offers.

“Thanks.”

Azula scoffs, “Why are you wasting your time in the past? They’ve probably all been taken by animals or tourists.”

Zuko shrugs, “It’s just something I want to find, Azula. It’s just my way of keeping Lu Ten close.” And she’s hoping she can bring something back for Uncle. She hasn’t gotten the courage to visit him yet, but maybe soon. And when she does, it would be nice to bring something of Lu Ten back for him.

Li and Lo meet them by the docks and show them to the beach house they’ll be staying at. It’s… underwhelming to say the least. Zuko imagined a large sprawling property, like the one their family used to go to, not this *shack*.

Judging by the other girls' faces, they think the same.

Zuko wrinkles her nose when she enters. It’s the strong musty smell of old dying people. Like a fermenting cheese about to go bad.

“Ugh.” She groans.

“Tell me about it.” Mai mutters. “It looks like the beach vomited all over the place.”

“If they keep doing poses like that, *I’m* going to vomit all over the place.” Zuko says watching Li and Lo pose for Ty Lee.

Then to everyone’s horror, the two old advisors strip off their robes to show off the bathing suit underneath.

Zuko has faced dangerous bandits, fought off spirits, went against the Avatar, but this—this is the most terrifying thing she’s ever witnessed.

The way their sagging breasts swing in a hypnotizing motion. The wrinkled folds of their skin dragging against the fabric and the way everything *jiggles* as Li and Lo do a shimmy.

*Agni, please please strike me down now.* She prays. There’s no such respite, and they all have to live with the haunting image of Li and Lo in their bathing suits for the rest of their lives.

“Well…I need to stare at the sun for a moment.” Mai says.

“I think it's empowering that they're so comfortable with their bodies.” Ty Lee remarks.

“When I become that saggy and ugly, just kill me.” Azula says.

“Gladly.” Zuko answers.

Once all four change, they head to the beach. Zuko is sharing an umbrella with Mai, and she watches Azula *destroy* a child’s sandcastle to get a spot on the beach.

Zuko sighs resignedly. “Azula, can we just go *one* day without you destroying someone’s hopes and dreams?”

“No.”

“Figures.”

It’s no surprise to see the boys start sniffing around Ty Lee. The simpering teen offers to get her stuff, which Ty Lee eagerly dumps on the man. Zuko can see Azula’s sour expression growing on her face, and she says a silent prayer for the acrobat.

“Hey, ladies. Need some help?”

Zuko and Mai turn to see two teenage boys offer a hand to them.

The princess starts, “We’ll be fi—”

“Yeah you can help set up the umbrella.” Mai interrupts, tossing their things to the boys. They eagerly get to work, and Zuko grabs Mai’s arm.

“What are you doing?” she hisses. “I could have set it up.”

Mai shrugs, “Now you don’t have to. Besides I felt like ordering some people around and pissing off your sister.”

Zuko feels a shiver go down her spine, and she turns to see Azula glaring daggers at them. She tries to suppress a sigh. “Mai if we die, I’m going to find a way to bring us back to life just so I can kill you again.”

“Fair. I would probably do the same to you.”

“All done ladies.” One of the teens say. The spot is well-made and the umbrella will provide plenty of shade for Mai and Zuko. The gloomy girl uncharacteristically smiles eagerly and plops herself down on the towel.

“Ah perfect.” she says.

The taller of the two boys goes up to Zuko and leans into her space. He gives her a winning smile and asks, “Hey wanna go get a drink with me?”

“I shou—”

“She’d love to!” Mai interrupts. “While you’re there get me an iced coconut will you? I’m parched.”

Mai doesn’t look parched at all, but Zuko sighs and goes with the teen.

“I’m Ryu by the way.” He introduces.

“Um…Rina.” Zuko lies. She doesn’t want to deal with the hassle of being recognized by her name.

Ryu proceeds to try and flirt with Zuko, who tries to just keep polite conversation. It’s not that he’s not handsome. He is. Strong corded arms, sharp jawline. He’s just not…Sokka. Every time she turns, she expects to see the tanned water tribesman telling a joke or teasing her.

It sends an ache to her heart.

“You know my dad is a general in the Fire Lord’s army. We’ve got a lot of connections and perks.” Ryu brags.

Zuko wants to roll her eyes. Her Father is the Fire Lord but you don’t see her bragging about it.

“Is that so? Must be nice.” she says instead.

“Yeah, we’ve got a nice beach house here. We can take off and hang out there?”

Alright time to get out.

“My friends waiting for her drink, and I need to stick by my little sister.” She says grabbing the coconuts.

Ryu looks like he’s about to protest, but Zuko’s already heading towards the beach again. She gives Mai her coconut and glares.

“I hate you.”

“Hate you too.” Mai retorts slurping on the cold drink. “How was buffy-dude?”

“Boring. Stop trying to push me at guys.”

Mai shrugs. “Just trying to help you get over water dude.”

“I’m *over* him.”

“Liar.”

Zuko purses her lips. “Okay well I’ll get over him.”

“Lies.”

The princess huffs. “Why are you so invested in this anyway?”

“Because I’m bored, and I’d rather push you at guys than your crazy sister.” Mai explains.

“Azula might do okay.”

Mai gives her a disbelieving look.

“What? She might. We’ve never really given her the chance.” Zuko says.

“…this is the girl who burned turtleducklings.”

“So we just don’t tell them about that. She outgrew that phase anyway.”

“What are you two gloomy faces talking about?” Azula says walking over to them.

Mai answers, “The guy flirting with Zuko.”

Azula glares. “Well, not surprised that he wasn’t interested after talking to my dull sister. But enough about Zuko’s depressing love life, I propose we play a little game of kuai ball.” She says motioning to the net and players.

Zuko groans, “Do we *have* to?”

“Yeah it’s hot, and we’ll get sweaty.” Mai adds.

A tick is forming in Azula’s brow, but Ty Lee comes in before Azula does anything drastic. “C’mon girls just *one* game.” She wheedles.

Mai and Zuko share a look, but they eventually get up and head to the court.

It goes as well as expected. Azula pretty much crushes the opponent and sets the net on fire. She’s gloating victoriously over the other players when two boys come over.

They sidle up next to Mai and Ty Lee.

“Hey, saw your game.” One of them says. “I liked your style. Why don’t you two lovely ladies come over for a party tomorrow?”

Mai looks like she’d rather jump into a pit of fire, but Ty Lee nods eagerly. Azula, not someone who wants to be left out, call to their attention.

“And what about my sister and I?” she asks motioning to her and Zuko.

The two boys look between the siblings. Their eyes seem to linger on Zuko’s full figure, and they smile. “Sure, you two should come to.”

Zuko wants to protest. She wanted to spend the next day finding Lu Ten’s treasures, but Azula is quick to silence her with a quick jab to her side.

When they get back to the beach hut, Azula huffs. “I can’t believe they wouldn’t invite *us*.” “Why didn’t you just tell them you were royalty?” Ty Lee asks.

“I suppose I just wanted to know how it felt not being royalty. Everyone always bows and kowtows to us, and I just want to experience normal life for a bit.” Azula answers. “But that still doesn’t excuse those two for not inviting me and Zuko.”

“You mean ‘invite *you’*.” Zuko corrects. “I’m going to the forest tomorrow.”

“Oh come *on*, Zuzu. You’d rather go digging for trash than go to a party?”

“Yes.”

Azula scoffs in disgust. “Fine. Do whatever you want, but *we* are going to the party.”

“Can I also pass?” Mai asks raising her hand.

“No.”

Ty Lee turns to Zuko, “After you’re done, you should join us.” She says. “It’ll probably go on for a while.”

Zuko sighs, “Fine. If I finish early; I’ll come over.”

“Just don’t forget to shower off whatever dirt and particulates you attract. I don’t want to be known as the girl with a beggar sister.”

Zuko flips her off. “For your information I *was* a beggar in the Earth Kingdom. So at one point you did have a beggar sister.”

“Ugh it’s a wonder why Father let you home.”

&#95;&#42;&#95;&#42;&#95;

Zuko heads out early and sees the other three starting to head out too. “…what are you doing?” she asks.

“Going to the party of course.” Azula replies.

“The sun’s *barely* risen.” Zuko points out.

“They said party from dusk til dawn. So, we’re going to be punctual.”

“…you know that’s not what they meant right?” Zuko asks slowly. She’s pretty bad at common social cues, but even she knows that the party won’t start until at least noon or so.

“Well how many teenage parties have *you* been to?” Azula retorts back.

“None, but—”

“Then you don’t get to talk, sister.” Azula interrupts. “Now be on your way. We’ve got a party to get to.” And she struts out the door.

Zuko sighs and looks to both Ty Lee and Mai. “…Please make sure she doesn’t maim anyone.”

“We’ll try, but no promises.” Mai answers.

“Have fun on your treasure-hunt.” Ty Lee chirps.

&#95;&#42;&#95;&#42;&#95;

It’s an easy hike to the forest, and Zuko lets herself pause and take a deep breath in the familiar air. She ignores looming beach house behind her and goes towards the tall trees, retracing the trails she and Lu Ten used to cut.

Some of the spots she finds by chance, others she remembers through vague memories. Most of the items are papers that have long since rotted, but some are pretty shells that are still quite nice and pristine. She pockets those for her Uncle.

She’s up a tree when her hand lands on a familiar shape. She pulls it out and finds a pin in the shape of a dragon. She remembers this. It’s the pin Lu Ten used to wear in his hair; Azulon had commissioned it for his third birthday. The gold remains pristine, but the diamonds and sapphires might need to use a little polish. She holds it reverently in her hands.

The royal jewelers were so mad at him for losing the heirloom, and Azulon had looked vaguely disappointed.

She can’t believe she found it after so long.

Zuko smiles to herself and pockets it to keep it safe. The sun has started on it’s downward arc in the west. She figures she has time to shower and then head to the party.

The eldest princess had no intention of coming through the front door. Too many eyes. She’d rather just sneak in, find the darkened emo corner where Mai is and sit with her for the rest of the night. She’s climbing up the poles to the balcony when she hears her sister talking.

She pauses. Azula gives a strained laugh at a *terrible* joke the other boy made, and she hears them kissing.

*Ugh I did not need to hear this*. She thinks. *But good for Azula.*

They pull away and the boy murmurs how pretty Azula is, and then Azula responds with a, “Together you and I will be the strongest couple in the entire world. We will dominate the earth!” Blue flames light up against the darkness.

Zuko would have face palmed if she wasn’t busy holding on to the pole. Instead she just bashes her head against the wood. Repeatedly.

The boy makes an excuse and leaves Azula out there alone.

Zuko sighs and quickly climbs up. She reaches the balcony and pulls herself up, sitting on the railing. Azula doesn’t even send a glance her way, just stays quiet.

“So…nice party.” Zuko starts awkwardly.

Azula doesn’t even comment on her sister’s terribly awkward start. She just sighs and leans back against the railing.

“How much of that did you hear?” Azula asks.

“Pretty much everything.”

“Hm. Well, I guess if there is one thing I’m not perfect at it’s flirting.”

“You’re also fourteen.” Zuko adds. “All fourteen-year-olds are pretty bad at flirting.”

“Ty Lee isn’t.”

“Ty Lee is a special case.” Zuko retorts.

There’s silence, and then a breath.

“How did you do it?” Azula asks. “How did you…connect with boys?”

Zuko laughs, “I didn’t really. I guess I just managed to snag the few that were attracted to an awkward angry turtleduck. Plus, I never really *dated* any of them.” Sokka’s face comes to mind, but she pushes that aside. “I just slept with them.”

“You slut.” Azula says without any heat.

“I was a banished princess roaming through the Earth Kingdom. Didn’t seem like the time to be dating, and I still had needs.”

“…I guess you make a fair point. You’re at least responsible about it.”

…

“His joke was so *stupid*.” Zuko insults.

Azula grins sharply. “Wasn’t it? Are all men so insipid?”

“A lot of them are.”

“Ugh it physically pained me to laugh at that that ridiculous joke. And then I had to compliment his arms. *His arms*. That was probably the only decent thing about him.”

“He had a nice jawline.”

“You’re right it was quite sharp. Sharp enough to pierce through the hull of an imperial battle-class cruiser.”

Zuko snorts. “Pft did you actually tell him that?”

“No…I said that about his outfit.”

Zuko starts laughing. “Oh Agni, I wish I was there to see that.”

Azula laughs with her, and this time it’s a genuine laugh. “Well it impressed him enough to come out here with me.”

“You were doing very well.” Zuko says, “Until the whole world domination thing.”

“What’s the point of having a partner that doesn’t have the same ambitions as you?”

“You don’t always have to have the same ambitions, ‘Zula.” Zuko says. “You just have to be able to respect each other’s dreams and ambitions and know that sometimes…sometimes their dreams might lead them far away.”

Azula looks contemplatively at her sister. “I thought you never dated anyone.”

“I didn’t.”

They stay that way, Azula slightly leaning into Zuko’s arm. The siblings watching the stars.

“Hey, wanna burn some stuff down?” Zuko offers.

“What stuff?”

“The old crap in our beach house.”

Azula’s eyes shine. “Lets.”

Mai and Ty Lee tag along with the siblings and watch as Azula and Zuko bring out piles and piles of portraits and letters. They sit together a they watch the fire grow hot and large, reaching into the dark sky.

Azula pauses when she gets to a portrait of Ursa and her. She sneers and rips it apart before throwing it in the fire.

“Mother always loved you best.” Azula says to her sister.

Zuko sighs, “She loved both of us equally.”

“She called me a monster. She’s not wrong of course, but it still hurt.”

Zuko places a hand on Azula’s bare shoulder. “You scared her a lot, but that doesn’t mean she never loved you.”

Azula pauses and then shrugs her sister’s hand off her, throwing more things into the fire.

“It doesn’t matter now.” she mutters.

&#95;&#42;&#95;&#42;&#95;

When they get back to Caldera City, Zuko heads to the prison. Trepidation heavy in her heart. She nods to the guards, and slowly walks down the hall. Her steps echoing ominously.

She looks to the guard on hand and says, “Please leave us.”

Ming bows, and leaves the princess with her uncle.

Zuko sits down slowly and pauses. Words seem to fail her as she tries to find something to say. Uncle has his back turned to her, and he isn’t showing any signs of turning. She sighs and proceeds to take out the shells first, putting them near the bars.

“I went to Ember Island this weekend.” She starts. “Remember when Lu Ten and I used to go on adventures and bury our treasure? I went through all our old trails and found these. A lot of the stuff rotted or ended up broken, but some of these are still nice. I know you liked collecting them.”

She runs her hands on a particularly vibrant red shell. Then she goes into her pocket and pulls out Lu Ten’s hair pin.

“But you wouldn’t believe one of the things I found.” She says putting it down with the shells. “It’s Lu Ten’s hair pin.”

Iroh turns slightly, hands twitching at his side.

“…I thought you might like it.” She whispers.

The silence is deafening, and she feels frustration bubble up in her.

“I know—I know I’ve disappointed you. I know you wanted me to leave with the Avatar, but I—I just couldn’t. I’m not strong enough Uncle. I couldn’t make myself leave this all behind me. I thought I’d finally be happy where I am, now that father’s proud of me but I’m *NOT*! I’m just always so empty and numb and angry and I just don’t—I just don’t know what to do anymore.”

She takes a breath and stands. “I wasn’t even good enough to get you out of here. I’m sorry Uncle I’m just…a failure, as usual.”

She turns to leave, but a pinch on her cloak stops her. Iroh is looking at her with watery golden eyes, and Zuko can’t help herself.

She kneels and reaches out to him. The bars don’t even let her whole hand through, but she’s gripping tightly to the little bit of her Uncle she can touch. Iroh gives her a small smile, and reaches his hands up, tucking a stray lock of hair behind her ears and wiping a tear.

He takes the pin and puts it in her hands. “Keep this.” he says.

Zuko shakes her head, “No it’s—it’s yours you should have it.”

But Iroh just smiles, “I have all these other pretty shells my daughter and late son collected for me.”

A choked sob makes its way out of her lips and she bows her head against the bars. Iroh hums to her as she cries against the prison cell. When she’s done, she looks up, “I promise I’ll get you out of here.”

Iroh shakes his head, “No, you have other things you need to worry about. I’m comfortable here.”

“But—”

“No, Zuko. Please. Do not try to help me escape.” Iroh orders.

The princess wants to deny his request, but…Iroh’s eyes burn furiously into her. She swallows and nods.

Iroh relaxes and leans back. He touches her cheek one last time and says, “Stay safe, my fire-lily.”

“I will.” And she leaves, hands clutching Lu Ten’s pin.

&#95;&#42;&#95;&#42;&#95;

Sokka has a strict schedule he wants to keep to, but it’s so hard when you’re traveling with a bunch of excitable kids and bleeding-heart type sisters.

Case in point, the dirty fishing town they’re in.

“Katara the only way to help them is to end the war. Ergo we need to stick to the schedule. We already wasted a night for Aang’s stupid cave dance party!” Sokka exclaims.

But Katara gets that stubborn look on her face, “But Sokka we can’t just let these people live like this!”

“Yes, we *can*. Because there’s more important things to do right now.” Sokka replies bluntly. His sister goes red, and she throws her hands up.

“Ugh I can’t even believe you! You’re a heartless *jerk!*” she shouts stomping into the underbrush.

Sokka sighs. He’ll make it up to her later, but when he goes to set up camp, he comes face to face with Aang who’s looking at him with big baby saber-tooth-lion-moose-eyes. Sokka scowls and pushes him aside.

“No Aang. We’re not helping them. We don’t have time and we wouldn’t be able to do much.” “But Sokka…”

“No. We don’t have the time or luxury like we did back when we helped out in the Earth Kingdom.” Sokka says. “For one, we’re in enemy territory where everyone wants to see you *dead*. And two, we need to be *extremely* careful with any bending we do. You don’t know fire bending yet, so we’re essentially going into fights without any bending powers, and if we do bend, we have to be prepared to annihilate everyone we go against.”

Aang swallows. “Sokka, I don’t want to ki—kill people.”

“I don’t either Aang, but that’s the reality we’re facing here. If we bend, we better make sure it’s a subtle use of bending or we make sure those people can’t talk anymore.”

The two younger kids look pale, but they understand what he’s asking them. They nod.

“Okay, we’ll leave here Sokka.” Aang acquiesces.

Sokka gives him a small smile and pats his back, “I’m sorry. I don’t mean to be harsh, but I’m just trying to protect all of us.”

Aang and Toph go in for a hug.

“We know, Snoozles.” Toph says. “We’d be in a lot of hot water without you.”

Sokka smiles and lets himself snuggle into the hug.

Of course, their lives are never easy, and even when Sokka accounts for delays and trouble along the way, things always seem to blow up in their faces. Literally this time.

Sokka is looking despondently at the ruined remains of the factory, and nearly pulls all his hair out. He swivels to Katara and Aang.

“You *had* to blow up a factory, didn’t you?”

“Well it worked!” Katara argues. “Now the people will be able to start fishing again with the factory out of the way.”

“Okay, so what are they going to tell the supervisors who come investigating once they realize their shipments haven’t come through?”

“…what?”

“Oh wait, did you kill all the soldiers that were guarding the factory?”

“…no.”

“So who exactly are they going to come for when they see that the factory went KABLOO-Y?”

“They know the Painted Lady did that!” Katara says.

“No, they don’t!” Sokka retorts. “They just know some crazy person dressed up in a weird costume came in and destroyed their factory, and they’re going to go to the village and make them pay.”

Katara pales. “No-no they wouldn’t—they’re innocent.”

“Not in their eyes. For all they know, the village people finally snapped and took down the factory.”

The girl slumps and puts her head in her hands. “What am I going to do?”

Sokka sighs and kneels next to her, putting a hand on her head. “Well first, *we’re* going to need a few props.”

Most of the soldiers flee at the sight of Katara’s magic acts, but two stand their ground. They don’t fight. They only quirk a brow up and sigh.

“Look we know it’s not actually a spirit.” One of them says with exasperation.

Sokka starts to sweat and Katara does a horrible job acting like a spirit.

“Uhhh nooo I am a spirit! I’m the Painted Lady!”

The other solider sighs and puts a hand on the other, “Chang, don’t. They’re just going to keep on doing it.”

Chang sighs and nods, “Alright ‘Painted Lady’ we’ll be telling our supervisors that there was a malfunction in the blast furnace that caused destabilization in the plant. There’s substantial damage and it will take a while for the environmental poisoning to subside before we can start building a new plant. Is that good with you?”

“Uh…yes. Yes, the Painted Lady is pleased.”

“Okay. Let’s go Yama, I thought I was done with all this weirdness when we left the princess.”

“Face it, our lives will never be the same.” Yama sighs. “Speaking of the princess, we better start making our way to Caldera City. I wanna make sure she’s okay there.”

Sokka nearly blows their cover when he lunges at the men, desperate to ask for any information about the princess, but Toph holds him down.

“Sokka!” she hisses. “You’re going to get us caught.”

He has to force his hands to stop trembling as he painfully watches the two men leave. He wants to shake them by their ridiculous shoulder spikes and demand to know where Zuko was and how she’s doing, but Toph is strong, and Sokka knows better.

His hand clamps down on the pin he always keeps in his pocket, and he breathes like Zuko once taught him. Toph’s hands calming him as she whispers, “Sokka, we’ll find her. We’ll find her. Just wait.”

He watches them leave with a heavy pit settling in his stomach. This is good though; he has confirmation now. The princess is at Caldera City. Right where the invasion force will be.

&#95;&#42;&#95;&#42;&#95;

A letter comes early that day for Zuko. She smiles when she sees that it’s from Master Piandao, and he’s inviting her to visit him in Shu Jing. She hesitates. Her father did allow her free reign to go wherever she wanted in the Fire Nation, so long as she gets back to the capital immediately when he calls for her. She makes up her mind and calls one of the servants to her room.

Azula scoffs when she hears her sister is leaving to see her sword master in Shu Jing.

“Why would you even want to speak to your old teachers? They lose their use once you suck out all the knowledge and experience from them.”

Zuko pulls a disgusted face at the description. “First, gross. Second, just because you got all your teachers imprisoned or banished doesn’t mean everyone else does that.”

“Oh, I know everyone doesn’t do that.” Azula replies flippantly. “I’m the only one with enough talent and power to demand that of their teachers.”

“Well there you have it.” Zuko mutters. “Now can you leave? I’m trying to pack.”

“Fine. But if you ask me this is a waste of time.”

“Good thing I didn’t then.” Zuko retorts. Honestly, Azula can be such a brat.

The boat ride there is easy and relatively quick. Fat meets her by the docks, and she embraces him on sight. The older man smiles down at her, “It’s good to see you again, Princess.” He gives the scar a severe look, but shakes it off with a genial smile again. “Come. Piandao is waiting, and I’ve prepared your favorite: curry noodles.”

Zuko smiles, “Thank you, Fat. I always enjoy your cooking.”

Piandao doesn’t look any different from when she last saw him three years ago. He has a few more grey hairs, but he smiles and laughs just as he did the first time she met him. He hugs her tightly and smiles just as brightly as Fat.

“Princess Zuko. I’m so happy to see you safe and healthy.” He says. “Come come, let’s get you settled, and we can have dinner. I’ve already had your old room prepared.”

At dinner, Piandao regals her with stories of students he taught, and how he essentially retired from teaching. In turn, Zuko talks a little bit about her own adventures.

Piandao grins proudly when she retells the story of how she broke into Pohaui stronghold with only her dao. He nods approvingly at her stealth and tactics.

“Very impressive, Princess Zuko. I would expect nothing less from you.” He praises. “Those soldiers need a little challenge to keep them fresh.”

She smiles, but then her face falls. “I lost those dao blades though when pirates blew up my ship. I’m so sorry. I know you and Lu Ten worked hard on them.”

Fat nearly has to perform the Hien Li maneuver to save Piandao from choking. Zuko is by her master’s side instantly and offers him water, which he takes. They give the poor man a minute to regain his breath and bearings before he looks incredulously at the princess.

“Pirates blew up your ship?!” he exclaims hoarsely.

“Yes. Zhao send them thinking an explosion could kill me.” Zuko answers.

Piandao and Fat share an exasperated look, but refrain from saying anything.

“But don’t worry, Uncle killed them and Zhao was dragged into the Spirit World.” She finishes.

Piandao smiles slightly, “I wouldn’t expect anything less from you Uncle. He loves you like a daughter.”

Zuko smiles slightly at that. “Yes, but…I’ve disappointed him.”

“Hmm children do things that will disappoint their parents.” Piandao replies. “But that doesn’t make them love their children any less.”

A soft smile graces her face. She prays and hopes that’s the case, but that dark negative side of her doesn’t think it’s possible.

“Perhaps tomorrow, we can do some sparring and we can start work on some new dao blades for you.” Piandao offers. “Master Chong Li has finished a new batch of *tamahagane* ores. I’ll take you to her to choose the ores for your new sword.”

“Oh no, master you should use them for something else. I have some dao blades.” She says.

“Bah those are from the Earth Kingdom smiths, they don’t have the right temper and metal for you to use them effectively with your bending.” Piandao says. “Consider it a belated birthday gift.”

“But I just told you I lost the ones you and Lu Ten made.” she protests.

“From an explosion. That I’m sure you nearly died from.” Piandao grumbles. “You’re getting a new set whether you like it or not.”

Zuko sighs, “Fine, but I want to earn again like last time.”

The swordmaster grins, “I wouldn’t have it any other way.”

They dual at sunrise, Zuko letting Agni’s soft rays fueling the inner fire in her soul. Piandao stands at the other end, his blade gleaming in the soft light. She pulls out the dao blades and stands at the ready.

The world breathes

And releases.

Her twin blades meet the flat end of Piandao’s sword, and he parries it. She twists and glides along the ground. The blunt edge of swords sliding close to her body as she strikes with ferocious power. But Piandao holds his ground, his blade guarding close, and striking during her transitions. He’s trying to break her root.

Her swords tilt down and her elbow bends up, blocking a strike, and she uses that force to bring the blade around her head as she steps through and uses the momentum of her body to make a downward cut on Piandao. The master steps away in time and smiles.

They pause.

And continue.

Sweat forms on her brow as the sun rises higher, her blades reflecting Agni’s fiery light. A spin, a ring of blades. The sound of the air being cut as she spins her blades fiercely.

An opening forms and she takes it.

Elbows stay close to her body, and she uses the energy of her opponent to get in close. Her body flowing like water as she moves in. One blade blocks his and the other is at his unprotected side.

They stop.

And Piandao grins. They fall back and bow.

“That…was incredible.” Piandao praises. “You’ve truly grown into an amazing swordsman.”

Zuko beams. “I can only hope I’ll reach the same levels as you, Master.”

Piandao laughs, “Oh I have no doubt. You’ll be the greatest swordsman of your generation.”

She blushes. “Perhaps not…there’s someone with quite a lot of potential.” She says.

The master raises his brow. “Really?”

Zuko nods eagerly. “I helped with some of his sword training back in Ba Sing Se. He’s incredible. His style is nothing like I’ve ever seen, and he’s so smart and strong and—”

And she sees the look of bemusement on her master’s face. She clears her throat. “Ehem well… he’s just, very good.”

“So, it would seem.” Piandao teases. “I should like to meet this young man someday.”

“…I want to see him again too.” She says softly.

The old swordsmaster pretends not to hear and lets her change into fresher clothes before they head to Master Chong Li’s refinery. The metal master’s place sits high above the mountain of Shu Jing, and many men and women scurry around hard at work as they maintain the flames and shovel the raw materials in the furnace.

Master Chong Li prowls like a jaguar. Watching each flame carefully and spitting her fire into them as needed. She spots them coming in and bows.

“Master Piandao. Princess Zuko. A pleasure to see you again.” She says. “Come. Let us see the metals.”

In her cooler office, she spreads out the ores, and Zuko carefully picks each one up, holding them against her chest and letting her inner fire guide her.

One in particular sings to her and makes the fire in her roar.

Chong Li grins when the princess chooses that one.

“A good choice.” The old woman nods. “The material it came from had bits of meteorite in them, and the fires that tempered it were particularly hot that day. It is a fitting ore for you.”

Zuko bows to the master and thanks her. “Thank you, Master Chong Li. I hope I will wield the blade of this ore well.” She says.

Chong Li returns the bow. “And I hope my ore will give you a magnificent blade.”

They leave with the ore and a few others. Back at the house, Fat has started heating the furnace, and the three start on making the weapon. Zuko lets her flames heat the furnace and metal. The sounds of pounding hammers echo through the forge.

It takes them another three days to complete the weapon, and Zuko is tired from the near constant work. But her swords are finished, and they gleam in the sunlight. She does quick experimental twirls and spins, and nods. The balance, the weight, the handle. It’s perfect.

Piandao smiles as he watches her practice in the courtyard. It almost feels like the days when she was only a little taller than knee-high and swinging a pair of wooden swords while her cousin claps exuberantly in the background. Now she’s grown into an able deadly warrior. She’s seen and experienced things that most wouldn’t dare to dream in a lifetime.

He can see it in the lines of her stance, the dart of her eyes, and the grip on her sword. She has fought and fought and clawed her way through the many obstacles in her path that led her here. It makes him ache to see how fast she’s grown, but so so proud.

The princess turns to her master with a wide smile, “How about another spar?”

Piandao laughs. “Gladly.”

Just before she leaves, a meteor strikes and a fledgling warrior comes knocking at his door.

**Yue:** Remind me again what you used to say...? Oh, that's right, 'You burn brighter than the fire that I bend'.

**Lu Ten:** That was a great line!

**Yue:** Agni? Back me up here.

**Agni:** That was a terrible line. You've got none of your father's talent.

**Lu Ten:** The *disrespect.*

## Chapter 13

### Notes

#### Chapter Notes

**Note:** Hi guys! Sorry I think there was a misunderstanding in the last chapter. When I used 'eve of departure' I always thought it meant the day before the event, but I see that it could also mean 'morning of' so I apologize for the confusion. I've just edited the last line of the previous chapter. Sorry again for any confusion.

### Text

---

Sokka waits anxiously by the door. The impressive gates loom over him, and they hardly make a sound when someone answers the door.

The man looks at the boy with a critical eye. “May I help you?”

“Yes, I’m…I’m here to see Master Piandao.” Sokka says. “I want to train under him.”

The older man looks contemplative and nods. “The master turns almost everyone away. What have you brought to prove your worth?”

“Um…well uh—”

He sighs, “Let’s just go, young man.”

He introduces himself as ‘Fat’ (an unfortunate name) and leads him up the paved road to an expansive castle that overlooks the city. Sokka swallows the nervous lump in his throat and forges ahead. All along the walls of the hallway are various portraits of what he can only assume are past students. One in particular catches his eye.

Her face is younger and unmarred. There’s a hint of a smile on her face, and her hair is long and pulled into a high pony-tail (*It’s a phoenix tail!*).

The man stops when he notices the teen’s gaze on that portrait. There’s a bemused smile on his face when he says, “That’s Crown Princess Zuko. She’s his best student. A true sword prodigy. The way she handles the blade is truly beautiful.”

Sokka swallows and presses his hand tightly to his side. He wants to reach out and touch so bad, but he refrains. “S-so I’ve been told.” He answers hoarsely. “I—I’ve heard the princess returned.”

“Yes, she’s residing in Caldera City. It was a good day for our nation when we saw our princess come home.”

Sokka turns to the man, and he sees the look of relief and happiness crossing his face. It’s good to see this. Sometimes, Sokka needs a reminder of the other people residing in the Fire Nation. The ones who just want to live in peace like all the rest of them.

“Is she happy?” he asks.

Fat looks to the boy critically. “I can’t say. I only live here.”

Sokka nods and just let’s himself take in the picture of Zuko, happy and so carefree.

After a few minutes, Fat asks, “Shall we continue?”

“Uh—yes yes. Sorry I just…got caught up.”

“Hm so it would seem.” He says almost a little knowingly. Sokka feels his instincts trying to tell him something, but he pushes it aside for now.

He’s led to a spacious room, where a greying regal man is sitting in front of the wide windows. His back is turned, but Sokka can see that he’s dragging his brush slowly against the white paper, creating thick bold lines in stark contrast against the page.

“Master Piandao.” Fat introduces and leaves Sokka with the swordsmaster.

He doesn’t even look up when he asks, “And who might you be?”

“I’m…em Wang Fire.” He says.

There’s a strange smacking sound near the giant vases, and Sokka turns to look when the master clears his throat.

“Wang…Fire you say?”

“Yup that’s me. Wang Fire. Colonist from…the colonies.”

Again, there’s another sound coming from the vase but it sounds almost like moaning. Sokka points to it.

“Uh not to be rude but is there something wrong with your vase?”

“Oh no. It’s a wailing vase.” He answers in a deadpan. “We often have those to store our frustrations in, and they say the echoes of your moans will sometimes come out.”

The Fire Nation was really weird. Sokka concludes. But he presses on and bows low.

“Master Piandao, I’m here to ask you to teach me the ways of the sword.” He asks.

Piandao finally puts his brush down and turns to look at the young man. There is potential in him. The way he carries himself, the look in his eyes. They all show a strong warrior under the veneer of a teenage boy.

“And why do you think you’re worthy to be my pupil?” Master Piandao asks. “I have had nobles, royalty and peasants alike come in my halls. Some have come a long way to train with me, but I’ve turned many down. What makes you, different from the rest of them?

Sokka keeps his eyes firmly to the ground and sighs, “I don’t know. I—I honestly don’t now if I’m worthy. But I know that I have a lot to learn, and that if I don’t try to get better…I won’t be able to live with myself.”

“You may fail.”

He smiles thinking of Zuko and all the times she failed and failed but never once stopped. “That’s okay.” he answers. “Failure is inevitable, but it doesn’t mean I won’t lay down and wallow in it.”

Piandao smiles down at the boy. There’s a fire in this water tribe boy, and he likes it. The master stands and picks up his sword.

“Then let’s see what you are worth, Wang Fire.”

Sokka grins and shoots up. “I won’t let you down!” he exclaims. He doesn’t hear it, but his exclamations kept him from hearing the small squeal of joy coming from behind the ‘wailing vase’.

&#95;&#42;&#95;&#42;&#95;

Fat looks at the girl exasperatedly. She’s currently hiding behind some bushes, watching Sokka train with her master.

“You could just say hello. Like a normal person.” Fat says.

“I can’t.” she whispers, though the temptation to just jump up and run over to the boy is great. She doesn’t know if she can face him after her betrayal in the catacombs. More than that, she doesn’t know if she could stay strong and not leave with him.

“I’m pretty sure he hates me.” Zuko says. “I did…I did something bad when we parted.”

Fat hums in thought. “Seems to me that he might not be as mad as you think, princess.”

“How do you know?”

“Because he looked at your picture quite fondly.”

Zuko blushes, “I was his…friend. He might just be remembering the times we had together.”

“Oh yes I’m sure every boy you know looks at your pictures like you’re the sun and moon themselves.” Fat retorts dryly.

“Fat!” She cries in embarrassment. “It wasn’t like that.”

“So sorry, Princess did I miss seeing you in the hallway when this happened? I thought you were hiding behind the ‘wailing vase’ in Piandao’s office.”

She purses her lips. “I just…I don’t really have the courage to go to him right now.”

Fat nods in understanding. “Well, it would be cruel anyway. You’ll be leaving tonight.”

“Yes.” She says absently.

“He is quite worried about you.” Fat divulges. “He asked if you were happy.”

Zuko bites her lip. No, she’s not happy. Not really. There are small pockets of time where she does feel happiness, but never at the palace. Never in the place where she thought she’d be the happiest. A thought crosses her mind.

“I should be turning him into my Father.” She says. “He’s a danger to the Fire Nation, and he may have found a way to help the Avatar survive Azula’s blast.”

“Perhaps. We do have hawks nearby.”

She grits her teeth. It should be so easy, so simple. He represents rebellion and change, everything that the Fire Nation is going against, but…

His moves are a blended mix of fire nation and water tribe. Sprinkled in with the firmness of earth and the freedom of air. It’s everything her father stands against.

But it’s beautiful. It’s harmony and dissonance all at once, and she can’t imagine a world without it. If the Fire Nation were to take over the rest of the world, it would just be awash in red and gold. She takes a breath and turns to Fat.

“I need to go.” she says. “I have to finish something. Do you have any clean white handkerchiefs?”

Fat smiles, “Of course. And I assume you want some needle and thread?”

“Please.”

---

Training with Piandao is everything he ever dreamed of and more. Just on the first day he drills him in everything that he knows. The master looks at Sokka’s form appraisingly.

“You have good form and balance.” He compliments.

Sokka beams, “Thank you, Master. I actually had someone teach me before I came to you.”

“Oh? Do tell.”

“She’s really amazing.” Sokka gushes. “She’s a bender *and* a master swordsman. When she fights it’s like she literally embodies fire. The only time I’ve ever beaten her in a spar is when I trick her. Most of the time, I end up landing on my ass.”

“How interesting.” Piandao remarks with a bemused smile. “You’ll have to tell me more on a later date. For now, let’s go through that set once more.”

He trains until the sun sets and his muscles ache. And he does it the next day. And the next. Interspersed between the session, Piandao teaches him calligraphy, meditation, painting, and other activities. All to teach him how to read the environment and learn what makes Sokka’s style of fighting unique. It’s honestly the very thing he needed. Especially with the invasion looming ahead.

After a particularly grueling session with Fat, Piandao nods and says, “Now you’re ready to make your sword.”

He takes him to the forge and shows him various ores, but nothing catches his eye. Finally, he’s struck by an idea and rushes out. He brings a chunk of the meteorite to the master and smiles. “I’m going to make a space sword.” He declares.

Piandao and Fat can’t stop the laughter bubbling in their throats, but when Sokka questions them, they just grin and smile, waving off his concerns.

It takes three days to finish his sword, and Piandao brings him and his friends to his office to present the boy his blade.

“Wang Fire.” Piandao announces. “You have shown your tenacity, ingenuity, and ferocity. I see a true warrior spirit within you, and I am proud to give you this sword.” He sheathes it and the black metal gleams in the sunlight. “Bear it well with honor, you deserve it.”

Sokka looks at the beautiful blade, and then to his master. A heavy weight sits in his stomach as he takes the blade and looks at his reflection on the metal. He sighs and offers it back up.

“Master, thank you for all you taught me, but…I can’t accept this.”

The other three gasps behind him in shock, but Sokka continues.

“And…I’ve deceived you. I’m not Wang Fire of the colonies. I’m Sokka of the Southern Water Tribe.”

There’s echoing facepalms from behind him, but he pays them no mind as he bows and offers the sword back to his master.

“I can’t take this.” Sokka says. “I’m not worthy.”

Piandao hums thoughtfully. “I see.”

The sound of metal releasing against a sheath is the only warning he gets. Sokka narrowly misses his master’s blade, and leaps into a fighting stance. The other three stand at the ready, but Sokka puts his hands up.

“No this is my fight.” Sokka says. The betrayal and deceit are on his head, and he should be the one to pay the consequences.

Master Piandao is fierce and quick. Sokka has to use every trick in the book to get away from him. All throughout the fight, the master comments on his strategy and ingenuity. Any other time, Sokka would have greatly appreciated the praise. But when a master swordsman is out trying to decapitate you? Not helpful.

He’s able to blind the master and nearly gets out of the way, but a twig conveniently appears underfoot, making the master aware of his position.

They clash blades, and Piandao spins his sword until it flings Sokka’s blade to the wayside. The water tribesman tries to retreat, but his balance is broken and he falls. Piandao’s blade ominously close to his neck.

The other three are quick to come down and stand ready to defend their friend. But master relaxes. Killing intent dissipating in the morning air. He tilts his head up and motions to Fat who throws the sheath to him. Piandao does an impressive spin and easily sheathes the blade in midair. He wipes the dirt from his eyes and nod to the boy.

“Very good Sokka.” Piandao praises. “You are an excellent swordsman and have found your worth here.”

Sokka gaps incredulously. “Wait…so you weren’t trying to kill me?”

“No, never kill. Just seriously maim at best.”

The boy starts to open his mouth, and then shuts it with a click. Fire Nation people are just *crazy*.

Piandao turns to the others and smiles, “I’m a little too old to be fighting the Avatar.”

Well isn’t that just another kicker?

“Did you know this whole time?” Sokka asks.

The master chuckles. “I have traveled a long way, and lived a long life. I knew you were Water Tribe as soon as you walked through my doors. Also no one is named *Wang Fire*. Colonies or not.”

Sokka blushes, “Rrriiigghht. I thought that was a good name.”

Piandao quirks his eyebrow. “No. No it wasn’t. Try Li next time. There are a million Li’s.”

The teen nods and bows. “Thank you for everything, Master Piandao.”

“Thank you for being a good student, Sokka.”

As they leave, Fat calls out to them and hands them a pouch, saying, “I hope this will aid you on your journey.”

Sokka looks quizzically at the others and opens it. The first item is a white lotus tile. The second is a golden dragon hairpin wrapped in a white handkerchief.

When the handkerchief fully unfurls, Sokka feels his breath catch. In the middle is a frog sitting on a boomerang, and in the corner is a small red fire-lily. He presses it against his nose and the scent of fire-lilies and citrus overcome his senses. He dashes back towards Fat.

“Wait! Fat! Was she here?” he exclaims grabbing onto the man’s arm.

Fat nods. “Yes, she saw you enter, but had to leave soon after. Her father called her home.”

Sokka feels his heart drop. Zuko was here. She was here and she didn’t come see him. Didn’t want to go with him.

A hand on his shoulder cuts him off from his spiraling thoughts. Fat looks at him with a sympathetic eye.

“The princess is not yet ready to let go, but I believe it will be soon.” He says. “She spoke very fondly of you to the master. She called you a great swordsman.”

Sokka blinks, taken aback. “She did?”

“Yes.” Fat answer amused. “Now, shouldn’t you be going?”

Sokka leaves reluctantly, wishing he had a chance to talk to Piandao about her, but they needed to go. The invasion is getting ever closer.

“Zuko knows you were there?” Katara asks.

“Yeah, this is her work.” He says reverently stroking the fire-lily.

“Do we need to worry about it? What if she tells the Fire Lord?”

He pauses. “…I don’t think she would.” He says. “For one, if she and Azula told Ozai that they killed Aang, it would look pretty bad on them if one of their known companions happened to be here, it might imply that the Avatar is not as dead as they originally claimed.”

“But what’s your real reasoning?” Katara asks.

“My instincts say she didn’t, and I trust her.”

His sister sighs, “Alright. I guess I’ll trust you on this.”

“Thanks.”

&#95;&#42;&#95;&#42;&#95;

The palace seems more oppressive than normal. It’s filled with generals and ministers from all over the colonies and the nation. Zuko is currently being helped into her formal armor, and Azula meets her outside her door.

“Well, you’re looking more like a true royal already.” She comments. “Just don’t forget to act like it. We wouldn’t want another war room mishaps.”

Zuko glares. “Does anyone ever tell you how annoying you can be?”

“Oh, all the time. Mostly by you.”

Her heart feels like it’ll spring out of her chest at any moment as she walks towards the dais. Flames licking at her as she walks to her father’s right side. She bows and sits. Her father smiles flintly at them and announces how happy he is to have his children here when he unravels his plan. The man speaks.

“At summer’s end, Sozin’s comet will arrive, and we will once again be given the power of a thousand burning suns. We will use this power to *crush* and *burn* the earth kingdom.”

Horror starts to well in her chest as her father outlines the plans. Images start to fill her mind.

Song.

Lee.

Shandi.

Hina.

Kuei

Everyone.

They would be burned as the Fire Lord razes their land and brings them to their knees. All in the name of glory and honor.

*This isn’t honor*. *This isn’t glory.*

The words are trapped in her throat. Something yells at her to keep silent, and she does. She keeps completely silent. Unmoving. Unblinking.

When he ends, he turns to them and raises his hand. “My children, we will see through Sozin’s dream and bring glory to our family. This will be our *legacy*.”

When she gets back in her room, she runs to the bathroom to retch the last of her meal. She tears the armor off her, strewing them around her room, and pulls the topknot off her hair. She sits there, heaving and crying. Her head lifts and she comes face-to-face with her reflection. A disheveled, mess of a girl. Angered she pushes the mirror down, letting the pieces smash all over the floor.

The guards knock urgently at her door, but Zuko waves them all away. She doesn’t want to see anyone, and she throws herself on the bed, curling tightly under the sheets.

Night falls, and she hasn’t moved in hours. There’s an insistent knock at her window, and she grumpily trudges there about to shoot a fireball at whoever is disturbing her.

A hawk greets her and bows so she can get the letter form the bird. She opens it and finds a blank piece of paper.

“What the fucking?” she curses and brings her hand up to burn the paper, but suddenly dark ink starts to appear. She pauses and reads it carefully. After she puts it down, she turns to the bird and sighs.

“Don’t tell anyone I’m sneaking out okay?”

The hawk just caws in response.

Deep in the dragon catacombs, Zuko finds the last accounts of her great-grandfather Sozin.

It speaks of his close friendship with Avatar Roku, and the painful separation. It talks about his disgust at the ill-bred savagery and poverty that runs rampant in the world, and his desire to bring order and glory. Just like the Fire Nation. And then it talks of his decision to betray his longtime friend and begin the great war.

Zuko feels cold and confused. Was that it? Was that all? Why would the letter ask her to read it? It basically told her everything she knew before. Other than the friendship with Avatar Roku. Why would she need to a first-hand account of how horrible and depraved her great-grandfather was?

She rubs a hand through her hair. There’s only one person who could answer this.

The next morning, she confronts her uncle.

“Why would you send me the letter?” she asks. “Why do I need to read about great-grandfather Sozin? Are you just trying to throw all the evil deeds of our family in my face? I know we’re terrible. I know we’ve done horrible things. So *why*?”

Iroh turns and sighs, “My niece, that was never my intention. I wanted to show you the great good in your blood as well and to shed light on the battle you feel in your soul.”

“Well you did a shitty job of saying that.” she grumbles.

Iroh laughs, “Perhaps I did. But let me just say: don’t forget about your *other* great-grandfather.”

Zuko pauses. “…A-avatar Roku?”

Iroh nods and goes to a loose brick in the wall. He pulls it out and hands her a long-lost heirloom. The hairpiece of the crown prince.

“This is…”

“The same hairpiece your great-grandfather gave to your other great-grandfather.” Iroh answers. “We never told you this because Azulon ordered it to be kept a secret. The only ones here who know are me, the Fire Lord, and now you.”

“Why?” she asks. “Why would grandfather want to have Avatar Roku’s blood in me?”

Iroh sighs, “My brother wanted a bride who would give him strong fire bending children. My father agreed, and so they searched, and they finally found your mother. A descendant of Avatar Roku. She was not a bender, but they believed her blood and lineage to be potent and would give strong fire-bending children.”

“So…mother was taken? By force? Just because she’s the granddaughter of Roku?”

Iroh nods. “Yes. It was…difficult on her.”

Zuko’s lips tremble, “No wonder she left.”

Her uncle comes close and places his hands over hers as best he could. “No Zuko. She left to protect you. She would never have gone if she wasn’t forced to.”

The princess silently muses and then stands. “Uncle…I think I’m going to do something crazy.”

Iroh smiles, “I’m not surprised, but please, please be careful. For this old man’s heart.”

“I’ll try.”

He nods, “If I can make a suggestion, you might find some friends in the Hopping Dragon.”

Zuko narrows her eyes. “Isn’t that a seedy tavern near the docks?”

“The very same.”

&#95;&#42;&#95;&#42;&#95;

Zuko is dressed in a dark cloak and moves with the shadows as she travels through the docks. Even at this time of night, it’s still bustling with activity. Ships come in at all times of the day and the sailors are always ready to stop for a drink. She quietly sneaks through an opened window in the Hopping Dragon and heads towards the bar.

“What can I getcha?” the bartender asks. Zuko flounders for a bit and asks for some plum wine.

The bartender raises his brow. “We don’ serve that noble stuff here.”

“Um…just what’s on the tap then.”

He scoff, but then gets out a slightly dirty mug and fills it with an amber liquid.

“Here ya go little miss.” Zuko goes to pay, but the bartender holds up his hand.

“Already paid for.”

“…by who?”

He points to a table in the corner. “By those people.”

Zuko feels her heart race and breath catch in her throat. She takes the drink and quickly walks over to the table, not minding the spilled liquid trickling over her hand.

It’s unbelievable. It can’t be. But…

All around her, the familiar faces smile broadly.

“Look who finally joined.” Shindo says.

“Took ya long enough.” Kyoko grins. “We were wondering if your uncle ever got the message to you. These boys have been spending most of the nights drinking their asses off.”

“I’m just trying to enjoy a few nights of drinking before we head back into whatever crazy schemes our captain will lead us into.” Yama retorts.

They laugh, and Zuko can feel tears build up in her eyes. She sniffles and they pause to look at their princess.

Teruko pats her shoulders, “Aw princess. We didn’t mean to worry you. We all got out safely.”

Zuko nods. “I’m so glad you’re all okay.”

Jee smirks. “Well we did get a direct order to stay alive.” And then he takes the drink out of her hand. “But you’re still not drinking this.”

Chang laughs, “Aw c’mon Jee let her take a sip.”

“No! This is filthy. I’m not letting her get infected with whatever dirty things are floating around in here.”

Teruko laughs and slings an arm around him, “Aw c’mon dear. She’s almost of age.”

Zuko chuckles and puts her hands up, “No, I’m actually grateful you’re not making me drink it. It looks like it’s seen…better days.”

They all have a good laugh and start chatting. They were all reassigned within the Home Guard. Patrolling the waters on ships or in one of the islands. After the fiasco in the North, the generals decided that any survivors should be rotated with the Home Guard members just to prevent massive defection. Even they knew the men can only take so much, and an attack by the ocean spirit would be enough to crush any soldier’s courage.

“So what’s the plan, captain?” Ying asks.

Zuko looks serious. “I can’t ask you to help me with this. No matter how much I want to. If…if anyone finds out about your involvement, you could be branded for treason.”

They pause and then smile widely.

“Is that it?” Hansung jokes. “We’ve been committing that for a *long* while.”

Zuko blinks. “What are you talking about?”

Her lieutenant puts a hand on her shoulder, “Almost two years ago, your Uncle told us about your banishment. And we all made a pact then and there. We’d stand by you. No matter what. Even if it means we have to go against your father.”

“You guys…” she whispers. She’s touched. For so long these men had sailed with her and now they’re willing to commit treason for her. She swallows the choking sob clawing it’s way out.

“For now, I’ll need some of you to help get Uncle out.” she says. “And I’m going to need your help to find the Avatar.”

They grin sharply. “Just like old times.” Toshi cheers.

“To honor and glory?” Chang toasts raising his glass.

“To honor and glory.” They echo.

&#95;&#42;&#95;&#42;&#95;

Ozai calls her to his office. He sits at his desk and looks over to her. “Princess Zuko. It seems that there’s an invasion force coming for us in a week’s time.”

Zuko feels her heart drop. She knew Sokka was planning one, but she didn’t realize it would be so soon. Not only that...

She looks to her father. “When did you get this information?”

He waves a hand. “It doesn’t matter. I need you to assist with the evacuations. I don’t want the citizens to start rioting when they see Water savages at our doorstep.”

“Very well, Father. I’ll help with the evacuations.”

She bows and leaves the room, feeling heavier than before. He was lying. He’s known about it for some time, but he never told her. She pauses in the middle of the hall. He also never told her about the royal family’s evacuation plan. That’s something the Fire Lord would always tell his family and personal guards when something like this happened. It's like she's seeing everything clearly now.

Ozai doesn’t care what happens to her. He never did.

&#95;&#42;&#95;&#42;&#95;

Mai and her are sitting in the secluded garden, watching the wisteria-willow sway above them.

“When you were with the Earth King, did he say anything to you?” Zuko asks.

Mai looks at her oddly. “He said a lot of things to us. But why are you asking now?”
