Zuko shrugs. “Just…things are happening quickly so...I just wondered if there was some Earth Kingdom soldiers we needed to worry about.”

The placid girl scoffs, “You worry too much. Besides Azula made sure we would be protected.”

“Against who? It’s not like we’re particularly vulnerable here.” She asks, hiding the fact that she knew of the invasion.

“Did you forget? The invasion force that’s coming on the Black Sun. We found out because the Earth King didn’t know how to keep a secret.” Mai informs.

So, Father’s known since Azula’s return. It makes sense. The factories have been ramping up production. The airships being built so quickly. She had assumed it was because they wanted to get them ready for Sozin’s comet, but it looks like he has a plan for them during the invasion

“O-oh right. I forgot about that.” she lies.

Mai narrows her eyes. “Did you? That doesn’t sound like you at all.”

Zuko shrugs. “I’ve had a lot on my mind.”

Her friend doesn’t look convinced. “Zuko, if Azula or your Father didn’t tell you—”

“Mai, Father told me. I know about it.” She says. “I’ve been helping with the evacuations of the city. You probably have a place to get to during the invasion, right?”

She nods. “Yeah. Do you?”

“Of course. The royal bunkers.” Zuko replies. “I’ll be safe and sound there.”

“Somehow I don’t believe that.”

The princess laughs, “Agree to disagree then. How about some target practice? I’ve been staring at paperwork all day.”

Sigh. “I guess I’m bored enough to.”

&#95;&#42;&#95;&#42;&#95;

Zuko confers with Jee and Teruko on their ship. “This is the perfect opportunity for you to get my uncle out of prison.” She says.

“And what about you?” Jee asks.

“I’ll be joining with Aang’s group.” She announces. “I’ll try to find a way to get a message to them. Let them know my Father and sister will be prepared. Have Yama or Chang made any headway on their location?”

Teruko shakes her head, “No. Nothing solid yet. There were rumors in a town of a Runaway, but then they lost the trail. Looks like those guys can be pretty sneaky after all.”

“Of all the unfortunate times to learn stealth.” She mutters. “Okay I’ll try to find a way to message them. Jee, would you be able to stay behind in the palace to give the Avatar the message?”

He nods, "Of course. I can sneak in once everyone is gone, but you still haven't told us your plans."

“If everything goes well, I’ll be ready to order a ceasefire.” Zuko responds. “But if it doesn’t…I’m prepared to defect and stop my Father during Sozin’s comet. That would be the best time to strike.”

“Princess…”

“Jee, I’m going to need one of you to stow a small war balloon for me.” Zuko orders. “On the off chance that this might fall to pieces.”

The lieutenant nods. “Very well. And if it does fall to pieces, what are we going to do?”

“You need to stay with Uncle and support him. I know he has some secret underground group that will help him escape, but I’d feel better knowing there are good loyal people around him. And I’ll need some of you back here, gathering information. I’ve given my personal hawk to Ying, she’ll be ready to send her after a few days.”

Jee has a sour look on his face, “I don’t like that you’ll be going after them on your own.” He says. “They could hurt you or take you prisoner!”

Zuko sighs, “I know, but after everything I put them through, I would gladly accept that punishment. I can’t come to them peacefully if I bring a troop of men with me. I have to do this alone.”

Jee looks like he’s about to protest, but Teruko puts a hand on him. She looks sternly at her, “Okay princess. We’ll let you go, but I hear one word of the Avatar harming you…well we won’t just stand by and let it happen.”

She bows. “Thank you, I don’t think I would be able to do this without all your support.”

The lieutenant and engineer sigh. “Well life just doesn’t feel the same without all your crazy adventures.” Jee replies wryly.

&#95;&#42;&#95;&#42;&#95;

In the early morning of the invasion, Zuko gets ready. She writes a letter to Mai, Ty Lee, and her sister. As the ink dries, she puts a hand over Azula’s name. Her relationship with her was always so complicated. On one hand, they tease and bitch to each other like normal sisters, but on the other hand, Azula is her biggest tormenter after her father. Maybe once this is over, she can help Azula mend their relationship, and help her little sister out of their father’s shadow.

She pulls her hood over her head and takes her first steps towards a new path.

&#95;&#42;&#95;&#42;&#95;

On the day of the invasion, there’s just clear blue skies all around. The sea is steady as they urge the ships towards the Great Gates of Azulon. As expected, the Fire Nation patrol sound the alarms and the gates are raised, but by the time the soldiers get on the ship, they’re already underwater.

Sokka waits anxiously as his machines swim under the surface. He puts a hand over his heart where the handkerchief is kept.

*I’m coming for you.*

&#45;-

Aang flies through the empty city. His heart pounds as he tries to spy for any movement in the deserted streets. This can't be right. Why isn't anyone home? It doesn't make sense. He finally reaches the palace and swings the doors open.

Nothing.

He goes to the throne room and announces his presence.

Still nothing.

It's all empty.

Aang groans and clutches his head. Why is this happening now? They were so close.

Suddenly, he hears movement from behind a pillar. He readies his staff and shouts, "Who's there?"

A solider comes out and takes off his helmet. He bows low, "Avatar Aang." he intones.

Aang blinks in surprise. He wasn't expecting this. Slowly, he inches closer, staff still held up at the ready. But the man doesn't make a move. He only keeps his head bowed and speaks in a low voice.

"I am Lieutenant Jee. The Fire Lord knows of your plans. He's in the royal bunkers." Jee says.

Aang narrows his eyes suspiciously. "How can I trust you?"

Jee smiles wryly, "If you're smart you wouldn't trust me without proof. But here's something Princess Zuko gave me to give to you." He holds out a handkerchief, and Aang carefully swipes it from his hands.

Jee continues, "Tell that upstart water tribe warrior that the palace is empty. That they know of the invasion plans. I can guarantee that he'll say that they're in an underground bunker."

"How do you know that?"

"Because that's what the princess told me. She has high expectations of that boy."

Aang glares. "Sokka's more than just a boy. He's an able warrior and leader."

Jee hums. "Yes, she said the same. Now get going. The eclipse will be here soon, and I'm needed elsewhere."

"Where are you going?"

"To hatch a prison escape." Is the easy answer.

Aang nearly double-takes. "...Why are you helping us?"

"Because Princess Zuko asked us to, and because I want this war to be over just as much as you."

He leaves, and Aang is left alone in the throne room. The young teen shakes his head and heads off, already dreading to tell Sokka the news.

Everything is going well; Aang flew off to the palace, while they fend off the home guard and take the city. After Hakoda is injured, Sokka leads the charge riding on Appa. His heart races as he bellows out orders and destroys the first gate into the city. They regroup to finalize the plan and get their people up the volcano when Aang flies to them.

“What happened?” Sokka asks. “Did you defeat Ozai already?”

He shakes his head sadly, “No, no I couldn’t find him. The whole palace was empty. They know. It’s too late.”

*No. nonono!* He can’t believe it. After months of planning, all for *nothing*?

“We need to retreat.” Katara says. “The Fire Lord could be miles away by now.”

No. No that’s not right. His instincts were tingling. There’s no way that the Fire Lord would just run during a siege that just looks bad on the rulers. Instead, he’d have someplace safe to hide in while the siege his happening. Somewhere close…

“There must be a secret bunker.” Sokka says. “Somewhere near the palace.”

Aang nods. "Sokka's right. I ran into a solider named, Jee. He told me that they knew and went into the royal bunkers. Then he gave me this." In his hands is an embroidered white handkerchief.

Sokka takes it carefully, at a glance it looks like a simple fire-lily design, but the lines of gold threading through the flower are in a strange pattern. Almost like a…

“Toph, we’re going to need you to dig a path to the bunker.”

“Aw yeah, my favorite kind of activity.”

&#95;&#42;&#95;&#42;&#95;

Zuko enters the bunker quietly. Azula and her father had gone much earlier that morning to prepare, leaving Zuko alone in the quiet empty palace. She entrusted the map to the bunker with Jee who kept himself hidden in the vast throne room. A sure place the Avatar would go. Hopefully, he's already delivered the map and message to them. The rest will be waiting by the prisons to help Uncle out.

The eclipse will be coming soon. She can feel it. Agni’s light is slowly fading. She hastens and heads to the room where her father is, but instead of Ozai it’s Azula.

*Fuck*.

Of course, they would do a switch. It’s only logical.

“Zuzu? What are you doing here? I thought you’d be fighting, and why aren’t you wearing your armor?” Azula asks.

“Everything seems to be handled up there, and I needed to speak with Father.” She lies.

Her sister narrows her eyes. “Hmph he’s in the other room. I’m here as a decoy for whoever’s dumb enough to come here.”

“I see. Well then I hope you stay safe, Azula.”

Azula rolls her eyes. “Ugh spare me your pleasantries. I know I’ll be fine.”

“Right…” In a fit of melancholy, Zuko goes to embrace her sister. Azula immediately stiffens and her body heats up threateningly.

“Sorry.” Zuko mumbles. “I just…we’ve never really hugged before. Not since you were really little.”

Azula glares at her elder sister. “Well for good reason!” she exclaims. “That was absolutely *horrible*.”

Zuko laughs softly. “Yeah I guess it was. Um…be careful.” She turns to leave when Azula calls out to her.

“Wait.”

Zuko turns.

“…If you see any invaders, don’t forget to slice their heads off. You’re soft and it would be a pity for you to die by the hands of some savages.” She advises.

“I’ll try.”

She leaves her sister behind and moves forward.

&#95;&#42;&#95;&#42;&#95;

It’s quick work to get into the bunker and towards the Fire Lord. No one was around accept for an attendant which confirmed the way to the Fire Lord. Aang bursts through the doors and yells, “Fire Lord Ozai surren—Azula!?”

Azula is draped over the chair and turns to them languidly. “My my, if it isn’t the Avatar. Seems you’re not as dead or paralyzed as my sister said. Oh well, I guess I can enjoy taking you down again now.”

“With what?” Toph growls. “You got no bending, toots.” And she encases the girl with stone. Azula laughs.

“Oh I guess you’re right. How silly of me to forget.”

“Cut the bullshit, Azula. Where’s Ozai?” Sokka demands.

Her brow twitches. “That’s no way to refer to the Fire Lord, *peasant*. Show your respect.”

“Sorry I don’t respect nasty imperialists or child abusers.” Sokka quips.

“Too bad.” She mutters, and then the stones around her break apart. Sokka yelps as he avoids the stones, and two Dai Li agents fall from the ceiling.

“Shit.” He curses. Aang and Toph immediately engage in a fight with the agents while Azula runs. Sokka is about to go after her, when an arm stops him. He turns and thrusts his sword against the soldier, but she dodges and says, “Wait, the princess is here!”

“Yeah and she’s getting away!” Sokka yells.

The woman rolls her eyes. “The *other* princess.”

His eyes widen. “Zuko? Zuko’s here?”

“Yes, she’s at the other room facing the Fire Lord. You need to get your friends over there. Azula is trying to bait you so you run out the clock.” She explains.

It makes sense. Azula wasn’t fighting them to win. She dodged and evaded, but never engaged. He needs to get them out and towards the Fire Lord.

“How do I know you’re not lying?” he asks.

There’s a sympathetic look on her face, “I guess you can’t, but I care for Princess Zuko. I don’t want her to face her father alone.”

Sokka pauses for a second. “Okay I’ll go to her and you need to get Toph and Aang to follow.” He hands her the map, and then the handkerchief he stored under his armor. “Give them these. They’ll know I sent you to them.”

“Okay.”

&#95;&#42;&#95;&#42;&#95;

Ozai is in the room, sipping tea like it was an ordinary day. It makes her sick to see him so calm and collected.

"Father." She says.

The Fire Lord raises his eyes and lifts a brow, "Zuko. What are you doing here?" he asks accusingly.

"I need to speak with you."

He smirks and waves the guards away. "This should be good. What other insipid thing are you going to talk to me about?"

"I didn't kill the Avatar. I tried to save him from Azula." She declares.

This time, Ozai freezes. His eyes grow wide, and he glares at her with barely repressed rage. "What did you *say?"*

"I didn't kill the Avatar. I wanted him to live." Zuko repeats. "I'm going to leave this place, and join with the Avatar to stop you."

"Stop me?" Ozai throws his head back and laughs. "You're just a pathetic excuse of a fire bender, and you think you can stop *me?* You've truly lost your mind."

Zuko clenches her fists and raises her head high. "I'm not pathetic, Father. I never was, and it took me a long time for me to understand that. I'm finally going to do the right thing and stop you. Our nation has hurt so many people. The world is broken and scarred, and I'm going to help the Avatar bring balance peace and balance back."

"You little-!"

"I'm not done yet!" She says drawing her swords out. "All I ever wanted was your love an approval, but I shouldn't have had to fight for a Father's love. I see that now. What you did to me was cruel, and I'm not going to stand for it anymore! I'm going to leave, I'm going to ask Uncle for forgiveness, and I'm going to bring honor back to my nation!"

Sokka rushes down the hall. Zuko’s here, and he’s just minutes away from seeing her again. The path stretches for so long, and he feels like he’s running forever. But he hears the sound of Zuko’s voice echoing through the open room. “I know my destiny, it’s to help the Avatar defeat *you*.” Sokka wants to leap for joy. She’s okay, and she’s talking back to her dad!

He enters the room and shouts and exuberant, “Zuko!”

The princess turns and eyes widen when they land on him. “Sokka?” she whispers.

He’s immediately at her side, pointing his sword at the Fire Lord. “It’s over Ozai. The Avatar is coming soon, and he’ll end you before the eclipse is over!”

The Fire Lord looks down at the boy with contempt. “Who is this *peasant*?”

Really? What is with the royal Fire family calling him a peasant?

“He’s not a peasant, Father.” Zuko retorts. “He’s a warrior of the Southern Water Tribe, and a better leader than you.”

Oh shit if he wasn’t facing her angry dad he’d kiss her right now. However, Ozai is not amused with this statement and sneers.

“I should have known you would put your lot in with someone like *him*.” He says. “You’ve always been a disgrace. I should have thrown you over the castle walls when you were a baby.”

Zuko looks back at her father with disgusted eyes. “I don’t know why I ever thought you could love me. You’re just a selfish greedy, empty man.”

Ozai looks almost amused at this statement. “How alike you and your mother are. She told me the same thing before I banished her.”

Zuko’s heart stutters. “She’s alive?”

“Yes…” Ozai muses. “Though I see that I banishment is too lenient a punishment.”

Both fire benders feel it instantly. The inkling rays of the sun coming out from behind the moon’s shadow. Ozone crackles in the air, and Zuko gets in front of Sokka, just as twin lightening strikes burst from Ozai’s fingers.

The power coursing through her is much stronger than the one she took from Azula; it’s wild and destructive, but she lets it flow like water.

Through her fingertips, into her stomach, and back *out*.

She aims the blast at his feet, and it throws him to the wall. Zuko takes Sokka’s hand and drags him out.

“We need to go!” she exclaims.

“Wait we need Aang and Toph!” Just as he says this, Aang and Toph come barreling towards them with Kyoko in tow.

“Princess!”

“Sokka!”

But Zuko waves them all off, “There’s no time, we need to get out of here.”

“But the Fire Lord!” Aang protests.

“No, they have an armada of airships waiting out there, we need to get out before they completely block any paths out of here.” Zuko insists.

Aang and Sokka both look pained, but they agree and run towards the exit.

Zuko stays planted on the ground while the others climb up the bison.

“Zuko what are you waiting for?” Sokka urges.

“I need to make sure my uncle escaped.” She explains. “I’ll meet up with you on my war balloon.”

The teen looks pained, but Zuko gives him a reassuring smile. “Don’t worry. I’ll be right behind you.” And she runs with Kyoko on her heels.

Aang touches his arm, “Sokka, we need to get back to Katara and the others.”

He sighs. “Okay let’s go.”

True to Zuko’s words, the air armada looms above them. Four massive balloons rise from the ground and slowly move over them. Sokka can feel the utter despair at the sight as he urges the people towards the submarines.

But it’s too late. The subs are destroyed, and they had to retreat to higher ground. It’s the most painful separation of his life. He and Katara hug their father tightly and hop onto Appa. They get one last look at their friends and family before they leave them behind.

Sokka presses the heel of his hand to his eyes. It happened again. He let the Fire Nation win again.

Katara puts a hand on his shoulders. “Sokka, it’s not your fault.”

“Yeah it just wasn’t our day.” Toph adds. “We’ll get back at him, and besides now we have Zuko on our side.”

That little bit of information lifts his spirits, but then he looks about in a panic. “Wait, where is she? She said she’d be right behind us in her war balloon.”

They all look around, but don’t see a trace of a balloon. Toph puts a hand on his shoulder, “She’s probably just behind. We’ll see her soon. You said she had a knack for finding you guys.”

“…yeah.”

The group lands in the Western Air Temple and set up camp there. Haru, the Duke and Teo all go out to explore the temple while the other four gather around and take comfort with one another. It was a crushing defeat, but they all had to move past it if they want to get another shot of the Fire Lord.

Toph suddenly sits up, “Someone’s coming.”

They stand, tense and ready to defend, but then a pair of familiar boots come into view, and the girl jumps down onto the ledge. She smiles awkwardly and waves.

“Hello, Zuko here.”

### End Notes

#### Chapter End Notes

**Lu Ten:** Tell him, sister!  
**Yue:** Now I'm tear-bending. That was beautiful.

&#45;-

Art is based on a comment from recomment&#95;me&#95;fics!

So just as an aside, I don't think Ozai would have told Zuko anything just because well...big Daddy never thought anything much of Zuko. He'd just ship her off to fight while he cruises in the bunker. It's why he never told her of the plans. I'm sure he only told Azula because she's his preferred heir and she's useful to him. Not to mention that she's the one who gave the information to him.

Thank you for reading!

## Chapter 14

---

Zuko expected a lot of things when she goes to join the Avatar. She expects, rage, derision, scorn, and honestly the thought of being chased out by a group of angry benders was also on the table.

But this was not at all what she expected.

Sokka hugs her tightly and spins her around, exclaiming how happy he is to see her. Aang is by her side and chattering loudly about welcoming her to the group and the small Earth Nation girl is punching her arms and welcoming her. The only one who seems to be acting normally is Katara who hangs back with her arms crossed.

“Sokka let go of me.” Zuko says blushing.

“Nope. Not until you promise not to leave again.” He says.

She rolls her eyes. “Ok I promise not to leave again without telling you.”

“I don’t like that stipulation added to the end but I’ll take it.” He puts her down and smiles. “I’m glad you’re okay.”

“I’m glad you made it out too.” She says with a small smile. “I’m sorry you had to leave your people behind.”

Sokka looks downcast. “Yeah well…we have another chance at him.”

“Which is why you better start teaching Twinkletoes some fire bending.” Toph adds.

Aang looks a little queasy at the thought. “Or I could just *not* learn fire bending?” he suggests.

Toph and Sokka give him an unimpressed look. “Aang.” Sokka starts.

“I’m just…I’m just really nervous.” Aang says. “I’m worried I’ll hurt someone.”

The princess nods in understanding. “I know what you mean.” She says sympathetically. “Fire can be dangerous and wild. You need to be careful when you’re working with it.”

The young Avatar looks a little appeased by this, and then turns to Katara. “Isn’t this great, Katara?”

She purses her lips and frowns. “Sure, I guess.” She mutters.

“Katara.” Sokka warns.

“Look Sokka, I told you I would give her a chance.” His sister retorts. “But I can’t just forget the times she chased after us. And I still don’t know if she actually did the right thing in the catacombs.”

“Oh I didn’t.” Zuko replies. “I went back to Azula and my father instead of joining you.”

“And you also hurt Aang!” Katara shouts.

The princes blinks. “I did? I—I’m sorry I thought I took out most of the energy that hit him.” She turns to Aang apologetically and bows. “I’m sorry I wasn’t in time to completely stop the blast.”

Aang shrugs. “You did your best, and you did take out a lot of the punch from Azula’s hit.”

On the other side, Katara gaps. “S-so you did help Aang?”

“Tried to, but he still got hit. I managed to convince Azula that he was either dead or severely injured.”

“Oh…thanks.”

“Um, you’re welcome.”

Katara and Zuko awkwardly stand fidgeting their hands and not really knowing what to say to the other. Fortunately, Sokka breaks the atmosphere and takes her hand. “I’ll show you to you room.” he says and flounces off with her.

Toph snickers and elbows Aang. “How long do you think they’re going to try to keep it a secret?”

“I’d give it a week.” Aang bets.

“You’re on.”

Katara rolls her eyes at the two. “How can you guys be so easy going about this? Especially you Aang?”

He shrugs, “Well…she helped us out, and she went against her whole family and nation to join us. That takes a lot of courage.”

“Her family’s Azula and the Fire Lord. How hard can it be?” she gripes. “Toph was able to leave everything behind easily.”

“Yeah, but I knew that I wasn’t committing treason when I went with you guys.” The blind girl retorts.

“…I guess.”

Aang rubs her arms comfortingly. “I think you just need to give it some time. These things can be hard to forgive, but I know you can do it. You have a lot of compassion and kindness in your heart Katara.”

She nods. “Thanks, Aang.”

The water bender trusted Zuko before, so there’s no reason why she can’t do it again.

Meanwhile, the two teens walk towards an open room. Sokka shows her the area and smiles, “Here you go. A lot bigger than your room in Ba Sing Se I’ll bet.”

She takes a tentative step inside and looks around. “Yeah a little…” she trails off running her hands on the walls.

“Hey…you okay?”

“…after I was banished. This was the first place I went to.” She says. “Uncle was so worried when I started climbing down the rope with only one working eye.”

“That’s a pretty reasonable reason to be scared.” Sokka comments.

“Yeah, I was pretty reckless back then.”

“Still are, princess.”

Zuko punches his arm playfully. “Hey, I’m getting better.”

The two smile and bask in the others company for a little while longer. Then Sokka clears his throat, “Oh, I actually—em I have something for you.” He reaches into his pocket and pulls out the fire-lily hair clip. “I saw this at the market and thought of you.”

Pale hands take the clip from his, and she looks at it in awe. “Sokka, it…it’s beautiful.” She peeks at him through her lashes. “Will you put it in for me?”

The boy swallows hard. He’s never been asked to put jewelry on a woman before, and his hands tremble at the implications of that declaration. He takes it and carefully slides it on the left side so the hair wouldn’t fall on her scar. Her golden eyes gleam in the dim light and he can see a faint blush on her cheeks as his hands caress her face.

“It—it looks good.” Sokka stutters.

She nods. The warmth bubbling through her chest as she leans close to Sokka. It’s like their time in Ba Sing Se all over again. The separation seems like a distant memory, and they’re just gravitating towards each other once more.

She puts a hand on his hair where the dragon pin rested on his wolf-tail. “I guess we match now.” she says.

Sokka smiles sheepishly. “Yeah. I didn’t want to risk losing this when you gave it to me. Is it alright with you?”

“Yeah…I think Lu Ten wouldn’t have minded having you wear his pin.” She whispers.

Somehow, they end up in a semi-embrace. Sokka’s nose pressed against her hair as he breathes her in, his hands gently caressing her arms in smooth up and down motions. Zuko’s own hands rest on Sokka’s hip making small circles with her thumb and her head is resting on his shoulder. Neither speak. They just let the silence comfort them as they refamiliarize themselves with each other again.

“Hey you two—*whoops*!” Aang blushes and backtracks quickly at the sight of the two. The princess and the warrior pull back and Sokka coughs awkwardly in his hand.

“A-aang.” He squeaks. “You should knock next time.”

“Uh, your door was wide open.” Aang retorts.

“Oh…well don’t just come around here willy-nilly. This is a *girl’s* room.”

“But *you’re* in it.”

“Because I’m the exception—*an* exception. Just for *today*. Because I had to show her to her room…today. Only.”

Zuko is a bright red and shoving her face in her hands. “Sokka, shut up.”

“Will do.”

Aang looks back and forth between the two and grins. “Well&#126; I was just going to tell you guys that dinner is ready, but you kids take your time.”

“We’re *older* than you!” Sokka protests.

“Technically I’m 112!” Aang retorts from down the hall.

Sokka sighs and turns to Zuko. “Shall we go, princess?”

“Might as well.”

Dinner is surprisingly nice for her. Everyone seems to be okay with her joining the group, or at least don’t have an opinion either way. Teo tries to ask her about Fire Nation machinery, but after just a minute of talking to her, he soon realizes that she has no idea how they build their machinery. Impressively, Sokka seems to understand the mechanics and engines much better than her.

*Ugh is there anything he* can’t *do?*

Even Katara seems a little less hostile. She even seems grateful when Zuko offers to clean the dishes. She’s just drying the last of the plates with her fire bending when Sokka comes behind her.

“Hey, wanna see something cool?”

“Are you stealing my lines?”

“Uh no.” he denies. “And they’re not your lines if a million people already said it.”

She snorts, “Okay fine. What’s the cool thing you wanted to show me?”

He takes her up a set of stairs that wind all the way to a small opening at the top; it’s a secluded area, nestled among thick trees and shaded from the outside. Sokka already has a blanket spread out for them and he pats the empty spot close to him. They both lie down and look at the vast sky peppered with millions of tiny silvery light.

“Cool or what?” he asks.

“Really cool.” She says turning her head towards him. Sokka’s profile looks softer and his blue eyes seem to reflect the stars. He catches her eye and turns to her. Eyes soft and achingly beautiful. She doesn’t ever want to let this out of her sight again. His hand reaches out and rubs her cheeks soothingly.

“Why didn’t you come the first time?” he asks.

She looks down. “I wasn’t ready. I was…too scared. I was still chasing phantom memories.”

“And now?”

“I’m still scared of messing up. But I feel…*good*. Like I can finally feel happy and content.”

Sokka marvels at the strength and assuredness in her voice. In Ba Sing Se, she never sounded as confident or as grounded as she does now, and it takes his breath away to see how free she looks underneath the night sky.

He scoots closer to her and smiles. “I’m glad. You look…you look like you’re free now.”

She looks contemplative. “I guess I am.”

They stay close like that as they end up drifting to sleep against the backdrop of the night sky.

&#95;&#42;&#95;&#42;&#95;

The warmth of Agni presses against her consciousness as she’s dragged out of sleep. She groans curls herself against the warmth, tucking her face against the comforting smell of metal and the sea. Two arms curl around her, bringing her close and she hears someone mutter above her.

That gets her attention.

Her eyes spring open and she finds herself in the comforting embrace of Sokka’s arms. A bright blush makes its way against her pale face as she feels how close the arms are to her butt. She shuts her eyes and lets herself revel in the comfort for a second before slowly and carefully extricating herself form Sokka’s arms.

The water tribesman grumbles and mutters her name as he tries to reach for her. It’s absolutely adorable, and it makes her heart flutter. But she can’t stay any longer. She needs to start fire bending training with Aang.

She shakes the boy awake, and he grunts and peels his eyes open.

“Whuuzzz it? ‘uuko?” he mumbles.

“I have to go.” she says quietly. “And you should probably get to a real bed.”

“Mmrmm no, we in r’l bed. C’mn back ‘ere. Miss uuu.” He whines still half asleep.

Zuko holds in her coos and laughter. “I can’t I have to train Aang.”

“Booo, stoop’d Avatar. Takin’ my princess away.” he glares. “I’ll fight ‘im.”

“Don’t fight him.” She chides. “It’s just training. I’ll be back.”

“m’kay. Don’ take too long.” He grumbles and falls back asleep. Zuko looks at him fondly and in that small moment of weakness, she leans down and gives him a soft kiss on his forehead. She leaves her outer vest laying on top of him and heads back down to the temple.

Aang yawns as he trudges to the meeting spot Zuko set up for them. The princess is already meditating. Skin glowing under Agni’s light.

“Good morning Sifu Hotman!” Aang greets happily.

Zuko is not amused by the nickname and growls a, “Don’t call me that. Just because you’re the reincarnation of my great-grandfather doesn’t mean you get to sound as old as him.”

Aang beams. “I’m your great-grandfather?” he asks excitedly. “I can’t believe I have a great-granddaughter. You make me so proud, dearie!” he says pinching her cheeks.

The annoyed princess slaps his hands away and glares. “Don’t. That’s fucking disturbing.”

“Language, great-granddaughter or I’ll have to soap your mouth!”

Zuko’s smile turns feral. “Wanna try it, Avatar?”

Aang gulps. “Ah ehehe no sorry, Sifu.”

She nods and leans back. “Okay why don’t we start with the first lesson, breathing.”

They go through the breath of Agni, seven breaths to represent the seven tongues of the sun god. Things go very well…until they don’t.

Zuko is furiously trying to throw a fireball out, but only a small puff comes out. Nothing like the blasts she used to have.

Why is this happening to her *now*? She ends up spending a good majority of the morning trying to figure out why her fire isn’t working, and when Sokka comes to ask how the fire bending is going, she yells in frustration and stomps off to cool down.

She ends up at a small river and makes small talk with a badgerfrog who just croaks in response to her plights.

“You’re no help at all.” she declares.

It just stares at her with wide eyes and croaks again.

When she gets back, she informs the group of her problems, and Toph suggests to that she needs to get in touch with the source of fire bending. She mules on the idea and decides to take an impromptu fieldtrip with Aang to the sun warrior ruins.

As they pack Appa up for the trip, Sokka gives her an encouraging smile. “Come back with some awesome new fire bending okay?”

Zuko scoffs. “I better or you’ll need to find another teacher for Aang.”

“Yeesh and I thought I was pessimistic.” He jokes.

“Not funny, Sokka.”

“*Yes* funny, Sokka.”

She glares, but he can see that her lips are twitching upward. “I’m not doing this with you. I’ll see you later.”

“Bye Aang, bye Princess!” Sokka calls out as they start to fly. “Don’t maim Aang! He’s a little delicate.”

“Sokka!” Katara hisses.

“What? Just saying. Zuko can have a *little* bit of a violent streak.”

“My kind of girl.” Toph grins. Haru and Teo look a little pale at the thought.

“You’re not helping her cause.” Katara deadpans.

“What cause?”

“The ‘Get people comfortable with Zuko’ cause.”

Sokka wrinkles his nose. “Isn’t that just *you*?”

Katara glares and throws her arms up before heading to the fountain to do some water bending. Toph looks up at the boy with an evil smile on her face.

“Now that Sugarqueen’s gone, why don’t you spill the beans?” she says.

“What beans?”

Toph gets a glint in her eyes that send a chill down his spine. “You forget that I’m a master earth bender, and I just happened to hear two footsteps that climbed up a stairway that leads to a nice secluded patch in the woods where said two people stayed there for the *whole* *night*.”

Sokka blushes and Haru covers the Duke’s ears. “We were just *stargazing*!”

“Oh is that what the kids are calling it these days?”

“You are *twelve*.”

“So? I’ve been in the Earth Rumbles since I was ten. I hear things. *Nasty* things.”

The boys all look horrified at the tiny girl.

“Spirits, Toph. You’re such an *animal*.”

“Eh, raised by badgermoles.” She shrugs. “Now tell me!”

Sokka rolls his eyes. “There’s nothing to tell. We looked at stars and then fell asleep and next thing I knew she was gone.”

“…”

“…”

“Wow you’re actually telling the truth.” Toph remarks.

“Of course, I am!” Sokka says. “What were you expecting?”

“Something not as blandly vanilla and oogie as *that*.”

“You are *twelve*, child!”

She shrugs. “Whatever. I can’t believe I thought you had some juicy gossip I could pry out of you. Unless…” she turns to him. “Unless something happened in Ba Sing Se?”

The water tribesman shifts. “No nothing like *that* ever happened between us. We’re just friends.”

Even the boys look skeptically at him.

“I don’t wanna be rude, Sokka, but you don’t just spin a girl around like *that* if you’re just friends.” Teo says.

“Yeah and the stargazing and sleeping in moonlight thing is not really a…platonic event.” Haru adds.

“It’s a platonic friend thing with us!” Sokka denies. “We did it all the time in Ba Sing Se.”

“Wait, that’s why you came home in the early morning sometimes?!” Toph exclaims.

“Yes!”

She tuts and shakes her head. “And I thought you were getting some.”

“Toph!” Sokka screeches. “Not in front of the kid!” he says pointing to the Duke.

“It’s okay Sokka. I’ve seen a lot of Freedom Fighters do adult stuff.” The Duke chimes in. The poor warrior wants to bash his head in and forget this conversation happened at all.

“I’m going to practice my swordbending.” Sokka declares.

“Your swordbending or your *swordbending*?” Toph teases.

He doesn’t bother with a response and just flips her off.

“I’m blind, dummy!”

“Fuck you, Toph!”

“That’s better.”

&#95;&#42;&#95;&#42;&#95;

On Appa, Zuko is not fairing any better. Worst part is, she can’t even jump out of the bison to avoid the conversation.

“So…you and Sokka huh?” Aang starts.

“What about me and Sokka?” she grumbles.

“Oh nothing just that…you two seem really close. And I see you have a new hairclip there. It’s very nice.”

Zuko touches the clip carefully and blushes. “It’s just a silly little trinket.”

“Right. A silly little trinket like the handkerchief Sokka keeps near his heart, or the hair pin he wears every day, or the tunic that he only wears when he knows that he’s in a safe place so he won’t get it dirty…want me to go on?”

“No.” she mumbles, face a burning red.

“So how did Sokka become your boyfriend anyway?” Aang asks. “I’m kinda…trying to get a girl’s attention and I don’t really know how to go about it.”

“Is it Katara?” Zuko asks blandly.

“How did you know?!”

“Let’s just say I recognize the look.” She answers. “And for your information, Sokka isn’t my boyfriend.”

Aang blinks like he can’t even comprehend. “Sooo he’s your *husband*?”

Zuko sputters incoherently, “W-what? That’s just—there’s no!—I mean we aren’t even boyfriend and girlfriend!”

“Whaaatt? There’s no way.” Aang says incredulously. “Are you guys just trying to keep it a secret? Because I can keep a secret you don’t even have to worry about it! Once when Sokka and I were setting up came he told me that he—”

“Didn’t you *just* say that you could keep a secret?”

“Oh right.” He laughs sheepishly. “But point is, you can tell me if you’re in a relationship. I won’t judge. Air nomads are the least judge-y people.”

Zuko sighs. “We’re not keeping anything a secret. We really are just friends.”

“…That can’t be right.”

“And why not?” she grouses.

“Because you two were my example of true love!”

“What?!” she shrieks. “There are *so* many things wrong with that! I don’t—he’s not my—!”

Aang looks at her placidly.

“He’s *not* my true love.” She denies. “Besides, I’m a princess of the Fire Nation. They expect me to marry someone in my class.”

The Avatar frowns. “That’s dumb. Why would they do that?”

“…it’s just how it is.” She says. “The royal family has always married someone with strong fire bending abilities so that their own powers will grow stronger. It’s how we’ve been able to produce so many strong benders in the past.”

“That seems like a terrible thing to impose.” Aang says. “You should be allowed to marry whoever you want!”

“Well…” Zuko bites her lip. Would it be alright? When everything is over, would they still cling to the old traditions? She shakes her head. “Sometimes…sometimes it’s not that simple.”

They thankfully land in the clearing soon after, and head to the Sun Warrior ruins.

&#95;&#42;&#95;&#42;&#95;

Zuko groans as her face is pressed against the grates. “This is a disaster.” She mutters.

“Well you had to touch the glow-y egg.” Aang quips.

“And you had to do that stupid dance.”

“…So what now?”

“Contemplate our place in the universe?” she offers.

Aang chuckles. “You’re so broody. I guess that’s why you and Sokka are a good match.”

“I keep telling you that we’re not dating.”

“But you *want* to.”

“But I *can’t*.”

“But you *should*.” Aang insists. “It’s okay to want things, Zuko. Not everything should be about your people. You need to be happy too.”

She sighs and turns away. “…we’ll see.”

“I’m rooting for you!”

As dawn breaks, they realize that the ancient ruins aren’t as empty as they thought. They end up taking a soul-searching journey to see the masters and suddenly, Zuko’s staring down the maw of a red dragon.

She can’t believe their still alive, and when she dances with them something inside her seems to light with joy and familiarity.

*Remember this feeling*. *This is what true fire-bending should feel like.*

It’s a dance, it’s the passion and life and breath of her people. When the dragons stop and blow their flames to them, Zuko sees a rainbow of colors in the flame. Blues, and greens, and yellows, all intermingled in harmony.

She stares into the flames and visions comes to her.

*Uncle working in the kitchen. Talking to her and serving her tea.*

*The crew laughing as they drink and play music.*

*Lu Ten urging her along on another grand adventure.*

*Her and Azula sniping at each other while getting their nails done.*

And finally,

*The palace gardens. The sounds of tinkling laughter. Zuko’s hair is down to her back and swinging* *wildly around her as she chases a taller, broader Sokka around the wisteria-willows. He taunts her* *playfully, and she pounces on him. His strong arms catch her with ease and spin her around. Blue* *eyes clear as the sky look into her and mouths something she can’t quite catch.*

It ends, but she can still feel the blooming love in her chest. The inner flame fueled and beating strongly within her as she revels on the life and love she has experienced. All too soon the dragons leave, and they go back down to the Sun Warriors.

“That was…incredible.” She says. “I never thought that fire held so much life and love and passion.”

The chief nods sagely. “Fire is life. The beating heart of the world. It protects and destroys in equal measure, and you must understand both parts to achieve balance within you.”

Zuko nods and goes through a kata, releasing a huge plume of flame that shimmers in a wonderful rainbow of colors.

Aang grins widely and does the same. Joy and life gleaming in his eyes.

&#95;&#42;&#95;&#42;&#95;

It’s late when they get back to the temple, but everyone eagerly goes up to them to ask what they learned. Aang insists on showing them the Dancing Dragon, which Zuko is not as eager to do. Still, it feels nice to be able to fire bend with ease again. Her flames feel much better now than ever before, and it shows in the way she moves through her katas.

Sokka grins. “Nice dance, you two.”

“It’s an ancient fire bending form, Sokka. Not a silly dance.” Zuko retorts.

“And it’s called?”

The princess groans and mutters, “The Dancing Dragon.”

The kids laugh, but when Zuko sits down, Sokka nudges her and grins, “It was a beautiful dance.” He compliments.

Zuko smiles softly, “Thanks. It feels right. Like the way fire bending was supposed to be.”

“You said you lost the fuel for your bending; what’s your new source?” he asks.

“…love.” She admits.

“O-oh.” Sokka says blushing. “That’s erm…that’s nice. Much better than anger.”

She turns to him, and her eyes look like their molten gold in the firelight. “I think so too.”

By the look on Toph’s face, Sokka knows the little gremlin can feel his heart beating fast.

&#95;&#42;&#95;&#42;&#95;

Fire bending practice is both the best and the worst time, and Sokka can hardly focus on any other task when he catches a glimpse of Zuko training Aang. It doesn’t help that the other boys are ogling too. A burning jealously floods through him every time he sees Aang, Haru, or Teo look admiringly at her body.

It’s not fair.

He knows those three aren’t interested, but Zuko has a very very hot body. One can say sizzling hot. Right now, he’s trying not to openly drool as he watches her do a particularly graceful kick.

Aang’s pretty much gotten acclimatized to having a smoking hot teacher, and hardly bats an eye when she turns to him and asks him to copy the move. However, Sokka can’t tear his eyes off the rivulets of sweat that’s dripping down the luscious curves of her back. They go down down into the small of her back, before dipping underneath her pants to her plump butt.

*Fuck* he wants to bury his face in that ass. If she ever decided to sit on his face, he’d die a happy man.

And when she turns, he can see her washboard abs, strong broad shoulders, and narrow waist. Her breasts that strain against the wrap around her chest. The sunlight making her skin glisten and sparkle. Is it normal to want to just *lick* someone so badly? She stretches her arms up and Sokka just becomes a *puddle* of desire. He swears steam is literally coming off her bo—NNRUUUGH!!!

Cold icy water falls on his body, and he glares at the source. Toph is on the floor cackling and Katara just raises her brow and crosses her arms.

“Sorry you looked like you were overheating.” Katara says smugly. “I thought I’d help cool you off.”

“Not. Funny.” Sokka growls.

“Hey, Snoozles.” Toph grins. “Watch out behind you.”

“Wha—”

“Sokka, you okay?”

*Fucking lion-turtles.*

He turns slowly and tries to keep his eyes from wandering below her chin. It’s an effort of great restraint.

“Y-yes. Yes I’m fine. Katara was just messing with me.”

“Oh okay. Do you need me to dry you off? I promise I won’t throw fire at you like in the North.” she jokes holding up a warmed hand. It’s like the universe is testing him today, and he doesn’t appreciate it.

“No *I’mfine*!” he squeaks. The kids are snickering at him, and that’s just one more thing he doesn’t appreciate. “I—uh I’m just gunna go—dry off. There. Near my room. Yes.” And he bolts it before something embarrassing comes out of him. Words or otherwise.

Zuko watches him run with an odd look on her face. “Huh. Maybe it is a normal thing.” She muses.

“What’s a normal thing?” Toph asks.

“Oh just, Sokka doing—well doing whatever he did just then.” Zuko explains.

Toph gets a particularly devious look on her face. “Oh *really*? Do tell.”

The princess shrugs. “He kinda did it when we were getting out of the baths or that one time I ate the cream-filled bread.”

“Ah well. That’s Sokka for you.” Toph says flippantly. “He probably just isn’t used to seeing you like this.”

“Like sweaty?”

“*Yes* exactly.” Toph replies. Katara and Aang are holding their hands to their mouths, desperately trying not to laugh.

“I don’t really blame him.” Zuko says. “It is kinda gross. At least when we spared in Ba Sing Se, I wasn’t fire bending so I could keep my shirt on.”

“Such a tragedy.” Toph tuts lightly. “You know, Sokka just needs to get used to it. Maybe if you guys do your swordbending practice, you should go like this. Keeps your shirts fresher at least.”

“That might be a good idea; it is really warm here.” She says. “I’ll probably do it during our next sword session.”

Toph’s smile practically takes up her whole face. “Please do. We wouldn’t want you to overheat.”

Zuko smiles shyly at Toph. “Don’t worry we fire benders don’t overheat easily, but em…thanks for thinking about that.” and she goes off to do some cool downs, taking Aang with her to go through the motions.

“Oh Spirits, I feel kinda bad.” Toph admits

“…still wanna go spy on them?” Katara asks.

“Fuck yeah. This is the best drama I’ve had in *years*.”

&#95;&#42;&#95;&#42;&#95;

Most days after Aang’s fire bending practice, Sokka and Zuko spar together in one of the many courtyards at the temple. Her dao blades swing easily to parry his black sword. After a particularly grueling session, they both call it a day and start to do warm down stretches.

The boy groans as leans to the side and stretch his calf muscles. “I hate this part the most.” He gripes.

“I’ve seen old men have better flexibility than you.” Zuko teases.

“I’m a water tribe warrior. We have bulky fur armor that in no way supports those flexible movements you do.” He retorts. “If I tried, I’d tear through all the layers.”

“You weren’t wearing bulky armor during the invasion.” She says.

Sokka winces. The invasion is still a sore spot in his heart. Every time someone brings it up it throws all of his failures in his face. Zuko’s hand catches his.

“Hey, I know that look.” She says. “You’re not a failure just because the invasion didn’t work out.”

“How did you know I was thinking that?”

“I’ve seen the look on my face a lot of times.” She answers earnestly. Her hands holding onto his tightly and rubbing his palm comfortingly.

“I wish I didn’t have to leave my dad behind.” Sokka says. “Now he’s…well where would he be?”

Zuko shifts uncomfortably. “It’s not a nice place.”

“That doesn’t make me feel better about abandoning him.”

“…he’s probably in the Boiling Rock.” She answers. “It’s where we keep the worst prisoners of the Fire Nation.”

The other teen nods and mules on it. “And where is it exactly?”

Zuko glares. “Sokka, you’re not fooling anyone. I’m telling you, that place is nearly impenetrable, and no one has ever escaped.”

“That just sounds like no one’s tried hard enough.” Sokka retorts.

“Sokka.”

“*Zuko*.” He grips her hands and looks her in the eye. “Please. This is my dad. I let him down. I need to restore my honor.”

The princess gives a long drawn-out groan. “You did *not* just use the H-word on me.”

“It’s your one true weakness. Other than cute animals, but I didn’t have any of those on hand.”

She glares. “Fine. But if I tell you, I’m going with you to the prison.”

“What? No deal.”

“Then you won’t hear another peep from me.” she says crossing her arms and turning away. Behind her, she can hear him groaning and cursing under his breath. She’d give him *five…four…* *three…two…one…*

“Okay.” Sokka grumbles.

Zuko turns around and smiles sweetly. “I knew you’d see it my way.”

Later that night, Sokka sneaks past the sleeping occupants. He even checked Zuko’s room and found her sleeping soundly in her bed. Perfect.

He quietly goes to Appa and climbs up—

“Hello, Sokka.”

He nearly screams and falls, but Zuko is there to cover his mouth and grab him.

“How did you *know*?” he hisses.

Zuko rolls her eyes. “You seriously think I’m an idiot? I know you, Sokka. I knew as soon as I told you where it was that you’d try and leave me behind to go on our own because you feel like it’s your responsibility.”

“It is my responsibility.” He argues. “I can’t put you in danger like this.”

She glares and pulls him close, “Listen water boy, I’m not some damsel who can’t defend myself. In case you didn’t notice, I *betrayed* my nation to help you. I’m prepared to give my *life* to this, and honestly this isn’t the first time I’ve infiltrated a high security government building.”

She pushes him down and pokes his chest menacingly. “I’m not letting your stupid ass go on our own because that means I’d have to bust you out if you got caught, and I *want* to help you Sokka. I tried doing a lot of shit on my own because I was too prideful to ask for help and thought it was all my responsibility. And that did not go well. So you’re going to shut up and let me help you or I will tie you up and *make* you accept my help.”

Sokka nods mutely, and she smiles. “Good now let’s go get to my balloon. They don’t exactly have Bison daycares over there.” She slides down and Sokka follows after her a bit more slowly.

Appa rumbles when he gets down, and Sokka pats the bison’s horn. “Yeah buddy, she’s fucking awesome.”

Sokka gushes and coos at the new improvements the Fire Nation made on the balloon. “Ugh I wish I had some schematics of the engine.” He says looking at it forlornly.

“We could ask Teruko if she can sneak some in for their next letter.” Zuko says. “Although I don’t know if the harness would be big enough to fit it all in.”

Zuko’s personal hawk had finally found them a few days ago and based on the letters it sounds like the crew and Iroh were doing well. Of course, then the gang learned that Ozai is amassing all his troops on a final strike to the Earth Kingdom which really hampered their plan to fight the Fire Lord after the comet.

“Might be a good idea. We could find something that could help us take down those airships.” Sokka says.

It’s amazing how Sokka just thinks about the tactics and strategy without any prompting. His mind always seems to be at work, coming up with new ideas.

“If anyone can take down the Fire Nation Air fleet it would be you.” She says.

Sokka laughs nervously, “Ah well…I’ll try. I guess I just—I’m nervous.” He admits. “All my careful planning didn’t really help during the invasion.”

“That’s because my sister knew all about the plans back in the Earth Kingdom.” Zuko rebuts.

“Maybe, but honestly it kinda feels like I lost my stuff. Like every plan I think of is just…terrible. I mean I almost brough *Appa* to a Fire Nation prison.” He sighs and puts his head on his knees. “If I’m not the plan guy, what use do I have?”

Zuko shifts nervously and sits beside him. She leans towards him and nudges her shoulder against his trying to be comforting.

“It does sound like you lost your plan mojo.” She says.

“Thanks.” He mutters.

“But that’s why you’re on a life-changing field trip with me.” she says. “So you can get it back, and save your dad.”

Sokka looks up at her and grins. “Is this what this is?”

“I’ve decided to give you the honor of escorting me on this life-changing field trip, to regain your mojo.” Zuko replies primly.

“And my honor?”

The princess pinches his side. “Stick to your lane plan guy. I’m the honor-maniac.”

Sokka laughs. “Okay okay, princess. I’ll be glad to escort you on this life-changing field trip.” He takes her hand and kisses it, causing Zuko to blush a furious red.

“W-well good. I don’t give these out for just anyone.” She replies turning her had away so he can’t see the red flush as well.

Sokka smiles and put his head on her shoulder. “I’m glad you’re here with me.”

“…me too.”

They pass the time, looking at clouds and chatting about the most inane things, but it’s comforting to have this pocket of normalcy. Sokka hasn’t had anyone his age to talk to, and he’s glad to be able to play silly games with her or talk about the big stuff that he doesn’t want to expose to the younger kids.

They’re in a middle of a game of thumb war when Zuko spots the familiar arc of the volcano. She gets up and immediately starts the descent.

“There it is.” She points out.

Sokka feels his nerve jump and his stomach do flip flops. The most secure prison in the Fire Nation and they’re just going to fly in by the seat of their pants. He takes a breath. Okay no problem. Just another day for Team Avatar. Although now that it’s just Sokka and Zuko what would it be? Sokko? No. Zuso? Hmmm….

Zukka. Team Zukka.

He nods proudly. “Alright Team Zukka for the win!”

Zuko blinks and just shakes her head. She doesn’t need to know everything that goes on in his crazy mind.

Unfortunately, as soon as they get into the lake, the balloon starts dropping rapidly into the ground. Sokka burns his hand as they splash onto the boiling lake, and they had to ditch the balloon. As it sinks into the bubbling water, he feels a pit of despair form and turns to Zuko. “I just sank the only way out for you.”

Zuko scoffs. “Sometimes you can be really dumb. I already told you, I’m prepared to go down with you.”

Sokka tries to ignore the loud *ba-dump-dump* of his heart when he sees her eyes glow fiercely as she says this. There’s a rush of fondness and exasperation all rolled into one as he watches the princess make her way up to the prison.

They manage to sneak in and get guard uniforms.

“Okay we’ll go around and meet outside in an hour.” Sokka says. “Hopefully my dad’s here.”

“Okay. Good luck.”

While Sokka takes a peek in the prison yard, Zuko goes to the mess hall. If there’s one thing she learned on the *Dragon Claw*, it’s that soldiers *loved* to gossip in the mess hall. She’s listened in on *way* too many inappropriate conversations back then.

Unfortunately, from what the guards tell her, no prisoners of war had been sent here. She goes outside to deliver the bad new but is unsure if the guard leaning at the railing is Sokka. She’ll have to be stealthy about this…

“Hello there, fellow guard…uh nice day out?” *Nailed* *it*.

“Zuko?” Sokka asks incredulously. “Why are you talking so weird?”

“I was trying to be stealthy.”

He blinks. “That was not at all stealthy. You practically held up a sign that said, ‘I don’t belong here. Arrest me.’”

“No I didn’t.” she denies.

“Riiight. Well did you learn anything?”

Her face falls. “I’m sorry. He’s not here.”

Sokka groans and hits his fists on the rails. “Of *fucking* course. I should have known, now we just went here for no reason, and it’s my fault *again*.”

Zuko doesn’t hesitate to put her hand on his shoulder. “Sokka, no. You were trying to get your dad back. It’s just bad luck. I thought he’d be here too.”

“Yeah but you’re not the one who made the decision to get him out of jail.” Sokka retorts. “I could have waited until after the comet to save him, but instead I just forced you to be here.”

Two warm hands grab his face and make him turn to Zuko. Her brows are pinched and her lips are turned down. “Sokka, you didn’t *force* me into anything. I volunteered to be here, and you need to stop blaming yourself for everything that could go wrong.”

“But I’m the *pla—*”

“If you say plan guy I’m going to throw you over the rails.”

He doesn’t think she’s serious, but he doesn’t want to risk it. So he clamps his mouth shut.

“Look, things don’t always go the way we plan, but that doesn’t always mean it’s your fault it goes wrong. What matter is that you can think in the moment and adjust to your surroundings. The true hallmark of a leader isn’t in obsessive planning, but in flexibility.” She advises.

Sokka swallows and pushes down that surge of attraction and fondness going through him. He puts his hands on hers and nods. “Okay.”

Zuko smiles sweetly and he has to lean forward and put his head against her forehead. The metal clinks awkwardly, but it feels good to know that she’s by his side. The part and he nods. “Alright. Let’s see if we can get any records from other prisons to find out where Dad is, and while we’re here let’s make sure there’s no one—”

His eyes nearly pop out of his socket. He can’t believe it. He nearly throws himself over the rails when he sees her.

“It’s Suki!” he exclaims. What is she doing here? What happened to her after they parted at the Serpant’s Pass?

“Suki?” Zuko asks tightly.

“Yeah she’s a Kyoshi warrior who helped us get to Ba Sing Se and we…” he trails off. They kissed. Twice. But when he kissed her that last time, he didn’t really feel…anything. It was still a good kiss, but there wasn’t any big emotions or relief. He thought there would be; she’s pretty much everything he wants in a girlfriend, but…

He discretely glances at Zuko.

But maybe he already had someone in mind.

“We’re friends.” He finally says. “She helped us out on Kyoshi Island and later at the Serpant’s Pass.”

“Oh…that uh one island with all the female warriors?”

“Yup. You kinda burned it down.”

“Right…”

A pause.

“So.” Sokka starts. “I’m glad this wasn’t a total bust. We can get her out of here.”

Zuko nods. “Okay, we should find out what cell she’s in and then you can talk to her.”

The don’t get to plan much more than that since they get called onto the yard.

The two of them quietly walk through the hallways until they reach Suki’s room.

“Knock if you see anyone coming.” Sokka says. Zuko nods and watches as he enters Suki’s room, the tight feeling once again expanding in her chest. She shouldn’t be jealous. They weren’t even together. Besides, Suki is probably a much better match for Sokka. She’s not in charge of a nation, she doesn’t have snooty elders or nobles to appease, and she’s not from a family who literally burns people and things for fun.

She’s so engrossed in her musings that she doesn’t notice a guard coming her way.

“What’s going on?” the guard asks.

Zuko jumps and stutters, “Uh well em…there’s just…”

“Is someone in that cell?” the guard asks scandalized.

In any other situation, the princess would love to promote her and give her medals of honor for defending the virtue of her prisoners, but right now is a bad time for this.

“Yes but you can’t go in there!” *Ugh stupid!* Why did she have to say it like that?

The female guard growls, “Step aside, newbie.” She warns. “That’s not how we do things here! I can’t believe you’d let something like this happen!”

It pains her to have to take this guard down, but she can’t let her catch Sokka. He’s the plan guy he needs to be the one on the outside. Zuko’s just the hired muscle; she can take a few days in prison. With that resolve, she tackles the guard to the ground. Obviously, that did not endear her to the guard and she shouts for help.

Sokka manages to sneak out of Suki’s room during the scuffle, but the guard calls out to him.

“Help! She’s an imposter!” the guard yells.

Zuko feels Sokka’s hands grab her and roughly takes her up. Another guard comes by to help the one Zuko took down.

“Get her into a holding cell!” the guard orders.

Sokka nods and quickly pulls her up. He gently pulls her arms back and presses her close to his chest. “I’ll get you out. Don’t worry.” He whispers.

“I know.”

The guards are shocked when they take off the helmet. They immediately go on their knees and bow.

“Princess Zuko.” One of them says. “Forgive our rough treatment of you. We didn’t know.”

One of the guards slap Sokka’s leg, “Get on your knees fool! That’s the princess!” he hisses.

The disguised teen sinks down and immediately bows, feeling a little caught off guard. Zuko is also, very much confused by this treatment. Wasn’t she branded as a traitor? Why would they be doing this?

“Please rise. I have been branded as a traitor, and no longer in the royal line.” She says.

One of the soldiers look up, “If we may freely speak?”

“Of course.”

“You saved my niece when she was captured by slave traders.”

“You helped my grandmother’s village when it was burning.”

st

“You stood up for the 41 regiment where my younger brother served.”

All three look up to her with reverence. “No matter what the Fire Lord says, you are the princess of our nation. You are the one who has cared for her people in the colonies when others haven’t.” Then they all glare to Sokka who is sweating nervously.

“Please don’t mind the young upstarts who think they understand.” They growl. “Princess, we will help you find a way out of here.

Zuko clears her throat, “Ah-em…it’s okay. You really should rise. I don’t want you to get in trouble with the warden, and I don’t want word to get out to the Fire Lord that there are people who still view me as a princess. As for escape…I need to stay here for a few days. I’m on a mission, so please just resume your normal duties.”

They reluctantly stand and nod. “We will leave you to change, Princess.” And they turn to leave, Sokka giving her a discrete thumbs up.

While they stand outside, Sokka glances at the three men. “So…Princess Zuko did all those things huh?”

“You think we’re lying?”

“No no! I just thought she was a bad banished princess who dishonored her father.”

One of them scoffs. “That is the biggest pile of hippo-cow shit I have ever heard. If anything her *father* dishonored her.”

“Wow but isn’t he like the *Fire Lord* and all that?”

“For now, he rules by the Grace of Agni.” One of them replies. “But I have friends in the temple who say that the sages have seen his fire burning low.” The other two nod knowingly.

Sokka has no idea what that means, but he’ll pretend he does. “How come I haven’t heard of this?”

They scoff. “The Fire Lord isn’t a spiritual man. He hasn’t visited a temple in years. Not since his wife disappeared. And the sages know that if they tell him, he’d burn them alive.”

“And how do you guys get this information?”

“You’re still a greenhorn ya? I betcha don’t have too many friends around the branches. Trust us, kid. We’ve got a lot of friends, and soldiers love nothing more than to gossip. We hear everything from who shits bricks in the colonies to which minister has the smallest dick in the palace.”

Sokka stifles a giggle. He really needs to start paying attention to what the soldiers are talking about. Zuko’s voice comes through and she says that she’s finished. They open the door for her and escort her to a holding cell.

“We’ll be by with dinner, princess.” The guards say, and they leave with a final bow. Sokka wants to stay so badly, but he has leave with them if he wants to keep his cover. For now, he’ll try to get as much intel as he can.

As they go, a group of men come towards them. There’s a stern man leading the group, with a headpiece over his forehead. The men near Sokka step to the side and bow.

“Warden.” They intone.

He nods and asks, “Is the spy in the holding cell?”

They shift nervously, and the warden clicks his tongue. “It’s a simple question.” He hisses.

“Yes, Warden.”

“Good. I have questions for them.”

Sokka feels the sweat bead against his neck. He needs to find a way out of here. *Fast*.

### End Notes

#### Chapter End Notes

**Lu Ten:** I guess he's okay.

**Yue:** I knew you'd see the light someday.

**Lu Ten:** But he better treat my baby sister right.

## Chapter 15

---

The warden barges in like an angry Komodo-rhino. He only needs one glance at the girl before smiling. “Ah, Princess Zuko. How nice it is to see you. I’m sure your father would be pleased to know that I found his wayward daughter.”

Zuko grimaces but doesn’t say anything.

“How did you get here anyway? And why are you here?”

The princess stays silent, and the warden growls and slaps her face, snapping her neck harshly to the side. One of the guards suck in their breath loudly. “W-warden.”

“Silence.” The warden hisses. “She is nothing but a traitor to the nation and to the crown. You *betrayed* my niece’s trust and now the Fire Lord is keeping a closer eye on her. To make sure your rebellious attitude hasn’t corrupted my niece.”

Zuko looks up. “Your niece?”

“Mai.” He answers.

Her breath catches. “I-is—is she okay? What did my Father do?”

“Don’t act like you care, *Princess*.” The warden warns. “You should have seen this coming when you betrayed your nation. Did you think the Fire Lord wouldn’t question my niece after the stunt you pulled? The only reason why I haven’t thrown you into the lake is because of that letter you sent her. It’s clear from that and from her confession that you didn’t speak of this to her.”

Zuko sighs in relief. “I’m glad she’s okay then.”

The warden harruphs. “I’ll make sure your stay here is very unpleasant, princess. Take her to the deepest levels!” he orders. The guards bind her hands and carry her out. They go deep underground. So far down that she can barely feel Agni’s energy. Her fire trying to reach out desperately to its guardian spirit.

The guards look pityingly at her and at least give her a blanket to ward off the coldness she feels being so far away from the source of her fire.

She curls up on the cot and closes her eyes. Sokka will think of a plan soon.

&#95;&#42;&#95;&#42;&#95;

Sokka combs through all the records, but there’s no sign of anyone else here they may know. He runs hand through his hair. There’s no more time to waste. He needs to get out of here with Zuko and Suki, fast. They don’t have much time since the warden probably sent a message back to the capital, and Sokka won’t endanger Zuko anymore.

He leaves the office and is walking down the hall when a guard comes up to him. “Hey help me get someone out of the cooler?” he calls.

Sokka nods. “Yeah no problem.”

They head towards a section with strange capsules and pods. The air feels colder here and there’s a loud humming beneath him.

“Woah.” He says.

The guard laughs, “Yeah pretty cool huh? These are the coolers. They keep a fire bender really uncomfortable by keeping them ice cold.” He opens a cooler and the large man from the morning comes sits there shivering violently. Sokka winces. That did not look fun, and by the feel of the air, it’s probably as cold as a winter’s day in the Poles.

The drag the man out, frost still sticking to his skin. It’s amazing how the cooler seems to insulate all that cold air inside…

An idea sparks in his head, and he glances back at the cooler. That might actually work.

Zuko is taken up and into the hall where the prisoners were busy moping and cleaning the floors. The guards hand her a mop and lead her to a more secluded area of the floor. Probably hoping that no one would bother her, but it seems to have the opposite effect.

Three burly men come swaggering towards her, and Zuko grips her mop tightly. The leader has a scar on his brow and yellowed teeth.

“My my look who’s here.” He says with a grin. “Princess Zuko of the Fire Nation.”

She ignores them and continues mopping, but the man grabs her arm. She glares menacingly and shows her teeth, but the men come in closer.

“Do you remember us?” the leader asks.

“No. I don’t remember *trash*.” She insults.

The hand on her arm grips her tighter, and she hides the wince of pain.

“We’re the ones you sent to here after you took away our merchandise!”

Merchandise? She looks closer at them. Earth Kingdom descent. Probably one of the bandits or poachers? Maybe even a pirate crew.

“Ill-gotten merchandise.” She growls.

“We spent a long time kidnapping all those people. All of them had some nice buyers lined up and then you had to show up and stop us two years ago.”

Oh…*oh.*

What a turn of fate. These were one of the first men she stopped after her banishment. They were the reason why Ying and Hansung were taken from their homes.

She smiles sharply. “You’re right. I was the one who stopped you from selling *people*, and I’d gladly do it again.” She jerks her arm forward causing the man to fall a bit and she slams her head against his nose.

The leader screams in pain as he clutches the probably broken nose. Blood dribbling down his chin.

“Yob bidch!” He shouts, and charges at her.

Zuko’s fought faster and stronger men than him. It’s easy to use his momentum and flip him into the wall. The other two shake the shock off and rush her. She ducks underneath their punches and slams her elbows against their knees. They go down just as she jumps up. She uses the force of that jump to slam her fists under their chins. Their heads snapping back and landing them on the ground. Stunned.

The leader is up and running to her; fists up, blood dripping, eyes mad with rage.

It’s almost too easy.

She ducks. Grabs his arm. She follows the force of his motion. Her body moving like water. And slams him to the ground. Knocked out cold.

She dusts off her hands. “That’s what you get for kidnapping my people.”

A beat.

Then the prisoners are nearly rioting. Hooting and laughing and hollering at her. They’d never seen a more impressive display in their life. The guards drag the unconscious men away and give the princess mixed looks of awe and respect. They settle the rest of the prisoners down and Zuko is happy to keep mopping in peace. Even with the occasional, ‘you’re fucking amazing princess’ directed at her.

Another girl comes up to her. She recognizes her as Suki. Sokka’s…friend (ex? Love interest?). She smiles at her and holds out her hand.

“That was *amazing*.” She says.

Zuko blushes and takes the hand. “Thanks. They weren’t really a challenge.”

“Oh I know. I bet you could beat five of those guys no problem.” Suki compliments.

“Haha maybe. It’s always fun to see the look of terror in a man’s eyes when they realize they’re about to be beaten by a skinny girl.”

Suki looks at her appraisingly and then beams. “You’re not so bad for the Princess of the Fire Nation.”

Ah Suki probably remembers her burning her village down. Zuko rubs the back of her head sheepishly. “Yeah sorry about burning your village down.”

Suki shrugs. “Well you didn’t burn it all the way down. Some roofs were on fire, but nothing was in danger of really burning.”

Zuko nods and bows. “Still that was wrong of me. I’m sorry.”

The other girl grins. “I forgive you. As long as you show me that move you just did.”

Zuko grins back. “I will if you show me some of the moves you did to take down my men.”

“Deal.”

When Sokka finds the two, the girls are mopping and chatting about the best moves to use to incapacitate someone. It’s horrifying and so so sexy.

“Wow you two look like you got to know each other.” He says with surprise.

Suki grins and throws her arms around Zuko’s shoulder. “Oh yeah, Zuko’s showed me some *killer* chi blocking moves.”

“I’m not as good as Ty Lee though.” Zuko replies. “But Suki showed me some of her fan dances, and they’re amazingly *deadly*. I’d love to try those in a spar.”

“Girl, I’ll spar with you *any day*.”

“Me too!”

Is it weird to feel jealous *and* aroused at the same time? Sokka shakes his head. Now’s not the time, brain.

“Okay before you two go on another tangent about weapons and fighting, why don’t we skooch on over there?” he says pointing to a little alcove under the stairs. Sokka gives them a basic run-down of the plan he’s hatched.

“How are you going to get inside the cooler?” Zuko asks.

“Yeah how are you going to get inside the cooler?” a voice from above calls.

Sokka nearly slaps his forehead. How did he *miss* a huge dude looming over them?

He tries to salvage the situation but from the pitying looks Zuko and Suki send him, it’s not working well. Finally, he sighs and lets the man join in on their plan.

“So how are we going to start a fight?” Sokka asks.

Chit-Sang grins. “Leave it to me.”

Sokka watches in mild amusement as Zuko and Chit-Sang pretend to be angry at one another. It was going well, until some of the prisoners start joining in.

“Chit-Sang! You just hit the princess!”

“How dare you!”

“Did you know she beat down those human traffickers!”

“You should be ashamed of yourself!”

Zuko nearly facepalms, but she takes a breath and says, “Calm down. Stop.”

The prisoners all stop.

“This is between me and Chit-Sang. It is my honor he has tarnished. I will be the one to handle it.” She says. Surprisingly, they all nod and mummer to themselves and stand aside.

“Of course, Princess.”

“Our apologies, Princess.”

“It’s a joy to see you beat down other men, Princess.”

Sokka doesn’t know to whether to be impressed or exasperated. Of course, she would end up as prison alpha in the ten minutes she’s in here. If they had time to wait, she could easily just take over the damn place and punt the stupid warden into the boiling lake. But as much as he would love to see that, he needs to be ready on her signal.

Her fire flares around her like a golden crown, and Sokka steps in.

“No fire bending allowed!” he says. “You’re going into the cooler.”

The glares from the other prisoners make him nervous as he puts Zuko’s hands behind her. Even some of the guards don’t look like they like this. As they walk along the halls, Sokka whispers which bolts she should be taking out. He hesitates as the cooler doors open. Zuko presses her head against the armored shoulder.

“It’ll be okay.” she assures quietly. Sokka nods and carefully helps her in. He takes one more look at her before he closes the door. Cold air hissing in his face.

It’s a nail-biting three hours, but he keeps himself grounded until he’s able to get to her. When the door opens, Zuko’s curled in a tight ball, but Sokka sees that she’s not shivering.

“Have you learned your lesson?” Sokka asks.

Zuko lifts her head and a plume of fire, escapes her mouth. She grins, “Yes I have.” And she shows him the bolts.

The teen’s knees nearly buckle at the sight, and his pants get desperately tight. Fuck that was really *hot*.

Suddenly there are footsteps, coming closer and Sokka pulls himself inside, shutting the door. They’re pressed close together and Sokka tries to point his groin away from her.

“S-sorry.” he says. “I kinda panicked.”

Zuko laughs, “I know. Are you cold?”

No. “A little.”

He watches her breathe in and release a cloud of steam that instantly warms the area around them. Her skin radiates warm heat and condensation starts forming around her.

“Wow.” Sokka croaks. Zuko shushes him and listens to the conversation outside.

*“—bandits, war prisoners. Even a pirate!” the guards say.*

*“A pirate you say…”*

The voices and footsteps trail off, and Zuko looks at him meaningfully. “That could be your dad coming in.”

Sokka bites his lip. “We’ve already spent a lot of time here.”

“But this could be your chance.”

He shakes his head, “No I’m not risking it. I’m not risking *you*.”

Zuko’s eyes grow hard. “Sokka. This isn’t about me. This is about you and getting your dad back.”

“But this is the exact same thing that happened in the invasion! I thought we could pull through even though the plan was failing all around us. I took a chance and I blew it! I got all of us in this mess because I didn’t know when to quit…I should have just quit before I *failed*.” He brings his hands to his head and lets it rest there, trying to get the feeling of absolute shame out of him. But then he feels Zuko’s hands gently pulling his hands back.

She looks at him with sympathy and understanding and cradles his hands in hers. “Sokka, you’re going to fail a lot. You’re going to fail over and over and over again.”

“That’s not making me feel better.”

“But you can’t just give up because you *think* you fail. You have to keep fighting. You have to give yourself hope even when things feel like they’re going to crash down on you. That’s the meaning of inner strength.”

Sokka’s lips tremble as he watches her eyes glow with each earnest word. He knows that Zuko understands failure better than anyone, and to hear her say this is comforting. But still…

“I’m the leader of this mission, Zuko. I have a responsibility to look after you.”

“Sokka I don’t need a babysitter—”

“I know you don’t, but I don’t want to lose you again!” Sokka exclaims.

Zuko pauses looking shocked. “Sokka…you won’t lose me.” she says quietly while putting a hand on his cheek.

“You don’t know that.” he replies stubbornly. “The warden has sent out word that you’re here. When your father finds out he’s going to send people here to take you away, and then—and then…” He grips her hand tight. He can’t even think about what Ozai might do to her.

The princess bites her lip and slowly leans close, hugging the teen in a soft embrace. “Sokka, I knew the consequences of betrayal. If I do end up captured, and returned to him, I’m ready.”

“Don’t say that.”

“We’re at *war*, Sokka. I know the price.” She pulls back and cups his face, making him look into her eyes. “I know you think you lost your stuff, and you’ve failed as a leader, but you *haven’t*. You’re still the same amazing brilliant man I know and trust. And I *know* you’ll find another way.”

“…Zuko.” He’s touched. The sheer trust she put in him, and the sincerity in her eyes. He hooks his pinky in hers and puts a kiss on it, like he did in Ba Sing Se.

“I promise I’ll get you out of here.” He says. Zuko nods and rests her head on his shoulder. Her warmth soaking all the way through his skin.

Sokka reluctantly has to pull back, “We should get moving. Suki and Chit-Sang are waiting for us down there.”

Zuko nods and they get to work taking the cooler out of the wall. It’s a slow and arduous trip bringing the heavy metal down the slope of the prison.

Chit-Sang and Suki help them out towards the end, and they push into the lake. Sokka steps back, “You guys go ahead. I need to stay and see if my dad is coming in.” Of course, Zuko stands beside him, reassuring smile on her face.

Sokka turns to Suki, “You should go. You’ve been here long enough.”

But Suki shakes her head. “No, I’m staying with you. You’re going to need all the help you can get.”

The teen smiles gratefully, and they watch Chit-Sang and his crew float away.

They’re halfway to the top when the alarms ring and Chit-Sang is captured. Sokka sighs in defeat. Another one of his plans failed.

Zuko is tapping his shoulder, “Sokka, the gondola’s coming.” Sokka looks up and sees it parking into place. One by one his heart seems to sink further and further down. None of these men are his father. He made them stay here for nothing.

But then they hear someone say, “Hey you in the back! Get out!”

As the man steps out into the sun, Sokka feels his heart soar. It’s his dad.

He quickly helps Zuko and Suki get back inside and runs to the platform where the warden is giving his speech to the new prisoners.

Sokka nearly howls with laughter when his dad trips the pompous warden.

Once all the prisoners are taken in and assigned rooms, Sokka goes in to meet up with his father. Hakoda is ecstatic and worried when he sees his son here.

“I’m not sure whether to hug you or ground you.” He says exasperatedly.

“I’d rather just have you hug me.” Sokka says. “It’d be kinda weird to have you grounding me just before Sozin’s comet arrives.”
