“Alright.” He acquiesces as he brings Sokka in for a tight hug.

The two relish in the reunion and pull back.

“So what’s the plan?” Hakoda asks.

Sokka looks down, “I—I don’t really have a solid one yet…”

“Really? Because it sounds like you do, but you’re just scared to tell me.”

The teen sucks in a breath, “I just—none of my plans have been working and I don’t feel so confident. A lot is riding on this to work and I made a *promise*…” he rubs his pinky absently. “I can’t fail. Not again.”

Hakoda rubs his son’s shoulders. “And you won’t.” he says confidently. “We’re all here supporting you, Sokka. And I know that you can do this. You’re a great leader, and I believe in you, son.”

He nod. “Okay. Lets work on this together.” He spends a while talking to his dad about the plan and polishing it up before heading to the other rooms. He goes to Suki’s, which is the closest, first, and then heads down to the bowls of the prison where they’re keeping Zuko.

He knocks quietly on the door and Zuko answers.

“What is it?” she whispers.

“Me and my dad made a new plan, here’s what’s going to happen—”

“Hey!”

Sokka’s heart jumps in his throat, and he swivels to the two guards coming down.

“What are you doing here?” they ask menacingly.

Sokka swallows, but he recognizes that voice. He’s one of the guards who bowed to the princess.

“I—I was just going to tell her my thanks…she actually saved one of my cousins back in the colonies.”

The guard narrows his eyes. “Is that so?”

“Yes. Please I just…I just need a few minutes.”

Zuko’s voice calls out, “I trust him. He’s not going to do anything.”

The guards nod to one another and step back, “Okay. But only a minute. The Warden called the princess up.”

Sokka nods and quietly tells her the plan. Then he bows low and moves out of the way of the other two guards. Her eyes briefly meet his before she has to go up and meet the warden.

If she has any hope of escaping, they’ll need to make that distraction as big as possible.

Zuko’s led to an interrogation room, and she’s surprised to see Mai sitting in the chair, cross-legged and glaring.

“Leave us.” She orders. The two guards bow and leave the two girls alone.

Zuko swallows nervously, “…Mai I—”

“What were you *thinking*?” Mai hisses. “Are you asking for a death sentence? I thought you knew better than to *disobey*!”

The princess steels herself against the torrent of accusations. She’s weathered through worse after all.

“Mai. It’s not like that. I did what I believe is *right*.” She answers. “I had to leave so I can restore the honor of our *nation*.”

“Do you think I care about the honor of our nation?” Mai says incredulously. “You *idiot*. I care about you! You’re one of my best friends, and you just left me with a stupid letter! You didn’t even hint or tell me—”

“I didn’t want my father to hurt you too!”

“Let him try.” She snarls. “I would have rather been punished for knowing than learning about your treason through a stupid measly letter.”

Zuko’s eyes start to swim with tears, “Mai…I didn’t—I never meant to *hurt* you like this. You’re my best friend too. I didn’t want anything to happen to you.”

Mai slumps, anger leaving her, and she puts her hands on Zuko’s shoulders. “How did I get stuck with a noble idiot for a friend?” She mumbles. “Your sister didn’t take it well.”

Zuko winces. “I had a feeling.”

“She nearly burned down that wisteria-willow tree you liked sitting under.”

“At least she didn’t burn the whole thing down. Progress, right?”

Mai’s lips quirk up. “So, what are you going to do?”

“…I have an escape plan. I need to get to the yard soon.”

Mai nods. “Okay. I’ll cover for you.”

“…Mai if they find out.”

“Hey, I’ll just tell them I got bored and lazy.”

Zuko flings her arms around her friend and holds her close. “Thank you, Mai.”

The other girl is tense but pats her back. “Just go. I don’t think I can take anymore sentimental shit.”

They part reluctantly, and Zuko looks back one last time to her friend.

Sokka’s able to get all the prisoners out, and after dad’s rather pathetic attempt to incite a riot, Chit-Sang shows them all up by creating a riot with ease. He looks around impatiently.

*Where is she?*

As if summoned by him, Zuko runs up to them after throwing a man down.

“What’d I miss?” she asks.

“Zuko!” Sokka exclaims. “Good you’re here. We need to capture the warden.”

“Okay how?”

Sokka grimaces. “Eh…I’m kinda still working on that.”

The princess facepalms. “Unbelievable!”

“You said a great leader has flexibility!”

“Yeah but not like this! This is a pretty important and crucial part of your plan.”

“Hey guys.” Chit-Sang interrupts. “I think your girlfriend is taking care of it.”

Sokka nearly says, ‘my girlfriend is right here’, but he looks up to see Suki running on people’s heads. Zuko eyes widen in understanding and urges them forward, “C’mon, I think I know what path she’s taking.”

“We can’t do death-defying acrobatics!” Sokka reminds.

“Fine. We’ll take the long way.” She pouts.

“I have a better idea.” Chit-Sang says and lifts the princess on his shoulders. He yells, “Princess Zuko coming through!” and the crowd parts around them as they run towards Suki.

Even most of the guards seem reluctant to stop them. Sokka would be laughing hysterically at the strange stunned look on Zuko’s face if he wasn’t trying to escape with their lives.

Suki grins as she shows off the captured warden to them. Hakoda grins and nudges Sokka. “That’s some woman.”

The teen chuckles. “Yeah she’s pretty great.”

Chit-Sang puts Zuko down and bows. “Apologies, Your Highness.”

Zuko waves him off. “Much more fun than taking a palanquin. Now let’s go.”

“How’d you even know that was going to work?” Sokka asks incredulously.

The large prisoner shrugs, “She’s prison alpha. Why wouldn’t they do it?”

The poor teen sighs. Sometimes it feels like his entire life has just defied logic at every turn.

The guards put up minimal effort when they see that they are with the princess and the Warden, and they manage to get onto the gondola. Sokka turns and sees Zuko standing there as the gondola continues to go. The air leaves his lungs and he shouts, “Zuko get on!”

But she doesn’t listen. She first kicks off the handle, and he sees her do a running start.

*Oh shit!*

He leans out the window hand outstretched as the crazy princess jumps.

Her hands reach out to his.

Sokka is nearly out the window, and he can feel his father holding tight to the seam of his pants.

He catches her.

And immediately pulls her up.

“Are you crazy?!” he yells holding her close. “What were you thinking?”

“I had to make sure they couldn’t stop us.” Zuko explains.

Sokka groans. Why did that have to be such a good reason? “You could have tried to jump sooner.”

“Not my fault the metal was hard to kick down.” She shrugs

*Tui and La give him strength.*

“Hey it looks like we have trouble.” Chit-Sang calls out. They look down and Zuko feels her heart stop when she sees the glaring eyes of her sister.

“Azula.” She whispers.

Suki grits her teeth beside her. “Time for a rematch.” She says and flips up to the top.

Sokka puts a hand on her shoulder. “Are you ready?” he asks.

Zuko nods and climbs up. They watch with trepidation as Ty Lee runs on the cables and Azula blasts herself with the handcuffs on the rope.

Ty Lee lands first, grey eyes sad as she looks to Zuko.

“Zuko…” she starts but presses her lips together before going for a punch. Suki takes her hit and the two start to fight.

Azula is the next to land. She blasts hot blue fire at them, but Zuko disperses it with a wave of her arms.

“*Sister*.” She hisses. Eyes gleaming like molten gold. “I should have killed you at the catacombs.” Her feet kick out and blast her. Zuko stomps down on the flames and sends a small blast to distract Azula.

As Zuko falls back, Sokka goes in with his sword and takes a swing. Azula is caught off balance and nearly falls. She catches herself and swings her legs forward to shoot a powerful blast at them. Zuko cuts it down with her hands, while Sokka stays behind him.

Suddenly the Warden cries to “Cut the ropes!” The gondala stops and suddenly lurches forward.

Sokka loses his balance, and his screams reverberate to her bones. Zuko lunges down to grab him just in time. She manages to hoist him up just as she sees the glint of a saw at the end.

Ty Lee watches in horror as they see two guards start cutting the ropes. “They’re cutting the ropes!” she exclaims, looking intently at Zuko and Azula.

Azula glares at her sister; her hands twitch at her side. Zuko sees another gondola coming towards them and sends a blast of fire at Ty Lee and Azula. Both girls dodge easily and jump to the incoming gondola. Ty Lee reaches out her hand, “Zuko!” she cries out, but Zuko stands back to Sokka, shaking her head slightly.

Azula grabs the acrobat’s hand and pushes it down forcefully. Her eyes a molten raging gold, her mouth in a downturn sneer. “Die with them then.” She snarls.

Zuko bites her lips, and tries to say something, but Sokka is pulling her back into the gondola.

“I hope this floats.” Hakoda says.

Even if it did, they won’t make it a minute in the boiling water.

All of a sudden, they see the men fall, and a group of guards flood the platform with Mai at the lead. They remove the metal bars on the winch and their gondola starts up again.

“Who is that?” Sokka asks.

“That’s Mai. And those are the guards who talked to me earlier.” Zuko says. She can’t believe it. Mai went against her sister. Why? Azula’s going to kill her. She wants to go back, she wants to try and save her, but Sokka’s grip on her arm is tight.

“Zuko…Zuko we *can’t*.” he whispers.

“I know.” she replies hoarsely.

They all tumble out of the gondola and run out of the crater’s lip. Zuko finally takes a breath of cool air and looks around.

“Zuko what are you doing? We gotta go.” Sokka says.

“Azula had to get here somehow.” Zuko says. “I think we can find her airship.”

They just have to walk a little further out and they find her large airship, docked on the port. After fighting Azula and Ty Lee, the few guards that are there are easy to dispose and they’re soon on their way back to the Air Temple.

Zuko sighs and finally let’s herself slide down the walls. She left Mai behind, *again*. And this time there was no letter to prove her ignorance.

Sokka sits beside her and stays quiet, letting her go through her emotions. Zuko, feeling the heavy weight of guilt, drops her head against his shoulder.

“I left them behind again.” She whispers. He puts his arms around her trembling frame and pulls her in close.

“Mai did it because she cares for you. So did those soldiers. Just think of them as another reason to win this.” He says.

“…okay.” she whispers. They sit there, silently. Thinking about the events of the past two days.

Unbeknownst to the two, Hakoda and Chit-Sang spy on the teens from a crack in the doorway.

“Your future, daughter-in-law is going to be Princess Zuko.” Chit-Sang whispers.

“I told you didn’t I?” Hakoda teases.

“I thought the princess was with Suki.”

“And I thought it was rude to spy?”

The two grown men yelp and back away as they face an unamused Suki.

“Whaat? Spying? Us?” Chit-Sang says in a high-pitched voice.

“Mmmhmm get your asses out of here.”

“Yes ma’am.”

Katara is nearly in tears when she sees her dad come out of the airship. She flings herself on him and laughs joyfully. Aang and Toph hug Suki tightly, and Appa comes to give her the biggest lick any of them have ever seen.

Sokka and Zuko stand back, letting everyone get to know each other. The princess turns to Sokka and nudges his shoulder.

“You did good, Leader.” She praises.

Sokka rubs his head, “Well, I couldn’t have done it without you.”

“I doubt that—”

“No.” he says seriously. “I honestly couldn’t. You took me out of my funk. Looks like the life-changing Zuko fieldtrip method works.” He grins.

Zuko laughs, “Don’t expect a fieldtrip every time though.”

“Hey lovebirds!” Toph yells. “Get in on this group hug!”

The two blush, but Sokka bows with a flourish and holds his hands out. “After you, my princess.”

“Thank you, Lord Sokka.”

The kids and two adults pile on one another, Appa curling his giant tail around them all and rumbling happily. It was the best group hug so far.

After dinner Katara goes with Zuko to wash the dishes. The two girls work silently for a while, the sound of clinking dishes were the only things between them.

“…thanks for rescuing my dad.” Katara says.

“Your welcome. It was the right thing to do.” Zuko replies.

“I’m also…sorry that I haven’t always been the most welcoming.” Katara says remorsefully. “I just didn’t really trust you, especially since Sokka said he saw you reaching into your sister’s lightening. I thought he was mistaken, and you were helping her kill Aang.”

The princess nods understandingly. “I didn’t really do a lot of things to endear you to me. I hurt a lot of people before I ended on the right path, and I’m grateful for your forgiveness and trust.”

Katara smiles at the older girl. “I’m glad you’re on our side now. Sokka really missed you.”

Zuko blushes. “Oh em—well I…I missed him too. When I was back at the palace.”

“You two are so cute.” Katara coos.

“Not cute.” She mumbles, but Katara just laughs and flicks a soap stud at her.

“Very cute. And you can’t stop me from thinking that.”

Zuko sighs as she dangles her legs over the ledge of the temple. The night air cool against her skin and the easing the aches and pains of her body.

“Am I allowed here? Or are you trying to brood in the dark by yourself?” Sokka asks walking over.

“No, you’re always allowed next to me.” Zuko replies.

“Better remember that when you’re Fire Lord. That means I get to sit in all your meetings.”

The princess laughs. “Sokka, I won’t be Fire Lord for a while, and those meetings are pretty boring.”

The warrior shrugs, “I bet I could make them fun.”

“Or you’d be challenged to Agni Kai’s on a daily.”

“As long as I have my trusty boomerang I can take you fire benders down. I did to you before remember?”

Zuko purses her lips. “That was one time. You caught me off guard.”

“That makes me the greatest boomerang master.” Sokka brags jokingly. “No one sees it coming.”

“You do know how to throw a wicked curve.”

“It’s all in the math.” He grins.

“…math huh?” It triggers a memory from long ago. Something with Lu Ten, and a saber-tooth lion moose? Something about…a husband?

Zuko blushes at the thought. No, there’s no way Lu Ten would have picked her husband like that. it’s nearly inconceivable.

“What are you thinking about?” Sokka’s voice jolts through her musings.

“Oh, um…ehm…Something my cousin told me.” she murmurs.

“About?”

Zuko turns away, “A good…husband.”

Sokka’s face turns red, “Oh oh really? So…em what did he say?”

The princess takes a quick glance at the water tribesman. “Just—just that he should have a saber-tooth-lion moose friend and is a boomerang master.”

The boy hums and rubs the back of his head, “Oh…well…I did make friends with Foo-Foo-Cuddly-Poops a little saber-tooth lion moose cub and I am a boomerang master.”

“Is—is that so?” she says in a high-pitched tone.

“Yep. Yeah, a reeaaall master.” He replies haltingly; he knows he’s probably so red right now, but he doesn’t want to hide it. The thought of being by her side, cherishing her, helping her, supporting her, it’s his sweetest dreams.

They lean closer together, shoulders brushing, heads dipping towards each other.

“Zuko?” Sokka whispers.

“Yes, Sokka?” Her eyes meeting his, golden irises shining in the moonlight.

He doesn’t fight it anymore. There’s just too much he can lose in this war, and he doesn’t want to waste one more moment.

They lean in for a kiss, and there’s just…relief and so much warmth and fire. Zuko’s clutching his shoulders tightly as he kisses her deeply, moans and whimpers flooding from her mouth. Sokka can’t get enough of the feel of her mouth on his, the tongue stroking his own.

They have to part, and Sokka marvels at the beautiful look of bliss on her face. He cups it gently in his hands and presses one more chaste kiss on her plush lips.

“Is…is this okay?” he asks hoarsely.

Zuko swallows and nods, “Yes, it’s more than okay.” she wraps her arms around his neck and pulls him close again for another kiss. They end up on the ground, Sokka on top of her and kissing her like a starving man. Zuko moans sweetly and presses her hips up, meeting his erection. Sokka groans and pulls away.

“I think we should stop for a moment.” He says.

Zuko nods, “Yeah, yeah we probably shouldn’t be doing it in the open.”

The rise and brush off their clothes, both flushed and smiling to themselves. Zuko put her head on his shoulder, “So, when did you make friends with a saber-tooth lion moose?”

Hakoda can’t really sleep. Maybe he’s it’s the excess adrenaline that still lingering in his system, or maybe he’s just getting old. Either way he finds himself wandering the peaceful halls of the temple. As he looks around, he wonders what it would have looked like when it was filled with life. The air nomads flying and cruising around the skies high above here.

Now that it’s just an empty monument. Filled only with echoes of memories of distant pasts.

He’s walking along the lengths of the open walkway when he spots two figures near the edge. It’s the princess, with her feet dangling over the ledge and his son’s head resting on her lap fast asleep.

One of her hands is tangled in Sokka’s loose hair, and in the princess’s other hand is a dark red flame. Steady and strong, almost as if it’s waving to the beat of her breath. Hakoda goes up to the two and stands next to the princess. The area is warm and comforting.

“May I sit?” he asks. Zuko nods and motions for him to get down before returning the hand back in Sokka’s hair.

“I’m glad you found my son again.” Hakoda says. “It’s a relief that you’re fighting with him now.”

“Why?” she asks. “I—I did a lot of things to hurt your children and village, and I’m so sorry and ashamed for my actions.”

The chief nods, “I believe you, and I forgive you.”

“Even when I did all those terrible things?”

“Wanna go again?” the little girl asks.

“Just give me a minute to catch my breath.” Zuko groans. Why does everything hurt? How does such a tiny little thing pack such a mighty punch?

“Aw c’mon, Sparky. I know you can take more of a beating.”

“Just because I can take a beating doesn’t mean I want to.” Zuko grumbles.

The tiny girl laughs and make a step for her to sit on. “Okay fine, then let’s talk about your little night trips to Sokka’s room.”

Zuko goes red. “How did you know about that?!”

Toph wriggles her feet, and the princess sighs. Of course. How did she forget?

“Please keep this quiet, Toph.” Zuko asks.

“Why? Are you ashamed of him?”

The princess shakes her head. “No way. I’m so proud of Sokka and everything he’s done. It’s just…since I was young, I’ve always had my life so closely scrutinized and I just want this for myself.”

The younger girl nods, “I get it. My parent always kept their nose in my business.”

“Yeah, I heard about that.”

“Noble life huh?”

“Not as great as people always think it is.”

Toph nods in understanding. “Well, when you have your wedding, I’m calling dibs on Best Girl.”

“Isn’t it Maid of Honor?” Zuko asks with a smile.

“I like Best Girl better, and we’ll throw the most kick ass party that’ll make the old people have a stroke.”

Zuko laughs, “Well *if* Sokka and I get married I’ll let you be Best Girl.”

“Sweet. Now get up and let’s go again.”

&#95;&#42;&#95;&#42;&#95;

“This is probably one of the most important techniques I’ll be teaching you.” Zuko says.

Aang shifts eagerly. “Are you going to teach me how to shoot lightening?”

“No.”

“Aw.”

“I’m going to teach you how to *redirect* lightening.”

Aang immediately perks up at this. “So cool!” he says.

The princess chuckles slightly at his eagerness. “Pay attention carefully. This isn’t the type of bending you’re used to. This is an internal bending. You are bending the energies and chi of your body.”

She goes through the motions first. “Let the energy of the lightening flow through the tip of your finger, then guide it down to your stomach, where your chi reserves are. Then release it once more.”

Aang goes through the motion with her and frowns. “How do I know if I’m doing it right?” “Feel the flow of energy.” Zuko answers.

“But what does that mean?”

Zuko stops and pauses. When she learned with Iroh, she just naturally felt her chi move, but maybe that’s because she’s a fire bender. Her chi is all internal.

An idea pops up and she places his hand against her stomach. Aang blushes but one quick glare from his teacher snaps him out of it quickly.

“Do you feel the energy here?” she asks.

Aang is unsure and gives her a helpless look. Zuko sighs, “Okay just like during fire meditations, focus on the energy of the body. Feel my inner fire and the way it spreads through me.

The young teen closes his eyes and stretches out his feelings. It’s hard, but then he focuses on Zuko’s breath. When she breathes, she expands her stomach first and then pushes her lungs down to get as much air as possible. He feels it. The flicker of her inner fire. Her energy. It originates in her stomach and travels through her entire body.

“Woah.” He whispers. “Is that in me too?”

“It’s in all of us.” Zuko answers. “But as fire benders we have a greater connection to it because we use it as our fuel to create fire. Now follow the flow of my hands.”

As she goes through the motion of the lightening redirection, Aang’s hands follow hers, and he can feel the energy almost *bending* inside her and then releasing.

He nods. “I think I got it.” He tries it again with her, paying close attention to the energies in his own body, and he feels himself bending it just like her. “It’s like I’m bending energy.” He exclaims.

Zuko smiles, “Yeah I guess we are. But this time it’s not an external reaction but an internal one.”

Aang stops. “What do you mean by that?”

The princess looks at his lost expression, and she guides him to the fountain and sits him down. “I’ll try to explain it as best I can, but Uncle is much better at it then me.”

She gives him the same lecture Uncle used to drill in her. About the nature of bending and incorporating all the nations into your style. The way the energies of the bodies react with the natural energies outside to create the external reaction.

Throughout it, Aang nods and listens carefully. By the end, he’s contemplative.

“I never thought of bending that way.” He says. “But it makes sense. We’re bending our internal energies to match those outside, and then with lightening redirection you’re manipulating your own energies to take in the lightening and guide it somewhere else.”

Zuko nods. “Well it’s almost lunch, we better clean up and get something to eat. You did good work today.”

Aang grins. “Thanks! Oh but will you teach me lightening, too?”

Zuko purses her lips, “I’m—I’m not sure I can actually make lightening.”

“Why not?”

She sighs, “When you bend lightening you need to have a calm peaceful mind in order to separate the negative energies from the positive energies. If you don’t do it properly it’ll blow up in your face. *Literally*. I can’t tell you how many times I’ve exploded myself trying to make lightening.”

“Well can’t you try it now?” Aang asks. “You seem pretty calm and peaceful.”

The princess pauses. He’s not wrong. And she was nearly able to do it in the catacombs.

“Okay but you need to stand *far* back, and don’t come anywhere near me.”

“Will do Sifu Hotman!”

“On second thought, come a little closer. You should practice your lightning redirection.”

The avatar squeaks in fear and scurries *far* away from her. Zuko smirks and then takes a breath. Just like Sokka told her before. Think of the things that get her into a peaceful state of mind.

She moves.

The energies separating in her hands.

Static jumping on her skin.

Ozone on her tongue.

A crackle of electricity dancing on her fingertips and then—

*Turn it back to fire.*

*Gently combine the energies again.*

From the white electric currents, it slowly turns back into a brilliant red-orange and explodes as a blast of fire. Aang shouts in awe at the size of the fire blast.

“Zuko that was amazing! I saw sparks and then it turned into fire again!”

“Well if I shot lightening here, that would look really suspicious.” Zuko replies. “We’re trying to lay low remember?”

Aang rubs the back of his head sheepishly, “Right, right. But that was amazing! Think you can teach me?”

Zuko shifts uncomfortably. When she held lightening in her fingertips, the power was so raw and forceful. It’s not a move you can control the intensity of. You can only direct the course.

“I think you should master the advanced fire katas first.” Zuko answers. “Then we can talk.”

“Aw.” Aang pouts.

Zuko smiles a bit. He’s still a child. A child that’s going to have to end her father. The thought makes her frown.

“There’s actually another reason why I don’t want to teach you lightening.” She says.

Aang’s eyes are wide, and he tilts his head questioningly.

“Lightening…you can’t control lightening. Not really. You’re just path for the energy. Once you create it, you have to release it.”

“But you just turned it back to fire.” Aang replies.

“Yes, but I didn’t finish the full kata and I had to carefully combine the energies or it would have exploded on me. In the heat of battle, you can’t just change your mind like that.”

“But—”

“Aang.” Zuko interrupts. “When you generate lightening directly at someone, you’re essentially aiming to kill. Always.”

The young boy goes pale. “…I didn’t think about that.” he admits. “I just thought…maybe I could use it to stop Ozai.”

“Maybe you can. But you have to have a clear mind and understand that a single misstep on your part can kill him. You have to prepare yourself for that option when you handle lightening.” She explains.

Aang gulps audibly. “I don’t want to kill anyone. It’s not in the air nomad way.”

Zuko looks sadly at him and puts a hand on his shoulder. “I know. Murder is…difficult.”

“…Have you killed before?” Aang asks.

Zuko nods, “Yes. Most in self-defense, but...” she pauses, “When I was rescuing some children from a pirate ship, one of the pirates was holding a child hostage, and Jee couldn’t make a move in case the child got hurt. But I was behind the pirate. He was holding a dagger to the child’s throat, and if I didn’t stop him properly, he could easily killed the child. So I snuck up on him, and I killed him with my dao. On swift slice to his neck and…well he was gone. That was the only person I ever killed on purpose.”

They’re silent. Aang contemplating and Zuko sitting peacefully.

“Do you regret it?” Aang asks.

“In a way.” She says. “I never wish to kill people unless it’s the last restort. But at the same time… the pirates would have sold the child or kept them as a slave on their ship. I deemed the life of the child worth more than the evil man who kidnapped him, but it still haunts me.”

She looks seriously at him, “I don’t regret killing the man to save the child, and I’ve made peace with the decision. But killing someone like that leaves a scar on your soul. I couldn’t sleep well for weeks. It’s why…” she pauses and takes a deep breath. “I could have killed my father.”

Aang gasps in surprise. “W-what? When?”

“On the day of the Black Sun.” she answers. “He shot lightening at me when I told him that I was going to join you, but I redirected it. In that brief moment, I really thought about it. I could have ended the war and killed my father then and there with his own lightening. It would be a fitting end I suppose, getting slain by the worthless daughter because of a technique that my true father gave me. But I didn’t.”

“…why not?”

She smiles ruefully. “I don’t fully understand. I guess, it’s because no matter how much terrible abusive shit he put me through, he was still my father, and I couldn’t bring myself to strike him down like that. And a part of me wishes I could have killed him. Maybe if I did, you wouldn’t have had to. I have supporters within the soldiers. I could have potentially taken power, banished or imprisoned my sister, and stopped the war at that moment.”

Aang is still looking at her with wide eyes, but there’s a glimmer of understanding in his eyes. “But you didn’t.”

“No…because I don’t think it would have really brought peace.” Zuko admits. “If I killed him, it’s just going to look like a power-grab, and it just substantiates the circle of violence and abuse of my family. And more than that…I just couldn’t do it.” She has to pause and take another breath.

“All my life, he’s always seemed like this immovable infallible figure. But when I confronted him…when he tried to keep me there by manipulating my emotions and destroying my self-esteem…I just saw a sad empty *void*. And I just couldn’t bring myself to murder something like that. I just…pity him.”

She turns to Aang. “I understand if you don’t want to kill the Fire Lord. I don’t think I can ever fight with the will to kill. To protect, yes. But not to just kill people. Whatever decision you make, I’ll support it. This fight is on you, and you should be the one to decide his fate. We shouldn’t be asking you, a twelve-year-old child, to take someone’s life. So whatever you do, make the decision that you can make peace with.”

They sit there in silence, mulling over what was said. Aang finally nods and hugs Zuko. “Thank you. Murder is a complicated thing in the Air Nomad culture too. When you kill without consent, you’re impeding on someone’s freedom to live. But…I’ve heard from some of the monks that there are rare instances where killing is just something that can’t be avoided. You just have to learn to make peace with the actions that you’ve made. I think Monk Gyatso had to do the same thing when the Fire Nation attacked.”

He bites his lip, “I don’t know if I can kill him, but…I’ll meditate on it. Pacifism is the way of the Air Nomads, and as the last of my people, I don’t want to be the one to go against that teaching.”

Zuko smiles sadly and pats his head, “I know. It’s harder for you because you want to keep the spirit of the Air culture alive. Taking someone’s freedom is considered the gravest offense for your people.”

“But he’s also taking the freedom of others.” Aang replies sadly. “I shouldn’t have an issue killing him. He’s done so much bad.”

“No matter how bad someone is…taking a life is still hard.” She says. “You still have time to think on it.”

“What if I make the wrong decision?”

“Then you fix it.” Zuko replies. “All decisions have consequences. Sometimes good, sometimes neutral, and sometimes really fucking bad. But no matter what happens, the most important thing is accepting and understanding the consequences and if you need to fix it. Then fix it. Simple as that.”

Aang chuckles. “It does sound simple when you say it that way.” He looks up at her with gleaming grey eyes. “You’re pretty wise, Sifu Hotman.”

“It’s all the tea Uncle gave me.” she grins.

They laugh together and then Toph, Katara, Suki and Sokka come over.

“What’s with the party?” Toph asks. “And why wasn’t I invited?”

“I just had a soul-searching moment with my fire bending Sifu.” Aang replies with a grin. “Nothing you would be interested in.”

The earth bender shrugs. “Eh you’re probably right. Now c’mon! It’s time to do your earth bending!”

Aang groans but let’s himself be dragged by the smaller girl. “I thought it was time for my lunch break!” Aang whines.

“No way. I don’t want you vomiting all over the place when I smack you around.” Toph declares.

While the two younger ones argue, Sokka sidles up to Zuko. “Had a good talk with him?” he asks.

“I think so.” Zuko replies. “It depends on what he’ll do with it.”

“Hmm sounds heavy.” He says seriously, but then he lights up and asks, Wanna head to the market after lunch?”

Zuko laughs, “You sure turned that around quickly.”

“Hey, we’re fighting a war here. We need some downtime once in a while or we’ll go crazy.”

The princess looks hesitant, “What if someone recognizes me?”

“Just wear a hood or large hat.” Sokka suggests. “I saw a lot of pale ladies and men wearing them while they’re out in the sun.”

“Okay. I guess I shouldn’t be showing my fire bending anyway.” She replies.

Katara and Suki looks inquisitively at her. “What does fire bending have to do with it?” Suki asks.

“Fire benders never burn, no matter how pale their skin is.” Zuko answers. “But we also don’t tan.”

“Huh no wonder you were still pale even after traveling all through the Earth Kingdom.” Sokka muses. “Turns out it was just another weird bending power.”

Zuko chuckles and punches his arm playfully, “Nothing about my bending is weird.”

“It’s totally weird.” Sokka grins. “But I like it anyway.”

The fire bender blushes but loops her arms around Sokka’s. “I like your weird non-bending powers too.”

Suki leans to Katara and whispers, “We sure they’re not together?”

“No way.” Katara whispers back. “Those two can’t keep a secret to save their life.”

&#95;&#42;&#95;&#42;&#95;

“Do you think they know?” Zuko asks as Sokka looks through the various wares. The boy shrugs.

“They must suspect.” Sokka answers. “But I’m fine with not confirming it just yet.”

“Me too, I just…” she picks up a random dish and pretends to look at it. “I just want this for ourselves right now.”

The boy wraps his arm around her waist and pulls her close. “I know what you mean. I’m sure Toph will find out soon enough though.”

Zuko winces, “Yeah she already knows.”

“Ugh I should have known.” He says slapping his head. He’s about to say something else when his face lights up with excitement. He runs out of the shop towards the other side of the street, talking avidly to a man putting up posters. The poster man smiles and hands him one and Sokka runs back to her excitedly.

“Look look!” he says excitedly holding up the poster. The princess scrunches her nose in disgust when she sees the actors.

“The Ember Island Players? Ugh, no they’re terrible. They always butcher Love Amongst the Dragons.” She complains.

Sokka rolls his eyes, “Not about that you theatre nerd, the name of the play!”

Zuko squints and looks further down. Her eyes widen and she’s shaking her head. “Nuh-uh. No way Sokka.”

“C’mon pleeeaaassee.” He wheedles.

She crosses her arms. “No way! This isn’t going to be a good play.”

Sokka clutches the poster close and gives her his best puppy-dog look. Trying to look as pathetic as possible.

Zuko sighs pinching the bridge of her nose. “Fine, let’s go ask the others and see if they want to see it. If I have to be subjugated to this travesty, you’re all going down with me.”

Sokka beams and kisses her cheek, “Yes! Thank you, babe.”

“Let’s just finish the shopping and ask them.” Zuko grumbles blushing deeply at the pet name.

&#95;&#42;&#95;&#42;&#95;

They all end up going to the play that night. The younger kids and Suki sit on the lower pew while Sokka and Zuko sit on top. He skootches close to her and slings an arm around her shoulders once the lights dimmed. The play’s portrayal of the siblings were not that…flattering, but Zuko can’t help her snickers.

“I don’t think about food the time!” Sokka exclaims after the actor spews out another joke about meat. “And I am *way* funnier than that.”

“Riiigghht what was it you did?” Zuko asks pretending to think. “Oh, comedy bending?”

The boy crosses his arms. “Yes and you can’t shame me for it. Just wait until your actress comes out.” he huffs.

It doesn’t take long, the curtains go up and the scene changes to Zuko looking over the bow wind, whipping the actress’s ponytail. She looks…surprisingly gallant? Zuko squints and leans forward in trepidation while Sokka snorts into his hand.

“Oh I forgot how terrible your haircut was.”

“It wasn’t that bad!” Zuko denies.

Sokka gives her a look and she huffs petulantly. “It wasn’t that bad.” She says again.

The actress starts to speak, “Uncle, look a light! After so many years, I’ve finally found the Avatar!” she declares dramatically. “Long have I searched and now the time is ripe!”

Zuko winces, “They make me sound so dramatic.”

“You kinda are.”

“I am not!”

It’s unfortunate that the actress repeats the same line a few seconds after, and Zuko has to hide her face in her hands. Sokka laughs, “Well, I like your dramatic-ness, Princess.”

“Thanks…and your jokes aren’t as bad.”

“You mean *brilliant*.”

“Don’t push it, water boy.”

As the play moves on, Zuko is feeling a little more uncomfortable at her actress portrayal. She’s… very confident? Why is she portrayed so nicely compared to the others?

“Geez they really like you in the Fire Nation.” Sokka comments. “It’s kinda nice.”

Zuko purses her lips, “I guess, but it’s dangerous for them. Especially since I’m technically a fugitive.”

“Eh just let it happen.” Sokka shrugs. “It’s nice to know that you’re so loved.”

Things come to a head when they reach the Northern Water Tribe. Actress Zuko is sneaking into the fortress, and Actor Sokka meets her there.

“Princess Zuko!” Actor Sokka exclaims, “What are you doing here?!”

“I’m here to protect my nation!” Actress Zuko declares. “And I must stoop down to ask you Southern Barbarians for help. For Zhao plans to kill the moon spirit and destroy our land!”

“Then we shall make a truce, for now!”

The two actors end up close as they shake hands. It almost feels like there’s electricity in the air as the two members shake hands for quite a while. The five on the bottom all look up and smirk at the two. Zuko blushes furiously. “I didn’t make goo-goo eyes at Sokka like that.”

“Yeah and I was not all over her.”

“Suuurrreee.” Toph drawls. “Even I can feel the tension and I’m *blind*.” She snickers.

“Just keep watching.” Sokka mutters.

In this version of events, Actress Zuko kills Actor Zhao very heroically and strikes a pose while Actor Sokka sighs behind her.

“Princess Zuko, you truly are the rarest and most beautiful flower!” Actor Sokka exclaims. “Please stay with me!”

The actress turns away and sticks her nose up. “No, Sokka! We can’t. It’s forbidden. I can never love you. Now I must leave!” She twirls her white cape (why did she have a cape?) dramatically and leaves.

At this point, the peanut gallery is laughing hysterically while Zuko is trying to hide her face in her hands. Even Sokka is muffling his chuckles.

Somehow it just devolves from there. Every scene with the actress and actor is charged with tension. They keep the audience on their toes with their will-they-won’t-they action. Even the audience seems engrossed in their relationship. It gets worse when they get to Ba Sing Se.

Actor Sokka stands on the ground as Actress Zuko leans out from her window. The actor hams it up as he declares himself to her.

“Oh my Sunshine, how I’ve longed to see you again.”

“Sokka, you know that this is forbidden! But you tempt me so. How can I resist someone so primal and manly?”

Katara smiles widely as she looks up at the two teens, “Is that why you were out at night sometimes?”

“I actually went into her apartment, okay?” Sokka retorts. “I didn’t just shout up at her from the ground; the neighbors would have killed me.”

“Not like they did anything fun.” Toph grumbles.

“Toph!” Zuko and Sokka hiss as they blush red.

They’re allowed some (very little) respite when the actors continue. The actor steals the actress away and takes her to romantic restaurants and scenery. When they part, the worst thing happens and the actors start *singing*.

“Oh Agni.” Zuko mutters as her counterpart croons about forbidden love and *feeling the pull of* *desire towards the Southern Boy.*

“It’s not that bad. It’s kinda catchy.” Sokka replies.

“I’m never speaking to you again.”

They finally get back to the main story when Zuko and the Gaang go to rescue Appa. It comes with a surprise though.

“Wait…Jet *died*?” Zuko whispers incredulously.

Sokka shifts uncomfortably, “Uh…yeah I guess no one ever told you that.”

She glares at him, “Yeah that would have been nice to know.”

They get to the Crystal Caverns, and Actor Sokka backs the actress into a wall as slams his hands into the walls caging her between his arms.

“My love.” He declares. “Come with *me*. Let’s leave this place together! I can’t stand another moment alone without you.”

The actress takes his face between her hands. “Oh Sokka, you tempt me so, but what about my nation? My people? I can’t just leave them.”

“Please my love! Forget about your duty and run away with me!”

Zuko purses her lips as the actors go on. “That is so out of character. You would never have asked me to do that.”

“Aw thank you.” Sokka replies touched. “And I know you would have kicked me ass if I asked you to.”

By the time the second act ends, Zuko is feeling distinctly uncomfortable as she relives the moment of her betrayal. Sokka pulls her close to him, “You okay?”

“Yeah, just…it’s hard watching it sometimes.”

“You wanna step out? I’ll go with you.”

“No, I’ll be okay.”

The second act is not even close to being better. Sokka shifts uncomfortably as the play mocks him for the failure of the Invasion. Zuko keeps her hand on his arm as they go through the events, and in turn Sokka keeps his hand on hers as they watch her confrontation with her father.

“Father! You’ve destroyed the image of our country! We’re tired of war and we need to end. That’s why I have to go with the Avatar!” she declares.

Even with an actor, the way Actor Ozai looks condescendingly down at her sends a chill down her spine. At least *that* actor was half-way decent.

“Then you are a fool daughter! I have given you everything and you would throw it all away? For what?”

“For my country and for the love of Sokka!” she declares.

Sokka whistles, “Wow I didn’t remember that part of your speech.” He whispers with a sly grin.

“Shut up, Sokka.”

The ending was…a surprise. In the major battle, Ozai was dealt with a terrible blow as he fought against Aang. In his death throws, he turns to Actress Zuko. “Avenge me, my daughter!”

Actress Zuko cries with anguish and turns against Aang. She rallies the troops and Azula as she fights against Aang. She kills him easily, and in the final scene faces Sokka in battle. It’s a dramatic scene where the two face each other. Hair whipping in the wind.

“Why Zuko? I loved you!” Actor Sokka cries.

“But I love my country more!” Actress Zuko declares and shoots fire at him. “Forgive me my love!” she cries as the actor disappears under a trap door.

Sokka looks wide-eyed. “Did you just kill me?”

“I think I did.” She whispers horrified.

In the end, Zuko stands victorious over the charred remains of the Earth Kingdom with the banners flying dramatically around her. “We have done it! I have avenged you Father and I’ve ended the war by destroying the Earth Kingdom! Now the world will be swept in flame and the Fire Nation will reign supreme with me at the helm.”

The end of the play leaves the group in a depressed state. The group trudges out of the playhouse with sullen hearts.

“That was not a good play.” Katara says.

They all chorus with similar sentiments and head to bed in silence. Sokka heads to Zuko’s room this time and snuggles close to her. He wraps his arms around her waist and buries his face in the nape of her neck.

“I’m sorry. I shouldn’t have taken us to it.” He apologizes.

Zuko hums in agreement and turns around. “I told you the Ember Island Players were shit…but it’s not your fault. You didn’t know, and you just wanted to give us all a fun time. I was pretty surprised too when I saw that ending. They’re not normally so explicit.”

“Still fucked up of them to have you kill me.” Sokka grumbles.

“I would never do that. You know that right?”

Sokka smiles and holds her close. “Yeah, I know. It’s actually kinda badass that they made you the Fire Lord though.”

She grimaces. “They really should have given it to Uncle.”

“Nah. They like you better. It’s like those prisoners at the Boiling Rock. At least your people know that you’re going to be a good ruler.”

She blushes. “Thanks, Sokka.

“Anytime, babe.”

The princess smiles weakly, “I’m so happy I have you guys.”

Sokka’s eyes get a little watery, and he peppers her face with kisses. “And we’re happy to have you too. Don’t ever forget that.”

Sheer warmth overtakes her as she buries herself deeper into Sokka’s embrace. Her heart thrums with each kiss, each whisper and seems to tell her to remember this feeling. They fall asleep together, bathed in moonlight.

### End Notes

#### Chapter End Notes

**Lu Ten:** I now understand why Auntie Ursa hated the Ember Island Players.

**Yue:** You like those crappy actors before this?

**Lu Ten:** So I liked their version of Love Amongst the Dragons! What's the big deal?

**Yue:** I can't even look at you anymore.

&#45;-

Thank you for reading! I hope you enjoyed the Ember Island Players!

## Chapter 17

### Notes

#### Chapter Summary

Hi guys! I won’t be able to update next week so I’m just going to release the last two chapters today and tomorrow. Sorry for changing the schedule so suddenly.

### Text

---

As the comet gets closer, Zuko feels…antsier. It’s more than just pre-battle jitters. She feels her inner fire stoking and flaring with each day. Already, she can feel the force of the comet pulling at her core, and when they train in mock-battles, she feels that her fire is more forceful than before.

Sokka has made up basic battle plans for the day of the comet, and runs them through drills every day. It’s nice to be able to train with everyone, but a problem continues to persist with each battle. Aang’s pacifism.

The Melon Lord is within reach once again, and Aang just…stop. His staff hovers mid-swing, and he turns away. Distressed and torn.

Sokka stomps over to him, “What was that?” he exclaims.

“I—I can’t do it, Sokka.” Aang replies. “He’s a person too. I can’t—I can’t kill him.”

The warrior glares down at him and with a fast swing, he cleaves the melon in half, letting the juicy pieces splatter against the ground.

“There that’s how you do it.” He hisses and walks away. Zuko sighs and goes after him.

“Sokka, that was a little harsh.” She says putting a hand on his shoulder. “And that’s me saying it.”

“…I know.” he answers. “But he can’t just keep dancing around the subject. He needs to kill the Fire Lord if we’re going to end this war.”

“He’s only twelve.” She answers. “We’re asking a twelve-year-old to kill another man…that’s just, *wrong*.”

Sokka presses his hand against his eyes. “I know. I know that, but we need to end this before Ozai completely destroys another nation.”

“Jee has smuggled all their plans to us. We can try to stop the airships before they take off and then we can capture my father.”

“And then what?” Sokka asks tiredly. “He’s a master fire bender. Dangerous and full of power, how are we going to safely lock him up?”

“…we could use the coolers.” she suggests.

Sokka puts his hand on her shoulders, “You know that’s not a true viable option. I don’t want to make him do this too, but it’s the only way. If anyone from another nation does it, there could be massive repercussions and retaliations afterwards. It has to be Aang. He’s the only one who has the power and clemency to bring about an era of peace.”

Zuko nods solemnly. “I know, Sokka. I know this better than anyone else, but you can’t ask him to do something that he’ll regret for the rest of his life. You have to trust Aang.”

“…okay. But one wrong move from Ozai, and I’ll kill him myself.”

Zuko nods. “I know, but please give Aang a chance first.”

He sighs and kisses her forehead. “I will. But I reserve the right to punch him in the face.”

“I wouldn’t have it any other way.”

&#95;&#42;&#95;&#42;&#95;

It’s no surprise that Aang vanishes just a few days before the big battle. He’s been under so much stress and so torn about taking another life. It doesn’t surprise Zuko in the least bit.

“Well, the plan hasn’t changed.” Zuko says. “We’ll still meet up with my crew and Uncle.”

“I thought you and the crew decided not to get any locations for security reasons.” Katara asks.

“They didn’t.”

“And how are we going to find them?” Suki asks.

“I know a woman.”

June is still just as annoying and just as lecherous as ever. She greets them with a charming smile.

“Well Princess, looks like you finally took my advice. How’s the threesome working for you?”

“I’m not in a threesome!” she yells. “We’re just here to hire you!”

June laugh, “Oh I know. We’ve been expecting you.”

“We?”

Suddenly a very familiar voice and figure runs towards them.

“Princess Zuko!” Ying bows. “I’m so glad to see you’re alright.”

Zuko blinks incredulously. “Ying? W-what are you doing here? I thought you were stationed elsewhere?”

“Oh I told them I went on a ‘surveyor mission’ to the mines.” Ying replies. “We knew you’d end up going to June for help to find the others so I volunteered to go to her first and greet you before the big day.”

“She’s been a real *help*.” June grins. “You sure, I can’t just take her, Princess?”

“Get your paws off my metal worker, June.” Zuko growls.

The bounty hunter laughs, “Too late for that, Princess. I’ve had my paws all over her.”

Zuko makes a face, while the others blush. “You’re such an animal.” She mutters.

June pays no mind and ushers them along, “Well now that you’re here, we can finally get going.”

“Don’t you need something of Uncle’s?” Zuko asks.

“I’ve already provided her with the sample.” Ying replies holding up one of her uncle’s stinky sandals. Zuko looks carefully at it and blinks.

“Huh, that was the same one I was going to use.” she says pulling up the other piece of the pair.

Toph grins. “It’s so cute that you’ve kept your uncle’s sandals.”

“How the hell have we not smelt that ‘til now?” Sokka exclaims plugging his nose.

“I’ve kept it deep in my pack, and far away from you.”

June’s Shirshu takes them to the outskirts of Ba Sing Se where a camp is filled with people in white and blues with the occasional red interspersed. The group look around with awe at the sheer number of people in uniform.

“What is this?” Suki asks.

Ying smiles and leads them towards the center where the large tents lie. “This is the base of the White Lotus. I’m taking you to the grandmasters.”

Zuko didn’t know who to expect when they saw the grandmaster, but she never expected to see Piandao among them. Her old master hugs her tightly. “I’m glad to see you safe. Your uncle has been waiting for you.”

A lump forms in her throat at the thought of facing Uncle again. “W-where is he?” she asks. Piandao takes her to his tent with Sokka following behind. When they reach the tent, Sokka puts a hand on her shoulder. “I’ll be right here.” He says reassuringly.

She nods and enters the tent. It’s lit with a dim orange light with Iroh sitting at the table, sipping on a cup of tea. Zuko takes a deep breath and bows low. Tears start running down her face.

“Uncle, I’m—I’m so so sorry for disappointing you and betraying your trust. I know you only wanted the best for me and I threw everything you taught me back in your face. There’s nothing I can do that will ever make you forgive me, but I just want to say how sorry I am an—”

She doesn’t get to finish because Iroh has pulled her close in a tight hug.

“I was never disappointed in you. I was just sad when you lost your way, but you found it again. And I am so proud of you.” He whispers.

Zuko lets herself cry into his shoulder and relish the feeling of being back in her uncle’s arms. When they exit, Sokka is still waiting expectantly for them. Iroh goes to him with open arms, “Sokka, it’s good to see you.”

Sokka grins and hugs the old man. “You too. I’m glad you’re safe. Now, what’s your plan? How many people do we have?”

Iroh chuckles and ushers them to the center tent. “I see you’re already thinking ahead, let’s go meet the others.”

Before they get to the tent, a familiar hunched figure comes by. “Iroh are these new recruits?” Master Shandi asks tersely. Zuko mouth is agape when she sees her old master dragging Katara, Toph and Suki behind her.

“M-master Shandi?” Zuko says.

“Oh it’s you Lin. Good you can help with some of my embroidery.” Shandi replies.

Iroh laughs and interrupts before the old seamstress can rope Zuko into the sewing. “Ah dear Master Shandi. They are not part of the group.”

“Hmph better not be. They’re a little small to be fighting.” She says.

Iroh shifts uncomfortably at that statement and Shandi gives him a dirty look. “Iroh, if you’re sending these young’uns to the battlefield, I’ll sew your balls up your ass.”

“Ahaha well you see…”

Zuko interrupts before she actually made good on her promise. “Master Shandi, it’s our duty to fight. We chose this path and we’re prepared to see it through.”

She narrows her eyes, but sighs and nods. “Very well. I’ll be in my tent if you need me.” and she leaves soon after that.

Zuko turns to her uncle. “What the *fuck,* Uncle?”

“Ah, you didn’t know? Master Shandi and her students are usually added as spy masters for the White Lotus.” Iroh replies. “Seamstresses hear everything and can go to high places with little suspicion. Master Hina is the one who brings back information to us when she goes around the city fitting the fire nation generals for clothes.”

The princess has to shake her head in disbelief. “How does she get the information to you?” Iroh grins and reaches inside his pocket for an embroidered handkerchief. “We find ways.” He says.

&#95;&#42;&#95;&#42;&#95;

“The White Lotus forces will free Ba Sing Se and the Earth generals. Lieutenant Jee and the rest of Zuko’s old crew are within in the Fire Nation armies and navy, and they’ve rallied a significant number of the Fire Nation forces and will mutiny as soon as they get the signal. But the most important part is the airships. They’ll be taking off from a small island on the western side of the kingdom and making their way through to burn down everything down from there.” Iroh explains on the map moving the pieces around.

“We can take care of those airships.” Sokka says. “If Ozai’s going to be there, we’ll be there to support Aang and thin out the numbers.”

“And what about the Avatar?” Jeong Jeong asks.

“He’ll be there.” Katara assures. “He just needs time to mediate on his decisions.”

“What decision? He needs to *kill* the Fire Lord.” Jeong Jeong growls.

“Jeong Jeong, I hope you understand what you’re asking a twelve-year-old child to do.” Zuko glares.

At least the ex-soldier has the decency to look a little abashed. “Still, it’s the only way to have peace.” He mumbles.

“And we will, but whatever decision Aang makes, I will support him.” Zuko says. “We cannot ask him to destroy his soul for the sake of peace.”

“And if the Avatar decides to spare him, how will we imprison Ozai? He’s a dangerous man with connections.” Pakku says.

“We’ll deal with it.” Zuko replies. “We’ve made prisons especially for fire benders before.”

“And then what about Princess Azula?” Pakku asks.

“I’ll deal with her.” Zuko replies. “I’ll take Katara with me to the palace and challenge her to an Agni Kai.”

“And she’ll accept?” Piandao asks.

“I think so.” Zuko replies. “From the news at the Caldera it sounds like she’s been…not doing well. I think if I go to her now, she’ll accept.”

“The palace should be clear at that time.” Iroh adds. “The only ones that might be potentially around would be the Fire Sages. It’ll be the perfect opportunity for Zuko to challenge her.”

“And when I win, I’ll make sure that Uncle gets the title of Fire Lord.”

The masters go still when they hear this, and they all look pointedly at Iroh who is starting to sweat.

“Ah my darling niece…you see…” He rubs his beard nervously. “I think it’s best if *you* take the position of Fire Lord.”

“…excuse me?” she says incredulously. “I can’t be Fire Lord! I’d mess up within a *day*! Who would support my rule?”

“Most of the noble families actually.” Piandao pipes up.

“The soldiers.” Jeong Jeong adds.

“And most importantly, the citizens.” Iroh says with a proud smile. “You’ve already done so much for them, and it’s time the Fire Nation has new blood ruling. I’ve done so much in my past that will make it harder for people to accept a new reign of peace.”

Zuko bites her lip, “What if I make a mistake?”

Iroh smiles. “Then I have no doubt that you know how to fix it, and I’ll be with you.”

She takes a breath and takes his weathered hand in hers. “Okay. I’ll take the throne, Uncle.”

Sokka grins and throws his arms around her. “I know you’ll be a great ruler, and we’ll support you too.”

“Thanks, Sokka.”

After a small moment they go back to planning. It takes a better part of the day, and by the end Zuko is exhausted. She doesn’t know how her father and grandfathers sustained this kind of thing for a hundred years. The tent is a welcomed peaceful sanctuary for her as she lays down to rest and gather herself for the big day. Even now she can feel the fire of the comet coming close, and boiling her blood.

The tents flap opens and Zuko is about to hurl a fireball at whoever is disturbing her, but she hears the familiar voice of Sokka chiming through.

“Hey, can I come in?” he asks.

“Always.” Zuko replies beckoning him inside. Sokka comes in and slips in beside her on the bed.

“You’re really warm.” He notices.

“I think it’s the comet.” She muses. “I’ve been feeling it coming closer for the past week.”

“How does it feel?”

Zuko pauses and puts his hands over her heart. Sokka feels a pounding heat beneath his hands, like a literal fire in her soul. “It feels like I’m slowly being charged by the force of a thousand suns. So overwhelming and terrifying at the same time.”

“Damn that’s hot.”

“Sokka!”

“Sorry can’t help myself.” He says nuzzling into the nook of her neck. “Just one more day.”

“Just one more day.”

&#95;&#42;&#95;&#42;&#95;

The morning of the battle draws near, and the camp has lulled to a brooding silence. Many soldiers look grim as they prepare for the coming battle. Zuko and Katara are getting Appa ready to fly them to the royal palace while Piandao fits an eel-hound for Sokka and his crew.

Iroh pulls Zuko in for a warm hug. “I believe in you, Zuko.” He says.

“Thank you, Uncle. May Agni bring you fortune in this coming battle.” She whispers. When they release, Zuko and Katara head towards Sokka, Toph, and Suki. They all share hugs and whispers of good luck, and Sokka pulls the surprised princess in for a deep kiss.

Zuko grips his shoulders and pours all her love and faith in the amazing man, and it feels like Sokka is doing the same as he grips her waist tightly.

Someone clears their throat and the two reluctantly part, Sokka just grinning boyishly at his stunned sister and friend. It disappears as soon as he spots Iroh and Piandao with arms crossed and eyebrows raised.

Fortunately, the princess interrupts before the other two men could descend on Sokka.

“We should get going.” She says taking Katara’s arm. “I’m sure you guys want to be on the island on time.”

Iroh and Piandao glance at each other. “Very well. Make sure you two survive so we can give you a stern talking to.” Iroh replies.

Sokka audible gulps and Toph is snickering in the back. They share one last good-bye before they split up.

The older men sigh to each other. “I knew it was coming, but it still shocked this old heart of mine.” Iroh says.

“After we take back Ba Sing Se, let’s get a drink with that Jee fellow. He seems like the type to understand this level of exasperation.” Piandao offers.

“Agreed. I wonder if Lu Ten is pulling his hair out right now?” Iroh muses.

Piandao smirks, “I’m sure he’s probably been doing that for a better part of the year.”

(He’s right. If Lu Ten had any physical hair, he would have gone bald by now. Seeing Lu Ten’s constant consternations are the highlight of Yue’s day.)

&#95;&#42;&#95;&#42;&#95;

As the eel-hound flies over the land and rivers, taking the trio towards the base of the airships, Suki nearly pushes Sokka off it.

“I can’t believe you just kissed Zuko like that in front of her Uncle and master.” She exclaims.

“Hey, I want to make sure I get one last kiss before we face an entire fleet of soldiers.” He retorts. “You’re just mad we never told you.”

“I am, but I’m also pretty impressed that you guys kept it quiet from us.”

“Not from me.” Toph declares. “But that’s because I heard Zuko walk to your room every night.”

Suki gives Sokka a friendly and knowing nudge. “OooOooh Sokka, engaging in premarital-sex. How scandalous.”

“Hey, it was consensual and great! That’s all that matters.” Sokka retorts.

The two girls snicker, and Suki raises her hand. “I call Best Man!”

“Nice! I already called Zuko’s Best Girl so now we can plan a kick ass party.”

“Ooh I like that.” Suki grins. “But what about Katara and Aang?”

“Katara can be the Maid of Honor and Aang can be the Flower Boy.”

Suki taps her chin, “I thought Momo should be the Flower Boy.”

“Momo can bring the sake.”

“That’s probably a better idea.”

Sokka cuts their argument with an exasperated groan, “I haven’t even proposed!”

The two girls grin evilly, “But you’re already thinking of marrying her.” Toph grins.

“How romantic.” Suki teases.

The boy just sighs and lets the girls have their fun. There’s no use in arguing with Toph and Suki when they get like that.

&#95;&#42;&#95;&#42;&#95;

Katara crosses her arms. “I can’t believe you and my brother have been a thing since the Western Air Temple.”

Zuko smiles sheepishly. “Sorry, we just wanted to keep it to ourselves for a bit.”

“Oh I understand that part.” Katara replies. “I just don’t get how we didn’t see it until now.”

“To be fair we were trying to be extra discrete.”

“Fine, but I call Maid of Honor at your wedding.” Katara grins.

The fire bender goes red. “Katara! He hasn’t even proposed.”

“Oh but you were *thinking* about it.”

“Shut up. You’ll have to work with Toph though. She called Best Girl when she found out.”

“Damnit. Wait what’s Best Girl?”

“I have no idea, but it’s the position Toph wanted and I can’t say no to her.”

&#95;&#42;&#95;&#42;&#95;

Katara and Zuko reach the steps of the empty palace. The whole city is abandoned, no doubt most of the citizens have been evacuated elsewhere while the Fire Lord unleashes the power of the comet on the world. Zuko hopes that her people are alright.

In the courtyard, Zuko sees Azula about to be crowned and calls for them to stop. The sages almost seem to sigh in relief when the see Princess Zuko step onto the courtyard.

It breaks her heart to see her sister like this. Face smeared with half-haphazard make-up, bags under her eyes and bangs cut lopsidedly. Even with the comet flying overhead, Azula’s inner flame seems to flicker unsteadily.

“Azula.” Zuko calls out. “Please step down. You need help.”

Her younger sister sneers. “Is that so, Zuzu? You think I’m as pathetic as you? That Father just left me here because he doesn’t think I can take the throne?”

“Father didn’t want to share his glory with you. That’s why he left you here.” Zuko answers. “We never meant anything to him other than a means to make him look more powerful.”

“NO!” she screeches. “Father is the only one who ever loved *me*. He wants me to protect our nation!”

“He never cared about our nation.” Zuko hisses. “He only ever cared about his ego and power. Please Azula. I’m sorry I left you behind. I’m sorry I never took you away from Father sooner. I love you.”

Azula trembles at the top of the steps. She shakes her head and sneers at her older sister. “No. NO you’re *LYING!* YOU ALWAYS LIE! YOU NEVER LOVED ME! Just like Mom never loved me.”

“No, she loved you so much—”

“STOP LYING!” she yells letting a wave of fire spread towards Zuko. The elder princess dissipates it with ease. She takes a breath and stands tall before the sages and Agni.

“Princess Azula. I challenge you to an Agni Kai. Whoever wins gets to be crowned Fire Lord.”

Azula sneers. “Very well *sister*. I think it’s fitting that I’ll burn you just as badly as Father did.” She immediately leaps off the steps and sends a barrage of enormous flames at Zuko.

Zuko doesn’t move much, she cleaves the flames around her and sends her own massive fire at Azula. While Azula strikes and rushes about the courtyard, Zuko stays firm, like the earth. Only moving with the slightest motions to strike.

Katara marvels at the way Zuko moves. She’s fluid like water, free like air, steady like the earth and just as passionate and strong as fire. Azula is nothing but an uncontrolled flame, against Zuko’s is precise and intentional strikes.

As the minutes tick on, Azula gets more and more enraged and frustrated. She’s supposed to be the perfect one. She’s supposed to be the stronger one. So why?

*WhywhywhyWHY*?

Why can’t she land a single hit? Was her father right? Did he see weakness in her? She lost everyone around her, Mai, Ty Lee, Zuko, her mother. Father was the only one who ever stayed and even now he left her.

It’s not fair. She worked so hard. She’s far superior than everyone, so why does everyone leave? Why does she even care? Why does it hurt so much?

Azula glares at her sister. Zuko left her because of the Avatar. Because of that stupid water tribe peasant.

IT’S ALL THEIR FAULT!!!

The lightening crackles on her fingertips as she shoots a bolt of lightening at the peasant.

Zuko’s eyes widen and she lunges towards the bolt, taking it into her chest and falling onto the ground unmoving.

Azula can’t tell if she’s screaming or if it’s the peasants, but her mind goes numb. Some part of her is angry that she’s forfeited the Agni Kai, but the majority of her is just a white haze. Staring at the prone form of her sister on the ground. Still. Like death.

*I killed her. I killed Zuzu. I killed her. IkilledherIkilledherIkilledher.*

No.

No of course not. She didn’t kill her.

It was the water tribe *peasant*. Because the water peasant was there, Azula had to shoot the bolt of lightening. Zuko had to protect that weakling because it’s *Zuko*, and she always had a soft spot for the weak. That water peasant is the reason why Zuko died. The same one who’s putting her hands on her sister’s body.

No.

*No I won’t allow it!*

Azula screeches and shoots fire from her fingertips. The peasant moves out of the way and brings up water from the grates. She’s fast, but Azula is just as fast. The water evaporates almost instantaneously with a touch of her flames. But the peasant keeps going. The filthy thing runs behind pillars, hoping to hide from Azula’s wrath. She burns through the palace, driving the pest out towards her. She smiles manically when she sees the peasant come at her. Her fingers start to twitch and arc with lightening.

*This is for Zuzu.*

But then it’s cold and she freezes. Literally. Everything is still. Her fire straining to get out, but nothing seems to work. She can’t get the flames to explode. Then the peasant breathes and *moves*.

*No. No this isn’t happening. This isn’t happening!*

Azula can feel the chains wrap around her arms, and her body struggles to fight against it. She needs to get out. She needs to win! How could she let this peasant take away her victory?! She’s Azula, daughter of Ozai. Heir to the throne! She should be PERFECT!

The water releases and suddenly she falls back against the chains. She struggles and screams. Breathing fire from her mouth. Wailing and crying against the sky at the injustice. The travesty of having one of Sozin’s line chained like an *animal*. Tears stream from her face as her mind is torn asunder with denial.

The peasant runs towards Zuko, and Azula *screams*. Why is she touching Zuko? She needs to be left alone. The murderer doesn’t deserve to touch her sister’s dead body!

“Don’t touch her *filth! Don’t touch my sister! Don’t touch ZUZU*” she screeches. But it’s unheeded. Azula continues to wail and scream until her voice is hoarse and no more tears come out.

&#95;&#42;&#95;&#42;&#95;

Katara blocks out the piercing sounds of Azula’s screams as she moves water through the wound. Already the Fire Sages and a few royal guards are hovering in the sidelines, waiting for news of Princess Zuko.

It feels like an eternity, but Zuko’s heart beats steadily under her hands and the princess coughs as she takes a breath.

“Katara?” she whispers hoarsely.

The water bender chokes on a sob as she lays her head on Zuko’s chest and thanks Yue and all the spirts. “Zuko, you’re okay.”

“I’m sorry, I didn’t mean to worry you.”

“I know. But please *please* try not to do it again.”

“I won’t.”

Katara helps her up and the Fire Sages come close. They bow low.

“Princess Zuko.” They say solemnly. “Because of Princess Azula’s forfeiture, you are the winner of the Agni Kai and Fire Lord of our nation.”

Zuko nods. “Good. Get the hawks ready. We have a lot of cease fires to send out.”

They nod and then turn nervously to Azula who’s still screaming hoarsely on the pillars. “And… Princess Azula?”

Zuko looks sadly at her broken sister and turns to Katara. “Help me to her?”

Katara hesitates. “Is that a good idea? She’s not exactly…stable.”

“I know, but I can’t leave her here.”

They walk slowly to Azula, and Zuko keeps her eyes on her sister.

“Azula.” Zuko says softly.

Her sister stops and stares at Zuko bug-eyed. A wide grin overtakes her face and she laughs hysterically. Her head thrown back in the air.

“Is this your punishment?!” Azula cries to the sky as she laughs. “Because I’m a monster? First mother and now Zuko? Well you have it now! I’m a loser now! I’m weak and pathetic!”

Zuko nearly wretches her face away from her sister who’s in the middle of a breakdown, but she keeps her eyes on her.

“Azula.” Zuko says softly. “Please stop.”

She laughs maniacally in response and doesn’t stop. Even as the guards drag her away to the Caldera prison.

“I don’t want to keep her there long.” Zuko tells the head guard.

He bows lowly, “We’ll keep her in a comfortable cell, but we really shouldn’t let her roam freely.”

“I know.” Zuko whispers. “Just…make sure she’s treated right.”

“We will.”

“And please send a letter to the Pikiran Institution on Shingri Island. I want to make sure that she’s treated well.”

The Fire Sages and guards look uncomfortable. “Wouldn’t it be better to just keep her in the cell? The institution isn’t the safest place to keep her.”

Zuko narrows her eyes and stands tall, “My sister is only fourteen. She’s been hurt just as much as I have and deserves a chance for rehabilitation. I know she can get better. Now please. Send that letter.”

They bow and scurry off, while Katara looks appraisingly at her.

“That was…I don’t know if I could have done the same thing you did.” Katara admits.

Zuko smiles sadly, “She’s still my baby sister, Katara. I can never leave her alone like that.”

“This is why you’ll always be a better person than your father.” Katara compliments. “Now let’s get you to a room. You’re not done healing yet.”

&#95;&#42;&#95;&#42;&#95;

When they reach the island, Toph easily gets them into one of the airships, and Sokka thanks every deity that Toph is on their side. Sozin’s Comet flies overhead with a great brilliant light, shedding a trail of bright fire through the sky.

Even with the stakes involved, Sokka can’t bring himself to feel his fear or doubts. He needs to have a clear mind when he stops the airship fleet. They infiltrate the airship and literally drop off the crew of the over the water. He’s already going to probably have to kill a lot of soldiers in the ensuing battle, but he wants to try and spare as many as he can.

“Alright, take the wheel Suki!” Sokka orders as he watches her steer the ship towards the others in line. He can see Ozai’s flagship in the distance, and he feels his soul burning with the need to slice his sword into his flimsy neck. They’re so far away though. They won’t make it in time.

Sokka watches in horror as Ozai unleashes the largest flame he’s ever seen. It burns his eyes as he watches the crazy lunatic burn the land to ash.

Then, out of the blue, an air slice cuts through to the airship and they cheer as they see Aang through the spyglass.

“It’s Aang!” Sokka cries happily. “He’s back. He’s going to fight Ozai!”

“Are we going to help him?” Suki asks.

“No, this is his fight. We need to focus on the airship fleet.” Sokka replies.

“And how are we going to do that? I’m blind in the air!” Toph retorts.

Sokka frowns and thinks before he’s hit with inspiration. “Airship slice!”

By some miracle no one seems to suspect them. Perhaps they think it’s part of the plan, but Sokka thanks the spirits that Fire Nation soldier’s aren’t the brightest bunch of people. The crash is terrific, and metal flies through the air as the airship collides and runs through the other in the fleet.

As the fire bursts through the ship and throws them off, he thinks of Zuko and the story of her ship blowing up. If he can’t survive an explosion, how could he proudly call himself her boyfriend?

It tears him to leave Suki behind, but they need to finish the mission. They need every airship to go down or else the whole thing is a bust. Just as it seems like they’re going to make it, a soldier comes up and blasts them with fire, causing Toph and Sokka to fall into one of the platforms. His leg throbs as he lands. The heat is intense and his hands are sweaty as he grips Toph’s hand tightly into his. In a last ditch effort he throws boomerang to stop two soldiers from firing at them, but then more come up. Ready to take aim.

He grits his teeth and apologizes to Zuko. It looks like he won’t be making it.

“I’m sorry, Toph.” He croaks. “It looks like the end.”

Tears gather in her eyes and he feels a stab of regret for bringing such a young child here.

Only twelve and already her life is going to be cut short because of this damn war.

In his last moments, he swears he sees Yue and another man who looks like Iroh smiling encouragingly at him and pointing to the horizon.

*What the hell?*

The soldiers suddenly run, and Sokka gets the chance to pull Toph up. He turns and grins, “It’s Suki!” he exclaims. The warrior saves them both and takes them into the airship. They did it. They took down the fleet. He can’t believe the survived.

In the distance, they see the massive battle between Ozai and Aang.

The young Avatar is glowing with power and energy as he harnesses all the elements at once. Then Aang has the Fire Lord trapped in stone and then he releases a great glowing light into the air. Sokka is nearly blinded by the intensity and has to look away, but the light fades and they continue to head towards him, Suki and Toph supporting Sokka.

The reach the top of the peak just as Aang calls for the water to stop the fires. Sokka relishes in the feeling of victory and gloats over the slumped Fire Lord.

“Looks like you lose Father Lord!” Sokka crows.

Ozai glares blearily at the peasant. “I’m not the Father Lord, I’m the all-mighty Phoenix Kin— URG!”

The fallen king is knocked out by the blow of Sokka punch to his face. The warrior smirks above him, “Don’t care, *NOzai*.”

“OoooOOooohhh.” Suki and Toph intones. Even Aang has to crack a smile at that.

“C’mon guys let’s get him tied up and head to Caldera City. Zuko and Katara should be waiting for us there.” Aang says.

They all nod and Sokka puts a hand on the young Avatar’s shoulder.

“I know it wasn’t easy for you, but I’m proud you found your own way.”

Aang smiles at him, “Thanks Sokka. I didn’t really know it then, but Zuko was the one who opened the door for me. I wouldn’t have gotten it as easily without her showing me the path.”

The water tribesman grins, “That’s my girlfriend for you.”

“Yea—*WHAT*? Since when?” Aang exclaims.

“Since the Western Air Temple.”

Aang groans while Suki and Toph laugh.

“And when did *they* find out?” he points accusingly at the two girls.

“Toph found out before Suki, who found out earlier today when I kissed Zuko in front of everyone.”

“Monkey feathers.” Aang huffs. “I always miss the fun stuff when I go on my spirit quests.”

“Yeah too bad, Twinkletoes.”

“Okay, but I call Best Man.” Aang demands.

Suki grins. “Sorry that’s taken by me.”

“What about Maid of Honor?” Aang tries.

“Taken by Katara probably. And I’m the Best Girl.” Toph answers.

“The what’s left?!” Aang cries.

Toph grins widely, “You can be the Flower Boy.”

Aang pouts. “Fine. But I’m going to overflow the entire place with flowers.”

“Good enough for me.”

“Is nobody going to ask me what I want?” Sokka asks.

They all answer with a resounding, “Nope.”

He sighs and turns away, but then a glint catches his eye.

*There’s no way. It’s not possible*…

Sokka limps towards the glinting metal as Aang and the others look strangely at him.

Buried in the stone is his space sword and boomerang.

“What the fuck?” Sokka whispers. “How the hell?”

Suddenly he’s face to face with a blue translucent figure. The same man who was standing beside Yue.

The man smirks. “You can’t be her husband without a space sword and boomerang.”

Sokka blinks and then the figure’s gone. The others finally catch up to him and Sokka turns incredulously at them.

“Did you guys see that?” he asks.

The others look at him strangely. “I can’t see dum-dum.” Toph retorts.

“Well did you *feel* that?”

They shake their heads. “Nope. Why? What did you see?” Aang asks.

“A-a young guy? Looks like a taller younger Iroh?”

Again he’s met with blank stares.

“Dunno, Sokka. Maybe you’re just seeing things after the rush of battle.” Suki says.

“Or maybe you saw a spirit!” Aang says. “He’s probably the one who left you the sword and boomerang there.”

“Huh imagine that.” Sokka says as he pulls out the sword and boomerang. Still, there was something eerily familiar about him.

As the group heads down to the airship and prepare to head towards the palace, the comet leaves above them. Sparking the first flames of change that will sweep through the world.

&#95;&#42;&#95;&#42;&#95;

Sokka didn’t know what to expect when they got to the palace. The sheer destruction and trails of scorch marks were pretty much on par with his vision, but a haggard-looking Zuko leaning against Katara’s shoulder with a blazing scorch mark on her stomach was not in the original picture.

He would have face-planted on the ground if it weren’t for Toph’s quick reflexes. He grabs her tightly and looks for any more signs of injury, but Zuko only leans forward and brings him in for a kiss. Sokka melts into it and cups her cheek carefully, but before they can deepen it, Sokka feels them tilting back and, again, Toph’s reflexes and bending were the only things that stopped them from sprawling on the ground.

Katara groans and smacks both of them in the head with a scolding look. “I can’t believe you two.” She says. “You’re both injured! This is not the time to be kissing.”

“This is *exactly* the time to be kissing!” Sokka retorts. “Can’t I show how happy I am to see my girlfriend who looks like she’s been struck by lightening?!”

“I was struck by lightening.” Zuko says. “Azula aimed it at Katara and I went in-between it.”

Everyone looked at her with wide eyes and Sokka just cups her face again. “Babe. You’re fucking unbelievable.” He says before leaning in for a quick kiss.

This time Toph’s the one to smack him in the head with a pebble. “I’m not saving you idiots from face-planting again. I’m *tired* damnit.”

They all laugh and bask in the moment of their reunion and victory.

“So what now?” Suki asks.

“Now comes the hard part. Bringing peace.” Zuko answers.

“I thought this was supposed to be the hard part.” Toph mutters.
