Zuko laughs and teases her with a, “Nope. We’re just getting started.”

“Ugh! I should’ve never sided with you guys if I knew peace was so much work!”

### End Notes

#### Chapter End Notes

**Yue:** Aw you saved his spacesword and boomerang. You *do* like him.

**Lu Ten:** Whatever...he's not that bad. Especially after that airship slice move.

&#45;-

Thanks for reading guys! Just one more chapter to go!

## Chapter 18

### Notes

#### Chapter Notes

**Warning!** Implied Self-Harm

### Text

---

Peace was a lot of work, but they manage. Zuko’s soldiers sent out cease fires everywhere and started the recall of Fire Nation troops. The princess makes sure to release all the people wrongfully imprisoned and began the peace talks even before Katara gave her the okay, much to the healer’s consternations. Iroh happily informed her that Ba Sing Se has been freed and that the Earth King has been found.

Zuko imprisons her father in the Caldera prison and also keeps Azula there until the officials from the mental institution can get her. At least her sister looked far calmer when Zuko came to see her.

Her hair was a tangled mess and face streaked with tears and snot. Her wrists show trails of red marks, some scabbing, some still oozing blood. Azula looks up tiredly when Zuko came to see her.

“Zuzu?” she says quietly.

Zuko gives her a small smile and sits down carefully. “Azula.”

“You’re supposed to be dead.”

“It’ll take a lot more than your lightening to kill me.”

Azula grins too widely. “So it would seem. I should have known a roach like you would have survived it.”

“I know.”

They sit quietly together. Azula looking at her sister as if she were a ghost and Zuko trying not to cry at the state of her little sister.

“Your letter was terrible.” She says. “I can’t believe how often you said ‘sorry’.”

“I thought it was an appropriate thing to do. Especially since I was leaving you with Father.”

Azula hums thoughtfully and they sit in silence for a while.

“I couldn’t burn your tree, you know.” Azula says.

“I heard. It looks…interesting.” Zuko comments. It was one of the first things she looked at when she went back to the palace. The left side of the tree was blackened and scorched but the right stayed pristine. “Like a strange reflection of my face.”

Azula laughs, “Yes I suppose so. Maybe that’s why I couldn’t do it.”

Another pause.

“So…you’ll be Fire Lord now.” Azula states.

“Yes. I’ll be crowned tomorrow.”

“Shame. You’ll bring the Fire Nation down with your *peace*.” She spits.

“Perhaps, but I think everyone’s too tired and been too hurt by the war to care.” Zuko replies giving a meaningful look to Azula.

The young girl only scoffs. “That means everyone in this nation is *weak*.”

The *including me* is left unsaid, but it hovers in the air. Zuko sighs and holds out her hand. “Give me your arm.” She says.

Azula glares. “Why? I don’t need your pity.”

“I know, but I’m a weakling remember? I don’t like seeing my sister hurt like this.”

“…I could burn your face you know.”

“Just make sure to make it even.”

Azula barks a sharp laugh at that and holds out her arm through the bars. The guards stiffen and tense. Ready to pull Zuko back if need be but Azula doesn’t even make smoke.

Zuko works quietly and patiently as she cleans the marks and wraps them up in bandages. She does the same to the other hand. When she finishes, Azula pulls her arm back and stares at the white, clean bandages. Zuko looks at her meaningfully.

“Please don’t hurt yourself anymore.” Zuko asks.

Azula grimaces and turns her head away. “Doesn’t matter.”

“It matters to me.”

Azula’s hand tightens against the bandages, but she doesn’t start to scratch or hurt herself. She just…presses her fingers against them.

“What will you do to me?” Azula asks. “Am I to be killed?”

“Don’t be so dramatic.” Zuko sighs. “You’re going to an institution.”

“An *institution*?” she asks incredulously.

“Yes. You need help Azula, but I don’t have the expertise here.” Zuko says. “You’ll be taken to an institution in one of the remote islands on the south side of the Fire Nation. I can visit once in a while and I hope that once you get better…you can come back and live here.”

Azula presses her lips together. A part of her is snarling and waging war inside her. How dare her sister send her away like an errant pup? Does she think Azula will give up?

But the bigger part is tired of everything. And just wants it to end.

“Fine.” She says hoarsely. “It might be better than death.”

Zuko sighs sadly and puts her hands on the bars. “Azula, of course it will be. Please just give it a chance. Father hurt us both deeply, and I want to help you heal.”

“And you?” Azula growls. “Did the Avatar magically take away your mental anguish?”

Zuko laughs. “No. Not even a little. I’m…I’ll be talking to a spiritual healer too. We both need it.”

Azula stares contemplatively at the wall. “Very well, sister.”

Zuko nods and stands, “I’ll be back tomorrow.”

“Don’t. You have a country to run now.”

“But I’ll still make time for my sister. Because I love you, Azula.” Zuko leaves soon after, pretending not to see the tremble in Azula’s lips and the glistening golden eyes.

&#95;&#42;&#95;&#42;&#95;

At her coronation, Jee and Teruko stand guard at her door. The rest of the crew patrolling the halls of the palace to ensure that it goes as smoothly possible. She’s trying to put on the robe when Sokka comes in, eyes bright when he spots her. He gives her a quick kiss and hug before brusher her hair out of her eyes.

“Need some help there?” he asks.

Zuko grimaces and hands him the robes. “Yes. And how did you get past Jee?”

“Teruko held him down.”

“I love that woman.”

“Me too. Guess you’ll know who you’ll station at your door at night.” He winks.

Zuko blushes and pushes him stiffly. “Don’t push your luck, Sokka. Jee is still a highly trained soldier and I know Piandao is still lurking around here somewhere.”

“Fine, then you come to my room.” Sokka shrugs.

“I’m still injured. You be a gentleman and come to my room.”

Sokka laughs and helps her pull up the robes, carefully tying them around her waist before putting on the shoulder spikes. “Okay, but I hope you know that I’ll be risking life and limb to get to you.”

“How romantic.” She replies dryly.

They share another kiss that’s soon interrupted by a knock on the door. Jee pops his head in to glare at Sokka. “The princess will be *late*.” He enunciates. “Don’t make me throw you out of here, *boy*.”

They see a hand pull him back and Teruko’s face appears. “Don’t listen to him too much, but he’s right. Kiss and do your hanky panky later. You’ll be late, Princess.”

Zuko clears her throat and looks up at him, “Escort me to the entrance, Lord Sokka?”

He puts out his arm, “My pleasure, Princess.”

Sokka takes great satisfaction in watching Jee’s face morph into blatant discomfort at the sight of the two while Teruko just smiles happily behind them.

The warrior peels away once she’s at the door to join with the crowd. Zuko gives him one last kiss, which is echoed by an “Oogie!” from Aang, before she lets him leave.

She turns to her friend who’s jokingly sticking his tongue out in disgust. “I know you’ll do worse with Katara.”

Aang immediately turns red and brings his tongue back. “Zuko!”

She laughs and leans down to hug him. “Just saying, Aang. Who knew I’d be making jokes with you a year later?”

The Avatar grins and hugs her back. “Things have changed.”

“And they’ll keep changing, for the better.”

The smile and head out, heads held high as the gong rings and brings about a new age.

&#95;&#42;&#95;&#42;&#95;

Zuko’s first stop on her Earth Kingdom reparations field trip is Song’s house.

The medic and her mother are understandably shell-shocked when the find Zuko, dressed in full Fire Lord regalia and bowing deeply to the both of them.

“I’m sorry that my uncle and I deceived you.” She says. “But I can’t express my gratitude for taking us in in our time of need.”

Song and her mother look to each other and then to the Zuko. Finally, Song speaks. “Well…I can understand why you lied. I can’t say we would have been quite as hospitable if we knew.”

Zuko winces. “Yes…I’m sorry. For everything my nation has done. I want to make it right, and you’re the first ones on my list.” She motions to the soldiers who come up with an ostrich-horse and a chest full of gold. Song and her mother’s eyes go wide at the amount of gleaming gold in the chest. Then Zuko pulls out a seal and hands it to them.

“I don’t think there’s anything I can give you that will properly pay back the kindness you’ve shown us, but I hope that this will help, and I will personally make sure that my soldiers will not hurt anymore innocent civilians.” Zuko says seriously.

Song looks to Zuko and then smiles before flinging her arms around the girl. The royal guards and Song’s mother stiffen, but Zuko smiles and returns the hug.

Song whispers, “It looks like your wounds have healed very well, Fire Lord Zuko.”

“I couldn’t have done it with the people I met along the way.”

They pull apart and Song grins brightly. “If you’re ever back in town. Come see us and bring Uncle Mushi too.”

“We will.”

&#95;&#42;&#95;&#42;&#95;

The next place is Master Shandi, who just looks at her and nods. “Hmm much better look for you, girl.”

Zuko grins and bows, “Yes. Thank you for your seal. I couldn’t have gotten a position at Master Hina’s and King Kuei without you.”

Master Shandi waves her hand. “Bah of course you would have gotten a position with Hina. She’s not a fool, and Kuei may be an idiot, but he’s an idiot who knows good clothes when he sees it.”

“I wanted to give you something as thanks for giving me a chance.” Zuko says.

The seamstress looks petulant. “I don’t need anything, Lin. I have everything I need here.”

“I know but think of it as a gift from a student to her master.” Zuko says before motioning for the guards to bring the box. When she reveals a purple robe, Master Shandi’s brow rise up.

There’s the black and white koi fish in the back with a dragon intertwining around them towards the bottom. White and gold thread form the boomerang and twin swords at the top.

Master Shandi takes the robe and inspects it with careful eyes. She smiles and nods approvingly.

“Bold of you to bring the two styles of water and fire together like this.” she says. “But I like it. I look forward to seeing the fruits of your reign, Fire Lord Lin.”

“It’s Zuko.”

“Lin is who you introduced yourself as and Lin you shall remain.”

&#95;&#42;&#95;&#42;&#95;

Lee and his family are the last one she visits before going to Ba Sing Se. They’re unsurprisingly shocked, and Lee looks a little betrayed. But their eyes soon fill with joy when they see that their eldest son walking beside the Fire Lord.

The younger boy leaps at his older brother who laughs joyfully and hugs his sibling tightly. Kai and Nami soon join and tears of happiness stream from their eyes.

Kai looks to Zuko who is standing in the back and watching the family reunion. He peels himself away and walks to her before bowing.

“Thank you for bringing my son back.”

Zuko blushes, “It’s the least I can do. You showed your hospitality to us and I wanted to make sure that your eldest son returned home to you as soon as possible. I’ve made sure any prisoner of war is nursed to health before sending them home.”

Kai smiles warmly. “It’s appreciated…ah—”

“Zuko. Just…Zuko.”

He nods and then Lee comes barreling towards her. He wraps his arms around her waist and buries his face in her stomach.

“Thank you!” he exclaims. “You brought my brother back. I thought fire benders were all terrible people, but you brought my brother home.”

She pats his head awkwardly. “Um…no problem it’s the least I can do.”

He looks up with bright eyes. “When I grow up, I’m going to be someone who’ll protect his people.”

Zuko smiles, “Okay. But you better get better with your swordsmanship first.”

“I will.”

Nami is next to come up and hug the girl. “Thank you. For everything.”

Zuko swallows the lump in her throat. “It’s the least I can do.”

Before she leaves, the guards hand them another chest of gold, and the family nearly gives it back to her before Zuko insists that it’s a gift for them. The family takes it reluctantly, and wave good-bye as Zuko leaves for Ba Sing Se.

&#95;&#42;&#95;&#42;&#95;

Ba Sing Se is a mess of metal and scorched earth, but the people cheer and laugh as they walk through the streets of their liberated city. She came there to deliver the good new to her uncle personally and tell him the fates of Ozai and Azula. She also came to have the first peace talks with King Kuei.

But before the peace talks, she wanted to have a minute to apologize with the Earth King. Zuko shifts nervously and bows low.

“I’m sorry for deceiving you. I hope you understand that I never worked with my sister or shared any information to infiltrate the palace. I didn’t even have any idea that she would be here.”

Kuei holds his hand up, “I know. Sokka told me, and I understand why you would want to keep your royal blood a secret.”

“I still want to apologize for deceiving you.”

“And you are forgiven, my friend.” Kuei says serenely. “The war is over. You’ve ended it and are withdrawing your troops. And now you’re here to make peace between the nations. I know that you’re sorry, and I am happy to be working with an honorable Fire Lord who wants peace as much as we do.”

She smiles back at him, “I’m happy to be working with you too, King Kuei. Together, I hope we can work together to rebuild our country and bring peach and prosperity.”

He smiles, “Yes. Although I’m quite sad that I just lost a very good seamstress and embroiderer.”

They share a laugh and Zuko grins widely, “Well if the royalty thing doesn’t work out, I can come back and be your seamstress again.”

“I’ll hold you to that.” he replies.

&#95;&#42;&#95;&#42;&#95;

Zuko had assumed that Uncle would want to stay in Ba Sing Se, but he had a different plan. Despite her initial protests, Uncle told her that he would be with her for a half a year before going back to Ba Sing Se for the other half. He’ll spend his time in both nations every year to make sure that Zuko takes care of herself. He won’t take an active part in the council, but he’ll have his door open to her whenever she needs advice or just someone to talk to. When she asked why he wanted to stay, Uncle had grinned and said, “I would miss my daughter too much.”

Zuko didn’t protest anymore after that and just lets herself sink into his warmth.

As night falls, Zuko smiles as she leans over the railing of the balcony watching the fireworks and celebrations commence. Sokka comes out soon and sidles to her side.

“I can’t believe it’s over.” He starts. “I honestly never thought I’d see the day when the war ends and peacetime comes.”

“Me neither.” She replies. “I always thought the world would end up in a wash of red.”

“Good thing it didn’t huh?” he teases.

“Yeah. I probably wouldn’t have had you.” She replies.

“Probably not...” he trails off, looking seriously at the night sky. “…When I was holding onto Toph for dear life, I thought I saw Yue and another person…someone who looked like a younger Iroh.”

Zuko still. “But taller and had sideburns?”

“Yeah that’s the guy. I think he gave me back my spacesword and boomerang. He said I needed it if I was going to marry you.”

“…T-that sounds like Lu Ten, my cousin.”

Sokka grins, “So does that mean I get his stamp of approval?”

Zuko laughs, “Yeah I think so. He seems to like you.”

They share a smile and stand there in silence, watching the people celebrate below them.

“I wish I could stay with you, but I need to go to the Southern Water Tribe after this. I need to help rebuild things there first.”

“I know.” Zuko says sadly. “I’ve always known. You’re meant for great things.”

Sokka puts his hands over hers. “That doesn’t mean I won’t come back to you.”

“Sokka…” she says quietly. “I don’t want to hold you back, and my nation needs me. I can’t always travel to be with you.”

“I know.” he smiles. “It’s a good thing that Southern Chiefhood is voted in huh?”

Zuko snorts, “But you’d be a great chief.”

“I guess, but I’d rather be with you. I can be an ambassador.”

“Or an inventor, advisor, councilman, minister—”

Sokka cuts her off with a kiss. He pulls back and smirks. “Stop talking or I’ll get a big head, and you won’t be holding me back, you’ll be my beacon. My home.” He pauses and strokes he cheek, “I love you.”

Tears are welling up in her eyes and her lips tremble. “I love you too.” But there’s still a current of worry in her eyes. A deep-rooted idea that she couldn’t possibly have good things, and that the love of her life would leave her permanently.

Sokka seems to understand and read her discomfort and fears. He hooks his pinky to hers and looks deeply into her eyes.

“I promise, no matter what, I’ll always come back to you. I’ll stay by your side forever.” He states.

Zuko swallows. “Careful. That almost sounds like a proposal.”

Sokka just smiles. “Someday.”

The princess is too stunned to react when he leans over to kiss her. They melt into each other, twining their arms around the other as fireworks bloom in the night sky.

&#46;

&#46;

***.***

***Seven Years Later***

Sokka takes a deep breath and waits inside the room for the other three to enter. He’s going to do it. He’s going to face the three most intimidating people he’s ever known, and he can only pray that Yue is watching his back.

Honestly, sometimes it feels surreal to even be sitting here in the Royal Palace.

The first three years after the war were hard and laborious. Zuko practically worked herself to the ground before Sokka and Iroh finally intervened and forced her to take breaks and vacations. With the Earth King’s help, they established the United Republic of Nations, an independent nation that represents the new harmony between all people.

A year after the formation of the United Republic, Zuko found her mother again with help from Azula and the rest of the gang. They found Ursa hiding in a small village and married to a nice man, Noren, and with an adorable daughter, Kiyi.

Zuko took to doting on Kiyi like a turtleduck to water. It was so cute, and it made Sokka’s own heart burst with love.

The little girl had just taken one look at Zuko and threw herself on her declaring that she had “the prettiest, most amazingest big sister ever!”

Peace is difficult. There’s fights and disagreements from all sides. In the first year of her rule, it was rife with assassination plots and coups. Thankfully, all the crew of the *Dragon Claw* gain a position as part of her royal guard or royal household like Kursuri and Toshi. With the help of Sokka and Kiyoshi warriors, they manage to stop of the worst of the coups and assassination attempts. But it still makes him anxious thinking about it.

Still, change does come in increments. With the war nearly a decade behind them, it’s easier to forgive past hurts and look forward to the future.

Like peace, their relationship took some work. They had their fights, their tears, and epic screaming matches, but they learned and grew together. It was hard to be away from her for so long during the early years, but it worked out. Now that things are settled, he’s usually in the Fire Nation for most of the year unless an emergency comes up, and with the Fire Nation Air Fleet, it’s a lot easier and faster to travel long distances.

They had talked a lot about marriage in the first few years of their relationship, and they knew they wanted to wait before settling down. Not because they didn’t love each other, but because there was just so much *work* to be done. Now with everything settled, they had given the idea a seriously thought.

Which is why Sokka is here waiting in the room. The door suddenly slides open, and Sokka has to swallow the nervous lump in his throat. Azula, Iroh, Ursa, and Kiyi come in. They sit silently and look at Sokka with equally piercing golden eyes.

“Kiyi really wanted to be a part of this meeting.” Ursa says apologetically.

The young girl crosses her arms, “Of course! I want to know what big brother Sokka is going to ask!”

It releases the tension in the room and Sokka laughs, “I guess it wouldn’t be fair to leave you out of it.”

The warrior swallows audibly again and gathers his courage. He bows low to the people and says, “I’m going to purpose to Zuko, and I just wanted to express my intentions to her family members as per my tribe’s traditions.”

The four look at him then to each other. Ursa and Iroh smile widely while Azula just huffs and crosses her arms. Kiyi exclaims loudly and throws her arms in the air. “Can I be a bridesmaid?”

“You’ll have to ask Katara and Toph.” Sokka says. “They called it a long time ago.”

Kiyi frowns. “Hmmm okay, but I wanna be part of the wedding party!”

Sokka ruffles her hair, “We wouldn’t have it any other way.”

“Why are you asking us? We’re not the one you’re going to marry.” Azula scoffs.

“Now Azula.” Ursa replies softly. “He’s following the Southern Water Tribe’s Traditions, and I would be happy to give you my blessing, Sokka. You are a delightful young man and I know you’ll support and love my daughter well.”

“I just want to say: it’s about time.” Iroh chuckles. “I know you two wanted to wait a while before marriage, but you two took your sweet time.”

Sokka grins sheepishly. “Sorry, Uncle. I know you’ve wanted the wedding for a while, but well… Zuko and I just didn’t want to yet.”

“Understandable. You two are still so young.” He says wistfully. “I always forget that you were all barely teenagers during the war. But you have my blessings.”

“Ugh barf Uncle. Are we done here? I’m supposed to go painting with Ty Lee and Mai.” Azula says.

“Um…just wanted to ask for your blessings, Azula.” Sokka says.

She narrows her eyes and scoffs. “I *suppose* you could have my blessings.” She says. “Agni knows my sister won’t marry another, and I guess you’ve proven yourself.”

“Wow…that’s actually the nicest thing you’ve said to me Azula.” He says.

The younger girl glares balefully and tosses her hair. “Don’t get used to it Water Tribe. Just because you’re getting married into the family doesn’t mean you get to talk to me so familiarly.”

Sokka grins. “I’d never dream of it.”

“I hear that sass.” She mutters but gets up and flounces out of the room. Before she opens the door, she turns back and says, “Better do it soon. Or I’ll burn you alive.”

And she’s quickly out of the room, like smoke.

Kiyi looks to Sokka and pats his head, “She likes you.” She whispers conspiratorially. “She gave you some time before she actually burns you.”

Sokka snorts. It seems that sass runs in Roku’s side of the family.

Iroh sighs, “Ah that girl.”

Sokka laughs, “Yeah, but I think she only kinda meant it.”

“Do you have a necklace done?” Ursa asks.

“No, I’ll be going to the South Pole to tell my dad so he can plan for a trip here once the wedding planning starts.” Sokka says. “I’ll make the necklace while I’m in the South. They have the stones that I want to use. I’ll propose to her once I get back.”

Iroh nods and smiles to Ursa. “It’s strange how fate works doesn’t it?”

The lady laughs, “Indeed. You know I think it was actually around this time when Lu Ten said it.”

Sokka raises his brow. “When Lu Ten said  
what?”

The two older guardians smile at him. Iroh answers, “When Lu Ten listed the requirements for Zuko’s husband.”

The warrior blushes and rubs his head nervously, “Oh yeah. Zuko told me about that. She said I had to be a boomerang master and a friends with a saber-tooth lion moose?”

“Oh he said more than that.” Ursa and Iroh grin. “He wanted someone taller than her.”

“Someone friends with a flying lemur and saber-tooth-lion moose.”

“A master tactician and poet.”

“Funny and kind.”

“Can be a good sparring partner and a master of the boomerang.”

Sokka’s eyes are practically wide in shock. That…that pretty much fit him to a T.

“Was Lu Ten a fortuneteller?” He asks incredulously.”

Iroh and Ursa laugh. “Who knows?”

&#95;&#42;&#95;&#42;&#95;

A week after his conversation with Zuko’s family, he’s on his way to the South Pole. Then two weeks later, he’s breathing in the familiar humid air of the Fire Nation, his hands clench around an old handkerchief he keeps safely near his heart. High above the ship, he spots the familiar white and brown fur of Appa, and hears the exuberant cries of Aang. Even at twenty the young man is still so excitable.

Sokka grins and waves back, hollering at them to land on the deck. Beside him, Jee just rolls his eyes and wonders when he can finally retire. Teruko, his other half, just teases him and tells him Zuko’s been dropping hints about his retirement. He’s just too stubborn to accept her offer.

Appa lands on the deck with a resounding thud. The giant bison licks Sokka’s face and rumbles a greeting as Aang and Katara come down. They hug their brother tightly.

“We’ve missed you so much!” Aang exclaims. “This is going to be an amazing reunion!”

“You say that every year.” Sokka grins.

“Yeah but they do get more amazing every year.” The air bender retorts.

Sokka grins, “We’ll have to do our best to make it live up to standards.”

“You didn’t stay in the South Pole for long.” Katara says. “Normally you’d be there for a month if you’re going on a trip. You only stayed for two weeks.”

Her brother shrugs. “I didn’t need to stay long so I decided to head back earlier. Besides, I didn’t want to be late for the reunion.”

“Why didn’t you just take Druk with you?” Aang asks.

“Are you kidding me?” Sokka scoffs. “Zuko would throw a fit and tell me that he’s too little to start flying to the poles.”

“He’s nearly as long as Appa!”

Sokka shrugs. “Still not old enough to travel to the poles. She wants to wait another year or two and then we can take a family trip.”

“Aw that’ll be sweet.” Katara coos. “It’s like Druk is your practice baby.”

Her brother laughs nervously. “Yeah I guess so. But I think a human baby’s going to be a lot more different.”

“How? They’ll probably both breathe fire and try to burn your hair.” Aang teases.

Katara grins, “Or they could be a water bender and you’ll have to live in wet socks again.”

“Ugh, can’t I just have a nice non-bending baby?” Sokka asks to the sky.

“That can be your second one.”

“We haven’t even had *one* yet! Not to mention that we’re still not married.”

Katara scoffs, “And who’s fault is that? Aang and I ended up being the first ones married. I swear it would have been you two.”

Sokka shrugs. “Wasn’t the right time. We both wanted to wait a bit.”

“I honestly don’t know how you can keep it in for so long.” Aang says. “It was so hard waiting for me to turn eighteen before I proposed to my forever girl.”

“Aw Aang.” And Katara pecks his cheeks.

“Ugh, oogie you two.”

Katara slaps the back of his head, “You’re twenty-three and you’re still saying ‘oogi’?”

“Always have been.” He grins. “Remember I was the first one to call it between you two.”

“Yeah yeah.” Katara grumbles.

They reach the newly named, Gates of Peace in no time. In the seven years after the war, Zuko worked hard to make it a beautiful open harbor, full of colorful ships who have the colors of the nations hoisted on their poles. It’s beautiful to see the greens, blues, yellows, and reds of the world combined in a swirling harmony.

At the docks, Zuko waits for them with a young Druk who’s wagging his tail happily when he sees his ‘dad’ land.

Sokka is immediately on the dragon, giving him kisses and pets. Cooing at the overgrown beast. Druk relishes in the attention and purrs happily.

“Who’s a good boy? Yes you are! Yes you are! I bet you’ve been so good for mom!” Sokka coos in a high pitched voice.

Zuko rolls her eyes beside him while Aang and Katara hold in their laughter.

“Remember when he was really against having a dragon?” Katara whispers.

“You know how it is.” Zuko mutters. “The protesting dad end up being the most enamored.”

“I heard that.” Sokka says, turning around and kissing his beautiful girlfriend. Even after so long, she still takes his breath away. Every time he sees her long black hair swaying in the wind, the playful glint in her golden eyes, and the soft smile on her pink lips.

Druk, never one to be left out of kisses, sticks his nose in-between them and licks them both happily. Zuko laughs and gently pushes his big snout away.

“Not now Druk. It’s mom’s turn to kiss dad.” she gently chides. Druk pouts but does step away, waiting eagerly at their side for more kisses.

Sokka laughs and holds her close, lifting her feet of the ground and spinning her around.

“Sokka!” she protests.

The water tribesman is sheepish and puts her down. “Sorry, I got a little too excited to see you again.”

She rolls her eyes. “You’re worse than Druk sometimes.”

Said dragon, perks his head up at the mention of his name and wags his tail eagerly, almost as if he’s asking if it’s time for more kisses. Zuko and Sokka finally indulges and peppers kisses all over the dragon’s snout. The dragon rumbles happily and nuzzles Sokka’s cheek before nuzzling Zuko’s stomach. The man grins at the gesture and turns to Aang and his sister.

“Race you to the palace?”

Katara rolls her eyes, “Why? It’s not even a competition.” She grins.

“Oh you’re on sister.” Sokka grins back. “Ready to beat them, babe?”

“Always.” Zuko replies.

Jee just sighs as he watches the princess and Avatar leave a trail of dust behind them. He turns to the crew who are trying to hide their smiles, “Okay men, let’s unload and get the luggage in the carts.”

He could be sitting back and sipping on cold coconuts right now. Teruko unhelpfully tells him that the princess already built a beach house for them; he’s just too stubborn to retire.

&#95;&#42;&#95;&#42;&#95;

Suki and Toph are waiting for them at the entrance, both waving their arms high as Appa is the first to land, followed shortly by Druk.

Sokka pouts as he gets off the dragon. “You guys used air bending!” he accuses.

“What are you talking about?” Aang replies innocently. “Appa naturally air bends.”

Before an argument can break out, Suki and Toph both go to hug Sokka.

“Good thing you’re back soon, Snoozles.” Toph says punching his arm. “I thought you’d miss the reunion.”

“Never.” Sokka grins. “I just had some things to take care of in the South.”

“And things are okay?” Zuko asks nervously.

Sokka smiles at her and takes her hand. “Obviously. You don’t have to worry about anything.”

“You’re such a mother-hen.” Suki teases. “Now let’s go. I’m starving and the food in the dining room smells *amazing*.”

The group catch up and the room is filled with their laughter and jokes. Toph triumphantly tells them of her metal bending students and Suki reports that the people in the United Republic are doing well. Sokka grins throughout the meal, keeping close to Zuko as they revel in the good times. Iroh, Kiyi, and Ursa join them later for tea and dessert. By the time they’re done, everyone’s nearly laying on the floor with full warm stomachs and ready for bed.

Eventually, Zuko tells them that if they want to head to Ember Island tomorrow, they should get some rest.

“Ugh just give me a few minutes.” Toph groans. “I need to let my food babies digest.”

“Aww that’s so sad.” Aang says. “I don’t want to digest my babies.”

“No one’s digesting babies.” Sokka declares. “Now c’mon.”

He’s greeted with a chorus of groans, and Iroh laughs. “Why don’t you two head to your rooms. I’ll make sure these youngsters get to their rooms alright.” He says.

Zuko smiles and hugs Iroh. “Okay, just don’t let them bully you. You need your rest, Uncle. And you too, Mother.”

Iroh laughs and pats her hand. “Yes yes, now head to bed. You have a big day ahead tomorrow.”

Ursa gives her a kiss on the forehead, “Go ahead, dear. We’ll help your friends.”

Sokka takes Zuko’s hand and they head out.

Once their footsteps disappear, Toph sits up and grins. “They’re heading to the garden.” She says. “I bet he’s going to do it under the wisteria-willow tree.”

Aang jumps up and smiles widely, “Ooohh I can’t wait.” He squeals. “It’s going to be so romantic.”

“Put me up on your shoulders so I can see better!” Kiyi demands tugging on Aang’s robes. The Avatar laughs and complies. Hefting her up to his shoulders.

“I’ve already got the other Kiyoshi warriors on the lookout and they’ll help us sneak around them.” Suki tells them. “Plus Mai, Azula, and Ty Lee are waiting there too.”

“Great.” Katara says. “Let’s go. I can’t believe, Sokka tried to keep this a secret from me.” she complains.

“How did you even find out?” Aang asks.

Katara grins, “Gran Gran told me. He and dad tried to be discrete, but those two really don’t know how to keep a secret. She sent a letter immediately.”

“Less talking, more walking!” Toph declares impatiently. “I don’t want to miss it.”

“Yeah!” Kiyi agrees.

Iroh and Ursa just give each other secretive smiles as they walk with the others towards the gardens. This will be fun.

They head out to the gardens and wait with baited breath as they see Sokka and Zuko already sitting under the wisteria-willow tree heads bent and smiling softly at each other.

Sokka nervously rubs Zuko’s hand as he swallows the thick lump in his throat. “I know I shouldn’t be so nervous, but it’s still pretty nerve wracking.”

“Want me to do it then?” Zuko asks with a sly grin.

Sokka laughs, “No, you’ve already got enough to do. I just need to get my head out of my ass.”

The torchlight illuminating the garden really brings out that golden hue in Zuko’s eyes, and Sokka feels his heart stutter. He knows his eyes are welling up, but he can’t help himself. This woman is just so beautiful that it’s bringing him to tears. Is that even possible?

Finally, he takes a breath and reaches into his pocket. He pulls out an old white handkerchief with frogs and boomerangs embroidered on it. He unwraps the handkerchief and reveals a shiny betrothal necklace. The ribbon is a twined blue and red leather and the stone center contains the High Fire Nation letters, “My Love” etched on it. Crowning the symbols is the picture of Sokka’s boomerang.

He holds it up to her and asks, “Will you marry me?”

A tear falls from her eyes, and she smiles sweetly as she takes his hands. “Yes. Of course, I will Sokka.”

Elation floods through him as his hands tremble, tying the necklace on her. It falls perfectly onto the hollow of her neck, and Sokka takes a minute to admire how his work looks on her ivory skin. Once she feels the cool stone start to heat under her skin, she tilts her head towards Sokka, and the man leans into the familiar motion as they kiss under the swinging flowers.

They smile as they part and Sokka can’t stop the wide elated smile from overtaking his face. “I can’t wait to tell everyone.”

“They have been waiting a while.” Zuko laughs.

Sokka rubs his head sheepishly. “I know. I know we both wanted to wait a little longer, and I honestly wouldn’t have minded putting it off for another year, but…”

Before he could finish, Aang jumps out from a hidden spot in the garden with Kiyi squealing on top of his shoulders. Wind whipping around him as he tries to contain his excitement.

“I can’t stand it anymore I have to *hug* you guys!!” he hollers.

“That was amazing big bro Sokka!”

Sokka rolls his eyes but allows his brother-in-law and sister-in-law to bring them both into a hug. The rest of their friends emerge from different parts of the garden, all bearing wide smiles.

“Okay how did you guys know?” Sokka asks exasperatedly.

“Gran Gran.” Is Katara’s reply. The newly engaged warrior sighs. Of course, but he honestly shouldn’t expect any less. They were going to wait for the morning to tell them, but this is good too. Zuko is smiling at Ty Lee who’s gushing over the necklace and saying how romantic it was while Mai just grins and tells her that it’s about time. Azula has her arms crossed and peers at the necklace critically.

“Hmm, it’s not bad.” She admits. “I guess you didn’t waste your time in the South Pole.”

Ty Lee gasps, “Azula, did you know before today?”

She rolls her eyes. “Obviously. That man had to ask for our ‘blessings’ before he asked Zuko. Not like my sister would have said no. She’s too smitten by him.”

“You said some nice things to me that day.” Sokka grins.

“I can still burn you alive.” She glares.

Sokka brings his hands up in surrender. “Okay no burning.”

“We should head to the bar to celebrate!” Toph shouts. “I’ll make it rain alcohol.

Zuko winces. “Ah I don’t think that’ll be good.”

Toph narrows her eyes. “Why not?”

The newly engaged couple look to each other, and Sokka just shrugs with a ‘they’ll figure it out eventually’ look on his face.

She takes a breath and says, “Because I’m pregnant.”

&#46;

&#46;

&#46;

Then everyone shouted all at once.

“YOU’RE PREGNANT?!!”

“Your aura has been looking hazier.”

“Sokka you got her pregnant!?”

“I’m gunna be a big sister!”

“I thought it was just your arrhythmia!”

“I’m going to have a *grandchild*!”

“You almost had a child out of *wedlock*?!”

Sokka and Zuko let them shout themselves out before finally speaking.

“Yes, I’m pregnant.” Zuko replies. “And I was a little afraid that you’d burn him alive if he didn’t marry me before the baby was born.” She says to Azula.

The younger girl nods imperiously. “Well obviously. It’s not befitting of a royal heir to be born from wedlock.” She says. “Especially since it’ll be my future niece or nephew.”

“I would have married her regardless.” Sokka retorts. “I know we were taking our time with it, but I guess we’ve waited long enough, and if I’m going to coin Aang’s term: Zuko’s my forever girl. It’s why we never really worried too much about when we’d be getting married.”

Zuko nods, “Sokka’s my forever boy and it didn’t matter to me if we were wed or not. It’s just that with the little one coming in, it seemed like a sign from the spirits.”

Everyone smiles at the sweet sentiment before Katara pauses.

“Wait…how far along are you?” Katara asks.

“Three months now.” Zuko admits. “Dr. Kusuri promised to keep it secret from *everyone*.”

“I should have known.” Iroh sighs. “He’s been looking particularly smug during our pai sho games.”

“Not only that we have less than six months to plan your wedding!” Katara exclaims. “Is that why you went to the South?” she points to Sokka.

He grins, “Yeah it’s for the betrothal necklace and to ask Dad to come over here soon for the wedding and wedding preparations.

Mai sighs, “You two are unbelievable. You’re lucky you’re the Fire Lord or this might be nearly impossible.”

“Sorry Mai. But I believe in you and Ty Lee.” Zuko says.

The gloomy girl groans and lets herself be dragged into Ty Lee’s happy dance.

Kiyi grins and puts her hands on Zuko’s stomach. “I get to be a big sister.” She says happily.

Zuko smiles and pats Kiyi’s head. “Yes, you’ll have to take good care of the baby okay?”

“I will. I’ll be the best big sister. Just like you.” Kiyi grins.

Zuko and Sokka have to clutch at each other to keep themselves from crying.

While their friends and family chatter and bicker about the incoming wedding, Sokka and Zuko just sit by watching with amusement. Sokka hooks his pinky with hers and puts a kiss on it. A familiar, but comforting motion he’s done throughout the years.

“I promised you, didn’t I?” he says.

“You did; you always do.” Zuko smiles and leans against him, letting herself feel the blissful contentment as the wisteria-willow branches flutter overhead.

***End***
